Penpulimab-kcqx, as a single agent, is indicated for the treatment of adults with metastatic non-keratinizing NPC and disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of therapy.

L41: ## 1. Executive Summary
L42: ## 1.1. Product Introduction and Regulatory Background
L43: Penpulimab-kcqx (hereafter referred to as penpulimab) is a human IgG1 monoclonal antibody directed against programmed death receptor-1 (PD-1), thereby blocking the immunosuppressive interaction between PD-1 and programmed cell death ligand 1 (PD-L1) and programmed cell death ligand 2 (PD-L2). It is not approved for any indication in the U.S.
L44: An initial Biologics Licensing Application (BLA) was submitted for penpulimab as a single-agent for patients with previously treated nasopharyngeal carcinoma (NPC) on July 16, 2021, based on the results of the single-arm trial, Study AK105-202. FDA issued a Complete Response on January 19, 2024, as penpulimab did not demonstrate a clear advantage over currently available therapeutic options due to changes in the treatment landscape for NPC, to grant accelerated approval. Additionally, deficiencies were identified in CGMP operations in the penpulimab drug substance and drug product manufacturing facility, and in the manufacture of penpulimab.
L45: On February 27, 2024, the Applicant requested a Type B meeting under IND 138576 to discuss whether an interim analysis of the randomized trial, Study AK105-304, could be conducted to support a BLA for penpulimab in combination with either cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine for the first-line treatment of patients with recurrent or metastatic NPC. The Applicant also wanted to discuss plans to address the chemistry, manufacturing, and controls deficiencies identified in the Complete Response Letter. The meeting was granted with preliminary comments from the FDA issued to the Applicant on April 12, 2024. After additional email communications between the Applicant and FDA on April 17, 2024, and April 19, 2024, agreement was reached to cancel the Type B meeting.
L46: In the current Class 2 BLA resubmission, the Applicant is seeking two indications in NPC with two different dosing regimens. For the Applicant's proposed indication for use "in combination with gemcitabine and cisplatin or carboplatin, as first-line treatment of patients with recurrent or metastatic nasopharyngeal carcinoma", the proposed dosage regimen is penpulimab 200 mg intravenously (IV) once every three weeks (Q3W).
L47: The Applicant's proposed indication for penpulimab as a single agent is for "the treatment of adults with metastatic non-keratinizing NPC with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of therapy". The Applicant proposed dosage regimen is penpulimab 200 mg IV once every two weeks (Q2W).
L48: ## 1.2. Conclusions on the Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
L49: The Applicant intends for this marketing application to support approval of penpulimab for two indications: 1) in combination with either cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine for the first-line treatment of patients with metastatic or recurrent NPC and 2) as a single agent for the treatment of adult patients with metastatic non-keratinizing NPC with disease progression on or after platinum-containing chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of therapy.
L50: The primary study providing evidence in support of the first-line indication is Study AK105-304. In Study AK105-304, 291 adult patients with metastatic or recurrent WNPC who had not received previous systemic chemotherapy for recurrent or metastatic disease were randomized (1:1) to receive either penpulimab in combination with cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine Q3W for up to 6 cycles followed by single-agent penpulimab Q3W or placebo Q3W in combination with cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine Q3W for up to 6 cycles followed by single-agent placebo Q3W. Treatment was administered until disease progression, unacceptable toxicity, or a maximum of 24 months. The primary efficacy endpoint was progression free survival (PFS) by blinded independent review committee (BICR) per RECIST v.1.1, with a key secondary endpoint of overall survival (OS). At an interim analysis of PFS based on 189 events (85% information fraction [IF]), the trial demonstrated a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in BICR-assessed PFS favoring the penpulimab arm, with a hazard ratio (HR) of 0.45 (95% CI 0.33, 0.62, p-value &lt;0.0001), corresponding to a median PFS of 9.6 months (95% CI 7.1, 12.5) in the penpulimab arm and 7.0 months (95% CI 6.9, 7.3) in the placebo arm. Although the observed 2.6-month improvement in median PFS is modest for an add-on therapy, the treatment effect, as measured by the HR, was consistent across subgroups and withstood rigorous sensitivity analyses, highlighting the robustness and reliability of the results. These results are also supported by a longer median DOR in the penpulimab arm of 9.8 months (95% CI: 7, 17.4) compared to 5.7 months (95% CI: 5.5, 6.7) in the placebo arm. While a statistically significant improvement in OS has not been demonstrated in Study AK105-304, the OS results at the first formal interim analysis (70% IF) indicate no detrimental effect of penpulimab on OS. At this interim analysis, the OS HR was 0.91 (95% CI: 0.64, 1.31), with median OS not reached in the penpulimab arm and 31.3 months (95% CI: 27.4, NE) in the placebo arm. Notably, 80% of patients receiving placebo either crossed over to open-label treatment with penpulimab or received another anti-PD-(L)1 antibody as the next subsequent therapy upon confirmed disease progression, which may impact interpretability of the OS results. OS will be tested again at a second prespecified interim analysis (80% IF) and a final analysis.
L51: Study AK105-202 provides the primary efficacy data in support of the indication for the treatment of patients with non-keratinizing NPC with disease progression on or after platinum-containing chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of therapy. In Study AK105-202, 125 patients with metastatic non-keratinizing NPC received penpulimab 200 mg IV Q2W until disease progression, unacceptable toxicity, or a maximum of 24 months. The major efficacy
L52: ## Penpulimab
L53: outcome measures were objective response rate (ORR) and duration of response (DOR) according to RECIST v1.1 as assessed by an Independent Radiology Review Committee (IRRC). The IRRC-assessed ORR was 28% (95% CI: 20, 37) and the median duration of response (DOR) was not reached (95% CI: 9.2, not evaluable). Among the 35 patients with an objective response, 46% had an observed DOR of \ge 12 months. The Study AK105-202 results are considered clinically meaningful for the evaluated patient population, and the results of the randomized Study AK105-304 provide further confidence in the benefit of penpulimab for the treatment of patients with advanced NPC.
L54: A major limitation of this application was that Study AK105-304 was conducted mostly in China (96% of randomized patients) and Study AK105-202 was conducted exclusively in China. Section 21 CFR 314.106(b) outlines three requirements to establish the acceptability of foreign data as the sole basis for marketing approval:
L55: - The foreign data are applicable to the U.S. population and U.S. medical practice.
- The studies have been performed by clinical investigators of recognized competence.
- FDA is able to validate the data through an onsite inspection or other appropriate means.
L56: FDA will apply this policy in a flexible manner according to the nature of the drug and data being considered.
L57: A flexible regulatory approach is warranted given the high unmet need for effective treatment options for NPC. These trials were initiated prior to the approval of regimens with toripalimab, the only other anti-PD-(L)1 antibody approved for NPC in the U.S. Cisplatin or carboplatin plus gemcitabine was considered an appropriate control arm in Study AK105-304 when the trial was designed, making the study results relevant to U.S. medical practice. Cisplatin plus gemcitabine was considered standard of care for the first-line treatment of recurrent or metastatic NPC in the U.S., and carboplatin plus gemcitabine was considered an appropriate option for patients who may be unable to tolerate or who have contraindications to cisplatin. The incidence of NPC is much higher in East Asia relative to the U.S., where NPC is a relatively rare disease, making enrollment to clinical trials more feasible in that region of the world. Clinical site inspections were conducted by FDA to validate the data supporting this application. To further address the issue of applicability to the U.S. population, the Applicant will be collecting additional clinical data to further characterize the safety and efficacy of penpulimab in combination with either cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine for the treatment of non-keratinizing NPC in a study population whose demographic and disease characteristics are more representative of the population of U.S. patients with NPC (see Postmarketing Commitments later in this review for details).
L58: Although patients with both non-keratinizing and keratinizing NPC were eligible for enrollment into Study AK105-304, only one patient (0.7%) with keratinizing histology was enrolled to the penpulimab plus chemotherapy arm. While this patient had a confirmed response to the
L59: ## Penpulimab
L60: investigational therapy, the contribution of penpulimab to the overall treatment effect cannot be isolated as it was administered in combination with chemotherapy. Only patients with non-keratinizing NPC were eligible for enrollment into Study AK105-202. No additional data are available outside of these two trials evaluating the anti-tumor activity of penpulimab as a single agent in patients with keratinizing NPC. Given almost no clinical data available for penpulimab in patients with keratinizing NPC, FDA recommends that the indications are limited to patients with non-keratinizing histology.
L61: The clinical data demonstrate substantial evidence of effectiveness and a positive risk-benefit assessment, which are supportive of traditional approval for penpulimab for the following indications:
L62: - Penpulimab, in combination with either cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine for the first-line treatment of adults with recurrent or metastatic non-keratinizing NPC.
- Penpulimab, as a single agent, for the treatment of adults with metastatic non-keratinizing NPC and disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of therapy.
L63: ## 1.3. Benefit-Risk Assessment (BRA)
L64: ## Benefit-Risk Summary and Assessment
L65: There were 133,354 new cases of NPC diagnosed globally in 2020, and approximately 80,000 people died from NPC in 2020. More than 75% of the global cases of NPC develop in South and East Asia, compared with 1.6% in Northern America. NPC is rare in the U.S., with &lt;1 case per 100,000 people each year. The World Health Organization classifies NPC in three major subtypes: non-keratinizing undifferentiated, non-keratinizing differentiated, and keratinizing squamous cell carcinoma. While the non-keratinizing undifferentiated subtype of NPC is the predominant subtype in both Southern China (95%) and the U.S. (63%), the keratinizing subtype of NPC is uncommon in Southern China (2%) relative to the US (25%).
L66: The prognosis for patients with recurrent, unresectable, or metastatic NPC is poor. On October 27, 2023, FDA approved toripalimab (a monoclonal antibody targeting programmed cell death protein 1 [PD-1]) with cisplatin and gemcitabine for the first-line treatment of adults with metastatic or recurrent, locally advanced NPC, based on the randomized, double-blind JUPITER-O2 trial. In the JUPITER-O2 trial, toripalimab with cisplatin and gemcitabine demonstrated a median PFS of 11.7 months and a median OS not yet reached. Patients who received placebo with cisplatin and gemcitabine had a median PFS of 8.0 months and median OS of 33.7 months. On the same day, FDA also approved toripalimab as a single agent for adult patients with recurrent unresectable or metastatic NPC with disease progression on or after a platinum-containing therapy based on the results of the open-label, multicohort POLARIS-O2 trial, which demonstrated an ORR as assessed by BICR of 21% and a median DOR of 14.9 months. Despite these recent approvals, metastatic NPC remains a fatal, incurable disease, and an unmet need exists for patients with advanced NPC.
L67: Penpulimab is a monoclonal antibody targeting PD-1, an immune checkpoint inhibitor with an established pharmacological class of PD-1 blocking antibody. The proposed dosage regimen for penpulimab in combination with cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine is 200 mg intravenously (IV) once every 3 weeks (Q3W). The Applicant's proposed dosage regimen for penpulimab when administered as a single agent is 200 mg IV Q2W. The Applicant's proposed indications for penpulimab are: 1) penpulimab, in combination with either cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine, for the first-line treatment of adults with recurrent or metastatic NPC, and 2) penpulimab, as a single agent, for the treatment
L68: of adults with metastatic non-keratinizing NPC and disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of therapy.
L69: The primary study supporting the first-line indication is Study AK105-304, in which 291 patients with recurrent or metastatic NPC were randomized 1:1 to receive either penpulimab 200 mg IV Q3W in combination with 6 cycles of cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine (n=144) or placebo with cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine (n=147). The primary efficacy endpoint was PFS by BICR per RECIST v1.1, with a key secondary endpoint of OS. An interim analysis of PFS was initially proposed at 155 events (70% IF) but was conducted at 189 events (85% IF) instead. This overrun was a result of a change in the analysis population that stemmed from difficulty enrolling patients outside of Asia, due to the rarity of NPC in other parts of the world, and the approval of toripalimab plus cisplatin and gemcitabine during the conduct of the trial. These changes in the analysis plan were deemed acceptable to the FDA review team for this particular application. At the interim analysis of PFS based on 189 events, the trial demonstrated a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in BICR-assessed PFS favoring the penpulimab arm, with a HR of 0.45 (95% CI 0.33, 0.62, p-value &lt;0.0001), corresponding to a median PFS of 9.6 months (95% CI 7.1, 12.5) in the penpulimab arm and 7.0 months (95% CI 6.9, 7.3) in the placebo arm. In total, 31% and 11% of patients were alive and progression free after 12 months of follow-up in the penpulimab and placebo arms, respectively. The presence of a tail in the Kaplan-Meier PFS curves, which begin to separate at approximately 7 months, suggests that some patients may derive prolonged PFS benefit or that there is potentially late treatment effect with penpulimab, which are not captured by median PFS alone. The PFS treatment effect, as measured by the HR, was consistent across subgroups and withstood rigorous sensitivity analyses, highlighting the robustness and reliability of the results. The ORR was 68% (95% CI: 60, 76) for the penpulimab arm and 64% (95% CI: 56, 72) for the placebo arm. There was a longer median DOR in the penpulimab arm of 9.8 months (95% CI: 7, 17.4) compared to 5.7 months (95% CI: 5.5, 6.7) in the placebo arm.
L70: While a statistically significant improvement in OS has not been demonstrated in Study AK105-304, the OS results at the first formal interim analysis (70% IF) indicate no detrimental effect of penpulimab on OS. At this interim analysis, the OS HR was 0.91 (95% CI: 0.64, 1.31), with median OS not reached in the penpulimab arm and 31.3 months (95% CI: 27.4, NE) in the placebo arm. Notably, 49% of patients receiving placebo crossed over to open-label treatment with penpulimab upon confirmed disease progression, and an additional 31% of patients on the placebo arm received an anti-PD-(L)1 antibody as the next subsequent therapy outside of Study AK105-304, which may impact interpretability of the OS results. OS will be tested again at a second prespecified interim analysis (80% IF) and a final analysis.
L71: Regarding the indication for penpulimab as a single agent, Study AK105-202 provides the primary efficacy data which evaluated patients with metastatic, non-keratinizing NPC with disease progression on or after platinum-containing chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of
L72: ## Penpulimab
L73: therapy. In Study AK105-202, 125 patients with metastatic, non-keratinizing NPC received penpulimab 200 mg IV Q2W until disease progression, unacceptable toxicity, or a maximum of 24 months. The major efficacy outcome measures were ORR and DOR according to RECIST v1.1 as assessed by an IRRC. The IRRC-assessed ORR was 28% (95% CI 20, 37) and the median DOR was not reached (95% CI 9.2, not evaluable). Among the 35 patients with an objective response, 46% had a DOR of \ge12 months. These results are considered clinically meaningful when considering the intended patient population, and the results of Study AK105-304 provide further confidence in the benefit of penpulimab for the treatment of advanced NPC. Given the recent approval of toripalimab plus cisplatin and gemcitabine, and the results of Study AK105-304 for penpulimab, it is expected that the majority of U.S. patients with NPC will receive an anti-PD-(L)1 antibody in combination with chemotherapy as first-line treatment; however, there will be patients with NPC who may have already received first-line treatment with chemotherapy alone and a subsequent line of therapy who would be eligible to receive and may benefit from penpulimab as a single agent.
L74: Among the 146 patients who received penpulimab in combination with cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine in Study AK105-304, the most common (\ge20%) adverse reactions were nausea (58%), vomiting (55%), hypothyroidism (45%), constipation (41%), decreased appetite (36%), decreased weight (26%), cough (25%), COVID-19 infection (25%), fatigue (25%), rash (24%), and pyrexia (21%). Serious adverse reactions occurred in 51% of patients. The most frequent serious adverse reactions (\ge2%) were thrombocytopenia (19%), decreased neutrophils (14%), decreased white blood cells (12%), anemia (8%), myelosuppression (3.4%), decreased sodium (2.7%), pneumonia (2.1%), and nausea (2.1%). There was one fatal adverse reaction (0.7%) due to syndrome of inappropriate antidiuretic hormone secretion (SIADH). Permanent discontinuation of penpulimab due to an adverse reaction occurred in 3.4% of patients. Adverse reactions which resulted in permanent discontinuation of penpulimab were acute myocardial infarction, acute kidney injury, brain edema, diabetic ketoacidosis, increased potassium, increased lipase, and thrombocytopenia (0.7% each).
L75: The evaluation of safety for penpulimab as a single agent included analyses of safety data for 130 patients with NPC treated with penpulimab 200 mg IV Q2W as a single agent in Study AK105-202 and a monotherapy pooled population of 372 patients with advanced solid tumors (including the 130 patients with NPC) or classical Hodgkin lymphoma treated with penpulimab 200 mg IV Q2W. Among the 372 patients who received single agent penpulimab, the most common (\ge20%) adverse reactions were anemia (25%) and hypothyroidism (23%). Among the 130 patients with NPC treated with penpulimab 200 mg IV Q2W as a single agent in Study AK105-202, the most common (\ge20%) adverse reactions were hypothyroidism (39%) and musculoskeletal pain (25%). Serious adverse reactions occurred in 22% of these 130 patients. Serious adverse reactions in \ge1% were pneumonia (3.8%), pneumonitis (1.5%), respiratory failure (1.5%), and rash (1.5%). Fatal adverse reactions occurred in 1% of patients with NPC treated with single-agent penpulimab, including a case each of pneumonitis, septic shock, colitis, and hepatitis. Permanent discontinuation of penpulimab due to an adverse reaction occurred in 3.8% of patients with NPC. Adverse reactions which resulted
L76: ## Penpulimab
L77: in permanent discontinuation of penpulimab in \ge 1\% of patients with NPC were rash, hepatitis, herpes zoster, spinal cord compression and pleural effusion (0.8% each).
L78: Immune-related adverse events (irAEs) occurred in 31% of patients (including Grade 3 to 4 irAEs in 4.1% of patients) in Study AK105-304 and 22% of patients (including Grade 3 to 4 irAEs in 10% of patients) in Study AK105-202. In Study AK105-304, the most common (\ge 2\%) irAEs included hypothyroidism and dermatologic toxicity. There were no fatal immune-related adverse reactions. In the monotherapy pool, irAEs occurring at an incidence \ge 2\% included hypothyroidism and dermatologic toxicity. Infusion related reactions occurred in 3.4% and 10% of patients in Study AK105-304 and Study AK105-202, respectively.
L79: The overall safety profile of penpulimab was similar to that observed with other PD-1 blocking antibodies. Safety issues identified as significant and serious during the BLA review were immune-related adverse events and infusion-related reactions, which are adequately addressed by the information in the Warnings and Precautions section included in product labeling. There were no significant safety concerns identified during BLA review requiring risk management beyond labeling or warranting consideration for Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategy (REMS). Penpulimab appears to have an acceptable safety profile when assessed in the context of a life-threatening disease with unmet medical need.
L80: A major limitation of the data is that the two primary trials supporting this marketing application were conducted predominantly or entirely in China. Of the 291 randomized patients in Study AK105-304, only 11 patients were enrolled from outside of China, specifically from the U.S., Canada, and Brazil. Study AK105-202 trial was conducted exclusively in China. Therefore, the patient populations in the trials are not reflective of the racial and ethnic demographics of patients with NPC in the U.S. While both Studies AK105-304 and AK105-202 were conducted predominantly in China, a flexible regulatory approach is warranted given the high unmet need for treatment options for patients with NPC. These trials were initiated prior to the approval of regimens with toripalimab, the only other FDA-approved anti-PD-(L)1 antibody for U.S. patients with NPC. Cisplatin or carboplatin plus gemcitabine was considered an appropriate control arm in Study AK105-304 when the trial was designed, making the study results relevant to U.S. medical practice. Cisplatin plus gemcitabine was considered standard of care for the first-line treatment of recurrent or metastatic NPC in the U.S., and carboplatin plus gemcitabine was considered an appropriate option for patients who may be unable to tolerate or who have contraindications to cisplatin. The incidence of NPC is much higher in East Asia relative to the U.S., where NPC is a relatively rare disease, making enrollment to clinical trials more feasible in that region of the world. Clinical site inspections were conducted by FDA to validate the data supporting this application. To further address the issue of applicability to the U.S. population, the Applicant will be collecting additional clinical data to further characterize the safety and efficacy of penpulimab in combination with either cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine for the treatment of non-keratinizing NPC in a study population whose demographic and disease
L81: characteristics are more representative of the population of U.S. patients with NPC (see Postmarketing Commitments later in this review for details).
L82: Although patients with both non-keratinizing and keratinizing NPC were eligible for enrollment into Study AK105-304, only one patient (0.7%) with keratinizing histology was enrolled to the penpulimab plus chemotherapy arm. While this patient had a confirmed response to the investigational therapy, the contribution of penpulimab to the overall treatment effect cannot be isolated as it was administered in combination with chemotherapy. Only patients with non-keratinizing NPC were eligible for enrollment into Study AK105-202. No additional data are available outside of these two trials evaluating the anti-tumor activity of penpulimab as a single agent in patients with keratinizing NPC. The available data do not provide sufficient evidence to support the efficacy and safety of penpulimab in patients with keratinizing NPC. Keratinizing and non-keratinizing NPC are biologically distinct subtypes with different etiologies, molecular profiles, and responses to treatment. Non-keratinizing NPC is strongly associated with Epstein-Barr virus (EBV) infection and typically demonstrates higher sensitivity to immunotherapy and chemotherapeutic agents. In contrast, keratinizing NPC is more frequently linked to environmental factors such as smoking and exhibits different treatment response patterns. Given almost no clinical data available for penpulimab in patients with keratinizing NPC, FDA recommends that the indications are limited to patients with non-keratinizing histology.
L83: The results of Study AK105-304 and Study AK105-202 support a positive risk-benefit assessment and are supportive of traditional approval of penpulimab for the following indications:
L84: - Penpulimab, in combination with either cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine for the first-line treatment of adults with recurrent or metastatic non-keratinizing NPC.
- Penpulimab, as a single agent, for the treatment of adults with metastatic non-keratinizing NPC and disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of therapy.
L85: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Analysis of Condition	Nasopharyngeal carcinoma originates in the nasopharynx from the base of the skull to the palate.
There were 133,354 cases worldwide in 2020 with the majority of cases (>75%) in South and East Asia and 1.6% in Northern America, and approximately 80,000 people died from NPC in 2020. NPC is rare in the United States, with <1 case per 100,000 people each year.
There are three major histologic subtypes of NPC based on the WHO classification, with differences in subtype distribution between North America and China: Type 1 keratinizing squamous cell carcinoma (25% vs 2%), Type 2 non-keratinizing differentiated carcinoma (12 vs 3%), and Type 3 non-keratinizing undifferentiated carcinoma (63% vs 95%).
For metastatic NPC, the median OS is 29.1 months with first-line platinum-based chemotherapy alone. A statistically significant OS improvement was also observed with median OS not reached (95% CI: 38.7 months, not estimable) for toripalimab-tpzi in combination with platinum-based chemotherapy (based on the POLARIS-O2 trial).
The incidence of NPC is substantially higher in Southeast Asia relative to the U.S. A higher proportion of patients with NPC in North America develop the keratinizing squamous subtype compared with China, where the vast majority of patients develop the non-keratinizing undifferentiated subtype.
The prognosis for metastatic NPC remains poor with limited therapeutic options.
L86: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Current Treatment Options	Toripalimab-tpzi, in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine, is approved for the first-line treatment of metastatic or recurrent NPC based on results from the JUPITER-02 trial. The trial demonstrated a median PFS of 11.7 months with toripalimab-tpzi versus 8.0 months with placebo, both in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine (HR 0.52; 95% CI: 0.36–0.74; p=0.0003). A statistically significant OS improvement was also observed with median OS not reached (95% CI: 38.7 months, not estimable) for the toripalimab-tpzi-containing regimen and 33.7 months (95% CI: 27.0, 44.2) for the placebo-containing regimen (HR 0.63 [95% CI: 0.45, 0.89], p-value=0.0083).
Toripalimab-tpzi is also approved for NPC with disease progression on or after a platinum-containing chemotherapy based on an ORR of 21% (95% CI 15, 28) and median DOR of 14.9 months (95% CI: 10.3, not estimable) in the POLARIS-02 trial.
Prior to the approval of toripalimab-tpzi, in the metastatic setting, standard of care treatment for first line therapy was cisplatin plus gemcitabine, with a reported median PFS of 7.0 months and median OS of 29.1 months.
Metastatic NPC represents a life-threatening disease. For patients with metastatic or recurrent NPC, FDA approved toripalimab-tpzi in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine in the first line setting and toripalimab-tpzi as a single agent with disease progression on or after a platinum-containing therapy.
Benefit	The primary trials supporting this BLA are Study AK105-304 and Study AK105-202.
The efficacy of penpulimab combined with cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine was assessed in Study AK105-304, a randomized, double-blind, multi-center trial. A total of 291 patients with recurrent or metastatic NPC received either penpulimab or a placebo alongside cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine every three weeks for up to six cycles. This was followed by single agent penpulimab or placebo every
The trial demonstrated a statistically robust improvement in the primary endpoint of BICR-assessed PFS. Efficacy is further demonstrated with a longer median DOR in the penpulimab plus chemotherapy arm vs the placebo plus chemotherapy arm. While not statistically significant, the results for
L87: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
three weeks until disease progression, unacceptable toxicity, or a maximum duration of 24 months.
Study AK105-304 met its primary endpoint of BICR-assessed PFS at an interim analysis of PFS (85% IF). Median PFS was 9.6 (95% CI: 7.1, 12.5) in the penpulimab arm versus 7.0 (95% CI: 6.9, 7.3) in the placebo arm, with a PFS HR of 0.45 (95% CI: 0.33, 0.66) and p<0.0001.
In Study AK105-304, the ORR was 68% in the penpulimab arm and 64% in the placebo arm. The secondary endpoint of DOR demonstrated that penpulimab with cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine had a longer median DOR at 9.8 months (95% CI: 7.0, 17.4) compared to cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine alone at 5.7 months (95% CI: 5.5, 6.7).
In Study AK105-304, OS results were not statistically significant at the first formal interim analysis, with 70% of pre-specified OS events having occurred in the overall population. At this interim analysis, the OS HR was 0.91 (95% CI: 0.64, 1.31), with median OS not reached in the penpulimab arm and 31.3 months (95% CI: 27.4, NE) in the placebo arm. In total, 80% of patients on the placebo arm received penpulimab or another anti-PD-(L)1 antibody following confirmed disease progression, which may impact interpretability of OS results.

These results provide substantial evidence of effectiveness for penpulimab, in combination with cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine, for the first-line treatment of adults with recurrent or metastatic non-keratinizing NPC.
Regarding an indication for the treatment of adults with metastatic non-keratinizing NPC after disease progression on platinum-containing chemotherapy and at least one additional line of therapy, the ORR of 28% with 46% of responders having a response \ge 12 months, demonstrated in Study AK105-202, is considered clinically meaningful for the intended patient population. Furthermore, the results of Study AK105-304 provide further confidence in the benefit of penpulimab for advanced NPC.
L88: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
The efficacy of single-agent penpulimab was evaluated in Study AK105-202, an open-label, multicenter, single-arm trial conducted in a single country (China). A total of 125 patients with unresectable or metastatic non-keratinizing NPC, who had disease progression after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other line of therapy, received single-agent penpulimab 200 mg IV Q2W until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity, or a maximum of 24 months. The ORR per BICR was 28% (95% CI: 20, 37) with a median DOR not reached (95% CI: 9.2, not estimable). Among the 35 patients with an objective response, 83% had an observed DOR \ge6 months and 46% had an observed DOR \ge12 months.

Risk and Risk Management	FDA's safety analyses for penpulimab 200 mg Q3W in combination with cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine was assessed in 146 patients treated in Study AK105-304.
FDA's evaluation of safety for penpulimab as a single agent focused on analyses of safety data for 125 patients with NPC treated with penpulimab 200 mg Q2W as a single agent in Study AK105-202 and a pooled population of 372 patients with advanced solid tumors or classical Hodgkin lymphoma who were treated with penpulimab 200 mg Q2W (monotherapy pool).
Serious adverse reactions occurred in 51% of patients in Study AK105-304; the most common serious adverse reactions (\ge2%) were thrombocytopenia (19%), decreased neutrophils (14%), decreased white blood cells (12%), anemia (8%), myelosuppression (3.4%), decreased sodium (2.7%), pneumonia (2.1%), and nausea (2.1%). There was one (0.7%) fatal adverse reaction due to SIADH.
The overall safety profile of penpulimab is similar to other PD-1 blocking antibodies.
Safety issues identified as significant and serious during the BLA review were immune-related adverse events and infusion-related reactions, which are adequately addressed by the information in the Warnings and Precautions section included in product labeling. There were no significant safety concerns identified during BLA review requiring risk management beyond labeling or warranting consideration for Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategy (REMS).
L89: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Permanent discontinuation of penpulimab due to an adverse reaction occurred in 3.4% of patients in Study AK105-304. Adverse reactions which resulted in permanent discontinuation of penpulimab were acute myocardial infarction, acute kidney injury, brain edema, diabetic ketoacidosis, increased potassium, increased lipase, and thrombocytopenia (0.7% each).
Serious adverse reactions occurred in 22% in Study AK105-202. Serious adverse reactions in \ge 1\% in Study AK105-202 were pneumonia (3.8%), pneumonitis (1.5%), respiratory failure (1.5%), and rash (1.5%). Fatal adverse reactions occurred in 1% of patients treated with penpulimab, including a case each of pneumonitis, septic shock, colitis, and hepatitis.
Permanent discontinuation of penpulimab due to an adverse reaction occurred in 3.8% of patients in AK105-202. Adverse reactions which resulted in permanent discontinuation of penpulimab in \ge 1\% in Study AK105-202 were rash, hepatitis, herpes zoster, spinal cord compression and pleural effusion (0.8% each).
Immune-related adverse events (irAEs) occurred in 31% of patients (Grade 3 to 4, 4.1%) in Study AK105-304 and 22% of patients (Grade 3 to 4, 10%) in Study AK105-202.
In Study AK105-304, the most common irAEs \ge 2\% were hypothyroidism and dermatologic toxicity. There were no fatal immune-related adverse reactions. In Study AK105-202, irAEs occurring at an incidence \ge 2\% were hypothyroidism and dermatologic toxicity.
Infusion-related reactions occurred in 3.4% and 10% of patients in AK105-202 and the pooled safety dataset, respectively.
Penpulimab appears to have an acceptable safety profile when assessed in the context of a life-threatening disease with unmet medical need.
The clinical data supports a positive risk-benefit assessment and is supportive of traditional approval penpulimab for the following indications:
Penpulimab in combination with either cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine for the first-line treatment of adults with recurrent or metastatic non-keratinizing nasopharyngeal carcinoma (NPC)
Penpulimab as a single agent for the treatment of adults with metastatic non-keratinizing NPC with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of therapy.

L94: Cross-Disciplinary Team Leader
L95: ## 2. Therapeutic Context
L96: ## 2.1. Analysis of Condition
L97: NPC is a rare malignancy in most parts of the world but is endemic in Southeast Asia, Eastern Asia (especially Southern China) and North Africa (Chen YP, et al. 2019). According to the International Agency for Research on Cancer, in 2018, there were about 129,000 new cases of NPC and &gt;70% of new cases are in Eastern and Southeast Asia, with an age-standardized rate of 3.0 per 100,000 in China to 0.4 per 100,000 in populations that are mainly Caucasian (Bray F, et al. 2018). Among the U.S. population, Chinese American accounted for approximately 36% of new cases of NPC according to the U.S. SEER 18 registries. NPC remains an important cause of cancer-related death worldwide with an incidence of approximately 50,000 deaths annually (Carioli G, et al. 2017; Tang LL, et al. 2016).
L98: NPC is currently classified by the World Health Organization (WHO) into 3 major histological subtypes: keratinizing squamous cell carcinoma (WHO type 1), differentiated nonkeratinizing carcinoma (WHO type 2), and undifferentiated carcinoma (WHO type 3) (Ou SI, et al. 2007). Nonkeratinizing NPC, for which Epstein Barr virus (EBV) is an etiologic factor, constitutes most cases in endemic areas (&gt;95%), including Southeast Asia, Eastern Asia and North Africa. The keratinizing subtype is more closely associated with cigarette smoking/alcohol consumption and accounts for a higher proportion of cases in nonendemic regions including the U.S (Wang Y, et al. 2013; Argirion I, et al. 2020). It has been noted that the incidence of the nonkeratinizing subtype is increasing in North America (Argirion I, et al. 2020), and similarly, findings of nonkeratinizing carcinoma being the predominant histology have been reported among white populations living in Europe (Svajdler Jr M, et al. 2016; Ruuskanen M, et al. 2019; Slevin F, et al. 2020). Despite differences in etiology and histology, current clinical management of NPC is consistent across the histologic subtypes.
L99: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L100: There were approximately 133,354 cases of NPC diagnosed worldwide in 2020 with the majority of cases (&gt;75%) in South and East Asia and 1.6% in Northern America, and approximately 80,000 people died from NPC in 2020 (Globocan, 2020). NPC is rare in the United States, with &lt;1 case per 100,000 people each year (Cancer.Net, 2022). A review of data from the Surveillance, Epidemiology, and End Results (SEER) registry reported by Sun et al (2017) identified 3919 patients with histologically confirmed NPC between 2004 and 2013. Among the 3919 patients, median age was 54 years (range 44, 63) and 71% of patients were male. Information on race was included for 3672 patients with 49% White, 12% Black, and 39% Asian (including 18% Chinese ethnicity). Information on histology was included for 1087 patients; of these patients, 83% had non-keratinizing and 17% had keratinizing NPC.
L101: ## 2.2. Analysis of Current Treatment Options
L102: ## The Applicant's Position:
L103: Guidelines for RM-NPC are consistent between the U.S. and China. Before the approval of PD-1 inhibitors in RM-NPC, the NCCN Head and Neck Cancer Clinical Practice Guidelines® recommend platinum-based chemotherapy regimens for first-line treatment of RM-NPC, with gemcitabine-cisplatin (GC) representing the preferred platinum-based regimen (category 1 recommendation). Other recommended regimens of first-line include cisplatin/5-FU, cisplatin or carboplatin/ docetaxel or paclitaxel, carboplatin/ cetuximab or gemcitabine/ carboplatin (*NCCN Head and Neck Cancer Clinical Practice Guidelines®*, V4, 2024). Although a high percentage of patients with NPC initially respond to chemotherapy, the duration of response is usually short (&lt;6 months on average); median PFS for first-line GP treatment is only 7 months (*Zhang L, 2016*).
L104: PD-1 inhibitors (toripalimab, camrelizumab and tislelizumab) combined with chemotherapy were firstly approved by the NMPA in China in the first-line treatment of RM-NPC, while in October 2023 FDA also approved toripalimab in combination with chemotherapy in the same indication. The recommendations on RM-NPC from clinical guidelines have converged again in the U.S. and China.
L105: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L106: FDA agrees with the Applicant that on October 27, 2023, FDA granted traditional approval to toripalimab-tpzi in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine for the first-line treatment of adults with metastatic or recurrent, locally advanced NPC based on the results of the JUPITER-O2 trial. The median PFS was 11.7 months for patients treated with toripalimab-tpzi with cisplatin and gemcitabine versus 8.0 months for patients treated with placebo with cisplatin and gemcitabine (PFS HR 0.52; 95% CI 0.36, 0.74; p=0.0003). A statistically significant OS improvement was also observed with median OS not reached (95% CI: 38.7 months, not estimable) for the toripalimab-tpzi-containing regimen and 33.7 months (95% CI: 27.0, 44.2) for the placebo-containing regimen (HR 0.63 [95% CI: 0.45, 0.89], p-value=0.0083). On October 27, 2023, FDA also granted traditional approval to toripalimab-tpzi as a single agent for the treatment of adults with recurrent unresectable or metastatic NPC with disease progression on or after a platinum-containing chemotherapy based on results of the POLARIS-O2 trial. In 172 patients, the ORR by BICR was 21% (95% CI: 15, 28) and median DOR was 14.9 months (95% CI: 10.3, not estimable).

L138: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L139: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position with the following additions. On July 16, 2021, the Applicant submitted BLA 761258 for the proposed indication for penpulimab-kcqx for the treatment of adult (b) (4) with metastatic non-keratinizing nasopharyngeal carcinoma with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of therapy. Data from Study AK105-202, a single arm, open label study of penpulimab in patients with metastatic non-keratinizing NPC who had received prior therapy, conducted entirely in China, was provided as the primary evidence in support of the proposed indication. Amongst the 111 patients in the primary efficacy population, the ORR was 30% (95% CI 21, 39) (data cutoff date of February 3, 2021). The median DOR was not reached (range 3.8, 17.1+); a total of 39% of the 33 responding patients had a DOR \ge 9 months.
L140: During FDA's review of this BLA, FDA toripalimab-tpzi, an anti-PD-1 antibody, in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine was granted traditional approval for the first-line treatment of adult patients with metastatic or recurrent, locally advanced NPC based on a randomized trial which demonstrated a 3.7-month improvement in progression-free survival (PFS) with the addition of toripalimab-tpzi to cisplatin and gemcitabine. This approval changed the standard of
L141: ## Penpulimab
L142: care for patients with metastatic or recurrent, locally advanced NPC with the inclusion of anti-PD-1-based therapy in the first-line treatment setting. At the time of this approval, toripalimab-tpzi was also approved as a single agent for patients with unresectable or metastatic NPC with disease progression on or after a platinum-containing chemotherapy based on ORR and DOR results from a single arm study of patients with metastatic NPC (ORR 21% (95% CI 15, 28); median DOR 14.9 months (95% CI 10.3, not estimable)). Given the approvals of toripalimab-tpzi for patients with NPC, the efficacy of penpulimab, based on the results of Study AK105-202, did not demonstrate a clear advantage over currently available therapeutic options for patients with metastatic, non-keratinizing NPC to grant an accelerated approval. In addition, following the pre-license inspection (PLI) of Akeso Biopharma, Co., Ltd., Guangdong, China (FEI 3017057933), deficiencies in CGMP operations were identified in the penpulimab drug substance and drug product manufacturing facility. FDA reviewed the responses from the facility and concluded that not all deficiencies had been satisfactorily resolved. Further, the Office of Pharmaceutical Quality (OPQ) identified various deficiencies related to the cell bank system used to manufacture penpulimab, process control strategy, validation of analytical procedures and drug product filling operation. After reviewing the Applicant's responses, OPQ concluded that the data were not sufficient to support a conclusion that the manufacture of penpulimab-kcqx was well-controlled and would lead to a product that is pure and potent. On January 19, 2024, the FDA issued a complete response (CR) letter stating that given the changing treatment landscape for NPC, the results of Study AK105-202 in patients with metastatic non-keratinizing NPC with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of therapy treated with penpulimab did not demonstrate a meaningful advantage over available therapy, as required by Section 21 of the Code of Federal Regulations (CFR) 601.41 Subpart E for accelerated approval.
L143: On February 27, 2024, FDA received a Type B meeting request under IND 138576 from the Applicant to seek advice on whether a BLA could be submitted for penpulimab for NPC if there was a statistically significant difference in PFS at an interim analysis of Study AK105-304, and if the plans to address the chemistry, manufacturing, and controls deficiencies listed in the aforementioned CR letter were acceptable. On April 12, 2024, FDA issued preliminary meeting comments indicating that the PFS results of an interim analysis for Study AK105-304 may be acceptable to support re-submission of the BLA; however, FDA sought further clarification regarding the Applicant's proposal to conduct an interim analysis of PFS that was not consistent with the one pre-specified in the statistical analysis plan. In a follow-up email communication on April 17, 2024, the Applicant indicated that while an interim analysis of PFS was initially proposed at 155 events (70% information fraction [IF]), there was an overrun of PFS events due to a change in the analysis population that stemmed from difficulties enrolling patients from outside of Asia. On April 19, 2024, FDA sent an email communication indicating that an interim PFS analysis based on 190 events (86% IF) may be acceptable for this particular application but that additional sensitivity analyses of PFS should be provided by the Applicant. Agreement was reached between the Applicant and FDA to cancel the Type B meeting.

L149: ## 4.2. Product Quality
L150: An initial Biologics Licensing Application (BLA) was submitted for penpulimab as a single-agent for patients with previously treated NPC on July 16, 2021, based on the results of the single-arm trial, Study AK105-202. During the review of that application, a pre-license inspection was performed on July 25, 2023, to August 07, 2023, of the drug substance (DS) and drug product (DP) manufacturing facility at Akeso Biopharma Co., Ltd., Shennong Road No. 6, Zhongshan City, Guangdong Province, China. An eight-item Form FDA 483 was issued to the firm at the end of the inspection on August 07, 2023, for the following observations: (1) The firm had not adequately validated its manufacturing processes for AK105 DS and DP in its manufacturing facility located in [REDACTED] (b) (4). Additionally, the firm failed to establish process controls to ensure that the drug products it manufactures have the identity, strength, quality, and purity they purport or are represented to possess; (2) Procedures designed to prevent microbiological
L151: ## Penpulimab
L152: contamination of drug products purporting to be sterile had not been established or followed; (3) The firm's manufacturing control strategy did not adequately control for the potential introduction of adventitious agents from other products manufactured in the facility; (4) The firm's manufacturing control strategy and procedures were inadequate to prevent cross-contamination and product mix-up from all products manufactured in the multiproduct manufacturing facility in (b) (4) (5) Written records of investigations into unexplained discrepancies, or the failure of a batch or any of its components to meet specifications, did not always include appropriate documentation, conclusions, and follow-up; (6) The firm's quality unit's oversight of its GMP manufacturing operations was inadequate; (7) There were discrepancies between the current DS and DP manufacturing processes at the facility and the manufacturing processes described in the BLA 761258; and (8) Standard Operating procedures or work instructions were not followed, or did not contain sufficient details.
L153: As a result, the Office of Pharmaceutical Quality (OPQ) recommended a Complete Response for the previously submitted BLA. A Complete Response Letter was issued to Akeso on January 19, 2024, citing deficiencies in CGMP operations identified in the penpulimab drug substance and drug product manufacturing facility listed in the application.
L154: Additional deficiencies identified during the CMC review included the lack of a working cell bank for product manufacture, unacceptable method performance for the PD-1 Reporter Gene Assay, reduced and non-reduced capillary electrophoresis sodium dodecyl sulfate (CE-SDS) methods used for routine testing, and inadequate validation of the visual inspection process.
L155: For this resubmission of the BLA, a second pre-license inspection was performed from February 21, 2025, to March 04, 2025, of the DS and DP manufacturing facility at Akeso Biopharma Co., Ltd., Shennong Road No. 6, Zhongshan City, Guangdong Province, China. A six-item Form FDA 483 was issued to the firm at the end of the inspection on March 04, 2025. The 483 observations and firm's written FDA-483 response dated March 10, 2025, and response to the request for additional information (RAI) dated March 20, 2025, were reviewed. The firm's responses and corrective actions to the FDA Form 483 observations and RAI are adequate. Consequently, OPMA/DPMA recommends approval of the BLA resubmission for penpulimab.
L156: Based on the totality of information provided in the original BLA submission and the Class 2 resubmission, OPQ has determined that the data submitted in this application are sufficient to support a conclusion that the manufacture of penpulimab-kcqx is well-controlled and will lead to a product that is pure and potent. It is recommended that this product be approved for human use under conditions specified in the package insert. Refer to the OPQ Executive Summary and Primary assessment memos of this application for detailed information.

L161: ## 5. Nonclinical Pharmacology/Toxicology
L162: ## 5.1. Executive Summary
L163: Penpulimab is a human IgG1 monoclonal antibody that targets programmed cell death protein 1 (PD-1); its established pharmacologic class is PD-1-blocking antibody. PD-1 is expressed on activated T and B cells, natural killer cells, and myeloid lineage cells. Binding of PD-1 to its ligands, programmed death-ligand 1 (PD-L1) and PD-L2, inhibits T-cell activity, T-cell proliferation, and cytokine production. In the tumor microenvironment, PD-1 signaling contributes to the inhibition of T cell-mediated immune surveillance of tumors. Thus, blockade of PD-1 signaling in the tumor microenvironment is intended to enhance tumor immunosurveillance and anti-tumor immune responses.
L164: In vitro studies, penpulimab bound to human and cynomolgus monkey PD-1 but did not bind rodent PD-1, supporting the use of cynomolgus monkeys as the single pharmacologically relevant species for toxicity assessment. In cellular assays, penpulimab blocked the binding of human PD-1 to human PD-L1 and PD-L2 and also competitively bound to PD-1 expressed on the surface of human PD-1-transfected 293T cells. Additionally, penpulimab induced the secretion of interleukin-2 (IL-2) and interferon-gamma (IFN-\gamma) in a mixed culture of peripheral blood mononuclear cells (PBMCs) and dendritic cells (DCs) and in a mixed culture of PBMCs, DCs, and Raji Burkitt lymphoma cells. Penpulimab also enhanced IFN-\gamma secretion by cytomegalovirus (CMV)-responsive human PBMCs during the recall response to CMV antigen.
L165: Given that penpulimab does not bind mouse PD-1, the Applicant investigated the in vivo antitumor activity of penpulimab in a human PD-1 knock-in genetically engineered mouse model (HuGEMM) implanted subcutaneously with MC38 mouse colon adenocarcinoma cells. Intraperitoneal administration of penpulimab at doses of 0.8 and 8 mg/kg twice a week for a total of 5 doses resulted in tumor growth inhibition (TGI) compared to the isotype control. The Applicant also evaluated the antitumor activity of penpulimab in immunodeficient mice implanted subcutaneously with Raji cells expressing PD-L1 or a Raji-PD-L1/PBMC mixture. Intravenous (IV) administration of penpulimab (10 mg/kg) on Days 7, 13, and 21 resulted in greater TGI compared to the isotype control.
L166: The Applicant evaluated the safety of penpulimab in cynomolgus monkeys in GLP-compliant repeat-dose toxicology studies of up to 13 weeks duration. In the 6- and 13-week studies, monkeys received IV infusion of penpulimab at doses up to 60 mg/kg once every 2 weeks. There were no mortalities or penpulimab-related findings of toxicological significance. Target
L167: ## Penpulimab
L168: organs of toxicity in the 6-week study were the kidney (increased cortical tubular pigment or pigmented interstitial macrophages), spleen (increased cellularity), adrenal gland (cortical hypertrophy), parotid salivary gland (acinar cell atrophy), thymus (increased weight); and in the 13-week study was lymph nodes (erythrophagocytosis and increased cellularity). The incidence of anti-drug antibody formation was 63.33% in the 6-week study and 93.33% in the 13-week study, which may explain the limited findings noted in the 13-week study. In clinical trials done to support the approval of penpulimab in the proposed patient population, toxicities associated with penpulimab were primarily immune-mediated adverse reactions that are predicted by its mechanism of action. At the recommended clinical dose of 200 mg, the human AUC0-336 was 26200 µg*h/mL.
L169: Based on its mechanism of action and data from the literature, there is potential for enhanced immune responses to infection following treatment with penpulimab. *Mycobacterium tuberculosis* (Mtb)-infected PD-1 knockout mice exhibited decreased survival compared to wild-type mice, which correlated with increased bacterial proliferation and inflammatory responses in these animals. It is unclear whether decreased survival resulted from an inability to mount an appropriate antibacterial response or a failure to control the immune response leading to normal tissue destruction and organ failure (Lazar-Molnar, et al. 2010). Furthermore, in mouse models of lymphocytic choriomeningitis virus (LCMV) infection, the absence of PD-1 pathway signaling led to fatal CD8+ T cell-mediated killing of virally infected vascular endothelial cells, systemic vascular leakage, and ultimately cardiovascular collapse (Frebel et al. 2012; Mueller et al. 2010). Similarly, PD-L1-deficient mice died early after chronic systemic LCMV infection (Barber et al. 2006). In addition, blockade of PD-1 with an anti-PD-1 antibody in Mtb infection in monkeys can exacerbate the infection and lead to death and severe pulmonary stress in infected monkeys (Kauffman et al. 2021). A brief description of these data was included in Section 13.2 of the label to convey these potential risks to prescribers.
L170: No reproductive or embryo-fetal developmental toxicology studies were conducted with penpulimab. Rather, the Applicant provided a literature-based assessment of the potential reproductive toxicity of penpulimab, consistent with the alternative approach described in the FDA Guidance for Industry Oncology Pharmaceuticals: Reproductive Toxicity Testing and Labeling Recommendations. Data from the literature demonstrate that the PD-1/PD-L1 pathway plays a critical role in maintaining maternal immunological tolerance to the fetus during pregnancy. Blockade of PD-1/PD-L1 signaling in murine models of pregnancy resulted in an increased incidence of fetal loss but no fetal malformations; however, late onset autoimmunity can occur in PD-1-deficient mice. Based on these findings, the potential risks of administering penpulimab during pregnancy include increased rates of abortion or stillbirth and an increased risk in offspring of developing immune-related disorders or altering the normal immune response. As a result, the pharmacology/toxicology team recommends a warning for embryo-fetal toxicity in the prescribing information. Based on a half-life of penpulimab of 23.9 days, females of reproductive potential are advised to use effective contraception during treatment with PENPULIMAB-KCQX and for 4 months after the last dose. The Applicant did not
L171: ## Penpulimab
L172: evaluate transfer of penpulimab to the fetus or milk, but IgG antibodies can be present in milk. Because of the potential adverse effects of PENPULIMAB-KCQX on a breastfeeding child, the review team included recommendations in the label not to breastfeed during treatment with PENPULIMAB-KCQX and for 4 months after the last dose. Neither genetic toxicology or carcinogenicity studies were conducted or required to support the use of an antibody in the currently proposed indication.
L173: ## Recommendation:
L174: There are no outstanding issues from a pharmacology/toxicology perspective that would prevent the approval of penpulimab (either in combination with gemcitabine and cisplatin or carboplatin, or as a single agent) for the treatment of adult patients with non-keratinizing NPC.
L175: ## 5.2. Referenced NDAs, BLAs, DMFs
L176: ## The Applicant's Position:

L178: ## 5.3. Pharmacology
L179: ## Primary Pharmacology
L180: ## The FDA's Assessment (all data presented by FDA):
L181: Primary pharmacology studies NCS105-0001 through NCS105-00010 were reviewed by the FDA under the original IND. All other pharmacology studies were reviewed under the current BLA.
L182: ## A. In Vitro Studies
L183: The Applicant determined the binding affinities of penpulimab for human (Study NCS105-0001), cynomolgus monkey (Study NCS105-0005), mouse (Study NCS105-0006), and rat PD-1 (Study NCS105-0007) using the enzyme-linked immunoassay (ELISA). Penpulimab (0.0014–1 µg/mL) bound to human PD-1-mFc fusion protein (human PD-1 extracellular domain fused to murine IgG Fc domain) and monkey PD-1-hFc fusion protein (monkey PD-1 extracellular domain fused to human IgG1 Fc domain) with EC50 values of 0.053 and 0.215 nM, respectively, but did not bind to mouse or rat PD-1, supporting the use of cynomolgus monkeys as a pharmacologically relevant species for toxicity assessment.
L184: The Applicant determined the binding activity of penpulimab to PD-1 expressed on the surface of human PD-1-transfected 293T cells (293T-PD-1 cells) by fluorescence activated cell sorting (FACS) analysis (Study NCS105-0008). Penpulimab (0.1–20 nM) bound to human PD-1 in a dose-
L185: ## Penpulimab
L186: dependent manner, with an EC_{50} of 2.53 nM. As assessed by surface plasmon resonance, penpulimab (1.56–50 nM) exhibited high binding affinity for human PD-1, with a K_D of 0.588 nM (Study NCS105-0002), which was similar to the K_D of the anti-PD-1 comparator antibody nivolumab.
L187: The Applicant used competitive ELISA to evaluate the activity of penpulimab in competing with PD-L1 (Study NCS105-0003) and PD-L2 (Study NCS105-0004) for binding to PD-1. Penpulimab (0.002–3 \mug/mL) blocked the binding of immobilized human PD-1-hFc to human PD-L1-mFc and human PD-L2-His fusion proteins in a dose-dependent manner, with EC_{50} values of 0.878 and 1.483 nM, respectively. The Applicant used FACS to evaluate the activity of penpulimab in competing with PD-L1 for binding to 293T-PD-1 cells (Study NCS105-0009). Penpulimab (0.07–150 nM) competitively bound to PD-1 on 293T-PD-1 cells in a dose-dependent manner, with an EC_{50} of 2.526 nM (FDA – Figure). Binding of penpulimab was comparable to the positive control, nivolumab, which competitively bound to PD-1 with an EC_{50} of 3.281 nM.

L191: To evaluate the effect of penpulimab on the secretion of IFN-\gamma and IL-2 from lymphocytes, the Applicant conducted an allogeneic mixed lymph reaction (MLR) experiment (Study NCS105-00010). In this MLR, investigators incubated in vitro-differentiated DCs of one donor (stimulator) with allogeneic PBMCs of another donor (responder) at a ratio of 10:1, followed by the addition of penpulimab (3, 30, 300 nM). Nivolumab Akeso (synthesized by the Applicant) was used as a positive control. IFN-\gamma and IL-2 levels were measured by ELISA. Compared with the isotype and negative controls, penpulimab significantly enhanced the secretion of IFN-\gamma (FDA – Figure) and IL-2 (FDA – Figure) in the mixed culture of PBMCs and DCs in a dose-independent manner.

In this MLR assay, penpulimab (3, 30, 300 nM) significantly enhanced the secretion of IFN-\gamma (FDA – Figure) and IL-2 (FDA – Figure) in the mixed culture of PBMCs, DCs, and Raji cells in a dose-dependent manner.

L205: The Applicant conducted a cellular assay to evaluate the effect of penpulimab on IFN-\gamma secretion by CMV-responsive human PBMCs during the recall response to CMV antigen (Study NCS105-0024). PBMCs from two donors were prescreened for a CMV recall response using a serially diluted CMV peptide pool. As assessed by ELISA on Day 4, the CMV peptide pool effectively induced the secretion of IFN-\gamma in a dose-dependent manner, demonstrating that the PBMCs were previously infected by CMV and could re-respond to CMV antigen. The CMV-responsive PMBCs were stimulated with CMV antigen in the absence or presence of penpulimab (0.1–300 nM). Pembrolizumab and nivolumab were assessed as positive controls, and ipilimumab (anti-cytotoxic T lymphocyte antigen-4 antibody) served as a negative control. After a 4-day incubation, the antigen-induced IFN-\gamma response was measured by ELISA. Compared with the isotype and negative controls, penpulimab significantly enhanced the secretion of IFN-\gamma in a dose-dependent manner by CMV-responsive human PBMCs during the recall response to CMV antigen, showing comparable activity with nivolumab and pembrolizumab (FDA – Figure ).
L206: Abbreviations: CMV, cytomegalovirus; IFN-γ, interferon-gamma; IgG, immunoglobulin G; PBMC, peripheral blood mononuclear  cell
L207: ## B. In Vivo Studies
L208: Because pempulimab does not bind to mouse PD-1, male C57BL/6 mice were genetically  engineered to express a chimeric human/mouse PD-1 gene; these mice have a functional  human PD-1 with expression regulated by the mouse PD-1 promoter. The Applicant  investigated the in vivo antitumor activity of penpulimab in this human PD-1 knock-in  genetically engineered mouse model (HuGEMM) implanted subcutaneously with MC38 mouse  colon adenocarcinoma cells (Study E3174-B1701). Nivolumab (manufactured by Brystol-Myers)  was used as a comparator control. Intraperitoneal administration of 0.8 and 8 mg/kg  penpulimab twice a week for a total of 5 doses resulted in 74.1% and 54.5% TGI, respectively,  compared to the isotype control (FDA – Figure ). Pempulimab exhibited similar in vivo  antitumor activity as nivolumab. There were no mortalities, and body weights remained stable  during treatment.

Intravenous administration of penpulimab (10 mg/kg) on Days 7, 13, and 21 resulted in greater TGI compared to the isotype control (FDA – Figure ), without inducing significant toxicity based on body weight loss.

L219: ## Secondary Pharmacology
L220: ## The Applicant's Position:
L221: In the Fc domain of penpulimab, point mutations were introduced to eliminate a series of Fc receptor mediated effects, including antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC), antibody dependent cellular phagocytosis (ADCP), complement dependent cytotoxicity (CDC) and Fc receptor mediated cytokine release. The affinity of penpulimab to Fc-receptor FcyRIIIa, FcyRI and C1q were evaluated by Fortebio Octet molecular interaction assay and confirmed lack
L222: ## Penpulimab
L223: of binding. Cellular assays were further conducted to evaluate the ADCC, ADCP, CDC and antibody dependent cytokine release (ADCR) activity of penpulimab, and penpulimab shows no ADCC, ADCP, CDC activity or ADCR of IL-6 and IL-8 from human macrophages. In binding selectivity assay, penpulimab binds to human PD-1 but not to human CTLA-4, which is a homologous protein of PD-1. Penpulimab also does not bind to human lymphocyte-activation gene 3 (LAG3) or human T cell immunoglobulin and ITIM domain (TIGIT), which are co-inhibitory receptors like PD-1. The activity of penpulimab to elicit cytokine IL-1\beta, IL-6 and TNF-\alpha release from peripheral blood mononuclear cells (PBMCs) was also tested and penpulimab does not elicit cytokine release of these cytokines from PBMC, suggesting the low risk of triggering cytokine release syndrome.
L224: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L225: FDA agrees with the Applicant's conclusions.
L226: ## **Safety Pharmacology**
L227: ## The Applicant's Position:
L228: Safety pharmacology studies were conducted during cynomolgus monkey toxicity studies, and effect of penpulimab on vital functions in cardiovascular system, central nervous system (CNS) and respiratory system were investigated. The evaluation of safety pharmacology endpoints including body temperature, respiratory rate, blood oxygen saturation, electrocardiograms, and blood pressure upon treatment with penpulimab was incorporated into the repeat-dose toxicology study in cynomolgus monkey conducted under GLP condition. There were no treatment-related effects on body temperature, respiratory rate, blood oxygen saturation, electrocardiograms, or blood pressure found in the repeat dose toxicology study. Thus no specific concerns were observed on vital functions in cardiovascular system, CNS and respiratory system.
L229: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L230: FDA agrees with the Applicant's conclusions. In vivo, cardiovascular parameters were assessed pre-treatment; 2 to 5 hours after dosing on Days 1, 15, 29, and 43; and 1 day prior to recovery necropsy (Day 84) in the 13-week repeat-dose monkey study. Findings were reviewed under the General Toxicology section below. There were no toxicologically significant changes observed at doses up to 60 mg/kg/day.
L231: ## 5.4. ADME/PK
L232: ## The Applicant's Position:
L233: Single dose pharmacokinetics (PK) and repeat-dose toxicokinetics (TK) evaluation of penpulimab were performed in cynomolgus monkey which is the species showing cross-reactivity to penpulimab. In single dose PK study, systemic exposure of penpulimab (AUC and Cmax) was approximately dose-proportional in cynomolgus monkeys from 1 mg/kg to 10 mg/kg. No significant differences in systemic exposure were noted. A high incidence of anti-drug antibody (ADA) formation was observed, which was related to the accelerated elimination of penpulimab in ADA-positive animals at the end of the elimination phase. Repeat dose PK (TK) of penpulimab was investigated in the 6-week and 13-week toxicity study. Dose-dependent increase in systemic exposure of penpulimab (AUC and Cmax) was noted following repeat intravenous dosing. Decreases in systemic exposure levels were noted in a few animals (Day 29 or 71, respectively), which is due to the formation of ADAs. There was no significant systemic accumulation after repeat dosing of penpulimab once every two weeks for 6 weeks or 13 weeks.
L234: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L235: FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of the nonclinical ADME/PK data. Traditional distribution, metabolism, and excretion studies were not conducted with penpulimab because it is a monoclonal antibody.

L242: ## 5.5. Toxicology
L243: ## 5.5.1. General Toxicology
L244: ## The Applicant's Position:
L245: In 6-week repeat-dose toxicity study, no apparent toxicity was observed in cynomolgus monkeys following intravenous infusion of penpulimab at 4, 15, or 60 mg/kg once every two weeks for 6 weeks. Treatment-related histopathological change of kidneys (increased cortical tubular pigment or pigmented interstitial macrophages) was noted in some animals but no impact on kidney functions. Pigment in the cortical tubular epithelium of the kidneys is not reported as a common background finding in cynomolgus monkeys. In this study, there were no apparent changes in clinical chemistry, urinalysis, or organ weights which would indicate an impact of treatment on kidney function. There were also no other histopathological changes associated with the pigment which were indicative of damage to the kidneys. These changes were therefore considered not to be adverse. The no observed adverse effect level (NOAEL) was considered to be 60 mg/kg.
L246: In 13-week repeat-dose toxicity study, no apparent toxicity and histopathological change of kidney was observed in cynomolgus monkeys following intravenous infusion of penpulimab at 4, 15, or 60 mg/kg once every two weeks for 13 weeks, the NOAEL was considered to be 60 mg/kg.
L247: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L248: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of the toxicology data. In the 6-week repeat-dose toxicology study, clinical chemistry findings included a dose-dependent decrease in total bilirubin and increase in creatine kinase levels at all dose levels in males, which were present at the end of the recovery period. In addition to the kidney, as noted by the Applicant, other target organs of toxicity in the 6-week study were the spleen (increased cellularity of the red and white pulp), adrenal gland (cortical hypertrophy), parotid salivary gland (acinar cell atrophy), and thymus (increased weight). Changes in the adrenal gland, spleen, and thymus (females) were present at the end of the recovery period. Of the 30 monkey serum samples tested, 19 were ADA-positive with an incidence of 63.33%.
L249: In the 13-week toxicity study, histological findings included erythrophagocytosis and increased cellularity in the lymph nodes (inguinal, mandibular, mesenteric), which were present at the end of the recovery period. Limited findings may be reflective of ADA formation. Of the 30 monkey serum samples tested, 28 were ADA-positive with an incidence of 93.33%.
L250: ## **Study title/number: A 13-Week Intravenous Infusion Toxicity Study of AK105 Injection With a 6-Week Recovery Period in Cynomolgus Monkeys**
L251: ## **Key Study Findings**
L252: - There were no mortalities or penpulimab-related findings of toxicological significance.
- Histological findings included increased cellularity and erythrophagocytosis in the inguinal, mandibular, and mesenteric lymph nodes.
- The NOAEL was 60 mg/kg.
L253: GLP compliance: Yes
