L267: ## 5.5.2. Genetic Toxicology
L268: ## The Applicant's Position:
L269: Genetic toxicology studies were not conducted with penpulimab. As described in ICH Guidance S6, the range and type of genotoxicity studies routinely conducted for pharmaceuticals are not applicable to biotechnology-derived pharmaceuticals.
L270: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L271: FDA agrees that genetic toxicology studies are not needed to support the use of penpulimab per ICH S6.
L272: ## 5.5.3. Carcinogenicity
L273: ## The Applicant's Position:
L274: Carcinogenicity studies were not conducted with penpulimab, which is consistent with its use in patients with advanced malignancies (see the ICH Guidance S9). If penpulimab is developed for use in other indications, an assessment of its carcinogenic potential may be warranted; however rodent bioassays are inappropriate, as penpulimab does not appear to be active in rodents.
L275: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L276: FDA agrees that carcinogenicity studies are not needed to support the use of penpulimab in the currently proposed indication per ICH S9.
L277: ## 5.5.4. Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology
L278: ## The Applicant's Position:
L279: Fertility, embryonic fetal development or pre- and postnatal developmental toxicity studies were not conducted with penpulimab. As described in ICH Guidance S9, fertility and early embryonic developmental toxicity study is not warranted for marketing of pharmaceuticals for the treatment of patients with advanced cancer. Information available from general toxicology studies on the pharmaceutical's effect on reproductive organs were used as the basis of the assessment of impairment of fertility. In the 6-week and 13-week repeat-dose toxicity studies, there were no notable effects in the male and female reproductive organs.
L280: ## Penpulimab
L281: Embryo-fetal development toxicity studies were not conducted with penpulimab. Literature data shows that a central function of the PD-1/PD-L1 pathway is to preserve pregnancy by maintaining maternal immune tolerance to the fetus. Blockade of PD-L1 signaling has been shown in murine models of pregnancy to disrupt tolerance to the fetus and to increase fetal loss. There are potential reproductive risks when giving penpulimab during pregnancy, including increased rates of miscarriage or stillbirth. Penpulimab is an IgG1 antibody drug with high affinity to human FcRn, thus it is expected to cross the placenta during third-trimester pregnancy in human, though the exposure of penpulimab in fetus under treatment dose level is undetermined. Based on its mechanism of action, fetal exposure to penpulimab may increase the risk of developing immune-mediated disorders or altering the normal immune response and immune-mediated disorders have been reported in PD-1 knockout mice.
L282: Pre- and postnatal toxicity studies were not conducted with penpulimab. As described in ICH Guidance S9, pre- and postnatal toxicity study is not warranted for marketing of pharmaceuticals for the treatment of patients with advanced cancer.
L283: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L284: FDA agrees that dedicated reproductive and developmental toxicity studies are not needed to support this BLA submission. To address the potential effects of penpulimab on embryo-fetal development, the Applicant submitted an assessment of the potential reproductive toxicity of penpulimab using a weight-of-evidence approach. FDA agreed in January 2021 that this approach appeared reasonable.
L285: The PD-1/PD-L1 pathway plays a critical role in maintaining maternal immunological tolerance to the fetus during pregnancy (Guleria et al. 2005). PD-L1 is expressed in the human placenta throughout pregnancy and at the maternal-fetal interface in fetal villous syncytiotrophoblasts and extravillous cytotrophoblasts (Petroff et al. 2003). Blockade of PD-L1 signaling in murine models of pregnancy disrupts tolerance to the fetus, resulting in increased fetal loss (Guleria et al. 2005).
L286: Penpulimab is a humanized IgG1 antibody, and human IgG is known to cross the placenta; thus, penpulimab may be transmitted from the mother to the fetus. Based on its mechanism of action and findings from murine models of pregnancy (Guleria et al. 2005), administration of penpulimab is likely to disrupt the maintenance of normal pregnancy. Potential risks associated with administration of penpulimab during pregnancy include increased rates of abortion and stillbirth. In addition, as PD-1 knockout mice develop late onset autoimmune phenotypes (Okazaki and Honjo 2007), fetal exposure to penpulimab may alter the normal immune response or increase the risk of developing immune-related disorders. As inhibition of the PD-1/PD-L1 pathway can lead to increased risk of immune-mediated rejection of the developing fetus, resulting in fetal death, a warning for embryofetal toxicity is recommended for PENPULIMAB-KCQX. Based on a half-life of approximately 24 days, females of reproductive
L287: ## Penpulimab
L288: potential are advised to use effective contraception during treatment with PENPULIMAB-KCQX and for 4 months after the last dose of PENPULIMAB-KCQX.
L289: Fertility studies have not been conducted with penpulimab and are not needed to support a BLA for the proposed indication. In 6- and 13-week repeat-dose toxicity studies in monkeys, there were no notable effects on male and female reproductive organs up to the highest dose tested of 60 mg/kg (approximately \ge 4.7 times the human exposure based on AUC at the 200 mg clinical dose); however, most animals in these studies were not sexually mature.
L290: ## 5.5.5. Other Toxicology Studies
L291: ## The Applicant's Position:
L292: The investigation of tissue cross-reactivity of penpulimab with normal human and normal cynomolgus monkey frozen tissues revealed that penpulimab reacted with normal human spleen and lymph nodes and normal cynomolgus monkey spleen, mesenteric lymph node, thymus, jejunum and colon. PD-1 is expressed on the surface of activated T cells, B cells, and macrophages, and as the results of penpulimab tissue cross-reactivity (TCR) studies showed no unexpected binding of penpulimab to human or cynomolgus monkey tissues was found. In hemolysis study, penpulimab at the concentration of 10 mg/mL had no hemolytic or aggregation effect on human red blood cells.
L293: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L294: FDA agrees with the Applicant's conclusions. Standard positive controls confirmed the sensitivity and validity of these GLP-compliant studies.
L295: ## Literature Based Assessment of Potential for Effects of Infection
L296: (Previously reviewed by FDA)
L297: While loss of PD-1 function enhances clearance of some tumors and viral infections, it increases susceptibility to certain other pathogens such as tuberculosis in some animal models. PD-1-deficient mice (C57BL/6) infected with *Mycobacterium tuberculosis* (Mtb) exhibited a decrease in survival, which correlated with uncontrolled bacterial proliferation and a larger inflammatory response in the lungs of PD-1-deficient mice compared with wild-type controls. Thus, PD-1 appears to be required to control infection and the inflammatory responses in the lungs of mice infected with Mtb (Lazar-Molnar, et al. 2010); however, the pathogenesis of this observation has not been clearly defined. In particular, it is unclear whether the decreased survival reflects rampant bacterial growth resulting from an inability to mount appropriate antibacterial responses and/or whether it is a failure to downregulate the immune reaction that leads to massive tissue destruction and organ failure.
L298: In a recent publication, Kauffman et al. (2021) evaluated the role of PD-1 during Mtb infection of rhesus macaques. Monkeys (~2 years old; males) were infected with 30-50 colony forming units of fluorescently labeled Mtb into the right lower lung lobe. Tissue from Mtb-infected monkeys was harvested 15–16 weeks post-infection and assessed for PD-1 expression using flow cytometry. Additional Mtb-infected monkeys were intravenously infused with 10 mg/kg of isotype control (DSPR4) or a primatized anti-PD-1 monoclonal antibody (n=6/group) at 2, 4, 6, 8, 10, and 13 weeks post-infection to determine the role of PD-1 during an Mtb infection. PET scans and 18FDG (18F-fluoro-2-deoxy-D-glucose) uptake, granuloma size and number, and colony forming units per granuloma were used to evaluate infection. Monkeys in both studies were necropsied at 15–16 weeks post-infection. Treatment of Mtb-infected monkeys with an anti-PD-1 antibody led to early euthanasia in one monkey due to severe pulmonary stress and another monkey developed cachexia and showed elevated 18FDG uptake in the lungs but survived until study termination. PD-1 expression in Mtb-infected monkeys occurred more frequently on antigen specific CD4 and CD8 T cells in lung granulomas compared to other tissues. Mtb-infected monkeys treated with an anti-PD-1 showed similar numbers of granulomas compared to controls, but these were characterized as larger granulomas, with increased 18FDG uptake and a higher bacterial load. The increased bacterial loads noted with PD-1 blockade occurred primarily within the site of infection (pulmonary lymph nodes and granulomas), suggesting it did not result in disseminated infection. The mechanism underlying the increased bacterial loads after PD-1 blockade in Mtb infection are not clear, but IFN-\gamma and TNF have been previously implicated. Evaluation of cytokines in granulomas of isotype and anti-PD-1-treated monkeys indicated that IFN-\gamma, TNF, and IL-18 were the most dysregulated cytokines and positively correlated with bacterial loads after PD-1 blockade. Data from Kauffman et al. (2021) indicate that blockade of PD-1 with an anti-PD-1 antibody can exacerbate Mtb infection in monkeys, consistent with previous findings noted in PD-1 knockout mice infected with Mtb (Lazar-Molnar et al. 2010).

L300: X
L301: Kelie M. Reece, PhD
Primary Reviewer
L302: Claudia P. Miller, PhD

L303: ## 6. Clinical Pharmacology
L304: ## 6.1. Executive Summary
L305: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L306: The Applicant seeks approval of penpulimab for the treatment of the following indications at the following proposed recommended dosages:
L307: - In combination with either cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine for the first-line treatment of nasopharyngeal carcinoma (NPC). The recommended dosage is penpulimab 200 mg administered via intravenous (IV) infusion every three weeks (Q3W).
- As a single agent for the treatment of non-keratinizing NPC with diseases progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one prior line of therapy. The recommended dosage is penpulimab 200 mg administered via IV infusion every two weeks (Q2W).
L308: The recommended dosage of penpulimab 200 mg IV Q2W as a single agent was selected from the pivotal Study AK105-202, which was previously reviewed during the initial BLA review cycle. The proposed dosage 200 mg Q2W was supported by the clinical benefit and safety profile from Study AK105-202. The clinical pharmacology review found the proposed dosage acceptable based on population PK (PPK), exposure response (E-R) and immunogenicity analyses. (Reference ID: 5313694).
L309: The recommended dosage of 200 mg IV Q3W in combination with chemotherapy (either cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine) was reviewed in this submission. The primary evidence of efficacy and safety was obtained from the pivotal study AK105-304, in which patients were randomized (1:1) to receive either penpulimab 200 mg Q3W by IV infusion in combination with chemotherapy or placebo in combination with chemotherapy.
L310: The clinical pharmacology review addressed the following two key review issues:
L311: **ISSUE #1 – Is the proposed dosage acceptable?:** The proposed dosage of penpulimab 200 mg Q3W is acceptable for patients with NPC given the following considerations from Study AK105-304:
L312: - The dosages of penpulimab 200 mg Q2W as a single agent and penpulimab 200 mg Q3W in combination with chemotherapy showed comparable exposures based on PPK analysis. No dosage adjustment is recommended for patients based on age, sex, race, body weight, serum albumin, baseline LDH, baseline C-reactive protein, baseline ECOG score,
L313: ## Penpulimab
L314: immunogenicity status, tumor type, co-administration with chemotherapy, mild or moderate renal impairment, and mild or moderate hepatic impairment. The effect of severe renal impairment or severe hepatic impairment on penpulimab PK is unknown.
L315: - The E-R analysis for efficacy showed a flat relationship but is considered exploratory due to a limited exposure range from one dose level in Study AK105-304. Any relationship between exposure and efficacy endpoints should be interpreted with caution.
- The E-R analysis for safety did not show a relationship with overall treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs). However, there is a positive correlation between exposure and immune-related adverse events (irAEs) and specific adverse events (AEs) including decreased white blood cells, hypothyroidism, rash, and upper respiratory tract infections. These AEs are not clinically significant due to comparable dose interruptions and discontinuations between treatment arms in Study AK105-304.
L316: **ISSUE #2 – Is the observed immunogenicity acceptable?:** The incidence of treatment emergent anti-drug antibodies (ADAs) and neutralizing antibodies (NABs) against penpulimab were 8.8% (12/137) and 33% (4/12), respectively in the penpulimab arm of Study AK105-304. Although observed trough concentrations (C_{\text{trough}}) decreased by 47% in ADA positive patients enrolled in Study AK105-304, ADAs or NABs did not have a clinically significant effect on penpulimab pharmacokinetics (PK). The PPK analysis showed &lt;20% differences in the Geometric Mean Ratios (GMRs) for clearance (CL), area under the curve at steady-state (AUC_{\text{ss}}), maximum concentration at steady state (C_{\text{max,ss}}) and minimum concentration at steady state (C_{\text{min,ss}}). The observed 47% decrease in C_{\text{trough}} in Study AK105-304 is attributable to patient factors such as weight and albumin, as the patients who tested positive and negative for ADA are not randomized. The effect of ADAs on safety and efficacy cannot be determined due to low numbers of ADA positive patients in Study AK105-304.
L317: **Recommendations:** The Office of Clinical Pharmacology has reviewed the information submitted in BLA 761258 and determined it is approvable from a clinical pharmacology perspective. The key review issues with specific recommendations and comments are summarized in the table below:
L318: **FDA – Table 5. Key Clinical Pharmacology Review Issues**
L319: Review Issue	Recommendations and Comments
Pivotal and supportive evidence of effectiveness	
The primary evidence of effectiveness is derived from 144 adult patients with metastatic NPC enrolled in Study AK105-304 who received penpulimab 200 mg IV Q3W plus chemotherapy. In Study AK105-304, the primary endpoint of PFS showed a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement favoring penpulimab in combination with chemotherapy versus placebo with chemotherapy. Refer to the FDA's assessment in Section 8.2 for details.
The E-R analysis for efficacy is considered exploratory due to a limited exposure range from one dose level (See Section 19.4.3.1).
L320: Review Issue	Recommendations and Comments
General dosing instructions	The proposed recommended dosage is 200 mg IV Q3W in combination with either cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine. The proposed dosing regimen is supported by the statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in BIRC-assessed PFS and the safety profile of penpulimab plus chemotherapy vs placebo plus chemotherapy in Study AK105-304. The PPK, E-R, and immunogenicity data from Study AK105-304 provide supportive evidence that the proposed recommended dosage of 200 mg IV Q3W is acceptable.
Dosing in patient subgroups	No dosage adjustment is recommended for age (18-91 years), sex, race (White and Asian), body weight (32-113 kg), serum albumin (22 to 54.5 g/L), baseline LDH (111 to 4,450 IU/L), baseline C-reactive protein (0 to 328 mg/L), baseline ECOG score (0 and 1), immunogenicity status, tumor type (NPC and other tumors), co-administration with chemotherapy, mild or moderate renal impairment (creatinine clearance [CLcr] \ge 30 mL/min), mild (total bilirubin \le ULN and AST > ULN or total bilirubin > 1 to 1.5 times ULN and any AST) or moderate hepatic impairment (total bilirubin > 1.5 to 3 times ULN and any AST) according to the results of PPK analyses.
Immunogenicity	Of the 137 evaluable patients in Study AK 105-304 with NPC who received penpulimab 200 mg IV Q3W, 8.8% (12/137) of patients tested positive for treatment emergent ADAs and 33% (4/12) of patients for treatment emergent NAbs. The PPK analysis indicates that treatment emergent ADA had no significant impact on penpulimab PK, with <20% differences in the GMRs for CL, AUCss, Cmax,ss, and Cmin,ss. Although observed PK data showed a 47% decrease in Ctrough in ADA positive patients, this is attributable to patient factors such as weight and albumin, as the patients who tested positive and negative for ADA are not randomized. There is insufficient information to characterize the effects of anti-penpulimab antibodies on the safety and efficacy of penpulimab due to low number of ADA positive patients in Study AK105-304.
Labeling	The proposed labeling is acceptable upon the Applicant's agreement to FDA revisions to the draft labeling.
L321: Abbreviations: ADA, anti-drug antibody; AST, aspartate aminotransferase; AUCss, area under the plasma concentration-time curve at steady state; CL, clearance; Cmax,ss, maximum plasma concentration at steady state; Cmin,ss, minimum plasma concentration at steady state; Ctrough, trough concentration; ECOG, Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group; E-R, exposure-response; GMR, geometric mean ratio; IV, intravenous; LDH, lactic dehydrogenase; NAb, neutralizing antibody; NPC, nasopharyngeal carcinoma; PFS, progression-free survival; PK, pharmacokinetic(s); PPK, population pharmacokinetics; Q3W, every 3 weeks; ULN, upper limit of normal
L322: ## 6.2. Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Assessment
L323: ## 6.2.1. Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics
L324: ## Data:
L325: Penpulimab is a humanized immunoglobulin G1 (IgG1) monoclonal antibody targeting programmed cell death-1 (PD-1). Penpulimab binds specifically to human PD-1 with high affinity
L326: ## Penpulimab
L327: and competitively blocks the binding of PD-1 to its ligand programmed cell death-1 (PD-L1) and programmed cell death ligand-2 (PD-L2), thereby relieving immunosuppression of T lymphocytes, promoting the proliferation of lymphocytes and the secretion of related cytokines, and ultimately recovering cytotoxic activities of T cells to block tumor growth.
L328: Pharmacokinetic (PK) data of penpulimab were derived from 8 clinical studies. A total of 771 patients were included in the population PK analysis (PopPK), and 31 patients who underwent intensive sampling were included in the non-compartmental PK analysis. The patients received penpulimab at 1.0, 3.0 or 10.0 mg/kg Q2W, 200 mg Q2W or 200 mg Q3W. According to the non-compartmental analysis (NCA), the C_{max} and AUC_{0-14d} increased relatively proportional within the dose range of 1.0 mg/kg to 10.0 mg/kg.
L329: Penpulimab is administered via IV infusion. Based on population PK (PopPK) model (n=771), after single dose IV infusion of 200 mg, the terminal elimination half-life (t_{1/2}) of penpulimab is 32.4 days. The mean (CV%) of volume of distribution was 10.2 L (43.5%). The mean (CV%) of CL was 0.229 L/day (32.7%).
L330: Steady state concentrations of penpulimab were reached by week 15 following 200 mg Q3W, with accumulation ratios for C_{max} and AUC of 1.53 and 2.10, respectively. The mode-predicted means of C_{max,ss}, C_{avg,ss} and C_{min,ss} are 87.6 \mug/mL, 45.5 \mug/mL and 30.1 \mug/mL, respectively. The effective half-lives (t_{1/2,eff}) calculated based on C_{max} and AUC were 13.6 days and 22.4 days, respectively.
L331: Receptor occupancy (RO) is an important measure of the in vivo biological activity of penpulimab and also an important parameter underlying the determination of the recommended dose in clinical studies. In the Phase Ia clinical study conducted in Australia (AK105-101 study), patients with advanced solid tumors received penpulimab therapy (1, 3 and 10 mg/kg Q2W). PD-1 RO reached 80% to 100% starting from two days post-dose for all these cohorts. No apparent correlation between RO value and dose level was noted.
L332: ## The Applicant's Position:
L333: Penpulimab exhibited linear PK characteristics in patients who received penpulimab by IV administration within the dose range of 1.0 mg/kg to 10.0 mg/kg. PopPK analysis indicated that distribution of penpulimab was limited to the central vascular compartment, and with an effective half-life of 22.4 days and mild accumulation upon repeat dosing.
L334: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L335: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that the exposure to penpulimab increased dose proportionally over the range of 1 mg/kg to 10 mg/kg after a single dose.
L336: ## 6.2.2. General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
L337: ## 6.2.2.1. General Dosing
L338: ## Data:
L339: In the Phase Ia dose-escalation trial, no DLT occurred at the highest dose cohort of 10.0 mg/kg Q2W and no maximum tolerated dose (MTD) was explored, therefore the maximum administered dose (MAD) for this study was 10.0 mg/kg Q2W. Phase Ia PK data showed a linear relationship between exposure of penpulimab and dose over the dose range of 1.0 mg/kg Q2W to 10.0 mg/kg Q2W. Receptor occupancy analysis showed 80% to 100% PD-1 receptor occupancy starting on day 2 after administration. There was no significant difference in receptor occupancy between 1.0, 3.0 and 10.0 mg/kg doses level. The efficacy results showed anti-tumor activities at doses ranging from 1.0 mg/kg to 10.0 mg/kg Q2W. Since higher drug concentrations can be obtained with the 3 mg/kg dosing regimen than with the 1 mg/kg dosing regimen, this may ensure to achieve more stable blood concentrations after multiple doses for better sustained receptor occupancy.
L340: Results of study AK105-101 showed that, following IV administration of penpulimab 1.0, 3.0 and 10.0 mg/kg, C_{max} and AUC_{(0-14d)} increased proportionally with dose; the 90% confidence interval (CI) of \beta values of AUC_{(0-14d)} and C_{max} was 0.676-1.15 and 0.844-1.13, respectively.
L341: Based on population PK (PopPK) model (n=771), the PK parameters following single-dose IV infusion of penpulimab 200 mg are as follows: the mean C_{max} (CV%) was 57.6 \mug/mL(19.5%), the mean AUC_{(0-inf)} (CV%) was 955 \mug*day/mL(29.6%), the mean CL (CV%) was 0.229 L/day(32.7%), the mean V_z (CV%) was 10.2 L(43.5%), and the mean terminal elimination half-life (t_{1/2}) (CV%) was 32.4 days(43.5%).
L342: Following multi-dose administration of penpulimab 200 mg Q3W, the steady state was reached by Week 15; the mode-predicted mean of C_{max,ss}, C_{avg,ss} and C_{min,ss} (CV%) are 87.6 \mug/mL(21.1%), 45.5 \mug/mL (29.1%) and 30.1 \mug/mL(38.1%), respectively; the mean (CV%) accumulation ratios for C_{max} and AUC were 1.53 (12.6%) and 2.10 (19.4%), respectively. The effective half-lives(t_{1/2,eff}) calculated based on C_{max} and AUC were 13.6 days and 22.4 days, respectively.
L343: The steady-state drug concentrations following administration of penpulimab 200 mg Q3W in 771 patients are simulated. *In vitro* studies indicated 0.5 \mug/mL penpulimab was associated with a receptor occupancy close to 90%. PopPK analysis showed that, with a penpulimab dose of 200 mg Q3W, the steady-state trough concentration in more than 95% of patients was &gt;10 \mug/mL, which is 100-fold higher than those at which 80% of the receptors (0.1 \mug/mL) could be occupied or 20-fold higher than those at which 90%~100% of the receptors (0.5 \mug/mL) could be occupied.
L344: ## Penpulimab
L345: A logistic regression analysis was performed to explore the exposure-efficacy (E-E) and exposure-safety (E-S) relationships. E-E analysis suggested a relative flat exposure-response (ORR/DCR) curve and no apparent difference in exposure between responders and non-responders. E-R safety analysis indicated that no apparent correlation between exposure and incidence of TEAE/TRAЕ (Grade 3+) in the wide dose range (1-10 mg/kg Q2W) with either monotherapy or in combination therapy.
L346: In NPC population, penpulimab (200 mg Q3W) in combination with chemotherapy, was safe and tolerable.
L347: ## The Applicant's Position:
L348: The recommended dosage of penpulimab was 200 mg Q3W administered with intravenous infusion (IV). Dose selection is based on the following considerations:
L349: ## **Safety:**
L350: - In phase 1 dose-escalation phase, Penpulimab was well tolerated up to 10 mg/kg Q2W, no DLT was observed.
- In NPC population, Penpulimab (200 mg Q3W) in combination with chemotherapy, was safe and tolerable.
- E-R safety analysis indicated that no apparent correlation between exposure and incidence of TEAE/TRAЕ (Grade 3+) in the wide dose range (1 - 10 mg/kg Q2W) with either monotherapy or in combination therapy.
L351: ## **Efficacy:**
L352: - Penpulimab 200 mg Q3W demonstrated notable clinical benefits in NPC population: the median PFS was 9.6 months (95% CI:7.1, 12.5), compared to placebo of 7.0 months (95% CI: 6.9, 7.3).
- E-R analysis suggested a relative flat exposure-response (ORR/DCR) curve and no apparent difference in exposure between responders and non-responders.
L353: ## **PK/PD:**
L354: - Penpulimab exhibited linear PK characteristics over large dose range (1-10 mg/kg Q2W) with terminal half-life of 32.4 day.
- No intrinsic/extrinsic factor had clinically meaningful effect on PK and exposures were similar between Caucasian and Asian patients.
L355: ## Penpulimab
L356: - At 200 mg Q3W dose, observed mean trough concentrations in NPC patients ranged from 27.9 – 43.7 µg/mL, which was much higher than the exposure (0.5 µg/mL) achieving 90% receptor occupancy (RO) in vitro.
L357: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L358: FDA generally agrees that the proposed recommended dosage of 200 mg IV Q3W is supported by the observed clinical safety, efficacy, and PK/PD data from Study AK105-304.
L359: FDA disagrees that the E-R analysis for efficacy is relatively flat and concludes that the E-R analysis for efficacy is inconclusive given that the analysis only included one dose level.
L360: FDA generally agrees that there appears to be no apparent correlation between the incidence of most TEAEs and penpulimab exposure, but there is a positive correlation between exposure and immune-related adverse events (irAEs) and some specific adverse events (AEs) including decrease in white blood cells (WBC), hypothyroidism, rash, and upper respiratory tract infections (Refer to Section 19.4.3.3). However, there were comparable dose interruptions (77% penpulimab arm vs 71% placebo arm) and discontinuations (12% penpulimab arm vs 9% placebo arm), and the relationship between exposure and irAEs and other AEs is likely not clinically significant.
L361: ## 6.2.2.2. Therapeutic Individualization
L362: ## Data:
L363: Intrinsic factors that might have an impact on penpulimab PK were subjected to covariate analysis by the PopPK model to identify main contributors to the PK variability.
L364: Covariates investigated for their potential to influence penpulimab PK included age, body weight (WT), creatinine clearance (CLcr), creatinine concentration (CR), total bilirubin (TBIL), aspartate transaminase (AST), alkaline phosphatase (ALP), alanine aminotransferase (ALT), lactic dehydrogenase (LDH), albumin (ALB), tumor size (TSIZEB), Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (BECOG) score, C-reactive protein (CRP) at baseline, tumor type (DIAG), sex, race, co-administration (COMBO) and total anti-drug antibody (TADA).
L365: A two-compartment model with zero-order IV infusion and first-order elimination adequately described penpulimab PK. The typical subject is a male Asian patient with NPC and negative TADA, WT of 62.0 kg, ALB of 41.9 g/L, CRP of 9.55 mg/L, LDH of 214 IU/L, age of 55 years old, and receiving penpulimab plus chemotherapy.
L366: The final model included the effects of (i) WT, age, ALB, LDH, race, sex, and TADA on clearance (CL); (ii) WT, ALB, SEX, COMBO and DIAG on volume of the central compartment (V_1); (iii) CRP, COMBO and DIAG on volume of the peripheral compartment (V_2). None of the other covariates
L367: tested had statistically significant effect on the PK parameters.
L368: The parameter estimates for the final PopPK model are shown in Applicant – Table 2.

L377: Data source: Module 5.3.3.5 Penpulimab PopPK and ER report Appendix 2-Table 12.
L378: ## Age:
L379: Among the 771 patients included in the PopPK analysis, the median age was 55 years (range: 18-91 years). The results of the stepwise covariate model (SCM) indicated that age had a statistically significant effect on CL of penpulimab. CL of penpulimab increased with age. This increase in penpulimab CL ranged from -8.82% to +4.74% of the median CL estimate when age increased from the 10% (34.0 years) to the 90% (70.0 years). However, comparison of model-predicted steady state exposures (Cmax,ss, Cmin,ss, AUCss) indicated exposure ratios of less than 30%, suggesting that age has no clinically meaningful impact on penpulimab exposures.
L380: ## Sex:
L381: Females accounted for 25.6% (n=197), and males accounted for 74.4% (n=574). The CL and apparent volume of distribution of central compartment V1 of penpulimab were 11.1% and 12.8% lower in females than in males with the same weight and conditions, indicating sex had a statistically significant impact on CL and V1. However, comparison of model-predicted steady-state exposures (Cmax,ss, Cmin,ss, AUCss) indicated exposure ratios of less than 30%. Overall, the impact of sex on PK exposure was not clinically significant.
L382: ## **Race:**
L383: There were 679 (88.1 %) Asians, and 92 (11.9 %) non-Asian patients. The non-Asians included 85 Caucasians (11.0%) and 7 patients of other races (0.908%). The results of the SCM indicated that race had a statistically significant effect on CL. The relationships between race and PK parameters are depicted in Applicant – Figure 1.
L384: Compared to Asians, the CL of non-Asians was 23.1% lower. Under the same body weight and other conditions, changes in AUC_{ss} and C_{max,ss} due to race were not clinically significant but were marginally significant in C_{min,ss}. However, compared to Asians, non-Asians have a higher body weight, particularly Caucasians. The median body weights of Asians, Caucasians, and other patients were 61.0, 72.1, and 69.5 kg, respectively. CL and V_1 of penpulimab increased with body weight. Based on PopPK model simulations following 200mg Q3W, the predicted median values of C_{min,ss} were 28.9 and 27.8 \mug/mL for Asians and Caucasians, respectively; C_{max,ss} were 84.9 and 84.7 \mug/mL for Asians and Caucasians, respectively; and C_{avg,ss} were 43.8 and 44.0 \mug/mL for Asians and Caucasians, respectively. Therefore, the individual PK parameters and steady-state exposures were no significant differences between Asians and non-Asians, especially between Asians and Caucasians. The details are shown in Applicant – Figure 2 and Applicant – Table 4.

L392: ## **Body weight:**
L393: The median weight was 62.0 kg (range: 32.0-113 kg). The SCM results showed that penpulimab Volume of distribution of central compartment (V_1) increased with CL and body weight. In a patient with an extremely low weight (48.0 kg, 10% quantile) comparing to the patient with median weight (62 kg), V_1 and CL was 11.5% and 9.85% lower, respectively; In a patient with an extremely high weight (79.4 kg, 90% quantile) comparing to the patient with median weight (62 kg), V_1 and CL was 12.6% and 10.5% higher, respectively. Weight had a statistically significant impact on CL and V_1. However, comparison of model-predicted steady state exposures (C_{max,ss}, C_{min,ss}, AUC_{ss}) indicated exposure ratios of less than 30%. Hence, it is considered that the impact of weight on AUC_{ss}, C_{max,ss} and C_{min,ss} was not clinically significant.
L394: ## **Renal impairment:**
L395: In accordance with the Guidance on Pharmacokinetics in Patients with Impaired Renal Function released by FDA in 2024, renal impairment in the 771 patients included into the population PK analysis set was graded by creatinine clearance (CL_{cr}). The grading criteria and included patients are summarized as follows. Normal renal function, CL_{cr} \ge 90 mL/min (n=405); mild renal impairment: CL_{cr} &lt; 90 mL/min and CL_{cr} \ge 60 mL/min (n=301); moderate renal impairment: CL_{cr} &lt; 60 mL/min and CL_{cr} \ge 30 mL/min (n=64); 1 patient with severe renal impairment (CL_{cr} &lt; 30 mL/min) has been included.
L396: Through PopPK covariate screening, it was found that the renal function related factor (CL_{cr}) did not have a statistically significant impact on population PK parameters of penpulimab. The results showed that there was no significant difference in clearance between patients with mild or moderate renal impairment and patients with normal renal function.
L397: Data of penpulimab in patients with severe renal impairment are not adequate.
L398: ## **Hepatic impairment:**
L399: Hepatic impairment of the 771 patients included in PopPK analysis was graded by TBIL and AST. Grading criteria and included patients are summarized as follows. Normal hepatic function: TBIL &lt; \text{upper limit of normal (ULN)} and AST &lt; ULN (n=652); mild hepatic impairment: TBIL \le ULN and AST &gt; ULN or TBIL = 1.0 to 1.5 \times ULN, regardless of AST (n=113); moderate hepatic impairment: TBIL = 1.5 to 3.0 \times ULN, regardless of AST (n=6); no patient with severe hepatic impairment (TBIL &gt; 3.0 \times ULN, regardless of AST) has been included.
L400: Through PopPK analysis covariate screening, it was found that the hepatic function related factors (TBIL, AST) did not have a statistically significant impact on population PK parameters of penpulimab. The results showed that there was no significant difference in clearance between
L401: ## Penpulimab
L402: patients with mild or moderate hepatic impairment and patients with normal hepatic function. Data of penpulimab in patients with severe hepatic impairment are not adequate.
L403: ## **Tumor type:**
L404: Among the 771 patients included in PopPK analysis, there were 302 (39.2%) patients with nasopharyngeal carcinoma and 469 (60.8%) patients with other tumor types. The SCM results indicated that tumor type had a statistically significant effect on V_1 and V_2. Compared to NPC patients, the V_1 and V_2 of other solid tumor patients were 9.81% and 21.2% lower, respectively. However, comparison of model-predicted steady-state exposures (C_{max,ss}, C_{min,ss}, AUC_{ss}) indicated exposure ratios of less than 30%, suggesting that tumor type has no clinically meaningful impact on penpulimab exposures.
L405: ## **Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) score:**
L406: Among the 771 patients included in PopPK analysis, there were 243 (31.5%) patients with a baseline Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) score of 0 and 528 (68.5%) patients with an ECOG score of 1. Population PK covariate screening results showed that ECOG score did not have a statistically significant impact on PK parameters of penpulimab.
L407: ## **Serum albumin (ALB):**
L408: Among the 771 patients included in PopPK analysis, the median albumin (ALB) was 41.9 g/L (range: 22.0 - 67.7 g/L). Population PK covariate screening results showed that penpulimab CL and V_1 decreased with increase of ALB; Clearance (CL) of the patient with ALB at the 10% quantile level (34.7 g/L) was 13.9% higher than CL of the patient with ALB at the median level; CL of the patient with ALB at the 90% quantile (47.4 g/L) was 8.16% lower than CL of the patient with ALB at the median level. the central compartment (V_1) of the patient with ALB at the 10% quantile level (34.7 g/L) was 5.92% higher than V_1 of the patient with ALB at the median level; V_1 of the patient with ALB at the 90% quantile (47.4 g/L) was 3.69% lower than V_1 of the patient with ALB at the median level. Therefore, ALB had a statistically significant impact on CL and V_1. The geometric mean ratios of PK parameters (AUC_{ss}, C_{max,ss}, C_{min,ss}) in patients with ALB at 10% or 90% quantile levels versus the typical patient were between 0.8 and 1.1; namely, the differences were less than 30%. Hence, the impact of ALB on AUC_{ss}, C_{max,ss} and C_{min,ss} was not clinically significant.
L409: ## **Lactic dehydrogenase (LDH):**
L410: Among the 771 patients included in PopPK analysis, the median LDH was 214 IU/L (range: 111 - 4450 IU/L). SCM results showed that penpulimab CL increased with increase of LDH; CL of the patient with LDH at the 10% quantile level (154 IU/L) was 13.9 % lower than CL of the patient with LDH at the median level; CL of the patient with LDH at the 90% quantile (447 IU/L) was
L411: ## Penpulimab
L412: 8.44% higher than CL of the patient with LDH at the median level. Therefore, LDH had a statistically significant impact on CL. The geometric mean ratios of PK parameters (AUC_{SS}, C_{max,SS}, C_{min,SS}) in patients with LDH at 10% or 90% quantile levels versus the typical patient were between 0.8 and 1.1; namely, the differences were less than 30%. Hence, the impact of LDH on AUC_{SS}, C_{max,SS} and C_{min,SS} was not clinically significant.
L413: ## **C-reactive protein (CRP):**
L414: Among the 771 patients included in PopPK analysis, the median baseline CRP was 9.55 mg/L (range: 0 - 328 mg/L). SCM results showed that CRP did not have a statistically significant impact on CL of penpulimab, but indicated that the V_2 increased with increase of ALB; the V_2 of the patient with CRP at the 10% quantile level(1.10 mg/L) was 16.7% lower than V_2 of the patient with CRP at the median level; V_2 of the patient with CRP at the 90% quantile(70.9 mg/L) was 18.4% higher than V_2 of the patient with CRP at the median level. Therefore, CRP had a statistically significant impact on V_2. The geometric mean ratios of PK parameters (AUC_{SS}, C_{max,SS}, C_{min,SS}) in patients with CRP at 10% or 90% quantile levels versus the typical patient were between 0.8 and 1.1; namely, the differences were less than 30%. Hence, the impact of CRP on AUC_{SS}, C_{max,SS} and C_{min,SS} was not clinically significant.
L415: ## **Impacts of extrinsic factors on PK:**
L416: Impacts of extrinsic factors (e.g., food, alcohol or smoking) on penpulimab PK have not been assessed in this study.
L417: ## **Impacts of immunogenicity on PK:**
L418: If ADA to penpulimab were detected at any visit, the patient was classified as TADA-positive immune response, otherwise as a TADA-negative immune response. Impacts of TADA on PK parameters were analyzed by a PopPK analysis.
L419: Among the 771 patients included in PopPK analysis, 235 patients (30.5%) were TADA positive, and 536 patients (69.5%) were TADA negative. The SCM results indicated that TADA had a statistically significant effect on CL of penpulimab.
L420: Compared to TADA-negative patients, the CL of TADA-positive patients was 16.8% higher. However, comparison of model-predicted steady state exposures (C_{max,ss}, C_{min,ss}, AUC_{ss}) indicated exposure ratios of less than 30%, suggesting that TADA has no clinically meaningful impact on penpulimab exposures.
L421: ## **Impacts of Combination Therapy (Co-administration) PK:**
L422: Among the 771 patients included in PopPK analysis, 380 patients (49.3%) received penpulimab
L423: ## Penpulimab
L424: monotherapy, and 391 patients (50.7%) received penpulimab plus chemotherapy. The results of SCM indicated that co-administration had a statistically significant effect on V_1 and V_2. Compared to penpulimab monotherapy patients, the V_1 and V_2 of penpulimab plus chemotherapy patients were 9.04% and 54.8% lower, respectively. However, comparison of model-predicted steady state exposures (C_{max,ss}, C_{min,ss}, AUC_{ss}) indicated exposure ratios of less than 30%, suggesting that co-administration has no clinically meaningful impact on penpulimab exposures.
L425: ## The Applicant's Position:
L426: The PK profiles of penpulimab could be described using a two-compartment model with zero-order IV infusion and first-order elimination. Covariate analysis showed that the impacts of age, body weight, ALB, LDH, race, TADA and sex on CL and impacts of body weight, ALB, combination therapy, tumor type and sex on V_1 were statistically significant, impacts of CRP, combination therapy and tumor type on V_2 were statistically significant, but these factors were not clinically significant.
L427: According to PopPK analysis, body weight (32.0 to 113 kg), age (18 to 91 years), sex, race (White and Asian), ADA, albumin (22.0 to 67.7g/L), C-reactive protein (0 to 328 mg/L), lactate dehydrogenase (111 to 4450 IU/L), tumor types (NPC vs. other tumors) and co-administration (monotherapy vs. combination therapy) were identified as significant covariates affecting PK of Penpulimab. However, these covariates did not have clinically meaningful effects on the steady-state exposures, thus no dose adjustment is required. Mild to moderate hepatic impairment or mild to moderate renal impairment did not have significant impact on the CL and steady-state exposures of Penpulimab, therefore no dose adjustment is required. There's no sufficient data on patients with severe hepatic impairment or severe renal impairment.
L428: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L429: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that no dosage adjustment is recommended based on age (18 to 91 years), sex, race (White [3%] and Asian [97%]), body weight (32 to 113 kg), serum albumin (22 to 54.5 g/L), baseline LDH (111 to 4,450 IU/L), baseline C-reactive protein (0 to 328 mg/L), baseline ECOG score (0 and 1), immunogenicity status, tumor type (NPC and other tumors), co-administration with chemotherapy, mild or moderate renal impairment (creatinine clearance [CL_{cr}] \ge 30 mL/min), mild (total bilirubin \le ULN and AST &gt; ULN or total bilirubin &gt; 1 to 1.5 times ULN and any AST) or moderate hepatic impairment (total bilirubin &gt; 1.5 to 3 times ULN and any AST) according to the results of the PPK analyses.

L437: ## 6.3. Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review
L438: ## 6.3.1. General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics
L439: ## Data:
L440: The clinical pharmacology of Penpulimab has been studied in patients with malignant solid tumors such as NPC or non-solid tumours such as cHL who received penpulimab by intravenous infusion administration at dose range of 1 - 10 mg/kg every 2 weeks, or 200 mg every 2 or 3 weeks. Levels of serum penpulimab were measured using validated methods and the results were used to evaluate PK parameters.
L441: PK data of penpulimab were derived from 8 clinical studies. A total of 771 patients were included into population PK analysis, and 31 patients who underwent intensive sampling into non-compartmental PK analysis. The PK profile is outlined as follows:
L442: ## Absorption
L443: Penpulimab is administered via IV infusion, and the drug directly enters blood circulation.
L444: ## Distribution
L445: According to PopPK analysis, following administration of penpulimab 200 mg as a single IV infusion, the mean (CV%) of volume of distribution was 10.2 L (43.5%).
L446: ## Elimination
L447: According to PopPK analysis, the mean (CV%) of CL was 0.229 L/day (32.7%). The effective half-
L448: ## Penpulimab
L449: life (t_{1/2,eff}) was 22.4 days (27.4%), following IV infusion of 200 mg every 3 weeks.
L450: ## Linearity/non-linearity
L451: The exposure of penpulimab increase dose proportionally in the dose range of 1 to 10 mg/kg.
L452: ## Steady state
L453: According to PopPK analysis, the penpulimab concentration was approximate to the steady state at Week 15 following IV infusion of penpulimab 200 mg Q3W, and the accumulation ratios for Cmax and AUC were 1.53 and 2.10, respectively. The mean steady-state trough concentration (CV%) was 30.1 µg/mL (38.1%).
L454: ## Other PopPK analysis Results
L455: A two-compartment model with zero-order IV infusion and first-order elimination adequately described penpulimab PK. The typical subject is a male Asian patient with NPC and negative TADA, WT of 62.0 kg, ALB of 41.9 g/L, CRP of 9.55 mg/L, LDH of 214 IU/L, age of 55 years old, and receiving penpulimab plus chemotherapy.
L456: According to PopPK analysis, body weight (32.0 to 113 kg), age (18 to 91 years), sex, race (White and Asian), ADA, albumin (22.0 to 67.7g/L), C-reactive protein (0 to 328 mg/L), lactate dehydrogenase (111 to 4450 IU/L), tumor types (NPC vs. other tumors) and co-administration (monotherapy vs. combination therapy) were identified as significant covariates affecting PK of Penpulimab. However, these covariates did not have clinically meaningful effects on the steady-state exposures, thus no dose adjustment is required. Mild to moderate hepatic impairment or mild to moderate renal impairment did not have significant impact on the CL and steady-state exposures of Penpulimab, therefore no dose adjustment is required. There's no sufficient data on patients with severe hepatic impairment or severe renal impairment.
L457: ## Immunogenicity results
L458: Total of 720 patients included in the immunogenicity evaluation were treated with penpulimab of 1, 3 and 10 mg/kg Q2W, or 200mg Q2W/Q3W (either monotherapy or combination therapy). 26.8% of patients developed treatment-emergent ADA (TEADA), and 7.9% of patients had positive neutralizing antibody (Nab).
L459: - 349 patients were treated with penpulimab monotherapy at 1, 3 and 10mg/kg Q2W, or 200mg Q2W/Q3W, 30.1% of patients developed treatment-emergent ADA (TEADA), and 5.2% patients had positive Nab.
L460: ## Penpulimab
L461: - 371 patients were treated with penpulimab of combination therapy at 200mg Q3W, 23.7% of patients developed treatment-emergent ADA (TEADA), and 10.5% of patients had positive Nab.
- For NPC patients who received penpulimab in combination with chemotherapy, Proportion of patients with ADA positive at baseline and post-baseline was 3.6%, and 10.9%, respectively. 8.8% of patients developed TEADA, and 2.9% of those patients had positive Nab.
L462: The overall incidence rates of TEAEs and immune-related adverse events(irAEs) between TEADA and ADA-negative patients were comparable. ADA has no clinically meaningful impact on penpulimab efficacy and safety.
L463: ## The Applicant's Position:
L464: The information from clinical studies contributing to the clinical pharmacology evaluation of penpulimab was included in the submitted reports (*Module 2.7.2 Summary of Clinical Pharmacology, Module 5.3.3.5 Penpulimab PopPK and ER report*). Penpulimab exhibited linear PK(from dose 1.0 mg/kg to 10.0 mg/kg) with an effective half-life (t_{1/2,eff}) of 22.4 days and the mean of CL was 0.229 L/day.
L465: No dedicated clinical drug-drug interaction studies have been conducted in humans. Penpulimab is a humanized monoclonal antibody, PK interactions with other drugs have not been studied. As monoclonal antibodies are not metabolized by cytochrome P450 (CYP) or other drug metabolizing enzymes, inhibition or induction of these enzymes by co-administered medicinal products is not anticipated to affect the pharmacokinetics of penpulimab.
L466: Overall, penpulimab administered by intravenous infusion at fixed 200mg Q3W regimen, demonstrated a favorable benefit-risk profile for patients in the target population of recurrent or metastatic nasopharyngeal carcinoma.
L467: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L468: FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of the PK characteristics of penpulimab. Of the 137 evaluable patients in Study AK 105-304 with NPC who received penpulimab 200 mg IV Q3W, 8.8% (12/137) of patients tested positive for treatment emergent ADAs and 33% (4/12) of patients for treatment emergent NABs. The PPK analysis indicates that treatment emergent ADA had no significant impact on penpulimab PK, with &lt;20% differences in the GMRs for CL, AUC_{ss}, C_{max,ss}, and C_{min,ss}. Although observed PK data showed a 47% decrease in C_{trough} in ADA positive patients, this is attributable to patient factors such as weight and albumin, as the patients who tested positive and negative for ADA are not randomized.
L469: ## 6.3.2. Clinical Pharmacology Questions
L470: ## 6.3.2.1. Does the clinical pharmacology program provide supportive evidence of effectiveness?
L471: Yes, the clinical pharmacology program provides supportive evidence of penpulimab effectiveness in patients in the target population of recurrent or metastatic nasopharyngeal carcinoma.
L472: ## Data:
L473: For AK105-304 study, patients with recurrent or metastatic NPC were enrolled in the study. Among the 286 patients included in the primary efficacy population, per the primary analysis of BICR-assessed PFS based on RECIST v1.1, 80 (55.6%) subjects in AK105 group and 109 (74.1%) subjects in placebo group had PD or died, median PFS of the two groups was 9.6 (95% CI: 7.1, 12.5) and 7.0 (95% CI: 6.9, 7.3) months, respectively. Compared with placebo group, median PFS in AK105 group was 2.6 months longer, the risk of PD or death was reduced by 55%, HR=0.45 (95% CI: 0.33, 0.62; P&lt;0.0001). Per BICR's assessment, the confirmed ORR or DCR for AK105 group was 68.1% (95% CI: 59.8%, 75.6%) and 91.0% (95% CI: 85.1%, 95.1%), respectively.
L474: To support the clinical efficacy observed with penpulimab dosed at 200mg Q3W regimen, exposure-efficacy relationship was evaluated in NPC population (AK105-304 study), see [Module 2.7.2, Section 3.3.1 and 3.3.2]. Univariate logistic regression analysis was conducted to assess the relationship between penpulimab PK exposures and model-predicted ORR response (CR/PR) or DOR response (CR/PR/SD). Results of E-R analysis suggested a relative flat exposure-response (ORR/DCR) curve and no apparent difference in exposure between responders and non-responders.
L475: A total of 380 patients received penpulimab monotherapy at 1, 3, 10 mg/kg Q2W, or 200 mg Q2W. There's no significant dose or exposure-dependent increase in the incidence of TEAEs, TRAEs or CTCAE Grade3+ AEs, and drug-related SAEs. A total of 391 patients received penpulimab 200 mg Q3W in combination therapy. Similar to monotherapy, there was no significant increase in TEAE/TRAЕ (Grade 3+) with increasing of PK exposure, while association with irAE was found to be statistically significant. For AEs with incidence rate of 10% or higher, white blood cell count decrease, hypothyroidism, rash and upper respiratory tract infection were associated with higher exposure, consistent with common adverse events observed in this population.
L476: Overall, E-R safety analysis suggested a flat exposure-response relationships between penpulimab exposure and most AEs in both monotherapy and combination therapy groups. However, higher exposure levels may be associated with a slightly increased risk of irAEs and some specific AEs.
L477: ## The Applicant's Position:
L478: Penpulimab 200 mg Q3W showed notable clinical benefits in patients with NPC, and E-R analysis suggested a relative flat exposure-response (ORR/DCR) curve and no apparent difference in exposure between responders and non-responders. In NPC population, Penpulimab (200 mg Q3W) in combination with chemotherapy was safe and tolerable. E-R safety analysis indicated that no apparent correlation between exposure and incidence of TEAE/TRAЕ (Grade 3+) in the wide dose range (1-10 mg/kg Q2W) with either monotherapy or in combination therapy.
L479: In conclusion, the interim analysis results of AK105-304 showed that the Phase 3 trial of penpulimab combined with gemcitabine and cisplatin/carboplatin as the first-line treatment for R/M NPC patients, met the preset primary endpoint of BICR-assessed PFS, and there was no evidence of a detrimental effect on the long survival follow up. Compared with the current standard chemotherapy regimen, penpulimab combined with gemcitabine and cisplatin/carboplatin did not increase the toxicity of chemotherapy. Compared with similar studies of other PD-1 inhibitors combined with chemotherapy as first-line treatment for R/M NPC, the efficacy and safety were generally consistent, no new safety signal was identified. Therefore, penpulimab combined with gemcitabine and cisplatin/carboplatin has a relatively significant benefit-risk ratio in R/M NPC patients globally.
L480: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L481: FDA does not agree that there is a flat E-R relationship for efficacy. FDA notes that as in the penpulimab monotherapy analysis during the original BLA submission, the E-R analysis for efficacy of penpulimab in combination with chemotherapy is inconclusive due to the limited exposure range of penpulimab at one dose level.
L482: ## **6.3.2.2. Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought?**
L483: Yes, penpulimab administered by intravenous infusion at 200mg Q3W regimen is appropriate for the first-line treatment for R/M NPC patients when combined with gemcitabine and cisplatin/carboplatin. The proposed dosing regimen is supported by the observed efficacy, safety, and PK/PD data.
L484: ## Data:
L485: In the Phase Ia dose-escalation trial, no DLT occurred at the highest dose cohort of 10.0 mg/kg Q2W and no MTD was explored, therefore the maximum administered dose(MAD) for this study was 10.0 mg/kg Q2W. Phase Ia PK data showed a linear relationship between exposure of penpulimab and dose over the dose range of 1.0 mg/kg Q2W to 10.0 mg/kg Q2W; receptor
L486: ## Penpulimab
L487: occupancy analysis showed 80% to 100% PD-1 receptor occupancy starting on day 2 after dosing. There was no significant difference in receptor occupancy between the 1.0, 3.0 and 10.0 mg/kg doses. According to the non-compartmental analysis, the drug exposure, expressed as Cmax and AUC(0-14d), increased proportionally with dose within the dose range of 1.0 mg/kg to 10.0 mg/kg following single-dose IV infusion of penpulimab.
L488: Simulation of steady-state concentrations with sparse sampling following Q2W administration in the 771 patients in the population PK analysis set who received penpulimab 200 mg showed that in more than 95% of patients the steady-state trough concentrations could reach over steady state trough concentrations &gt; 10 µg/mL, which is 100-fold higher than those at which 80% of the receptors (0.1 µg/mL) or 20-fold higher than those at which 90%~100% of the receptors (0.5 µg/mL) could be occupied in vitro.
L489: The population PK profile of penpulimab could be described using a two-compartment model with zero-order IV infusion and first-order elimination. Covariate analysis showed that the impacts of age, body weight, ALB, LDH, race, TADA and sex on CL and impacts of body weight, ALB, combination therapy, tumor type and sex on V1 were statistically significant, impacts of CRP, combination therapy and tumor type on V2 were statistically significant, but these factors were not clinically significant.
L490: Based on the final PopPK model, serum concentrations were simulated for 771 patients after IV infusion of 200 mg penpulimab, and PK parameters were calculated by non-compartmental modeling. Simulated PK parameters following single-dose IV infusion of penpulimab 200 mg are as follows: the mean Cmax (CV%) was 57.6 µg/mL (19.5%), the mean AUC(0-inf) (CV%) was 955 µg*day/mL (29.6%), the mean CL (CV%) was 0.229 L/d (32.7%), the mean V2 (CV%) was 10.2 L (43.5%), the mean terminal elimination half-life (t1/2) (CV%) was 32.4 d (43.5%).
L491: Based on the final population PK model, serum penpulimab concentrations at each time point within 42 weeks following multi-dose (200 mg Q3W) IV infusion in 771 patients were simulated. Following multi-dose administration of penpulimab 200 mg Q3W, the steady state was reached at about Week 15; the mode-predicted mean of Cmax,ss, Cavg,ss and Cmin,ss (CV%) are 87.6 µg/mL (21.1%), 45.5 µg/mL (29.1%) and 30.1 µg/mL (38.1%), respectively; the mean accumulation ratios for Cmax and AUC (CV%) were 1.53 (12.6%) and 2.10 (19.4%), respectively. The effective half-lives (t1/2eff) calculated based on Cmax and AUC were 13.6 days and 22.4 days, respectively.
L492: According to PopPK analysis, body weight (32.0 to 113 kg), age (18 to 91 years), sex, race (White and Asian), ADA, albumin (22.0 to 67.7 g/L), C-reactive protein (0 to 328 mg/L), lactate dehydrogenase (111 to 4450 IU/L), tumor types (NPC vs. other tumors) and co-administration (monotherapy vs. combination therapy) were identified as significant covariates affecting PK of Penpulimab. However, these covariates did not have clinically meaningful effects on the steady-state exposures, thus no dose adjustment is required. Mild to moderate hepatic impairment or
L493: ## Penpulimab
L494: mild to moderate renal impairment did not have significant impact on the CL and steady-state exposures of Penpulimab, therefore no dose adjustment is required. There's no sufficient data on patients with severe hepatic impairment or severe renal impairment.
L495: ## The Applicant's Position:
L496: The recommended dosage of penpulimab was 200 mg Q3W administered intravenous (IV) infusion, based on available safety, efficacy and PK/PD results from Phase 1 to 3 trials, and for details, see section 6.2.2.1 General Dosing.
L497: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L498: FDA agrees that a dosage of 200 mg Q3W in combination with chemotherapy is acceptable based on the results of the PPK, E-R, and immunogenicity analyses, in addition to the clinical benefit and safety profile observed from Study AK105-304 (Refer to Section 6.2.2).
L499: ## **6.3.2.3. Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors?**
L500: No, penpulimab administered by intravenous infusion at 200mg Q3W regimen is appropriate for the first-line treatment for R/M NPC patients when combined with gemcitabine and cisplatin/carboplatin.
L501: ## Data:
L502: The population PK profile of penpulimab could be described using a two-compartment model with zero-order IV infusion and first-order elimination. Covariate analysis showed that the impacts of age, body weight, ALB, LDH, race, TADA and sex on CL and impacts of body weight, ALB, combination therapy, tumor type and sex on V_1 were statistically significant, impacts of CRP, combination therapy and tumor type on V_2 were statistically significant, but these factors were not clinically significant.
L503: According to PopPK analysis, body weight (32.0 to 113 kg), age (18 to 91 years), sex, race (White and Asian), ADA, albumin (22.0 to 67.7g/L), C-reactive protein (0 to 328 mg/L), lactate dehydrogenase (111 to 4450 IU/L), tumor types (NPC vs. other tumors) and co-administration (monotherapy vs. combination therapy) were identified as significant covariates affecting PK of Penpulimab. However, these covariates did not have clinically meaningful effects on the steady-state exposures, thus no dose adjustment is required. Mild to moderate hepatic impairment or mild to moderate renal impairment did not have significant impact on the CL and steady-state exposures of Penpulimab, therefore no dose adjustment is required. There's no sufficient data on patients with severe hepatic impairment or severe renal impairment.
L504: ## Penpulimab
L505: ## The Applicant's Position:
L506: No dose adjustment is required based on age, race, body weight or sex. No dose adjustment is required in patients with mild, moderate (b) (4) renal impairment, data of penpulimab in patients with severe renal impairment are not adequate. No dose adjustment is required in patients with mild or moderate hepatic impairment. There's no sufficient data on patients with severe hepatic impairment or severe renal impairment.
L507: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L508: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position that no dosage adjustment is currently required based on intrinsic patient factors evaluated in the PPK analysis and that there are insufficient data in patients with severe renal and hepatic impairment. (b) (4)
L509: ## **6.3.2.4. Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate management strategy?**
L510: No, penpulimab is intended for intravenous infusion administration; therefore, food-drug interactions are not expected.
L511: ## The Applicant's Position:
L512: Penpulimab is intended for intravenous infusion administration; therefore, food-drug interactions are not expected.
L513: Penpulimab is a humanized monoclonal antibody, and PK interactions with other drugs have not been studied. As monoclonal antibodies are not metabolized by cytochrome P450 (CYP) or other drug metabolizing enzymes, inhibition or induction of these enzymes by co-administered medicinal products is not anticipated to affect the pharmacokinetics of penpulimab.
L514: Penpulimab belongs to a class of drugs called immune checkpoint inhibitors. Because the mechanism of action of this class of drugs is to block inhibitory signal that prevents activation of T cells there is a potential that these drugs may cause immune mediated adverse drug reaction. Withhold or permanently discontinue penpulimab depending on severity. In general, if penpulimab requires interruption or discontinuation, administer systemic corticosteroid therapy (1 mg to 2 mg/kg/day prednisone or equivalent) until improvement to Grade 1 or less. Upon improvement to Grade 1 or less, initiate corticosteroid taper and continue to taper over at least 1 month. Consider administration of other systemic immunosuppressants in patients whose immune-mediated adverse reactions are not controlled by corticosteroid therapy.
L515: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L516: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that no food-drug or drug-drug interactions for penpulimab are expected.

L542: ## 8. Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L543: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L544: ## 8.1.1. Study AK105-304
L545: ## Trial Design
L546: ## The Applicant's Description:
L547: AK105-304 is a randomized, double-blind, multi-center phase III clinical study to compare the efficacy and safety of penpulimab combined with chemotherapy and placebo combined with chemotherapy in the first-line treatment of R/M NPC.
L548: Approximately 278 subjects were planned to be enrolled in this study. The subjects were randomized in a 1:1 ratio to the 2 treatment groups, stratified by stages of disease (primary metastases vs. recurrent), ECOG (0 vs. 1), and liver metastasis (present vs. absent):
L549: Arm A (experimental arm): Penpulimab+ gemcitabine + cisplatin/carboplatin, every 3 weeks (Q3W) for up to 6 cycles, followed by penpulimab monotherapy Q3W.
L550: Arm B (control arm): Placebo+ gemcitabine + cisplatin/carboplatin, Q3W for up to 6 cycles, followed by placebo monotherapy Q3W.
L551: In each arm, carboplatin was selected as a replacement of cisplatin at the discretion of investigator prior to randomization. After randomization, change of chemotherapy regimen was not allowed during the treatment period. Subjects received up to 6 cycles of penpulimab or placebo combined with chemotherapy, followed by penpulimab or placebo monotherapy Q3W as maintenance treatment until lack of clinical benefit as judged by the investigator (comprehensive judgment by the investigator based on RECIST v1.1 imaging assessment and clinical condition), intolerable toxicity, completion of 24 months of treatment, or other criteria for discontinuation outlined in the protocol, whichever occurred first.
L552: The study consisted of a screening period (up to 28 days from the signing of the informed consent form to the first dose of study treatment), a treatment period (including on-treatment visits and an end-of-treatment visit), and a follow-up period (including a safety follow-up visit, a disease progression follow-up visit, and survival follow-up visits). Subjects underwent a screening evaluation within 28 days before the first dose to confirm eligibility. Tumor specimens (tumor tissue samples either archived or freshly obtained) were collected from subjects during the screening period.
L553: Regular tumor response assessments were performed for all subjects, with PFS evaluated by BICR based on RECIST v1.1 as the primary response variable. Subjects underwent a tumor assessment every 6 weeks (\pm 7 days) for the first 54 weeks after the first dose of study
L554: ## Penpulimab
L555: treatment, and every 9 weeks (\pm 7 days) thereafter until radiographic progression or loss of clinical benefit (whichever occurs later), or the start of new antitumor therapy, or lost to follow-up, or death, or withdrawal of consent, or study closure, whichever occurred first.
L556: If any radiographic disease progression was confirmed by BICR (based on RECIST v1.1), the treatment assignment was unblinded for the subject. The subjects could receive open-label penpulimab monotherapy (200 mg, administered on Day 1 of each cycle, Q3W) until intolerable toxicity, lack of clinical benefit as judged by the investigator, or completion of 24 months of treatment, or study termination by the sponsor, whichever occurred first.
L557: Subjects in Arm A were allowed to continue open-label treatment with penpulimab monotherapy beyond radiographic disease progression if, in the opinion of the investigator, the subject would benefit from continued treatment and if other criteria are met as outlined in the protocol.
L558: Subjects in Arm B had the opportunity to crossover to open-label treatment with penlulimab monotherapy after radiographic disease progression (confirmed by BICR) if, in the opinion of the investigator, the subject would benefit from treatment with penpulimab and if other criteria were met as outlined in the protocol.
L559: Adverse events (AEs) of each subject were recorded until 30 days after the last dose of study treatment (either penpulimab/placebo or chemotherapy) or the initiation of other antitumor therapy, whichever occurred first. Serious adverse events (SAEs) were recorded until 90 days after the last dose of study treatment (including chemotherapy) or the initiation of other antitumor therapy, whichever occurred first. Immune-related AEs (irAEs) were recorded until 90 days after the last dose of penpulimab (regardless of initiation of other antitumor therapy). Survival assessment was performed every 3 months (\pm 7 days) after the last dose to collect survival and subsequent antitumor therapy information.
L560: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L561: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of Study AK105-304. FDA clarifies that, in protocol version 2.1, dated August 31, 2022, based on FDA's recommendation for a multiregional clinical trial, the Applicant had proposed to enroll 218 (78%) of the patients in China and the remaining 60 (22%) patients from outside Asia. This update to the patient enrollment strategy was added after study initiation but prior to the conduct of any efficacy analysis. Despite these enrollment goals, as of the data cut-off date of April 29, 2024, the study had enrolled 291 patients, of whom only 11 (3.8%) were enrolled outside of Asia. The Applicant clarified that the study had enrolled 280 patients in China to adhere to Chinese regulatory requirements; however, due to enrollment difficulties outside of Asia, the study was only able to enroll 11 patients outside Asia. This change in enrollment was deemed acceptable for this particular application by the FDA review team and was communicated to the Applicant during preliminary meeting minutes issued April 12, 2024, for a pre-BLA meeting, and in subsequent email communications on April 17, 2024, and April 19, 2024. See FDA's assessment under
L562: section “Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments” for additional details.
L563: ## Study Endpoints
L564: ## The Applicant's Description:
L565: - Primary Efficacy Endpoint: progression-free survival (PFS) assessed by blinded independent center review (BICR) based on Response Evaluation Criteria in Solid Tumor, version 1.1 (RECIST v1.1).
- Key Secondary Efficacy Endpoints: overall survival (OS)
- Other Secondary Endpoints:BICR-assessed ORR, DoR, and DCR based on RECIST v1.1.
- Investigator-assessed PFS, ORR, DoR, and DCR based on RECIST v1.1.
- Safety assessment: incidence and severity of adverse events (AEs) and serious adverse events (SAEs), and clinically significant laboratory abnormalities.
- PK profile: serum drug concentrations of penpulimab in individual subjects at different time points after penpulimab administration.
- Immunogenicity: number and percentage of subjects with detectable anti-drug antibody (ADA).
- Health-related quality of life (HRQoL): the European Organization for Research and Treatment of Cancer Quality of Life Questionnaire-Core 30 (EORTC QLQ-C30) and EORTC QLQ-H&amp;N43.
- PD-L1 expression in tumor samples, and the correlation between PD-L1 expression and efficacy.
- Blood EBV level at baseline and changes from baseline after administration of study drugs, and correlation between blood EBV level and efficacy.
L566: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L567: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the study endpoints with the exception that DCR was considered to be an exploratory endpoint.
L568: ## Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
L569: ## The Applicant's Description:
L570: The sample size calculation was based on the primary endpoint of BICR-assessed PFS. At a two-sided alpha level of 0.05, a sample size of 278 patients (218 patients from China and 60 patients from ex-Asia countries) with 222 PFS events would provide 89% power for the trial to detect a hazard ratio (HR) of 0.65. Due to extreme enrollment challenges in ex-Asia countries which had
L571: been discussed with FDA, all 280 patients enrolled in China and 11 patients enrolled from ex-Asia countries at the time of data cutoff were included for the global analysis.
L572: Based on the FDA's agreement to the population change, it's documented in the statistical analysis plan that an interim efficacy analysis of PFS is planned to be performed by an independent external statistician overseen by Independent Data Monitoring Committee (IDMC) when over 190 PFS events have been observed. As of the data cutoff date of April 29, 2024, there were 189 BICR-assessed PFS events in the ITT population.

L575: ## Definition of Key Analysis Sets
L576: ## - Intention-to-treat (ITT) set:
L577: ITT set included all randomized subjects. Subjects were analyzed according to the treatment to which they were randomized. The ITT set was used for the disposition of subjects, demographics, baseline characteristics and primary efficacy analysis.
L578: ## - Safety Set (SS)
L579: SS included all randomized subjects who received at least one dose of the study drug. Subjects were analyzed according to the treatment that they actually received. The SS was used for drug exposure and safety analysis.
L580: ## - Pharmacokinetics Set (PKS)
L581: PKS included all randomized subjects who received at least one dose of penpulimab and had at least one valid post-dose serum concentration data.
L582: ## - Immunogenicity Set (IMS)
L583: For the penpulimab plus chemotherapy group, IMS included all randomized subjects who received at least one dose of the penpulimab and had at least one pre-dose immunogenicity assessment and one post-dose immunogenicity assessment. For the placebo plus chemotherapy group, it included all subjects who entered the crossover treatment phase and received at least one dose of penpulimab, and had at least one pre-dose immunogenicity assessment before the crossover treatment and one post-dose immunogenicity assessment.
L584: ## - Crossover Treatment Set (CTS)
L585: CTS included subjects of the control group who received AK105 at least once during the crossover treatment phase.
L586: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L587: The proposed sample size of 278 for Study AK105-304 was based on PFS. As of protocol version 2.1, dated August 31, 2022, the Applicant had proposed to enroll 218 (78%) patients in China and 60 (22%) from outside of Asia. As of the data cut-off date of April 29, 2024, the study had enrolled 291 patients of whom only 11 (3.8%) patients were enrolled outside of Asia. Based on version 2.1 of the statistical analysis plan, dated June 17, 2024, assuming a median PFS of 7 months in the placebo arm and 10.8 months in the penpulimab arm, 222 events would provide 89% power to detect a hazard ratio of 0.65 at a 2-sided level of significance of 0.05. The accrual duration for the study was 28 months with a follow-up of an additional 6 months. The annual dropout rate was assumed to be 5%. The primary analysis was a stratified log-rank test based on the ITT population.
L588: In protocol version 2.2 and in statistical analysis plan version 1.1, submitted to the FDA on September 16, 2022, one interim analysis for PFS was proposed at 155 (70%) events for efficacy, which was considered reasonable at that time by the review team. However, in the
L589: briefing document for the pre-BLA meeting, the Applicant stated that according to a data transfer from an imaging vendor, 172 PFS events had been observed at that time across the treatment and placebo arms and that the Applicant projected an immediate data cutoff date would result in 190 (86%) PFS events for the interim analysis. In an email communication dated April 17, 2024, the Applicant clarified that the study had enrolled 280 patients in China to adhere to Chinese regulatory requirements and that the Applicant had planned to include the first 218 patients from China in the efficacy population for the marketing application in the US and that the projected 155 PFS events would be based on those 218 patients from China, along with 60 patients from outside of Asia. The Applicant stated that substantial challenges were experienced during patient enrollment outside of Asia due to the approval of toripalimab, the conduct of the trial during the COVID-19 epidemic period, and the rarity of NPC outside of Asia. The Applicant also stated that patients with NPC in the U.S in the first-line setting could be prescribed immunotherapies, which would make patients less willing to participate in a randomized clinical trial with a 50% chance of receiving chemotherapy only in the first line treatment setting. Hence, the Applicant proposed to include all 280 patients from China in the efficacy population for the marketing application in the US. The Applicant further stated that this proposed change in the efficacy population required FDA's concurrence prior to the conduct of a formal analysis and therefore, the Applicant did not conduct the PFS interim analysis based on 155 events and the number of events had overrun the prespecified number of events at the time of the pre-BLA meeting. The Applicant also stated that the 222 events prespecified for the final analysis of PFS was expected to be reached on January 10, 2025. The review team informed the Applicant that a PFS interim analysis based on 190 events could be acceptable for a marketing application in the US. Consequently, the statistical analysis plan for Study AK105-304 was updated and version 2.1 dated June 17, 2024, reflected these proposed changes to the PFS interim analysis at 190 (86%) events. In this version, the Applicant stated that the efficacy boundaries at the interim and final analysis of PFS would be calculated based on the O'Brien-Fleming boundaries using the observed information fraction. The actual PFS interim analysis, based on the data cut-off date of April 29, 2024, was conducted based on 189 (85%) events. Based on O'Brien-Fleming boundaries, the 2-sided p-value boundary at the interim analysis of PFS was 0.030.
L590: The study was also designed to test OS. Assuming a median OS of 29 months in the placebo arm and 44.6 months in the penpulimab arm, 172 deaths would provide 80% power to detect a hazard ratio of 0.65 at a 2-sided level of significance of 0.05. Two formal interim analyses were proposed, at 103 (60%) deaths and at 138 (80%) deaths. Additionally, if PFS was statistically significant at the interim analysis, the Applicant proposed to spend a 2-sided nominal alpha of 0.0001 for a descriptive analysis of OS at the time of the primary PFS analysis. Efficacy boundaries would be calculated based on the observed information fraction using the O'Brien-Fleming boundary method. The Applicant proposed a hierarchical testing strategy where PFS would be tested first at a 2-sided alpha of 0.05, and OS would be tested only if PFS was statistically significant.

L614: ## **Patient Disposition**
L615: ## **Data:**
L616: The study was conducted from August 16, 2021 (the first subject signed the ICF) to April 29, 2024 (data cutoff) across 36 sites in China and 10 sites in ex-Asia countries, including 2 in U.S., 1 in Canada and 7 in Brazil.
L617: As shown in Applicant – Table 8, a total of 410 subjects were screened, and 291 subjects were randomized, including 144 in AK105 group and 147 in placebo group. Of these, 11 were ex-Asia patients from U.S., Canada, and Brazil. All except 3 subjects received study drugs.
L618: Among all the subjects enrolled in the study, by the data cutoff date, 259 (89.0%) subjects discontinued from the assigned treatment, with 124 (86.1%) in AK105 group and 135 (91.8%) in placebo group. In placebo group, 68 (46.3%) subjects crossed over to receive AK105 monotherapy after progressed on the initially assigned treatment. One hundred and four (35.7%) subjects had completed the study (49 [34.0%] in AK105 group, 55 [37.4%] in placebo group).
