L625: ## The Applicant's Position:
L626: AK105-304 was first initiated in China in August 2021. To reflect the global distribution of NPC, especially to represent the NPC population in the U.S. in line with FDA's recommendation, Akeso originally planned to enroll 60 patients outside of China. However, despite tremendous efforts Akeso has made to complete enrollment outside of China, we have been experiencing extreme challenges during the enrollment period. Among the three-year arduous efforts, we contacted a total of 234 ex-Asia sites and only initiated 19 sites in the US, Canada, Brazil and Australia. Up until September 18, 2024, we have enrolled a total of 16 patients outside of China, including 3 in the US, 6 in Canada, and 7 in Brazil. 11 subjects from ex-Asia countries were included in this analysis (as of April 29, 2024).
L627: As of 29 April 2024, compared with AK105 group, more subjects in placebo group had completed the study treatment assigned at randomization (86.1% vs 91.8%). The most common reason for discontinuation of study treatment in both groups was PD. 68 (34.5%) in placebo group crossed over to receive AK105 monotherapy after BICR confirmed radiographic PD.
L628: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L629: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of patient disposition for Study AK105-304. Substantially more patients in the placebo arm (71%) discontinued treatment due to radiographic disease progression, as compared to the penpulimab arm (43%).
L630: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L631: ## Data:
L632: As shown in Applicant – Table 9, a total of 63 (21.6%) subjects had at least one critical protocol deviation. 25 (8.6%) failed to report SAE within 24 hours; 15 (5.2%) related to study drug compliance, except for one subject who wasn't assigned the right drug code according to the IWRS, the others were failure to interrupt/permanently discontinue study medication after reaching the criteria for interruption/discontinuation of study medication specified in the
L633: protocol, mainly due to drug interruption beyond 6 weeks because of COVID-19 pandemic; 12 (4.1%) related to visit delay (out-of-window visit &gt;42 days for non-AE reasons); 7 (2.4%) related to tumor assessments, failure to perform at least 2 consecutive times of the planned tumor imaging assessments, or missing one or more areas in the imaging assessment at screening; 6 (2.1%) related to concomitant medications, short-term use of traditional Chinese medicinal products with anti-tumor effects; 5 (1.7%) were related to study procedures; 1 (0.3%) related to inclusion/exclusion criteria, use of glucocorticoids within 10 days before the first study dose, which did not meet the 2-week washout period; and 1 (0.3%) related to laboratory assessment.

L648: ## The Applicant's Position:
L649: The baseline demographic characteristics were well-balanced between AK105 group and placebo group. The racial distribution in AK105-304 is similar to that in the KEYNOTE-122 study and probably represents a reasonable racial characteristic of a global trial in NPC. On the other hand, JUPITER-02, the study used for FDA approval of toripalimab, exclusively enrolled patients from Asia countries.
L650: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L651: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of patient demographics for Study AK105-304. In the penpulimab arm, 10% of patients were 65 years or older, as compared to 3.4% of patients in the placebo arm. Otherwise, the demographics were well-balanced between the treatment arms. As of the data cut-off date of April 29, 2024, the study had enrolled 291 patients of whom only 11 (3.8%) patients were enrolled outside of Asia, specifically in the US, Canada, and Brazil. Otherwise, the demographics appear to be comparable between the treatment arms.
L652: Since Study AK105-304 was conducted primarily in China, with 96% of the patients enrolled in China, the patient population may not be representative of the US population of patients with NPC. A postmarketing commitment (PMC) will be issued to better characterize the safety and efficacy of penpulimab plus chemotherapy in a patient population whose demographic and disease characteristics are more representative of the population of U.S. patients with NPC. See Section 13 on Postmarketing Commitments for additional details.
L653: ## **Other Baseline Characteristics (e.g., disease characteristics, important concomitant drugs)**
L654: ## Data:
L655: The disease stage at enrollment was mainly recurrent NPC (67.7%), and 32.3% was primary metastasis. The majority of subjects had distant metastases (80.1%), of which liver metastases was most common (45.6%). The histological type was mostly non-keratinizing undifferentiated squamous cell carcinoma (76.6%), followed by non-keratinizing differentiated squamous cell carcinoma (19.2%). More than half of the subjects had ECOG score of 1 at enrollment (63.9%). PD-L1 expression TPS \ge 50% accounted for 37.5%, and EBV DNA \ge500 copies/mL accounted for 63.6%.
L656: A total of 198 (68.0%) subjects received previous radiotherapy, including 100 (69.4%) in AK105 group and 98 (66.7%) in placebo group. Except one subject with bone metastasis in AK105 group who received local palliative radiotherapy, the rest received radical radiotherapy. A total of 195 (67.0%) subjects received previous systemic therapy, including 98 (68.1%) in AK105 group and 97 (66.0%) in placebo group. (Applicant – Table 11).

L666: ## The Applicant's Position:
L667: The population treated in AK105-304 were managed according to the standard of care for patients with NPC in the US. In ITT population, the majority of patients had metastatic disease (80.1%); 45.6% had liver metastases. The histological type was mostly non-keratinizing undifferentiated squamous cell carcinoma (76.6%), followed by non-keratinizing differentiated squamous cell carcinoma (19.2%). Of the 11 subjects enrolled from ex-Asia countries, the disease stage at enrollment was mainly recurrent NPC (72.7%), and 27.3% was primary metastasis. The majority of subjects had distant metastases (63.6%), of which lung metastases was most common (45.5%). The histological type was mostly non-keratinizing differentiated squamous cell carcinoma (54.5%), followed by non-keratinizing undifferentiated squamous cell carcinoma (45.5%). Prior to study entry, radiotherapy or concurrent chemoradiation was administered to all of those with recurrent disease.
L668: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L669: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of baseline characteristics for Study AK105-304. One patient was randomized to the placebo arm per IWRS with an ECOG score of 1, whereas the correct ECOG score for that patient was 0 per electronic data capture (EDC).
L670: ## Penpulimab
L671: Additionally, six patients (four in the penpulimab arm and two in the placebo arm) were randomized per IWRS with stage of disease as "primary metastases," when the correct stage per EDC was "recurrent." Lastly, one patient was randomized to the penpulimab arm per IWRS with liver metastases status as "present", whereas the correct liver metastases status for that patient was "absent" per the EDC.
L672: ## Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
L673: ## Data:
L674: ## Treatment Compliance
L675: Penpulimab was administered intravenously, and each infusion was documented. Subjects who had AK105 interruption or permanently discontinued due to AEs were discussed in Section 8.2.4.3 and 8.2.4.4.
L676: ## Concomitant Medications
L677: A total of 144 (100.0%) subjects in AK105 group and 144 (98.0%) subjects in placebo group had at least one concomitant medication. The most commonly used (\ge 50\%) drug types were antiemetics and antinauseants, immunostimulants, drugs for acid related disorders, blood substitutes and perfusion solutions, corticosteroids for systemic use, antianemic preparations, bile and liver therapy, vitamins, and diuretics. Those frequently used concomitant medications were mainly used for pre-treatment of chemotherapy, as well as for the management of common chemotherapy complications such as nausea, vomiting, and hematological toxicity.
L678: ## The Applicant's Position:
L679: The use of concomitant medications did not affect the efficacy or safety of penpulimab.
L680: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L681: FDA agrees with Applicant's position.
L682: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses)
L683: ## Data:
L684: As of April 29, 2024, the median duration of follow-up was 19.1 months (range: 0, 31.5), with a 6-month follow-up rate of 93.1% and a 12-month follow-up rate of 81.8%
L685: In the ITT population, per BICR's assessment based on RECIST v1.1, 80 (55.6%) subjects in
L686: 103
L687: Version date: January 2020 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
L688: **Disclaimer:** In this document, the sections labeled as "Data" and "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
L689: AK105 group and 109 (74.1%) subjects in placebo group had PD or died (Applicant – Table 12). The median PFS was 9.6 months (95% CI: 7.1, 12.5) in AK105 group and 7.0 months (95% CI: 6.9, 7.3) in placebo group. Compared with placebo group, AK105 group reduced the risk of PD or death by 55% (HR=0.45, 95% CI: 0.33, 0.62, P&lt;0.0001) based on stratified Log-rank test (Applicant – Figure 3). The study crossed the O'Brien-Fleming boundary for this preset interim analysis (2-sided P-value boundary is 0.030 based on 189 PFS events).

L698: Forest plots for subgroup analyses of BICR assessed PFS were presented in Applicant – Figure 4. The results showed that penpulimab combined with chemotherapy demonstrated robust PFS benefits over chemotherapy alone across all pre-specified subgroups.
L699: Subgroup analyses are performed based on an unstratified Cox proportional hazards regression model with tied events handled by the Efron's method.
L700: Overall analysis is performed based on a stratified Cox proportional hazards regression model with tied events handled by the Efron's method.
L701: *: The subgroup has less than 10 PFS events and HR is not reported.
L702: Source Data: ADRS.xpt Module 5.3.5.1 AK105-304 CSR Figure 14.2.1.1.2 in Section 14
L703: To assess the robustness of the primary endpoint of this study, the following sensitivity analyses were conducted for BICR-assessed PFS:
L704: **Sensitivity analysis 1:** The unstratified log-rank test and unstratified Cox proportional hazards regression model was performed to compare PFS time between two groups, HR=0.53 (95% CI: 0.40, 0.71), P&lt;0.0001. The results were consistent with the primary analysis.
L705: **Sensitivity analysis 2:** For the stratified analysis, the stratification factors were stage of disease at study entry (primary metastases vs. recurrent), ECOG score (0 vs. 1), liver metastases (present vs. absent) and investigator's choice of chemotherapy regimens (carboplatin vs. cisplatin) all obtained from the EDC instead of randomization system (IWRS). The results (HR=0.45; 95% CI: 0.33, 0.63; P&lt;0.0001) were consistent with the primary analysis.
L706: **Sensitivity analysis 3:** Subject who has experienced radiological PD or death right after missing two or more consecutive tumor assessments were considered as progressed at the time of PD or death, whichever occurs first. The results of this analysis (HR=0.47; 95% CI: 0.34, 0.64; P&lt;0.0001) were similar to those of the primary analysis.
L707: **Sensitivity analysis 4:** Subject who had initiated a new anticancer therapy before radiological PD or death were considered as progression at the time of starting the new anticancer therapy.
L708: The results of this analysis (HR=0.48; 95% CI: 0.35, 0.65; P&lt;0.0001) were similar to those of the primary analysis.
L709: Sensitivity analysis 5: Per FDA request, included only the first 218 subjects enrolled in China and all 11 subjects enrolled in ex-Asia countries, using the same statistical methods as in the primary analysis. 65 (56.5%) subjects in AK105 group and 83 (72.8%) subjects in placebo group had PD or died, the median PFS was 8.4 (95% CI: 7.1, 12.5) and 7.0 (95% CI: 6.9, 7.2) months, respectively. Compared with placebo group, the risk of PD or death was reduced by 52%, HR=0.48 (95% CI: 0.33, 0.68; P&lt;0.0001). The results of this sensitivity analysis were similar to those of the primary analysis.
L710: ## The Applicant's Position:
L711: As of DCO date for this interim analysis, compared with placebo group, a significant benefit in terms of PFS was demonstrated in AK105 group (P&lt;0.0001), which reduced the risk of PD or death by 55% (HR=0.45, 95% CI: 0.33, 0.62), which is numerically better than the other PD-1 inhibitors (with the caveats of cross-study comparison). The PFS results assessed by BICR and investigator were consistent. In the subgroup analysis, PFS benefits of AK105 were observed across all subgroups (Age, [REDACTED], Stage of disease at study entry, ECOG score, Liver metastases, Investigator's choice of chemotherapy regimens, PD-L1 expression levels, EBV-DNA level, Region). In addition, the sensitivity analyses of PFS showed similar efficacy.
L712: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L713: FDA's stratified analysis of PFS did not include the stratification factor of investigator's choice of chemotherapy (cisplatin vs. carboplatin) as it was not a stratification factor originally considered at the time of randomization. Additionally, only 2.4% of all randomized patients received carboplatin which in turn resulted in sparse strata. FDA's analysis based on the data cut-off date of April 29, 2024, without using the above-mentioned stratification factor yielded results that were numerically identical to that of the Applicant's. This also indicated that the stratification factor of investigator's choice of chemotherapy had minimal effect on the primary analysis of PFS. FDA's sensitivity analyses that excluded the stratification factor of investigator's choice of chemotherapy also yielded results that were numerically identical to the Applicant's analysis.
L714: Although the difference in median PFS was only approximately 2.6 months (median PFS was 9.6 months [95% CI: 7.1, 12.5]) in the penpulimab-kcq arm and 7.0 months [95% CI: 6.9, 7.3] in the placebo arm), the PFS Hazard ratio was 0.45 (95% CI: 0.33, 0.62, two-sided p-value &lt;0.0001). There is a clear separation of the Kaplan-Meier curves at around 7 months which indicates a treatment effect favoring the penpulimab arm based on the Kaplan-Meier plot for PFS as assessed by BICR. At 12 months, 31% patients in the penpulimab arm and 11% patients in the
L715: ## Penpulimab
L716: placebo arm were alive and progression free, which is supportive of a PFS benefit favoring the penpulimab arm.
L717: In general, FDA agrees with the exploratory subgroup analyses provided by the Applicant. In the subgroup of patients with age below 65 years, the median PFS in the penpulimab arm was 9.8 months (95% CI: 8.1, 12.7) and 7.0 months (95% CI: 6.9, 7.3) in the placebo arm, with a hazard ratio of 0.49 (95% CI: 0.36, 0.67). In the subgroup of patients with age 65 years or older, the median PFS in the penpulimab arm was 5.6 months (95% CI: 2.8, NE) and 5.8 months (95% CI: 5.4, NE) in the placebo arm, with a hazard ratio of 1.60 (95% CI: 0.32, 7.99). The results of this exploratory subgroup analysis should be interpreted with caution since there were only 20 patients (15 in the penpulimab arm and 5 in placebo arm) with age 65 years or older in the study, based on the data cut-off date of April 29, 2024.
L718: ## Data Quality and Integrity
L719: ## Data:
L720: All the required components of the electronic Common Technical Document (eCTD) were included in this submission. AK105-304 and other studies included in the resubmission were conducted according to Good Clinical Practice.
L721: ## The Applicant's Position:
L722: The submission contained all the required components of the electronic Common Technical Document (eCTD) and was of adequate quality and integrity to allow for review of the clinical trial data supporting the proposed indication.
L723: The quality assurance procedures were implemented during the conduct of the study including but not limited to regular site monitoring, investigator meetings and training sessions for clinical research associates, and site initiation visits to review the clinical protocol, CRF, and study procedures. Additionally, independent Quality Assurance audits were conducted for selected sites, and no concerns on data integrity were reported following site inspections by the independent Quality Assurance audits.
L724: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L725: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L726: ## **Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints**
L727: ## **Data:**
L728: ## **Key secondary endpoint: Overall survival (OS)**
L729: Since PFS reached statistical significance, according to the statistical analysis plan, a descriptive analysis for OS was performed with the allocated nominal \alpha of 0.0001. As shown in Applicant – Table 13, Applicant – Figure 5, 48 (33.3%) and 49 (33.3%) subjects died in AK105 group and placebo group, respectively. The median OS for two groups was not reached (HR=0.94, 95%CI: 0.63, 1.40). 24-month survival rate was 66.3% in AK105 group and 61.9% in placebo group, respectively.

L738: 68 (46.3%) subjects in placebo group had crossed over to receive AK105 monotherapy after PD, which was explicitly allowed by design. An analysis based on the ITT principle will lead to a biased estimate of the true treatment effect and will likely underestimate the AK105 group's true survival benefit. Therefore, to investigate the impact of the existence of such a high percentage of crossover and to correct for the potential bias in the primary analysis, three commonly used statistical methods for correcting treatment-switching are considered as sensitivity analyses: Rank Preserving Structural Failure Time (RPSFT) model, Inverse Probability of Censoring Weighting (IPCW) method and two-stage Accelerated Failure Time (AFT) model. After adjusting for potential bias in OS caused by the crossovers, the trend of OS benefit became more evident, with an HR of 0.62 (0.41, 0.94) by RPSFT model, 0.75 (0.43, 1.30) by IPCW method, and 0.78 (0.52, 1.17) by two-stage AFT model (Applicant – Table 14).

L749: ## Other secondary endpoints
L750: ## Investigator-assessed PFS
L751: Per investigator assessment, 78 (54.2%) subjects in AK105 group and 112 (76.2%) subjects in placebo group had PD or died, the median PFS was 12.3 (95% CI: 9.7, 14.6) and 7.2 (95% CI: 7.0, 8.3) months in the two groups, respectively. Compared with placebo group, AK105 group reduced the risk of progression or death by 56% (HR=0.44, 95% CI: 0.32, 0.60; P&lt;0.0001) based on the stratified log-rank test. These results were consistent with those of BICR-assessed PFS.
L752: ## BICR-assessed ORR/DCR
L753: Per BICR assessment, 26 (18.1%) subjects in AK105 group and 13 (8.8%) subjects in placebo group had confirmed CR; 72 (50.0%) subjects in AK105 group and 81 (55.1%) in placebo group had confirmed PR. The ORR for the two groups was 68.1% (95% CI: 59.8%, 75.6%) and 63.9% (95% CI: 55.6%, 71.7%), respectively. The DCR for the two groups was 91.0% (95% CI: 85.1%, 95.1%) and 91.8% (95% CI: 86.2%, 95.7%), respectively.
L754: ## Investigator-assessed ORR/DCR
L755: Per investigator assessment, 11 (7.6%) subjects in AK105 group and 4 (2.7%) subjects in placebo group had confirmed CR, and 84 (58.3%) subjects in AK105 group and 80 (54.4%) subjects in placebo group had confirmed PR. The ORRs for the two groups were 66.0% (95% CI: 57.6%, 73.7%) and 57.1% (95% CI: 48.7%, 65.3%), respectively. The DCR for the two groups was 91.0% (95% CI: 85.1%, 95.1%) and 91.2% (95% CI: 85.4%, 95.2%), respectively.
L756: ## The Applicant's Position:
L757: Median OS has not yet been reached for either group, and the OS data is immature. Since this study began on August 25, 2021, the therapeutic landscape for advanced NPC has changed considerably, especially with the approval of immune checkpoint inhibitors. To maximize patients' clinical benefits, by design, this study allows patients in placebo group to receive AK105 treatment after PD. 73 (50.7%) subjects in AK105 group and 107 (72.8%) subjects in placebo group received subsequent anti-tumor therapies. Specifically, 68 (46.3%) subjects in placebo group crossed over to receive the same treatment as AK105 group after PD, and 42 (28.6%) subjects received other anti-PD-1 therapies. Furthermore, more subjects in placebo group (60 [40.8%]) received two or more types of systemic anti-tumor treatment sequences or combinations (e.g., targeted therapy, chemotherapy, and other immunotherapy) compared to AK105 group (39 [27.1%]). After adjusting for potential bias in OS caused by the crossovers, the sensitivity analyses showed that the HR adjusted for crossover effects ranged from 0.62 to 0.78. The lowest corrected HR, 0.62, was comparable to the HR observed for toripalimab in the JUPITER-O2 study.
L758: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L759: At the time of the BLA submission, the Applicant provided an analysis of OS based on the data cut-off date of April 29, 2024, when 97 (56%) deaths were observed in 291 enrolled patients. This was a descriptive analysis of OS conducted at the time of the PFS interim analysis. According to the SAP, the first formal interim analysis for OS (OS IA1) was expected to be conducted at 103 deaths (60% information fraction of pre-specified OS events for the final analysis). On January 23, 2025, FDA requested that the Applicant provide the results of OS IA1, and the results was submitted to the FDA based on a data cut-off date of January 24, 2025. At the time of the first OS interim analysis, the study had enrolled 296 patients, with the additional five patients being enrolled outside of China. Of these five additional patients, three were already under screening at the time of the pre-BLA meeting. The first interim analysis was conducted at 121 (70%) events, instead of the prespecified 103 (60%) events. Based on the pre-BLA meeting documents, the Applicant had projected that 103 deaths would be observed by January 10, 2025. It appears that deaths accrued faster than the Applicant's projection. Additionally, the Applicant also stated that an analysis at 60% information fraction would not be very informative, given that a descriptive OS analysis was conducted at 56% information fraction. FDA's analysis of OS did not include investigator's choice of chemotherapy as a stratification factor for analysis. The following table provides FDA's analysis of OS at 70% information fraction, based on the data cut-off date of January 24, 2025. OS was not statistically significant at the first interim analysis.

L770: ## Penpulimab
L771: Crossover is allowed in Study AK105-304. At the time of the first formal OS interim analysis, 74 (49%) patients in the placebo arm crossed over to receive open-label treatment with penpulimab monotherapy after BICR assessed disease progression. Due to the high crossover rate, the Applicant conducted three supportive analyses to adjust for treatment crossover, including the rank preserving structural failure time (RPSFT) model, the inverse probability of censoring weighting (IPCW) model, and the two-stage accelerated failure time (AFT) model. L785: ## Durability of Response
L786: ## Data:
L787: Per BICR assessment, the median TTR for the two groups was 1.5 (range: 1.2, 5.5) months and 1.5 (range: 1.2, 7.3) months, respectively. The median DoR for the two groups was 9.8 (95% CI: 7.0, 17.5) and 5.7 (95% CI: 5.5, 6.7) months, respectively, with AK105 group showing a longer DoR than placebo group with an HR of 0.40 (95%CI: 0.27, 0.59) (Applicant – Figure 6).
L788: Per investigator assessment, the median TTRs for the two groups were 1.5 (range: 1.2, 11.6) months and 1.5 (range: 1.2, 5.8) months, respectively. The median DoR was also longer in AK105 group (12.1 months, 95% CI: 8.5, 17.5) compared to placebo group (5.7 months, 95% CI: 5.5, 6.7) with an HR of 0.35 (95%CI: 0.23, 0.52) (Applicant – Figure 7).

L793: ## The Applicant's Position:
L794: For both the BICR-assessed and investigator-assessed responses, DoR in AK105 group was longer than in placebo group with HRs of 0.4 (95%CI: 0.27, 0.59) and 0.35 (95%CI: 0.23, 0.52), respectively.
L795: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L796: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. The median DOR in the penpulimab arm was 9.8 months (95% CI: 7.0, 17.4). The observed 6-month landmark rates of DOR were 57% in penpulimab arm, as compared to 38% in the placebo arm. The analysis of DOR is restricted to responders and thus the Applicant-provided analysis of DOR based on hazard ratio is not considered a valid comparison as it is not based on the ITT population. The results of ORR and DOR in Study AK105-304 are consistent with that of Study AK105-202. The median duration of response was not reached in Study AK105-202. See the Assessment Aid Addendum for additional efficacy information for Study AK105-202.
L797: ## Persistence of Effect
L798: ## Data:
L799: See the section on DoR, PFS, and OS above.
L800: ## The Applicant's Position:
L801: Significant benefits in PFS were observed in the AK105 group compared to placebo group, as assessed by both BICR and investigators. PFS benefits of AK105 were consistent across all examined subgroups. Furthermore, DoR in AK105 group was also much longer than that in the placebo group. Although the overall survival (OS) data is still immature, after adjusting for potential bias due to crossovers, there also appears an OS benefit. These findings indicate that NPC patients may derive sustained benefits from AK105 treatment.
L802: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L803: Refer to FDA's Assessment on Durability of Response and Key secondary endpoint: Overall survival (OS) above.
L804: ## **Efficacy Results – Secondary or exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints**

L806: ## Health-related Quality of Life (HRQoL) Analysis
L807: EORTC QLQ-C30 global health status/QoL and each functional scale score at baseline and each visit, as well as the change in score from baseline to the last assessment in the study are summarized in Table 14.2.6.1 in Section 14 of 5.3.5.1 AK105-304 CSR. The two groups had similar global health status/QoL scores at baseline, and the scores in both groups showed a downward trend after the start of treatment, followed by a slight increase with disease progression. There was no notable difference in the trend of score changes between the two groups.
L808: The changes in EORTC QLQ-H&amp;N43 up to the last assessment in the study are summarized in Table 14.2.6.2 in Section 14 of 5.3.5.1 AK105-304 CSR. No notable differences were found between the two groups.
L809: ## The Applicant's Position:
L810: The study is not intended to study quality of life. The overall PRO data from EORTC QLQ-C30 and QLQ-H&amp;N43 demonstrated some improvements in symptoms and functioning after treatment in AK105 group and placebo group.

L813: sample size of the trial. FDA agrees there were no notable differences observed between arms in terms of patient-reported symptoms and function.

L821: ## 8.1.3. Integrated Review of Effectiveness

L847: ## Additional Efficacy Considerations

L850: ## 8.1.5. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L851: ## Data:
L852: As of data cutoff date for this interim analysis, the median follow-up time was 19.1 months (range: 0.0, 31.5), penpulimab combined with gemcitabine and cisplatin/carboplatin significantly improved PFS in the first-line treatment of R/M NPC, as compared to gemcitabine and cisplatin/carboplatin alone. The key efficacy outcomes are summarized as follows:
L853: - Primary Endpoint BICR-assessed PFS:Primary analysis: 80 (55.6%) subjects in AK105 group and 109 (74.1%) subjects in placebo group had PD or died, and the median PFS was 9.6 (95% CI: 7.1, 12.5) and 7.0 (95% CI: 6.9, 7.3) months, respectively. Compared with placebo group, AK105 group reduced the risk of PD or death by 55% (HR=0.45, 95% CI: 0.33, 0.62; P&lt;0.0001). The study crossed the O'Brien-Fleming boundary for this PFS interim analysis (2-sided P-value boundary is 0.030 based on 189 PFS events).
- Sensitivity analysis per FDA request: Included only the first 218 Asian subjects and all 11 ex-Asia subjects, 65 (56.5%) subjects in AK105 group and 83 (72.8%) subjects in placebo group had PD or died, and the median PFS was 8.4 (95% CI: 7.0, 12.5) and 7.0 (95% CI: 6.9, 7.2) months, respectively. Compared with placebo group, AK105 group reduced the risk of PD or death by 52%, HR=0.48 (95% CI: 0.33, 0.68; P&lt;0.0001). The results of this sensitivity analysis and the primary analysis were consistent.
- Key Secondary Endpoint OS:Since PFS reached statistical significance, as planned in the SAP, a descriptive analysis of OS was conducted with a nominal \alpha of 0.0001. A total of 97 OS events were observed, with 48 from AK105 group and 49 from placebo group with an HR of 0.94 (95% CI: 0.63, 1.40). 24-month survival rate was 66.3% in AK105 group and 61.9% in placebo group. Median OS has not yet been reached for either group, and the OS data is immature.
- 68 (46.3%) subjects in placebo group had crossed over to receive AK105 monotherapy after PD, which was explicitly allowed by design. Three sensitivity analyses were conducted via the commonly used methods for correcting crossover effects: Rank Preserving Structural Failure Time (RPSFT) model, Inverse Probability of Censoring Weighting (IPCW) method and two-stage Accelerated Failure Time (AFT) model. After
L854: ## Penpulimab
L855: adjusting for potential bias in OS caused by the crossovers, the trend of OS benefit became more evident, with an HR of 0.62 (0.41, 0.94) by RPSFT model, 0.75 (0.43, 1.30) by IPCW method, and 0.78 (0.52, 1.17) by two-stage AFT model.
L856: ## • Other Secondary Endpoints:
L857: - Investigator-assessed PFS: 78 (54.2%) subjects in AK105 group and 112 (76.2%) subjects in placebo group had PD or died, and the median PFS was 12.3 (95% CI: 9.7, 14.6) and 7.2 (95% CI: 7.0, 8.3) months, respectively. Compared with placebo group, AK105 group reduced the risk of progression or death by 56% (HR=0.44, 95% CI: 0.32, 0.60; P&lt;0.0001).
- BICR-assessed response: the confirmed ORR for AK105 group and placebo group was 68.1% (95% CI: 59.8%, 75.6%) and 63.9% (95% CI: 55.6%, 71.7%), respectively. The median DoR for the two groups was 9.8 (95% CI: 7.0, 17.5) and 5.7 (95% CI: 5.5, 6.7) months, respectively, with AK105 group showing a longer DoR than placebo group with an HR of 0.4 (95%CI: 0.27, 0.59).
- Investigator-assessed response: the confirmed ORR for AK105 group and placebo group was 66.0% (95% CI: 57.6%, 73.7%) and 57.1% (95% CI: 48.7%, 65.3%), respectively. The median DoR was also longer in AK105 group (12.1 months, 95% CI: 8.5, 17.5) compared to placebo group (5.7 months, 95% CI: 5.5, 6.7) with an HR of 0.35 (95%CI: 0.23, 0.52).
L858: ## The Applicant's Position:
L859: The interim analysis results of AK105-304 showed that the Phase 3 trial of penpulimab combined with gemcitabine and cisplatin/carboplatin as the first-line treatment for R/M NPC patients, demonstrated large and robust treatment effect in terms of both BICR-assessed PFS (the primary endpoint) and investigator-assessed PFS. However, the study design allowing crossover after PD substantially affects the OS results. After correcting the confounding effect of crossovers via common statistical methods, the OS effect becomes evident and similar to those shown in other phase 3 studies on other anti-PD-1 agents. Compared with similar studies of other PD-1 inhibitors combined with chemotherapy as first-line treatment for R/M NPC, the efficacy was generally comparable. Furthermore, the sensitivity analysis of PFS including 218 Asian and 11 ex-Asia subjects showed similar efficacy. Therefore, penpulimab combined with gemcitabine and cisplatin/carboplatin has a favorable efficacy and provides a new beneficial treatment option for R/M NPC patients globally.
L860: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L861: Based on the results from Studies AK105-304 and AK105-202, the review team concluded that the Applicant provided substantial evidence of effectiveness for penpulimab in combination with cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine, for the first-line treatment of adult patients with metastatic or recurrent, locally advanced nonkeratinizing NPC and for penpulimab as monotherapy, in adult patients with recurrent unresectable or metastatic NPC of
L862: ## Penpulimab
L863: nonkeratinizing histology with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other line of therapy.
L864: Study AK105-304 demonstrated a statistically significant improvement in BICR-assessed PFS favoring the penpulimab arm, with a HR of 0.45 (95% CI 0.33, 0.62; p&lt;0.0001), corresponding to a median PFS of 9.6 months (95% CI: 7.1, 12.5) in the penpulimab arm and 7.0 months (95% CI: 6.3, 7.3) in the placebo arm. The proportions of patients with observed PFS of at least 12 months was 31% in the penpulimab arm and 11% in the control arm. The PFS HR of 0.45 and a 2.6-month improvement in median PFS, along with a 20 percent-point difference in observed PFS rate at 12 months are considered clinically meaningful in this disease setting. In Study AK105-304, while the numerical difference in median PFS between the two arms was less than 3 months (median PFS was 9.6 months (95% CI: 7.1, 12.5) in the penpulimab-kcqx arm and 7.0 months (95% CI: 6.9, 7.3) in the placebo arm), the substantial reduction in the risk of disease progression or death in the penpulimab plus chemotherapy arm as compared to chemotherapy alone, as indicated by the hazard ratio, underscores a clinically meaningful benefit. The Kaplan-Meier curves begin to separate at approximately around 7 months, which suggests that some patients may derive prolonged PFS benefit, or that there may be a potential late treatment effect, which was not captured by median PFS alone. The ORR was 68% in the penpulimab arm and 64% in the placebo arm. The secondary endpoint of DOR demonstrated that penpulimab with cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine had a longer median DOR at 9.8 months (95% CI: 7.0, 17.4) compared to cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine alone at 5.7 months (95% CI: 5.5, 6.7).
L865: These results provide substantial evidence of effectiveness for penpulimab in combination with cisplatin (or carboplatin) and gemcitabine, for the first-line treatment of adults with metastatic or with recurrent, locally advanced nonkeratinizing NPC. OS was not statistically significant at the first formal interim analysis, conducted at 70% information fraction. The HR was 0.91 (95% CI: 0.64, 1.31, p = 0.615). Median OS was not reached in the penpulimab arm (95% CI: 27.4, NE), while the median OS in the placebo arm was 31.3 months (95% CI: 27.2, NE). However, the interpretation of these results is challenging due to several reasons. Notably, a high rate of crossover from the placebo arm to the penpulimab arm (49%) may have impacted any potential OS difference between the treatment arms. Additionally, a substantial proportion of patients (31%) in the placebo arm received another anti-PD-(L)1 antibody upon disease progression. In total 80% of patients in the placebo arm received either penpulimab or another anti-PD-(L)1 antibody as subsequent treatment. This may have influenced survival outcomes and further complicated the assessment of treatment effect. Consequently, while no apparent OS detriment was observed at the time of the first formal OS analysis (DCO date: January 24, 2025), the impact of these post-progression treatments will be considered in future analyses when mature OS data are available.
L866: Among 125 patients with recurrent unresectable or metastatic NPC of nonkeratinizing histology with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other line
L867: ## Penpulimab
L868: of therapy, treated with penpulimab monotherapy in Study AK105-202, the ORR was 27% (95% CI: 20, 35). The median DOR was not reached (95% CI: 9.2, NE). Among the 35 patients with a complete or partial response, 83% had a DOR \ge 6 months and 46% had a DOR \ge 12 months. These results are considered clinically meaningful when considering the intended patient population. Furthermore, the results of Study AK105-304 provide further confidence in the benefit of single-agent penpulimab in advanced NPC.
L869: A major limitation of the data is that Study AK105-304 enrolled 96% of the patients in China (based on a data cut-off date of April 29, 2024), and Study AK105-202 was conducted exclusively in China. While both Studies AK105-304 and AK105-202 were conducted predominantly in China, a flexible regulatory approach is warranted given the high unmet need for treatment options for patients with NPC. These trials were initiated prior to the approval of regimens with toripalimab, the only other FDA-approved anti-PD-(L)1 antibody for U.S. patients with NPC. Cisplatin or carboplatin plus gemcitabine was considered an appropriate control arm in Study AK105-304 when the trial was designed, making the study results relevant to U.S. medical practice. Cisplatin plus gemcitabine was considered standard of care for the first-line treatment of recurrent or metastatic NPC in the U.S., and carboplatin plus gemcitabine was considered an appropriate option for patients who may be unable to tolerate or who have contraindications to cisplatin. The incidence of NPC is much higher in East Asia relative to the U.S., where NPC is a relatively rare disease, making enrollment to clinical trials more feasible in that region of the world. Clinical site inspections were conducted by FDA to validate the data supporting this application. To further address the issue of applicability to the U.S. population, the Applicant will be collecting additional clinical data to further characterize the safety and efficacy of penpulimab in combination with either cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine for the treatment of non-keratinizing NPC in a study population whose demographic and disease characteristics are more representative of the population of U.S. patients with NPC (see Postmarketing Commitments later in this review for details).
L870: ## 8.2. Review of Safety
L871: ## Data:
L872: The penpulimab is being studied in clinical trials across different tumor types. The primary evidence to support the safety of penpulimab is generated from 8 clinical studies of penpulimab as monotherapy or in combination therapy (including AK105-101, AK105-201, AK105-202, AK105-204, AK105-205, AK105-301 (Part I), AK105-302 and AK105-304) and presented in two separate parts, Monotherapy Part (n=558) and Combination Therapy Part (n=393).
L873: ## The Applicant's Position:
L874: ## **Monotherapy Part**
L875: ## Penpulimab
L876: Monotherapy Part includes pooled safety data from 558 patients with a variety of tumor types across 6 studies who have been exposed to penpulimab monotherapy at any dose levels. In the monotherapy part, safety data is presented for NPC 200mg Q2W population (n=130), Penpulimab 200mg Q2W (n=372) and Overall Population (n=558). In addition, safety data generated from different regions is also presented separately as Asia vs ex-Asia in population receiving penpulimab 200mg Q2W.
L877: - **NPC 200mg Q2W population (n=130)**NPC 200mg Q2W data was generated from AK105-202;
- **Penpulimab 200mg Q2W (n=372)**Ex-Asian data was generated from AK105-101 Phase Ib
- Asian data was generated from AK105-201, AK105-202, and AK105-204;
- Total data of all tumor types at 200mg Q2W were generated from AK105-101 Phase Ib, AK105-201, AK105-202, and AK105-204;
- **Overall Population (n=558)**All dose data were pooled from all patients with various tumor types who received penpulimab monotherapy at any dose in the above 6 studies.
L878: ## **Combination Therapy Part**
L879: Combination Therapy Part includes patients who have been exposed to penpulimab in combination with chemotherapy or other regimens from 4 studies. Since penpulimab was administered in patients with different tumor types in combination with different chemotherapies, safety data from different studies were not pooled. Instead, data from each study were presented and summarized separately:
L880: - AK105-304 in which penpulimab 200mg Q3W was administered in combination with cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine to 146 patients with NPC
- AK105-302 in which penpulimab 200mg Q3W was administered in combination with paclitaxel and carboplatin to 173 patients with sq-NSCLC
- AK105-301 in which penpulimab 200mg Q3W was administered in combination with carboplatin and pemetrexed to 46 patients with nsq-NSCLC
- AK105-205 in which penpulimab 200mg Q3W was administered in combination with chemotherapy with or without anlotinib to 28 patients with NPC (Arm A: penpulimab + gemcitabine + cisplatin + anlotinib, n=8; Arm B: penpulimab + gemcitabine + cisplatin, n=6; Arm C: penpulimab + gemcitabine + anlotinib, n=14)
L881: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L882: FDA's review of the safety profile of penpulimab 200 mg Q3W in combination with cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine was based on data from 146 patients with NPC who received at least one dose of penpulimab on Study AK105-304. FDA did not agree with pooling data from trials incorporating other combination regimens (such as from Studies AK105-302, AK105-301, and AK105-205), given differences in safety profiles and challenges in isolating toxicities specifically associated with penpulimab.
L883: FDA's review of the safety profile of penpulimab 200 mg Q2W was based on data from 130 patients with NPC who received at least one dose of penpulimab on Study AK105-202 supported by pooled data (n=372) from Studies AK105-202 (n=130), AK105-101 (n=83), AK105-201 (n=94), and AK1050-204 (n=65) of penpulimab 200 mg Q2W as a single agent for patients with advanced solid tumors and classic Hodgkin lymphoma. FDA did not pool safety data for penpulimab as a single agent with alternative dosing schedules.
L884: ## 8.2.1. Safety Review Approach
L885: ## Data:
L886: N/A
L887: ## The Applicant's Position:
L888: The safety evaluation performed as part of the penpulimab clinical development program was consistent with ICH guidelines.
L889: The safety analyses supporting this application were comprehensive and included all Serious TEAEs (SAEs,) all TEAEs, especially Grade \ge 3 all-causality and treatment-emergent AEs, AEs leading to drug discontinuations, AEs leading to drug interruption, laboratory abnormalities.
L890: A consistent approach for the categorization of an AE as immune-related AE (irAE) was also applied following a prespecified case definition consisting of both programmatic checks and medical review (see Module 5.3.5.1 AK105-304 CSR, appendices 16.1.9, irAEs Characterization Charter)
L891: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L892: FDA agrees that the safety data permitted an adequate assessment of penpulimab's safety profile. FDA utilized the safety datasets generated for Study AK105-304 and Study AK105-202 and pooled safety population to review the safety profile of penpulimab (described in Section 8.2). Throughout the review of safety data, FDA sent multiple information requests (IRs)
L893: soliciting additional clinical information about medical causes of death when the patient narratives were unclear or did not provide sufficient information.
L894: ## 8.2.2. Review of the Safety Database
L895: ## Overall Exposure
L896: ## Data:
L897: The summary of clinical safety includes safety data from 951 patients who have been exposed to one or more doses of penpulimab monotherapy or in combination with other regimens. The safety data was generated in two separate parts, Monotherapy Part (n=558) and Combination Therapy Part (n=393).
L898: ## **Monotherapy Part**
L899: 558 patients were included in the monotherapy part with a variety of tumor types who have been exposed to penpulimab monotherapy at any dose levels, including 372 patients treated with penpulimab at 200mg Q2W and 186 patients treated with penpulimab at other doses (1, 3, 10 mg/kg Q2W and 200mg Q3W).
L900: Regarding the tumor types, monotherapy safety population including 198 patients with NPC (AK105-202 and AK105-304 control arm cross-over), 112 patients with non-small-cell lung cancer (NSCLC) (AK105-302 control arm cross-over), 94 patients with classic Hodgkin's lymphoma (cHL) (AK105-201) and 154 patients with various other solid tumors (AK105-101 phase Ib and AK105-204).
L901: ## *NPC 200mg Q2W population*
L902: A total of 130 NPC patients have been treated with penpulimab 200mg Q2W. The median total duration of exposure was 139.5 days (range: 14-878 days), median number of penpulimab doses was 9.5 (range: 1-59), median relative dose intensity was 98.13% (range: 52.6 - 121.7). Approximately 68.5% of patients were treated for more than 3 months, 44.6% for more than 6 months, 30.0% of patients received treatment for at least 12 months.
L903: ## *Penpulimab 200mg Q2W population*
L904: A total of 372 patients have been treated with penpulimab 200mg Q2W. The median duration of exposure was 168.5 days (range: 13 to 878 days). The median number of doses received was 12.0 (range: 1-59), median relative dose intensity was 98.25% (range: 52.6 - 121.7). Approximately 66.1% of patients were treated for more than 3 months, 49.2% for more than 6 months, 33.6% of patients received treatment for at least 12 months.
L905: ## *Overall Monotherapy Population*
L906: A total of 558 patients with various tumor types have been treated with penpulimab at any dose level. The median duration of exposure was 126 days (range: 8 to 1022 days). The median number
L907: ## Penpulimab
L908: of doses received was 8 (range: 1-71), median relative dose intensity was 100.0% (range: 52.6% - 131.3%). Approximately 60.9% of patients were treated for more than 3 months, 42.5% for more than 6 months, 25.6% of patients received treatment for at least 12 months.
L909: ## ***Combination Therapy Part***
L910: Combination Therapy Part includes 393 patients who have been exposed to penpulimab 200mg Q3W in combination with chemotherapy or other regimens, including 146 patients with NPC treated with penpulimab in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine; 173 nsq-NSCLC patients treated with penpulimab in combination with paclitaxel and carboplatin, 46 sq-NSCLC patients treated with penpulimab in combination with carboplatin and pemetrexed and 28 NPC patients treated with penpulimab in combination with chemotherapy with or without anlotinib (Arm A: penpulimab + gemcitabine + cisplatin + anlotinib, n=8; Arm B: penpulimab + gemcitabine + cisplatin, n=6; Arm C: penpulimab + gemcitabine + anlotinib, n=14).
L911: ## **AK105-304**
L912: A total of 146 patients with NPC have been treated with penpulimab 200mg Q3W in combination with cisplatin/carboplatin and gemcitabine in AK105-304. The median total duration of exposure was 259.0 days (range: 13-753 days), median number of penpulimab doses was 11 (range: 1-35), median relative dose intensity was 100.0% (range: 60.0% - 100.0%). Approximately 82.9% of patients were treated for more than 3 months, 71.2% for more than 6 months, 38.4% of patients received treatment for at least 12 months.
L913: ## **AK105-302**
L914: A total of 173 patients with sq-NSCLC have been treated with penpulimab 200mg Q3W in combination with carboplatin and paclitaxel in AK105-302. The median total duration of exposure was 294.0 days (range: 11-763 days), median number of penpulimab doses was 14 (range: 1-35), median relative dose intensity was 97.14% (range: 50.2%-190.9%). Approximately 86.7% of patients were treated for more than 3 months, 68.8% for more than 6 months, 41.0% of patients received treatment for at least 12 months.
L915: ## **AK105-301**
L916: A total of 46 patients with nsq-NSCLC have been treated with penpulimab 200mg Q3W in combination with carboplatin and paclitaxel in AK105-301. The median total duration of exposure was 229.5 days (range: 16-784 days), median number of penpulimab doses was 9 (range: 1-35), median relative dose intensity was 90.81% (range: 59.7%-131.3%). Approximately 82.6% of patients were treated for more than 3 months, 60.9% for more than 6 months, 30.4% of patients received treatment for at least 12 months.
L917: ## **AK105-205**
L918: A total of 28 patients with metastatic NPC have been treated with penpulimab 200mg Q3W in combination with chemotherapy and with or without anlotinib in AK105-205. The median total duration of exposure was 459.0 days (range: 21-756 days), median number of penpulimab
L919: doses was 18 (range: 1-35), median relative dose intensity was 97.28% (range: 69.4%-100.0%). Approximately 89.3% of patients were treated for more than 3 months, 64.3% for more than 6 months, 50.0% of patients received treatment for at least 12 months.

L957: ## Penpulimab
L958: FDA agrees that regarding male predominance, this is consistent with what is expected in the US patient population with NPC. However, the median age of patients in AK105-304 (51 years) and AK105-202 (50 years) is slightly younger than the median age for US patients with NPC (53 or 54 years). Furthermore, given that nearly all patients in the efficacy populations for both studies were of Chinese ethnicity, the efficacy populations do not reflect the racial and ethnic diversity of the US patient population with NPC. Patients with keratinizing histology were permitted to enroll to Study AK105-304; however, only very few patients with keratinizing histology were included in this trial and were therefore greatly underrepresented relative to the incidence of this histology in the US population with NPC. In total, only 0.7% of patients in each arm of AK105-304 had keratinizing histology compared to 35% of patients in the US with NPC expected to have keratinizing histology. Only patients with non-keratinizing histology were eligible for enrollment into Study AK105-202. There were no additional patients with non-keratinizing NPC in the pooled dataset.
L959: Despite the differences noted above, FDA agrees that the safety data from AK105-304, AK105-202, and the pooled safety dataset provide sufficient data for evaluations of the safety profile of penpulimab in combination with cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine and as a single agent.
L960: ## **Adequacy of the safety database:**
L961: ## **Data:**
L962: Integrated penpulimab Monotherapy Part includes pooled safety data from 558 patients with a variety of tumor types across 6 studies who have been exposed to penpulimab monotherapy at any dose levels. Of those patients, 372 patients with penpulimab 200mg Q2W were included for overall safety evaluation.
L963: Integrated penpulimab Combination Therapy Part includes 393 patients who have been exposed to penpulimab 200mg Q3W in combination with chemotherapy or other regimens. Of those patients, 146 patients with NPC treated with penpulimab in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine were included for overall safety evaluation.
L964: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L965: The safety database was considered adequate to support the characterization of the safety profile of penpulimab in the proposed patient population.
L966: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L967: FDA did not independently verify data from the AK105-302 or AK105-301 trials because these patients received chemotherapy regimens other than cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine.
L968: FDA did not independently verify the data for the pooled monotherapy dataset of 558 patients because they received penpulimab at varying doses and schedules, ranging from 1 mg/kg Q2W to 10 mg/kg Q2W and 200 mg Q3W.
L969: FDA focused its safety analysis of penpulimab monotherapy on a subset of the pooled dataset, including 372 patients treated with penpulimab 200 mg IV Q2W, including 130 patients from Study AK105-202. FDA agrees that the size and quality of the safety data provide a sufficient basis for assessing the safety profile of penpulimab both in combination with cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine, and as a single agent.
L970: ## 8.2.3. Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L971: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L972: ## Data:
L973: All clinical trials in this package were conducted according to Good Clinical Practice. Monitors were responsible for ensuring adherence to the protocol; compliance with GCP. The study team and site personnel collaborated closely to ensure the completeness, accuracy, and consistency of the study data. Direct access to medical and laboratory records of all subjects was permitted to verify entries on the study-specific eCRFs.
L974: Central laboratories were preferred in the studies. Where local laboratories were used, their qualification was strictly evaluated by the study monitors.
L975: All safety evaluation procedures including monitoring for AEs, reporting SAEs, physical examinations, vital sign measurements, clinical laboratory tests, and ECGs were standard (i.e., widely used and generally recognized as reliable, accurate, and relevant).
L976: ## The Applicant's Position:
L977: Although there were several cases of untimely reporting of SAEs as described in critical protocol deviation, no issues were identified that could affect the quality and integrity of the submitted datasets and individual case narratives.
L978: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L979: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.

L995: ## Routine Clinical Tests
L996: ## Data:
L997: Clinical laboratory data were collected routinely throughout the study.
L998: Clinical laboratory values were graded according to CTCAE 4.03 (AK105-101, AK105-201, AK105-202) or CTCAE 5.0 (AK105-204, AK105-205, AK105-301, AK105-302, and AK105-304).
L999: Clinical laboratory tests included hematology, blood chemistry, coagulation function tests, thyroid function tests, urinalysis, virological tests, and pregnancy tests. The schedule of main laboratory tests for each study is presented in Module 2.7.4, Table 3.
L1000: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1001: The laboratory evaluation (laboratory tests and timepoints) as described in the protocols was reasonable and adequate to monitor the safety of the penpulimab.
L1002: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1003: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L1004: ## 8.2.4. Safety Results
L1005: ## 8.2.4.1. Deaths
L1006: ## Data:
L1007: ## ***Monotherapy Part***
L1008: ## *NPC 200mg Q2W population*
L1009: A total of 58 (44.6%) deaths occurred in NPC 200mg Q2W population. A total of 2 (1.5%) deaths occurred within 30 days after the last dose. Deaths between 30 and 90 days after the last dose occurred in 16 subjects (12.3%). Deaths more than 90 days after the last dose occurred in 40
L1010: ## Penpulimab
L1011: subjects (30.8%).
L1012: 18 (13.8%) patients had AEs that resulted in death, regardless of causality. Excluding disease progression, AEs with an outcome of death were reported in 7 (5.4%) patients: unexplained death in 3 patients (PID [REDACTED](b) (6), PID [REDACTED](b) (6), PID [REDACTED](b) (6)), respiratory failure in 2 patients, septic shock in 1 patient, and sudden death (PID [REDACTED](b) (6)) in 1 patient.
L1013: Drug-related AE leading to death occurred in 2 patients (PID [REDACTED](b) (6), “death” as PT, died 73 days after the last dose, causality unable to be assessed; PID [REDACTED](b) (6), “death” as PT, died 35 days after the last dose, causality unable to be assessed).
L1014: ## *Penpulimab 200mg Q2W population*
L1015: A total of 143 (38.4%) deaths occurred in penpulimab 200mg Q2W population. A total of 12 (3.2%) deaths occurred within 30 days after the last dose. Deaths between 30 and 90 days after the last dose occurred in 47 subjects (12.6%). Deaths more than 90 days after the last dose occurred in 84 subjects (22.6%).
L1016: 34 (9.1%) patients had AEs that resulted in death, regardless of causality. Excluding disease progression, AEs with an outcome of death were reported in 12 (3.2%) patients: unexplained death in 3 patients (PID [REDACTED](b) (6), PID [REDACTED](b) (6), PID [REDACTED](b) (6)), respiratory failure in 3 patients, and pneumonia, septic shock, gastrointestinal haemorrhage, haemophagocytic lymphohistiocytosis, cerebral infarction or sudden death (PID [REDACTED](b) (6)) in 1 patient each.
L1017: Drug-related AE leading to death occurred in 3 patients (PID [REDACTED](b) (6), “death” as PT, died 73 days after the last dose, causality unable to be assessed; PID [REDACTED](b) (6), “death” as PT, died 35 days after the last dose, causality unable to be assessed; PID [REDACTED](b) (6), “gastrointestinal haemorrhage” as PT, died 20 days after the last dose, causality unable to be assessed).
L1018: ## *Overall Monotherapy Population*
L1019: A total of 225 (40.3%) deaths occurred in overall monotherapy population. A total of 20 (3.6%) deaths occurred within 30 days after the last dose. Deaths between 30 and 90 days after the last dose occurred in 55 subjects (9.9%). Deaths more than 90 days after the last dose occurred in 150 subjects (26.9%).
L1020: 46 (8.2%) patients had AEs that resulted in death, regardless of causality. Excluding disease progression, AEs with an outcome of death were reported in 19 (3.5%) patients: unexplained death in 7 patients (PID [REDACTED](b) (6), PID [REDACTED](b) (6), PID [REDACTED](b) (6), PID [REDACTED](b) (6), PID [REDACTED](b) (6), PID [REDACTED](b) (6), PID [REDACTED](b) (6)), respiratory failure in 5 patients, pneumonia in 1 patient, septic shock in 1 patient, gastrointestinal haemorrhage in 1 patient, haemophagocytic lymphohistiocytosis in 1 patient, malignant neoplasm progression in 1 patient, cerebral infarction in 1 patient, and sudden death (PID [REDACTED](b) (6)) in 1 patient.

L1052: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1053: Most death cases in the clinical studies were related to disease progression. This is consistent with patients' underlying medical conditions, and possibly concomitant medications. SAE Narratives are available for all patients and include all TEAEs leading to death.
L1054: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1055: FDA reviewed all fatal adverse events in patients from the primary safety population in Studies AK105-304 and AK105-202. Based on this review, one patient (0.7%) in the investigational arm of Study AK105-304 experienced a Grade 5 adverse event that could be at least potentially related to penpulimab (due to syndrome of inappropriate antidiuretic hormone secretion).
L1056: Additionally, in the AK105-202 trial of penpulimab monotherapy, four patients (1%) had Grade 5 adverse events that were at least potentially related to penpulimab. Fatal adverse reactions included a case each of pneumonitis, septic shock, colitis, and hepatitis.
L1057: A summary of FDA's review of fatal adverse events in Study AK105-304 and Study AK105-202 is provided in the tables below.

L1072: ## 8.2.4.2. Serious Adverse Events
L1073: ## Data:
L1074: ## ***Monotherapy Part***
L1075: ## *NPC 200mg Q2W population*
L1076: 31 patients (23.8%) experienced at least one SAE. Excluding death and disease progression, the most commonly (the incidence of PT \ge 1%) reported SAEs included pneumonia, lung infection, respiratory failure, pneumonitis, hepatic function abnormal, and rash.
L1077: 10 patients (7.7%) had SAEs that were deemed to be drug-related by the investigator. Excluding death and disease progression, pneumonitis and rash occurred in 2 patients each, while other PTs were reported in one patient each, including pneumonia, abdominal infection, herpes zoster disseminated, transaminases increased, pleural effusion, hepatic function abnormal and pemphigoid.
L1078: ## *Penpulimab 200mg Q2W population*
L1079: 100 patients (26.9%) experienced at least one SAE. Excluding death and disease progression, the most commonly (the incidence of PT \ge 1%) reported SAEs included pneumonia, lung infection and diarrhoea.
L1080: 28 patients (7.5%) had SAEs that were deemed to be drug-related by the investigator. Excluding death and disease progression, pneumonia, alanine aminotransferase increased, transaminases increased, immune-mediated pneumonitis, pneumonitis, hepatic function abnormal and rash occurred in 2 patients each; other PTs were reported in one patient each, including lung infection, abdominal infection, herpes zoster, herpes zoster disseminated, aspartate aminotransferase increased, blood bilirubin increased, pleural effusion, pain, gastrointestinal haemorrhage, intestinal obstruction, pemphigoid, hypersensitivity, infusion related reaction, mesangioproliferative glomerulonephritis, renal injury and hypertension.
L1081: ## *Overall Monotherapy Population*
L1082: 142 patients (25.4%) experienced at least one SAE. Excluding death and disease progression, the
L1083: most commonly (the incidence of PT ≥ 1%) reported SAEs included pneumonia and lung infection.
L1084: 42 patients (7.5%) had SAEs that were deemed to be drug-related by the investigator. L1090: ## **Combination Therapy Part**
L1091: ## **AK105-304**
L1092: In patients treated with penpulimab 200mg Q3W in combination with cisplatin/carboplatin and gemcitabine in AK105-304, 74 patients (50.7%) experienced at least one SAE. Excluding disease progression, 73 patients (50.0%) experienced at least one SAE. The most commonly (the incidence of PT \ge 1%) reported SAEs (excluding disease progression) included platelet count decreased, white blood cell count decreased, neutrophil count decreased, blood creatinine increased, anaemia, febrile neutropenia, myelosuppression, pneumonia, upper respiratory tract infection, COVID-19 pneumonia, urinary tract infection, asthenia, epistaxis, hyponatraemia, diabetic ketoacidosis, arrhythmia and nausea.
L1093: 43 patients (29.5%) had SAEs that were deemed to be drug-related by the investigator, included:
L1094: ## Penpulimab
L1095: platelet count decreased occurred in 15 patients; neutrophil count decreased occurred in 11 patients; white blood cell count decreased occurred in 9 patients; anaemia occurred in 8 patients; pneumonia occurred in 3 patients; blood creatinine increased, arrhythmia, diabetic ketoacidosis, hyponatraemia and nausea occurred in 2 patients each. Other PTs were reported in one patient each, included creatinine renal clearance decreased, lipase increased, febrile neutropenia, myelosuppression, COVID-19 pneumonia, pulmonary tuberculosis, urinary tract infection, asthenia, disease progression, oedema peripheral, dyspnoea, organising pneumonia, pleural effusion, cardiac failure, acute myocardial infarction, palpitations, hypochloraemia, hypercalcaemia, hyperkalaemia, hypocalcaemia, hypokalaemia, pancreatitis, hepatic function abnormal, brain oedema, coma, dermatitis acneiform, arthritis, synovitis and hypersensitivity.

L1106: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1107: The frequency and nature of SAEs were generally similar between the NPC penpulimab 200mg Q2W population, penpulimab 200mg Q2W population and the overall monotherapy population, and there were no clinically meaningful differences between these three monotherapy populations for any SAEs.
L1108: In the pivotal AK105-304 study, the incidence of SAEs in the penpulimab group was generally similar to that in the placebo group. No serious unexpected AEs were observed.
L1109: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1110: When reviewing SAEs, FDA's process for grouping preferred terms resulted in slightly different percentages. In addition, the FDA included laboratory abnormalities in the review of SAEs.
L1111: In Study AK105-304, 51% of patients experienced an SAE. The most frequent serious adverse reactions (\ge 2\%) were thrombocytopenia (19%), decreased neutrophils (14%), decreased white blood cells (12%), anemia (8%), myelosuppression (3.4%), decreased sodium (2.7%), pneumonia (2.1%), and nausea (2.1%).
L1112: In Study AK105-202, 22% of patients in the NPC population and 27% of patients in the pooled safety dataset had one or more SAEs. The most frequent SAEs (\ge 1\%) included pneumonia, pneumonitis, respiratory failure, and rash.

L1135: ## Monotherapy Part
L1136: ## NPC 200mg Q2W population
L1137: 5 (3.8%) patients experienced at least one TEAE leading to drug discontinuation. Drug-related AEs leading to drug discontinuation occurred in 4 (3.1%) patients. Drug-related AEs leading to drug discontinuation included pleural effusion, herpes zoster disseminated, pemphigoid and transaminases increased (1 patient each).
L1138: ## Penpulimab 200mg Q2W population
L1139: 22 (5.9%) patients experienced at least one TEAE leading to drug discontinuation. Drug-related AEs leading to drug discontinuation occurred in 11 (3.0%) patients. Drug-related AEs leading to drug discontinuation included immune-mediated pneumonitis, pleural effusion, hypersensitivity,
L1140: herpes zoster disseminated, mesangioproliferative glomerulonephritis, renal injury, pemphigoid, gastrointestinal haemorrhage and transaminases increased.
L1141: ## ***Overall Monotherapy Population***
L1142: 32 (5.7%) patients experienced at least one TEAE leading to drug discontinuation. Drug-related AEs leading to drug discontinuation occurred in 20 (3.6%) patients. Drug-related AEs leading to drug discontinuation included immune-mediated pneumonitis (0.7%), dyspnoea (0.4%), pleural effusion (0.2%), hepatic function abnormal (0.2%), immune-mediated hepatitis (0.2%), anaphylactic shock (0.2%), hypersensitivity (0.2%), herpes zoster disseminated (0.2%), lung infection (0.2%), mesangioproliferative glomerulonephritis (0.2%), renal injury (0.2%), immune-mediated dermatitis (0.2%), pemphigoid (0.2%), gastrointestinal haemorrhage (0.2%), heart injury (0.2%) and transaminases increased (0.2%).

L1154: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1155: The incidence of AEs resulting in permanent discontinuation of penpulimab was low and similar between the NPC penpulimab 200mg Q2W population, penpulimab 200mg Q2W population and the overall monotherapy population. There were no appreciable differences between the three populations for any of the SOCs or PTs.
L1156: In the pivotal AK105-304 study, the incidence of AEs resulting in permanent discontinuation of penpulimab/placebo in the penpulimab group was slightly higher than that in the placebo group.
L1157: AEs resulting in discontinuation of penpulimab were distributed across a broad range of PTs, with no specific SOC or PT accounting for most of the events (occurred in ≤1% of patients).
L1158: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1159: In Study AK105-304, 3.4% of patients discontinued penpulimab due to AEs. Rates of discontinuation were similar between the penpulimab and placebo arms.
L1160: In Study AK105-202, 3.8% of patients discontinued penpulimab due to AEs. The FDA estimates of penpulimab discontinuation due to AEs generally aligned with Applicant's estimates.
L1161: In the pooled safety dataset, 6% of patients discontinued penpulimab due to AEs.
L1162: AEs leading to permanent discontinuation of penpulimab are listed in FDA – Table 16 and FDA – Table 17 below.

L1171: ## 8.2.4.4. Dose Interruptions, Delays, and/or Reductions Due to Adverse Effects
L1172: Data:
L1173: ## *Monotherapy Part*
L1174: ## *NPC 200mg Q2W population*
L1175: 35 (26.9%) patients experienced at least one AE leading to drug interruption. Drug-related AEs leading to drug interruption occurred in 20 (15.4%) patients. Common drug-related AEs leading to drug interruption (with an incidence of \ge 1%) included alanine aminotransferase increased, hypothyroidism, rash, hepatic function abnormal and anaemia.
L1176: ## *Penpulimab 200mg Q2W population*
L1177: 95 (25.5%) patients experienced at least one AE leading to drug interruption. Drug-related AEs leading to drug interruption occurred in 55 (14.8%) patients. Common drug-related AEs leading
L1178: ## Penpulimab
L1179: to drug interruption (with an incidence of \ge 1%) included alanine aminotransferase increased, hypothyroidism, rash and hepatic function abnormal.
L1180: ## ***Overall Monotherapy Population***
L1181: 130 (23.3%) patients experienced at least one AE leading to drug interruption. Drug-related AEs leading to drug interruption occurred in 75 (13.4%) patients. Common drug-related AEs leading to drug interruption (with an incidence of \ge 1%) included alanine aminotransferase increased (1.8%), aspartate aminotransferase increased (1.3%), hypothyroidism (1.3%) and hepatic function abnormal (1.1%).

L1196: ## Penpulimab
L1197: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1198: The overall incidence of AEs resulting in drug interruption were generally similar between the NPC penpulimab 200mg Q2W population, penpulimab 200mg Q2W population and the overall monotherapy population. There were no marked differences between the three populations for any of the SOCs or PTs.
L1199: In the pivotal AK105-304 study, the incidence of AEs resulting in drug interruption of penpulimab/placebo in the penpulimab group was similar to that in the placebo group.
L1200: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1201: FDA included laboratory abnormalities in the analysis of AEs leading to dose interruptions, resulting in the report of additional AEs leading to penpulimab dose interruption compared with Applicant's report.

L1225: ## 8.2.4.6. Treatment Emergent Adverse Events and Adverse Reactions
L1226: ## Data:
L1227: ## *Monotherapy Part*
L1228: ## *NPC 200mg Q2W population*
L1229: Excluding disease progression, 123 out of 130 (94.6%) patients had at least one TEAE. Most patients had Grade 1 or 2 AEs; 38 (29.2%) patients had at least one \ge Grade 3 TEAE. The incidence of drug-related TEAEs was 71.5% (93/130), and the incidence of \ge Grade 3 drug-related TEAEs was 8.5% (11/130).
L1230: The most commonly (the incidence of PT \ge 20%) reported TEAEs included anemia and hypothyroidism. Common \ge Grade 3 TEAEs (with an incidence of \ge 1%) were anaemia, hepatic function abnormal, liver injury, lung infection, aspartate aminotransferase increased, blood alkaline phosphatase increased, blood magnesium increased, gamma-glutamyltransferase increased, weight decreased, hyperuricaemia, hyponatraemia, respiratory failure, rash, disease progression and death.
L1231: ## *Penpulimab 200mg Q2W population*
L1232: Excluding disease progression, 355 out of 372 (95.4%) patients had at least one TEAE. Most patients had Grade 1 or 2 AEs; 127 (34.1%) patients had at least one \ge Grade 3 TEAE. The incidence of drug-related TEAEs was 71.8% (267/372), and the incidence of \ge Grade 3 drug-related TEAEs was 9.7% (36/372).
L1233: The most commonly (the incidence of PT \ge 20%) reported TEAEs included anemia and hypothyroidism. Common \ge Grade 3 TEAEs (with an incidence of \ge 1%) were anaemia, Diarrhoea, Vomiting, Lower respiratory tract infection, lung infection, pneumonia, Urinary tract infection, alanine aminotransferase increased, aspartate aminotransferase increased, gamma-glutamyltransferase increased, platelet count decreased, hyperuricaemia, hyponatraemia, hypertension and disease progression.
L1234: ## *Overall Monotherapy Population*
L1235: Excluding disease progression, 512 out of 558 (91.8%) patients had at least one TEAE. Most patients had Grade 1 or 2 AEs; 172 (30.8%) patients had at least one \ge Grade 3 TEAE. The incidence of drug-related TEAEs was 69.7% (389/558), and the incidence of \ge Grade 3 drug-related TEAEs was 10.8% (60/558).
L1236: The most commonly (the incidence of PT \ge 20%) reported TEAEs included anemia. Common \ge Grade 3 TEAEs (with an incidence of \ge 1%) were anaemia, lung infection, pneumonia, urinary tract infection, aspartate aminotransferase increased, gamma-glutamyltransferase increased, hypokalaemia, hyponatraemia, hypertension, disease progression and death.
L1237: Adverse drug reactions (ADRs) with penpulimab have been determined include the known risks associated with the anti-PD-1/PD-L1 drug class and most frequently AEs (\ge10% of patients).
L1238: The ADR for proposed patient population presented in the prescribing label excluded lab abnormality AE. The lab abnormality by the CTCAE grade derived based on actual value was presented under a separate table included in the prescribing label.

L1246: ## Combination Therapy Part
L1247: ## AK105-304
L1248: In patients treated with penpulimab 200mg Q3W in combination with cisplatin/carboplatin and gemcitabine in AK105-304, 146 (100.0%) patients had at least one TEAE. 132 (90.4%) patients had at least one ≥ Grade 3 TEAE. The most commonly (the incidence of PT ≥ 20%) reported TEAEs with a marginally higher incidence in the penpulimab group included aspartate aminotransferase increased, weight decreased, blood creatinine increased, anaemia, vomiting, hyponatraemia, hypoalbuminaemia, hypokalaemia, hypochloreaemia, pyrexia, COVID-19, cough and hypothyroidism. Common ≥ Grade 3 TEAEs (with an incidence of ≥ 2%) with a marginally higher incidence in the penpulimab group included lymphocyte count decreased, alanine aminotransferase increased, blood creatine phosphokinase increased, anaemia, pneumonia, COVID-19 pneumonia, hypokalaemia, hyponatraemia, hypocalcaemia, hypomagnesaemia, nausea, hypertension and disease progression.
L1249: The incidence of drug-related TEAEs was 89.7% (131/146), and the incidence of ≥ Grade 3 drug-related TEAEs was 57.5% (84/146). The most commonly (the incidence of PT ≥ 10%) reported drug-related TEAEs with a marginally higher incidence in the penpulimab group included aspartate aminotransferase increased, platelet count decreased, blood creatinine increased, anaemia, hyponatraemia, hypoalbuminaemia, hypokalaemia, hypertriglyceridaemia, hypochloreaemia, hypomagnesaemia, vomiting, diarrhoea, constipation, rash and hypothyroidism. Common ≥ Grade 3 drug-related TEAEs (with an incidence of ≥ 1%) with a marginally higher incidence in the penpulimab group included white blood cell count decreased, lymphocyte count decreased, anaemia, hypokalaemia, hyponatraemia, hypertriglyceridaemia,
L1250: ## Penpulimab
L1251: hypocalcaemia, hypomagnesaemia, diabetic ketoacidosis, pneumonia and nausea.
L1252: Regarding COVID-19 infection, the incidence in AK105 group was higher than that in placebo group. Considering the high infection rate of COVID-19, the incidence of COVID-19 infection was similar between the two groups after analyzed using grouped PT term (including COVID-19, COVID-19 pneumonia and asymptomatic COVID-19) under the SS and CTS sets in the study of AK105-304.

L1264: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1265: The most common AEs (regardless of severity and causality) in monotherapy observed in NPC penpulimab 200mg Q2W population were comparable to penpulimab 200mg Q2W population and the overall monotherapy population. Most of the AEs were Grade 1 or 2, indicating a tolerable safety profile of penpulimab.
L1266: In the pivotal AK105-304 study, only a few of TEAEs occurred at a slightly higher rate in the penpulimab group, such as abnormal transaminases, anemia, metabolic and nutritional disorders, electrolyte abnormalities, COVID-19, hypothyroidism, cough, and headache. Except for COVID-19, these are common AEs in cancer patients, and most of the AEs were grades 1 or 2, without affecting the function of important organs or systems.

L1299: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1300: Changes in laboratory parameters were predominantly Grade 1 or 2 in severity, and the incidence of any grade worsening to Grade 3 or 4 was generally low, suggesting a well-tolerated safety profile.
L1301: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1302: The FDA's review of laboratory abnormalities with penpulimab administration largely agrees with the Applicant's position and we agree penpulimab has a safety profile that is manageable and consistent with what's known for anti-PD-(L)1 antibodies
L1303: Laboratory abnormalities worsening from baseline, occurring in 20% or more of patients regardless of the absolute differences between the placebo and chemotherapy arm, based on FDA's review of the current datasets are presented below in FDA – Table 22 in AK105-304 and in FDA – Table 23 for AK105-202 and the pooled safety set.
