L1317: ## 8.2.4.8. Vital Signs
L1318: ## Data:
L1319: The changes of vital signs (pulse, systolic/diastolic blood pressure, respiratory rate, and body temperature) from baseline to minimum, maximum value, and the end of treatment visit were summarized in Module 5.3.5.3, ISS, Appendix 3.5, Table 8.1.1 and Table 8.1.2.
L1320: Median changes from baseline to minimum or maximum and from baseline to final value were generally small and not considered to be clinically meaningful for any of the vital sign parameters.
L1321: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1322: Changes from baseline to minimum or maximum and from baseline to end of treatment visit
L1323: were generally small and not considered to be clinically meaningful for any of the vital parameters.
L1324: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1325: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L1326: ## 8.2.4.9. Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L1327: ## Data:
L1328: Electrocardiogram (ECG) assessments are part of routine safety monitoring.
L1329: Shift table for ECGs of clinical significance from baseline to worst post-baseline was summarized in Module5.3.5.3, ISS, Appendix 3.5, Table 7.1.1 and Table 7.1.2.
L1330: ## ***NPC 200mg Q2W population***
L1331: No patients had a QTcF value &gt; 500 msec or an increase from baseline &gt; 90 msec. No patients had a QTcF value &gt; 480 msec, 3 (2.5%) patients had an increase from baseline &gt; 60 msec.
L1332: ## ***Penpulimab 200mg Q2W population***
L1333: 2 (0.6%) patients had a QTcF value &gt; 500 msec and 4 (1.1%) patients had an increase from baseline &gt; 90 msec. 6 (1.7%) patients had a QTcF value &gt; 480 msec and 14 (3.9%) patients had an increase from baseline &gt; 60 msec.
L1334: ## ***Overall Monotherapy population***
L1335: 6 (1.1%) patients had a QTcF value &gt; 500 msec and 7 (1.3%) patients had an increase from baseline &gt; 90 msec. 11 (2.1%) patients had a QTcF value &gt; 480 msec and 21 (4.0%) patients had an increase from baseline &gt; 60 msec.
L1336: ## ***Conclusions***
L1337: Individuals with clinically meaningful QTcF values and changes from baseline were found to have had either cardiac medical history or were receiving potentially cardioactive concomitant medications at the time of abnormality or had single occurrences above the defined threshold. There was no evidence of clinically meaningful cardiac effects associated with penpulimab exposure.
L1338: ## ***Combination Therapy Part***
L1339: ## ***AK105-304***
L1340: No patients had a QTcF value &gt; 500 msec, 1 (0.7%) patient had an increase from baseline &gt; 90 msec. No patients had a QTcF value &gt; 480 msec, 11 (8.0%) patients had an increase from baseline &gt; 60 msec.
L1341: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1342: Electrocardiogram (ECG) assessments are part of routine safety monitoring. There was no evidence of clinically meaningful cardiac effects associated with penpulimab exposure.
L1343: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1344: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L1345: ## 8.2.4.10. Immunogenicity
L1346: ## Data:
L1347: ## ***NPC 200mg Q2W population***
L1348: The proportion of patients with ADA positive at baseline and post-baseline was 5.9% and 30.3%, respectively. 27.7% of patients developed treatment-emergent ADA (TEADA), and 6.7 % TEADA patients had positive neutralizing antibody (Nab). Among patients with negative baselines, the incidence of TEADA was 25.2%, and the incidence of positive Nab was 6.7%. Only 3 (2.5%) TEADA patients had positive baselines and none of them had positive Nab.
L1349: The incidence rates of TEADA and Nab in NPC population who received Penpulimab monotherapy were comparable to other tumor types.
L1350: ## ***Penpulimab 200mg Q2W population***
L1351: 349 patients were treated with penpulimab monotherapy at 1mg/kg, 3mg/kg, 10mg/kg, 200mg Q2W.
L1352: The proportion of patients with ADA positive at baseline and post-baseline was 3.4% and 31.2%, respectively. 30.1% of patients developed treatment-emergent ADA (TEADA), and 5.2 % of patients with TEADA had positive neutralizing antibody (Nab). Among patients with negative baselines, the incidence of TEADA was 28.4%, and the incidence of positive Nab was 5.2%. Only 5 (1.4%) patients with TEADA had positive baselines and none of them had positive Nab.
L1353: Among the patients treated with penpulimab monotherapy (1~10mg/kg Q2W, or 200mg Q2W/Q3W), the incidence of AEs such as TEAE, TRAE (CTCAE Grade 3+), serious TEAEs, serious drug-related TEAEs, TEAEs leading to death, drug-related TEAEs leading to death, drug-related TEAEs leading to drug discontinuation in the patients with TEADA were similar to those from ADA-negative patients, and no special type of AE occurred in patients with TEADA.
L1354: For drug-related TEAEs leading to dose interruption, the incidence in patients with TEADA was slightly higher than that in the ADA-negative patients (20.0% vs. 13.8%).
L1355: ## Penpulimab
L1356: In ex-Asia patients who received penpulimab monotherapy (200mg Q2W), AE events in the TEADA were slightly higher than the ADA-negative patients, probably due to imbalance of the sample size (only 13 patients in ex-Asia group).
L1357: In NPC monotherapy population, the incidence of AEs in patients with TEADA was similar to that in the ADA-negative patients, and no special type of AEs occurred.
L1358: In addition, the incidences of most irAE-related events in the patients with TEADA were similar to those from the ADA-negative patients, except for the incidence in irAEs leading to drug interruption in patients with TEADA was higher than ADA-negative patients (11.4% vs. 6.3%).
L1359: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1360: In TEADA patients, the incidence and types of AEs were generally similar to those of all patients. Treatment emergent ADA had no significant impact on safety.
L1361: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1362: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.

L1376: ## Hypothyroidism
L1377: Hypothyroidism occurred in 81 (14.5%) patients, including 48 (8.6%) Grade 1 and 33 (5.9%) Grade 2 irAEs. No patient experienced a serious irAE. 7 (1.3%) patients experienced irAEs leading to drug interruption. No patient experienced irAEs leading to drug discontinuation.
L1378: ## Hyperthyroidism
L1379: Hyperthyroidism occurred in 33 (5.9%) of patients, including 27 (4.8%) Grade 1 and 6 (1.1%) Grade 2 irAEs. No patient experienced a serious irAE. 2 (0.4%) patients experienced irAEs leading to drug interruption. No patient experienced irAEs leading to drug discontinuation.
L1380: ## Other Thyroid Diseases
L1381: Other thyroid diseases occurred in 5 (0.9%) of patients, including 2 (0.4%) Grade 1 and 3 (0.5%) Grade 2 irAEs. No patient experienced a serious irAE. 3 (0.5%) patients experienced irAEs leading to drug interruption. No patient experienced irAEs leading to drug discontinuation.
L1382: ## Hyperglycaemia/Type 1 diabetes mellitus
L1383: Hyperglycaemia/type 1 diabetes mellitus occurred in 5 (0.9%) of patients, all Grade 1 irAEs. No patient experienced a serious irAE, irAEs leading to drug interruption, or irAEs leading to drug discontinuation.
L1384: ## Hypophysitis
L1385: In overall monotherapy population, only one patient developed immune-mediated hypophysitis,
L1386: ## Penpulimab
L1387: which was CTCAE grade 3 and a serious irAE. No irAE leading to interruption or permanent discontinuation of penpulimab.

L1416: ## Penpulimab
L1417: ## Endocrine Toxicity
L1418: ## *Hypothyroidism*
L1419: In patients treated with penpulimab 200mg Q3W in combination with cisplatin/carboplatin and gemcitabine in AK105-304, hypothyroidism occurred in 26 (17.8%) of patients, including 7 (4.8%) Grade 1 and 19 (13.0%) Grade 2 irAEs. No patient experienced a serious irAE. 1 (0.7%) patient experienced irAEs leading to drug interruption. No patient experienced irAEs leading to drug discontinuation.
L1420: ## *Hyperthyroidism*
L1421: In patients treated with penpulimab 200mg Q3W in combination with cisplatin/carboplatin and gemcitabine in AK105-304, hyperthyroidism occurred in 3 (2.1%) of patients, including 2 (1.4%) Grade 1 and 1 (0.7%) Grade 2 irAEs. There was no serious irAE. No irAE leading to interruption or permanent discontinuation of penpulimab.
L1422: ## *Hyperglycaemia/Type 1 diabetes mellitus*
L1423: In patients treated with penpulimab 200mg Q3W in combination with cisplatin/carboplatin and gemcitabine in AK105-304, hyperglycaemia/type 1 diabetes mellitus occurred in 4 (2.7%) of patients, including 1 (0.7%) Grade 1, 1 (0.7%) Grade 2, 1 (0.7%) Grade 3 and 1 (0.7%) Grade 4 irAEs. 2 (1.4%) patients experienced a serious irAE. 1 (0.7%) patient experienced irAEs leading to drug interruption. 1 (0.7%) patient experienced irAEs leading to drug discontinuation.
L1424: ## Dermatologic Toxicity
L1425: In patients treated with penpulimab 200mg Q3W in combination with cisplatin/carboplatin and gemcitabine in AK105-304, dermatologic toxicity occurred in 14 (9.6%) of patients, including 6 (4.1%) Grade 1, 7 (4.8%) Grade 2 and 1 (0.7%) Grade 3 irAEs. 1 (0.7%) patient experienced a serious irAE. 2 (1.4%) patients experienced irAEs leading to drug interruption. No patient experienced irAEs leading to drug discontinuation.
L1426: ## Pulmonary Toxicity
L1427: In patients treated with penpulimab 200mg Q3W in combination with cisplatin/carboplatin and gemcitabine in AK105-304, only one patient (0.7%) developed pulmonary toxicity, which was CTCAE grade 2 and a serious irAE. The irAE led to interruption of penpulimab but didn't lead to permanent discontinuation of penpulimab.
L1428: ## Hepatotoxicity
L1429: In patients treated with penpulimab 200mg Q3W in combination with cisplatin/carboplatin and gemcitabine in AK105-304, only one patient (0.6%) developed hepatotoxicity, Grade 2. The event was not a serious irAE. The event led to interruption of penpulimab but didn't lead to permanent discontinuation of penpulimab.
L1430: ## Penpulimab
L1431: ## Musculoskeletal Toxicity
L1432: In patients treated with penpulimab 200mg Q3W in combination with cisplatin/carboplatin and gemcitabine in AK105-304, only one patient (0.7%) developed musculoskeletal toxicity, which was CTCAE grade 3 and was a serious irAE. The irAE led to interruption of penpulimab but didn't lead to permanent discontinuation of penpulimab.
L1433: ## Hematological Toxicity
L1434: In patients treated with penpulimab 200mg Q3W in combination with cisplatin/carboplatin and gemcitabine in AK105-304, only one patient (0.7%) developed thrombocytopenia, which was CTCAE grade 4 and was a serious irAE. The irAE led to interruption of penpulimab but didn't lead to permanent discontinuation of penpulimab.
L1435: ## Pancreatic Toxicity
L1436: In patients treated with penpulimab 200mg Q3W in combination with cisplatin/carboplatin and gemcitabine in AK105-304, pancreatic toxicity occurred in 2 (1.4%) of patients, including 1 (0.7%) Grade 2 and 1 (0.7%) Grade 3 irAEs. 2 (1.4%) patients experienced a serious irAE. No patient experienced irAEs leading to drug interruption. 1 (0.7%) patient experienced irAEs leading to drug discontinuation.
L1437: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1438: In the overall monotherapy population, the most commonly (the incidence of PT \ge 1%) reported irAEs included hypothyroidism, blood thyroid stimulating hormone increased, thyroxine free decreased, hyperthyroidism, blood thyroid stimulating hormone decreased, rash, pruritus, rash generalised, alanine aminotransferase increased and aspartate aminotransferase increased.
L1439: Compared with monotherapy with penpulimab, the combination of penpulimab and chemotherapy did not increase the incidence of irAEs, and the incidence of irAEs in various organs was low. The incidence of hypothyroidism was relatively high, which may also be related to a history of previous radiotherapy. 67.5% of the enrolled patients had recurrent nasopharyngeal carcinoma and had previously received neck radiotherapy, so thyroid damage may have already existed at baseline. Moreover, immune-related endocrine toxicity can be controlled with hormone replacement therapy, and there was no significant safety risk.
L1440: The development of irAEs was expected based on the mechanism of action of the drug. The pattern, incidence and severity of irAEs was consistent with other PD-1 inhibitors and was manageable with the use of appropriate dose interruption and immunosuppressants.
L1441: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1442: FDA reviewed the Applicant adjudicated immune-related AEs (irAEs) in AK105-304, AK105-202, and in the pooled safety dataset. Given the potential delayed onset of irAEs, FDA's review includes adverse events that occurred up to 90 days after the last dose of treatment with penpulimab or placebo.

L1450: FDA's analysis of irAEs agrees with the Applicant's position. Overall, the most common irAEs were endocrinopathies and dermatitis. There was a very low rate of colitis or pneumonitis amongst populations receiving penpulimab with 2 cases of Grade 3 colitis in the integrated safety set and one case of less than Grade 3 pneumonitis in the penpulimab NPC population in AK105-202. Neither or these irAEs were noted in the first line indication alongside chemotherapy in AK105-304.
L1451: ## 8.2.5.2. Infusion-Related Reactions
L1452: ## ***NPC 200mg Q2W population***
L1453: 5 patients (3.8%) experienced infusion-related reactions, including Grade 1 in 4 patients, Grade 2 in 1 patient. No patient occurred Grade 3-4 infusion-related reactions. No patient experienced serious infusion-related reaction, infusion-related reactions leading to interruption of penpulimab or infusion-related reaction leading to permanent discontinuation of penpulimab. No patient received systemic corticosteroid therapies. All these 5 patients had recovered/resolved.
L1454: ## ***Penpulimab 200mg Q2W population***
L1455: 38 patients (10.2%) experienced infusion-related reactions, including Grade 1 in 14 patients, Grade 2 in 22 patients. 2 patients occurred Grade 3-4 infusion-related reactions. 3 patients (0.8%) experienced serious infusion-related reaction. Infusion-related reaction led to interruption of penpulimab in 3 patients (0.8%) and 1 patients (0.3%) occurred infusion-related reaction resulted in permanent discontinuation of penpulimab. 8 patients (2.2%) received systemic corticosteroid therapies. All these 38 patients had recovered/resolved.
L1456: ## ***Overall Monotherapy population***
L1457: 41 patients (7.3%) experienced infusion-related reactions, including Grade 1 in 15 patients, Grade 2 in 23 patients. 3 patients had Grade 3-4 infusion-related reactions. 4 patients (0.7%) experienced serious infusion-related reaction. Infusion-related reaction led to interruption of penpulimab occurred in 3 patients (0.5%) and 2 patients (0.4%) had infusion-related reaction resulted in permanent discontinuation of penpulimab. 9 patients (1.6%) received systemic corticosteroid therapies. All these 41 patients had recovered/resolved.
L1458: ## ***AK105-304***
L1459: 5 patients (3.4%) experienced infusion-related reactions, including Grade 1 in 3 patients, Grade
L1460: ## Penpulimab
L1461: 2 in 2 patients. No Grade 3-4 infusion-related reaction occurred. There was no serious infusion-related reaction, infusion-related reactions leading to interruption of penpulimab or infusion-related reactions leading to permanent discontinuation of penpulimab. 1 patient (0.7%) received systemic corticosteroid therapies. All these 5 patients had recovered/resolved.
L1462: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1463: In the overall monotherapy population, The most commonly (the incidence of PT \ge 1%) reported irAEs included hypothyroidism, blood thyroid stimulating hormone increased, thyroxine free decreased, hyperthyroidism, blood thyroid stimulating hormone decreased, rash, pruritus, rash generalised, alanine aminotransferase increased and aspartate aminotransferase increased.
L1464: Compared with monotherapy with penpulimab, the combination of penpulimab and chemotherapy did not increase the incidence of irAEs, and the incidence of irAEs in various organs was low. The incidence of hypothyroidism was relatively high, which may also be related to a history of previous radiotherapy. 67.5% of the enrolled patients had recurrent nasopharyngeal carcinoma and had previously received neck radiotherapy, so thyroid damage may have already existed at baseline. Moreover, immune-related endocrine toxicity can be controlled with hormone replacement therapy, and there was no significant safety risk.
L1465: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1466: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position. In AK105-304, no patients experienced an infusion-related reaction in the penpulimab arm. Infusion-related reactions occurred in 3.4% and 10% of patients in AK105-202 and the pooled safety dataset, respectively.

There was no notable difference in the trend of score changes between the two groups.

L1476: ## 8.2.7. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
L1477: ## Data:
L1478: This section summarizes the relationship between various intrinsic factors and the safety profile of penpulimab. These data are relevant to patient management based on demographic or other "intrinsic" factors. Only notable differences (\ge 10\%) are discussed.
L1479: The safety profile of penpulimab was assessed in relation to the following intrinsic factors:
L1480: - Age group (&lt; 65 years; \ge 65 years)
- Sex (male; female)
- ECOG (0;1)
- Race (Asian; Non-Asian)
L1481: All AEs Subgroup Analysis were summarized in Module 5.3.5.3, ISS, Appendix 3.5, Table 4.2.2, Table 4.5.1.2, Table 4.9.1.2, Table 5.2.4 and ISS Combined Supplementary Tables, Table 4.2.1, Table 4.5.1.1, Table 4.9.1.1, Table 5.2.3.
L1482: ## *Age (&lt;65 vs \ge 65)*
L1483: In overall penpulimab monotherapy population, there was no appreciable difference between age subgroups for any of the AE categories. The differences between age subgroups in the NPC 200mg Q2W population cannot be assessed due to only 2 patients with age \ge 65 years enrolled.
L1484: Among patients in AK105-302 study, serious TEAEs and TEAEs leading to drug interruption occurred at a higher rate in females than in males.
L1485: The differences between age subgroups in other studies of penpulimab combination therapy cannot be assessed due to the small sample size of the patients with age \ge 65 years (15 patients in AK105-304, 18 patients in AK105-301, and 3 patients in AK105-205).
L1486: [Redacted text area]
L1487: In overall penpulimab monotherapy population, the AE profile of penpulimab was generally similar between females and males.
L1488: In NPC 200mg Q2W population, serious TEAEs occurred at a slightly higher rate in females than in males (25.8% vs 23.2%, respectively), as well as TEAEs leading to drug interruption (29.0% vs 26.3%, respectively) and TEAEs leading to drug discontinuation (6.5% vs 3.0%, respectively), this observation should be interpreted in the context of the small sample size of the female subgroup (31 patients).
L1489: ## Penpulimab
L1490: Among patients in AK105-304 study, TEAEs of CTCAE grade 3 or higher, serious TEAEs, and TEAEs leading to drug interruption occurred at a higher rate in females than in males, which should be interpreted in the context of the small sample size of the female subgroup (26 females in penpulimab group).
L1491: Among patients receiving other penpulimab combination therapy, the number of female patients was too small to assess the differences (13 females in AK105-302, 14 females in AK105-301, and 3 females in AK105-205).
L1492: ## *ECOG performance status*
L1493: In overall penpulimab monotherapy population, the incidence of events was similar in patients with a baseline ECOG performance status of 0 and 1 for most AE categories. However, notable differences were observed for Grade 3 or higher TEAEs (29.9% vs 38.3%) and SAEs (20.4% vs 28.8%), all of which occurred at a higher rate in patients with an ECOG performance status of 1 compared with ECOG performance status of 0.
L1494: Among patients in AK105-304 study, the incidence of events was similar in patients with a baseline ECOG performance status of 0 and 1. Among patients in AK105-302 study, TEAEs of CTCAE grade 3 or higher, serious TEAEs, and TEAEs leading to drug interruption occurred at a higher rate in patients with an ECOG performance status of 1 compared with ECOG performance status of 0.
L1495: The differences between ECOG subgroups in other studies of penpulimab combination therapy cannot be assessed due to the small sample size.
L1496: ## *Race*
L1497: For penpulimab monotherapy, the safety subgroup analysis revealed no clinically relevant differences in the incidence, nature, or severity of AEs between Asian and non-Asian patients.
L1498: The differences between race subgroups in penpulimab combination therapy cannot be assessed due to only 6 non-Asian patients enrolled.
L1499: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1500: The AE profile for penpulimab monotherapy population and combination therapy was generally similar across subgroups based on age and sex.
L1501: The incidence of AEs of any grade was generally similar between ECOG subgroup, and lower incidence rate of grade 3 or higher TEAEs, SAEs, and TEAEs leading to drug interruption in patients with ECOG performance status of 0 were observed.
L1502: The similarity in the safety profiles across ethnic groups suggests that the pharmacological activity and safety profile of penpulimab are not significantly influenced by ethnicity.
L1503: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1504: FDA reviewed the incidence of AEs by age and sex in the primary and pooled safety populations and agrees with the Applicant's position. A review of safety by race and ethnicity was not possible for the primary safety population given that all patients enrolled in Study AK105-202 were Asian.

L1520: ## Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
L1521: ## Data:
L1522: N/A
L1523: ## Penpulimab
L1524: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1525: Penpulimab has not been studied in pregnant or breastfeeding women. As of the DCO dates for studies on dosing regimen of penpulimab 200 mg Q2W, there were no reports of pregnancies or pregnancy-related events in either study. Based on its mechanism of action, penpulimab has the potential to impact maintenance of pregnancy and may cause fetal harm when administered to a pregnant woman. Human IgG1 is known to cross the placental barrier; therefore, penpulimab has the potential to be transmitted from the mother to the developing fetus. Penpulimab is not recommended during pregnancy and in women of childbearing potential not using effective contraception during treatment and for at least 3 months after the last dose.
L1526: There is no information regarding the presence of penpulimab in human milk, the absorption and effects on the breastfed infant, or the effects on milk production. Human IgG is excreted in human milk. Because of the potential for adverse reactions in breastfed infants from penpulimab, advise a lactating women not to breastfeed during treatment and for at least 5 months after the last dose.
L1527: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1528: See FDA's Assessment in Section 5.5.4 (Reproductive and Developmental Toxicity) of this review.
L1529: ## **Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth**
L1530: ## Data:
L1531: The Sponsor planned to recruit adolescents with NPC aged \le 12 years in the study of AK105-304. However, due to the low incidence, no patient in this age group was enrolled.
L1532: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1533: Pediatric data is not available.
L1534: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1535: FDA agrees with Applicant's position that the safety and effectiveness of penpulimab has not been established in pediatric patients under 12 years of age.
L1536: ## Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
L1537: ## Data:
L1538: There are no available safety data for penpulimab overdose.
L1539: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1540: There is no specific treatment in the event of penpulimab overdose and possible symptoms have not been established.
L1541: As a result of its use, mode of action, physiological and pharmacological activity, and lack of stimulant properties, penpulimab monotherapy is unlikely to be abused. Because penpulimab has no known potential for dependence and is not continuously dosed, withdrawal or rebound events are not relevant to penpulimab dosing.
L1542: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1543: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L1544: ## 8.2.10. Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1545: ## Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L1546: ## Data:
L1547: Cumulatively, 866 post-marketing adverse events were received from IBD by 29 Apr 2024, including 430 serious adverse events and 436 non-serious adverse events. Serious adverse events with the highest number of reports (\ge 8 events) included white blood cell count decreased, neutrophil count decreased, platelet count decreased, myelosuppression, rash, immune-mediated lung disease, dyspnoea, chest discomfort, drug-induced liver injury, hepatic function abnormal, hypothyroidism, immune-mediated myocarditis, anaphylactic shock.
L1548: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1549: The first indication of penpulimab (Approval Number: GYZZ S20210033) was conditionally approved for marketing in China on 3 Aug 2021 (International Birth Date, IBD). Penpulimab is indicated, as a single agent, for the treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory classical Hodgkin lymphoma who have failed at least two lines of systemic chemotherapy. For classical Hodgkin lymphoma, the recommended dosage is administered by intravenous infusion at a recommended dose of 200mg every 2 weeks until disease progression or unacceptable
L1550: ## Penpulimab
L1551: toxicity. The second indication was approved for marketing in China on 10 Jan 2024. Penpulimab is indicated, in combination with paclitaxel and carboplatin for the first-line treatment of locally advanced or metastatic squamous non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC). For locally advanced or metastatic squamous NSCLC, the recommended dosage is 200mg every 3 weeks administered by intravenous infusion until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity. The third indication was approved in China on 24 Apr 2024. Penpulimab is indicated, as a single agent, for the treatment of adult patients with recurrent/metastatic nasopharyngeal cancer who have previously failed at least two lines of systemic therapy. For nasopharyngeal cancer, the recommended dosage is 200mg every 2 weeks administered by intravenous infusion until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity.
L1552: Estimated Post-marketing Exposure: cumulative sales was (b) (4) vials from IBD of penpulimab by 29 Apr 2024. The dose administered per patient was 2 vials each cycle; referring to the clinical trial data included in the approved package insert, the median duration of administration of single agent and in combination with chemotherapy was 113 days and 168 days, respectively, and so the median duration of administration of single agent and in combination with chemotherapy was about 8 cycles. Under these assumption, it is estimated that the cumulative number of patients treated with penpulimab in the post-marketing setting was (b) (4). However, considering medication compliance, the actual number of cycles should be fewer than 8 and the actual number of treated patients was more than (b) (4).
L1553: Based on the analysis of the post-marketing safety data, mechanism of action of the drug or adverse reaction profile of same class drugs, Marketing Authorization Holder applied to Center for Drug Evaluation, NMPA for revising the package insert of penpulimab to add the adverse reactions of exfoliative dermatitis, myocarditis and immune-mediated myocarditis on 12 Jul 2023 and was approved for revising the package insert on 30 Aug 2023. For other post-marketing reports, no sufficient evidence to prove that there was a definite causality relationship between the adverse events and penpulimab due to limited information or confounding factors. Lack of information on concomitant diseases, concomitant medication, diagnosis evidence of adverse events or medical history and limited follow-up information are prevalent in post marketing reports. Confounding factors such as different medication populations, different combined anti-tumor treatment regimens and differences in the patients' own conditions also existed during the assessment of adverse events.
L1554: Overall, the analysis of the post-marketing safety data supports the good tolerability of penpulimab and adequacy of the risk management plan. Benefit-risk profile continues to support further development of penpulimab.
L1555: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1556: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L1557: ## Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1558: ## Data:
L1559: New immune-related AEs may be identified in the postmarketing environment.
L1560: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1561: Akeso will continue to monitor the safety data of penpulimab and assess the benefit-risk balance of penpulimab. The immune-related AEs can be minimized by routine risk minimisation activities. According to the available safety and efficacy data, treatment with penpulimab is relatively safe and the benefit outweigh the risk.
L1562: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1563: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L1564: ## 8.2.11. Integrated Assessment of Safety
L1565: ## Data:
L1566: N/A
L1567: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1568: In monotherapy part, the safety profile of penpulimab 200mg Q2W in NPC population is consistent with the well-characterized safety profile in the penpulimab 200mg Q2W population and overall monotherapy population. These observations indicate penpulimab being a well-tolerated drug as monotherapy. For penpulimab combination therapy, in the pivotal study AK105-304, the incidence of TEAEs and SAEs were found to be generally similar between the penpulimab group and placebo group. The overall safety profile of penpulimab was tolerable, manageable, and consistent with other marketed PD-1 inhibitors.
L1569: The demographic and disease characteristics of patients in the NPC 200mg Q2W monotherapy population and AK105-304 study were representative of the intended population to be treated in clinical practice.
L1570: ## **NPC 200mg Q2W population in Monotherapy Part**
L1571: Excluding disease progression, 123 out of 130 (94.6%) patients had at least one TEAE. Most patients had Grade 1 or 2 AEs; 38 (29.2%) patients had at least one \ge Grade 3 TEAE. 35 (26.9%) patients experienced at least one AE leading to drug interruption. 5 (3.8%) patients experienced at least one TEAE leading to drug discontinuation. A total of 18 (13.8%) patients died due to AE,
L1572: ## Penpulimab
L1573: 11 of these deaths were due to disease progression.
L1574: A total of 58 (44.6%) deaths occurred in NPC 200mg Q2W population. A total of 2 (1.5%) deaths occurred within 30 days after the last dose. Deaths between 30 and 90 days after the last dose occurred in 16 subjects (12.3%). Deaths more than 90 days after the last dose occurred in 40 subjects (30.8%).
L1575: 18 (13.8%) patients had AEs that resulted in death, regardless of causality. Excluding disease progression, AEs with an outcome of death were reported in 7 (5.4%) patients: unexplained death in 3 patients (PID [REDACTED](b)(6), PID [REDACTED](b)(6), PID [REDACTED](b)(6)), respiratory failure in 2 patients, septic shock in 1 patient, and sudden death (PID [REDACTED](b)(6)) in 1 patient.
L1576: 31 patients (23.8%) experienced at least one SAE. Excluding death and disease progression, the most commonly (the incidence of PT \ge 1%) reported SAEs included pneumonia, lung infection, respiratory failure, pneumonitis, hepatic function abnormal, and rash.
L1577: 5 (3.8%) patients experienced at least one TEAE leading to drug discontinuation. Drug-related AEs leading to drug discontinuation occurred in 4 (3.1%) patients.
L1578: The incidence of irAEs was 32.3% (42/130). The most commonly (the incidence of PT \ge 1%) reported irAEs included hypothyroidism, blood thyroid stimulating hormone increased, thyroxine free decreased, tri-iodothyronine free decreased, hyperthyroidism, blood thyroid stimulating hormone decreased, thyroxine free increased, hyperglycaemia, rash, alanine aminotransferase increased and aspartate aminotransferase increased.
L1579: ## ***NPC population in Combination Part: AK105-304***
L1580: In patients treated with penpulimab 200mg Q3W in combination with cisplatin/carboplatin and gemcitabine in AK105-304, 146 (100.0%) patients had at least one TEAE. 132 (90.4%) patients had at least one \ge Grade 3 TEAE. The most commonly (the incidence of PT \ge 20%) reported TEAEs with a marginally higher incidence in the penpulimab group included aspartate aminotransferase increased, weight decreased, blood creatinine increased, anaemia, vomiting, hyponatraemia, hypoalbuminaemia, hypokalaemia, hypochloreaemia, pyrexia, COVID-19, cough and hypothyroidism. Common \ge Grade 3 TEAEs (with an incidence of \ge 2%) with a marginally higher incidence in the penpulimab group included lymphocyte count decreased, alanine aminotransferase increased, blood creatine phosphokinase increased, anaemia, pneumonia, COVID-19 pneumonia, hypokalaemia, hyponatraemia, hypocalcaemia, hypomagnesaemia, nausea, hypertension and disease progression.
L1581: In patients treated with penpulimab 200mg Q3W in combination with cisplatin/carboplatin and gemcitabine in AK105-304, 48 (32.9%) subjects in penpulimab group and 48 (33.8%) subjects in placebo group died. 2 (1.4%) and 3 (2.1%) subjects died within 30 days after the last dose; 6 (4.1%) and 3 (2.1%) subjects died within 31 to 90 days after the last dose; 40 (27.4%) and 42 (29.6%) subjects died more than 90 days after the last dose in penpulimab group and placebo group, respectively.
L1582: ## Penpulimab
L1583: In penpulimab group, 7 (4.8%) patients had AEs that resulted in death, regardless of causality. Excluding disease progression, AEs with an outcome of death were reported in 3 (2.1%) patients: death in 1 patient; COVID-19 pneumonia in 1 patient; cardiac failure in 1 patient.
L1584: In penpulimab group, 74 patients (50.7%) experienced at least one SAE. The most commonly (the incidence of PT \ge 1%) reported SAEs (excluding disease progression) included platelet count decreased, white blood cell count decreased, neutrophil count decreased, blood creatinine increased, anaemia, febrile neutropenia, myelosuppression, pneumonia, upper respiratory tract infection, COVID-19 pneumonia, urinary tract infection, asthenia, epistaxis, hyponatraemia, diabetic ketoacidosis, arrhythmia and nausea.
L1585: In penpulimab group, the incidence of AEs leading to permanent discontinuation was 3.4% (5/146). Drug-related AE leading to permanent discontinuation occurred in 4 (2.7%) patients, including platelet count decreased, lipase increased, brain oedema, acute myocardial infarction, diabetic ketoacidosis, and hyperkalaemia.
L1586: 45 of 146 (30.8%) patients had at least one irAE. The most commonly (the incidence of PT \ge 1%) reported irAEs included hypothyroidism, blood thyroid stimulating hormone increased, triiodothyronine free decreased, hyperthyroidism, diabetic ketoacidosis, hyperglycaemia, rash, pruritus and dermatitis acneiform.
L1587: In addition, compared to penpulimab monotherapy, penpulimab combined with chemotherapy did not increase the incidence of irAEs.
L1588: Changes in laboratory parameters were predominantly Grade 1 or 2 in severity, and the incidence of any grade worsening to Grade 3 or 4 was generally low. Laboratory abnormalities reported as AEs infrequently resulted in discontinuation of penpulimab, suggesting a tolerable safety profile.
L1589: The safety profile was generally not affected by age, [REDACTED] and other factors (eg, ECOG performance status).
L1590: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1591: FDA conducted the safety review of penpulimab in combination with cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine in 146 patients with locally recurrent, unresectable or metastatic NPC who received penpulimab 200 mg Q3W in combination with cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine in the AK105-304 trial. For penpulimab monotherapy, FDA conducted the safety review in 130 patients with locally recurrent or metastatic NPC whose disease progressed on or after platinum-containing chemotherapy and an additional line of therapy who received penpulimab 200 mg Q2W as a single agent in the AK105-202 trial and in a pooled dataset of 372 patients with various solid tumors or cHL who received penpulimab 200 mg Q2W as a single agent. FDA's review of deaths due to AEs in AK105-304, AK105-202, and the pooled safety dataset did not identify unexpected safety signals relative to other anti-PD-(L)1 antibodies.
L1592: ## Penpulimab
L1593: Discontinuation of penpulimab due to AEs occurred in 3.4% of patients in AK105-304, and in 3.8% and 5.9% of patients in the AK105-202 trial and the safety pooled dataset, respectively. In AK105-304, AK105-202, and the safety pooled dataset there were no AEs leading to discontinuation that were \ge 1\%. In AK105-304 one patient each discontinued penpulimab as a result of Grade 3-4 acute myocardial infarction, AKI, brain edema, DKA, lipase increased, and platelet count decreased. In AK105-202 one patient each discontinued penpulimab due to Grade 3-4 herpes zoster disseminated, pleural effusion, rash, spinal cord compression, and transaminases increased. For the pooled safety population 3 patients discontinued for any grade pneumonitis, 2 for pleural effusion, 2 for GI hemorrhage, and one each for herpes zoster disseminated, rash, spinal cord compression, transaminases increased, ascites, colitis, pelvic infection, septic shock, cholangitis, hemophagocytic lymphohistiocytosis, hypersensitivity, cerebral infarction, epilepsy, mesangioproliferative glomerulonephritis, and renal injury.
L1594: Adverse events leading to penpulimab dose interruptions occurred in 64%, 25%, and 26% of patients in AK105-304, AK105-202, and the pooled safety dataset, respectively. In AK105-304, the most frequent AEs leading to dose interruptions (\ge 2\%) were Covid19, anemia, thrombocytopenia, white blood cell count decreased, neutrophil count decreased, COVID 19 pneumonia, pneumonia, ALT increased, hyponatremia, AST increased, asymptomatic COVID 19, creatinine increased, pyrexia, and URI. In AK105-202, the most frequent AEs leading to dose interruptions (\ge 1\%) were pneumonia, anemia, hypothyroidism, hepatic function abnormal, pneumonitis, rash, and ALT increased. Amongst the pooled safety set these plus AST increased, lung infection, and diarrhea occurred in \ge 1\%.
L1595: Serious adverse reactions events (SAEs) occurred in 51%, 22%, and 27% of patients in AK105-304, AK105-202, and the pooled safety dataset, respectively. In AK105-304, the most frequent SAEs (\ge 2\%) were platelet count decreased, neutrophil count decreased, wbc count decreased, anemia, myelosuppression, COVID19 pneumonia, pneumonia, hyponatremia, and nausea. In AK105-202, the most frequent SAEs (\ge 2\%) was only pneumonia while in the pooled safety set no SAE was \ge 2\%.
L1596: In AK105-304, the most frequent irAEs (\ge 2\%) were hypothyroidism, TSH increased, hyperthyroidism, and rash. For AK105-202 and the pooled safety set the most frequent irAEs (\ge 2\%) were hypothyroidism, rash, hyperthyroidism, and TSH increased. On FDA review one patient in AK105-304 may have died from SIADH while on AK105-202 one patient may have died from pneumonitis, one from hepatitis, one from colitis, and one from septic shock.
L1597: Infusion-related reactions (IRRs) occurred in 3.4% and 10.2% of patients in AK105-304 and the pooled safety dataset, respectively. All IRRs were Grades 1 or 2, except for 2 grade 3-4 IRR in the pooled safety dataset. In AK105-304, the most frequent AEs (\ge 20\%) were nausea, vomiting, constipation, hypothyroidism, decreased appetite, weight decreased, cough, Covid19, fatigue, pyrexia, and rash. In AK105-202 the most frequent AEs (\ge 20\%) were hypothyroidism and musculoskeletal pain.
L1598: In AK105-304, the most frequent Grade 3 to 4 laboratory abnormalities (\ge 2\%) were wbc count decreased, hemoglobin decreased, lymphocyte count decreased, neutrophil count decreased, magnesium decreased, sodium decreased, triglycerides increased, AST increased, cholesterol increased, potassium increased, ALT increased, lipase increased, and serum amylase increased. In AK105-202 the most frequent Grade 3 to 4 laboratory abnormalities (\ge 2\%) were phosphate decreased, sodium decreased, triglycerides increased, alkaline phosphatase increased, AST increased, GGT increased, ALT increased, and hemoglobin decreased.
L1599: The safety profile of penpulimab was similar to other PD-(L)1 blocking antibodies when combined with platinum-based chemotherapy or administered as a monotherapy. The most significant adverse reactions include immune-related adverse events, which are adequately addressed by the information in the Warnings and Precautions section included in product labeling. There were no significant safety concerns identified during BLA review requiring risk management beyond labeling or warranting consideration for Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategy (REMS). Penpulimab, in combination with cisplatin or gemcitabine or as a monotherapy, appears to have an acceptable safety profile when assessed in the context of a life-threatening disease with unmet medical need.
L1600: ## 8.3. Statistical Issues
L1601: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1602: There were no major statistical issues identified during review of the application. The marketing application was based on results from two studies, Study AK105-304 and Study AK105-202. Study AK105-202 was conducted entirely in China, while 96% of patients enrolled in Study AK105-304 were from China. FDA was not able to examine the consistency of the treatment benefit by geographical location, race and ethnicity, or in a patient population that are more representative of the population of U.S. patients with NPC. As described in Section 1 and Section 8.1.5, given the high unmet need and the fact that only one other FDA-approved immunotherapy exists for NPC in the U.S., a flexible regulatory approach is applied in this case.
L1603: For Study AK105-304, as of the data cut-off date of April 29, 2024, the primary endpoint of PFS per BICR was statistically significant at the PFS interim analysis based on 85% information fraction. Of note, as the interim analysis of PFS was originally proposed at 70% information fraction, this interim analysis at 85% information fraction was based on an additional 34 PFS events. The review team considered the overrun of PFS events and the lower-than-expected patient accrual outside of Asia to be acceptable for this particular marketing application as the overrun is mainly due to the difficulties of enrollment outside of Asia. See FDA's assessment under Section 1.3 and 8.1 for "Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments" for additional details. Similar to PFS, the first interim analysis of OS was conducted at 70% information fraction,
L1604: instead of the prespecified 60% information fraction, resulting in an additional 18 OS events. OS was not statistically significant at the first interim analysis, and thus OS will be formally tested at the second interim analysis at 80% information fraction and, if necessary, at the final analysis when 172 deaths are observed.
L1605: There were no major statistical issues identified during the review of Study Ak105-202. Due to the single arm design, no statistical inferential procedures were applied, and the efficacy decision was based on the lower limit of a 95% confidence interval of the response rate in the trial to exceed a clinically relevant response rate with adequate duration of response. The ORR in this pre-treated patient population was clinically relevant, and the responses were durable. In general, the treatment benefit appeared to be consistent across all relevant subgroups. For this study, the time-to-event endpoints of PFS and OS were considered to be descriptive due to a lack of comparator, while the endpoint of DCR was considered to be exploratory in nature.
L1606: ## 8.4. Conclusions and Recommendations
L1607: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1608: The demonstration of a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in PFS favoring the penpulimab plus chemotherapy arm in AK105-304, along with a longer median DOR in the penpulimab plus chemotherapy arm vs the placebo plus chemotherapy arm and no evidence of a detrimental effect on OS, provides substantial evidence of effectiveness for penpulimab, at 200 mg IV Q3W in combination with cisplatin or carboplatin and gemcitabine, for the first-line treatment of adults with metastatic or with recurrent, locally advanced nasopharyngeal carcinoma (NPC). The indication for the treatment of adult patients with metastatic NPC of nonkeratinizing histology with disease progression on or after platinum containing chemotherapy and at least one other line of therapy is supported by Study AK105-202. The ORR was 28% (95% CI: 20, 37) and median DOR was not reached. The proportion of patients with observed DOR of at least 12 months was 46%. The results of AK105-202 are considered clinically meaningful for the intended patient population, and the results of the AK105-304 study provide further confidence in the benefit of penpulimab for the treatment of advanced NPC.
L1609: The overall safety profile of penpulimab was similar to that observed with other PD-(L)1 blocking antibodies. Safety issues identified as significant and serious during the BLA review were immune-related adverse events and infusion-related reactions, which are adequately addressed by the information in the Warnings and Precautions section included in product labeling. There were no significant safety concerns identified during BLA review requiring risk management beyond labeling or warranting consideration for Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategy (REMS). Penpulimab appears to have an acceptable safety profile when assessed in the context of a life-threatening disease with unmet medical need.
L1610: Section 21 CFR 314.106(b) outlines three requirements to establish the acceptability of foreign data as the sole basis for marketing approval:
L1611: - The foreign data are applicable to the U.S. population and U.S. medical practice.
- The studies have been performed by clinical investigators of recognized competence.
- FDA is able to validate the data through an onsite inspection or other appropriate means.
L1612: FDA will apply this policy in a flexible manner according to the nature of the drug and data being considered.
L1613: While both AK105-304 and AK105-202 were conducted nearly or entirely exclusively in Asia, a flexible regulatory approach is warranted given the high unmet need and lack of available therapies for US patients with NPC. The comparator arm of cisplatin or carboplatin plus gemcitabine used in the AK105-304 study is considered standard of care for the first-line treatment of NPC in the US and is therefore applicable to US medical practice. The incidence of NPC is much higher in Southeast Asia relative to the United States, where NPC is a relatively rare disease, making enrollment to clinical trials more feasible in that region of the world. While a higher proportion of patients with NPC in the US have keratinizing histology compared to patients in China (25% vs 2%), the majority of patients with NPC even in the US have non-keratinizing histology. The company attempted to enroll patients in the US but with little success and thus had only 2 cases in AK105-304 (one on the interventional and one on the placebo arm). Due to this very limited number of patients evaluated with keratinizing histology, and an absence of data for penpulimab as a single agent for patients with keratinizing NPC, the FDA narrowed the indication to locally advanced, unresectable or metastatic non-keratinizing NPC. AK105-202 enrolled only patients with non-keratinizing NPC.
L1614: To further address the issue of applicability to the US population, the Applicant should collect additional clinical data to further characterize the safety and efficacy of penpulimab in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine for the treatment of NPC in a study population whose demographic and disease characteristics are more representative of the population of U.S. patients with NPC (refer to Section 13).
L1615: In addition, clinical site inspections were completed and Study AK105-304 appears to have been conducted adequately and the data generated by the inspected clinical investigators and the imaging CRO and submitted by the Applicant appear acceptable in support of the proposed indication.
L1616: The clinical data supports a positive risk-benefit assessment and is supportive of regular approval to penpulimab for the following indications:
L1617: ## Penpulimab
L1618: • Penpulimab is indicated, in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine, for the first-line treatment of adults with metastatic or with recurrent, locally advanced non-keratinizing nasopharyngeal carcinoma (NPC).
L1619: • Penpulimab is indicated, as a single agent, for the treatment of adults with recurrent unresectable or metastatic NPC with disease progression on or after a platinum-containing chemotherapy and one additional line of therapy who have not received prior therapy with an anti-PD-(L)1 antibody.

L1645: ## 12. Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategies (REMS)
L1646: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1647: The risks of penpulimab could be managed by routine pharmacovigilance activities and labeling. REMS is not necessary to ensure that the benefits outweigh the risk for penpulimab for the following reasons:
L1648: ## Penpulimab
L1649: - The benefit of penpulimab in the treatment of NPC was demonstrated in the pivotal study of AK105-202 and AK105-304.
- The risks observed with penpulimab will be communicated through labeling. The risks associated with penpulimab are in part due to its inhibitory effects of PD-1. If approved, penpulimab will belong to a class with many other drugs (nivolumab, pembrolizumab, atezolizumab, durvalumab and avelumab, etc.) in which irAEs are labeled in Warnings and Precautions. The prescribers are likely aware of the risks of irAEs and the management with other drugs in this class.
- Sponsor has robust pharmacovigilance system, which includes data intake, case management, regulatory reporting, literature monitoring, signal management and aggregate reporting.
- In the postmarket setting, Akeso continually conducts routine surveillance of the safety profile of penpulimab including all reports of adverse drug experiences and product compliant reports, and revises RMP, RSI, and package insert if an evaluation of the safety data yields significant new information.
L1650: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1651: The clinical review team determined that a risk evaluation and mitigation strategy (REMS) was not required to ensure safe and effective use of penpulimab for the indicated populations. Recommendations for the safe and effective use of penpulimab are made in labeling and a patient package insert. There are no additional risk management strategies required beyond the recommended labeling. Although penpulimab can cause severe/serious toxicity, there are multiple other approved agents in the same class, and it will be prescribed by oncologists who, by training and experience, understand how to monitor and manage such toxicities.
L1652: ## 13. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
L1653: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1654: Two Post-Marketing Commitments were agreed upon with the Applicant, noted below.
L1655: ## 1. PMC
L1656: Complete the ongoing clinical trial AK105-304 (NCT04974398) titled "A Randomized, Double-blind, Multi-center Phase III Study of Penpulimab (AK105) combined with Chemotherapy versus Placebo Combined with Chemotherapy as First-Line Treatment for Recurrent or Metastatic Nasopharyngeal Carcinoma," to obtain mature overall survival (OS) data in accordance with the
L1657: pre-specified statistical analysis plan to formally test OS at 80% information fraction and at the final OS analysis when 172 OS events are observed.
L1658: Trial Completion: 05/2026
L1659: Final Report Submission: 10/2026
L1660: ## 2. PMC
L1661: Conduct a clinical trial enrolling an adequate number of patients in the United States (U.S.), that includes a sufficient representation of patients in demographic subgroups that are reflective of the U.S. patient population with nasopharyngeal carcinoma (NPC), to further characterize the efficacy and safety of penpulimab in combination with gemcitabine and either cisplatin or carboplatin in these patients. Conduct sparse sampling for supportive population pharmacokinetic and Exposure-Response (E-R) analyses. The E-R analyses may be used as supportive evidence for the efficacy and safety in the intended patient population. In the E-R analyses report, include an analysis of the presence and clinical impact of the neutralizing anti-drug antibodies on pharmacokinetics, efficacy, and safety of penpulimab.
L1662: Draft Protocol Submission: 04/2025
L1663: Final Protocol Submission: 08/2025
L1664: Trial Completion: 06/2029
L1665: Final Report Submission: 12/2029

L1708: ## 19.4. OCP Appendices (Technical Documents Supporting OCP Recommendations)
L1709: ## 19.4.1. Bioanalytical Review
L1710: FDA has determined that the bioanalytical methods used in the current submission are acceptable to support the clinical pharmacology program.
L1711: The concentration of penpulimab in human serum was quantitatively determined by enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay (ELISA). Bioanalysis for Study AK105-304 was conducted by (b) for samples collected outside of China whereas samples from all Chinese trials was analyzed by (b) (4). The method was identical and cross-validation was conducted by (b) (4). All methods were fully validated and adequate. All samples were analyzed within the demonstrated stability window, and all analytical runs met acceptance criteria.
L1712: ## 19.4.2. Population PK Analysis
L1713: ## 19.4.2.1. Executive Summary
L1714: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1715: The PK of penpulimab in the adult population was characterized by a 2-compartment model with first-order elimination. The population PK model described the observed data reasonably well.
L1716: The proposed dosages of 200 mg Q2W for monotherapy and 300 mg Q3W for combination therapy is considered acceptable for the respective proposed patient populations.
L1717: ## 19.4.2.2. PPK Assessment Summary
L1718: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1719: Penpulimab PPK model was based on pooled data from 8 clinical studies in patients with nasopharyngeal carcinoma, NSCLC, and other tumors. Potential covariates that may influence Penpulimab PK and its variability were also evaluated. A total of 5,849 PK concentrations from 771 patients were included in the PPK analysis.
L1720: ## Penpulimab
L1721: The final PPK model was a two-compartment with zero-order IV infusion and first-order elimination. The model indicated that baseline characteristics (body weight, C-reactive protein, albumin, lactate dehydrogenase), ADA, tumor type, co-administration, age, sex and race had statistically significant effects on PK parameters. However, these covariates did not have clinically meaningful effects on the steady-state exposure (\le 30\%). Also, mild to moderate hepatic impairment or mild to moderate renal impairment did not have significant effect on PK of Penpulimab.
L1722: Although Race was found to be correlated with clearance, the overall exposures (C_{max,ss}, C_{avg,ss} and C_{min,ss}) were essentially the same between Caucasian and Asian populations after taking into consideration of body weight.
L1723: According to PPK analysis, the mean (CV%) of volume of distribution was 10.2 L (43.5%), following administration of penpulimab 200 mg as a single IV infusion. The effective half-life and steady-state trough concentration were 22.4 days (27.4%) and 30.1 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} (38.1%), respectively, following IV infusion of 200 mg Q3W.

12.3 PK	
Penpulimab-kcqx exposure increased dose proportionally over the dose range of 1 to 10 mg/kg (0.35 to 3.5 times the approved recommended dosage in a 70 kg patient) after the first dose. The mean accumulation ratio was 1.5(b) for maximum concentration (Cmax) and 2.1(b) for area under the concentration curve (AUC) following multiple doses of 200 mg every 3 weeks. Steady-state concentrations of penpulimab-kcqx were reached by Week 15, with mean (CV%) steady-state trough and peak concentrations of 30(b) µg/mL (38(b)%) and 10(b) µg/mL (21(b)%), respectively.
Distribution
The mean (CV%) volume of distribution was 10(b) L (10(b)%).
Generally acceptable.
Reporting terminal half-life is preferred(b) given two dosages were approved.
L1728: ## Elimination
L1729: The mean (CV%) clearance was 0.2 (b) (4) L/day ((b) (4)%) and the mean (CV%) (b) (4)
L1730: ## Metabolism
L1731: Penpulimab-kcqx is expected to be metabolized into small peptides by catabolic pathways.
L1732: ## Specific Populations
L1733: No clinically significant differences in the pharmacokinetics of penpulimab-kcqx were observed based on body weight (32 to 113 kg), age (18 to 91 years), sex, race (White and Asian), serum albumin (22 to (b) (4) g/L), baseline LDH (111 to 4450 IU/L), baseline C-reactive protein (0 to 328 mg/L), ECOG score, immunogenicity status, tumor type, co-administration (with chemotherapy), mild or moderate renal impairment (creatinine clearance [CLcr] \ge 30 mL/min), mild (total bilirubin [TBIL] \le ULN and AST &gt; ULN or TBIL &gt; 1 to 1.5 times ULN and any AST) or moderate hepatic impairment (TBIL &gt; 1.5 to 3 times ULN and any AST). The effect of severe hepatic (TBIL &gt; 3 times ULN and any AST) or renal impairment (CLcr &lt; 30 mL/min) on the pharmacokinetics of penpulimab-kcqx is unknown.

L1767: ## 19.4.3. Exposure-Response Analysis
L1768: ## 19.4.3.1. E-R (Efficacy) Executive Summary
L1769: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1770: Positive E-R trends for ORR and PFS were observed across steady-state exposure with a fixed dosage of 200 mg Q3W, but this analysis is inconclusive due to the limited exposure range of penpulimab at one dose level.
L1771: ## **19.4.3.2. E-R (Efficacy) Assessment Summary**
L1772: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1773: Target indication: first-line treatment for recurrent or metastatic nasopharyngeal carcinoma (NPC). The E-R analysis included patients from the AK105-304 who received Penpulimab 200 mg Q3W in combination with chemotherapy (n=144). The univariate logistic regression analysis between Penpulimab C_{avg,ss} and ORR/DCR were explored. And Kaplan-Meier survival curve, stratified by quartiles of exposures, was applied to explore the association between exposure and progression-free survival (PFS). E-R analysis suggested a relative flat exposure-response (ORR/DCR) curve and no apparent difference in exposure between responders and non-responders.
L1774: [Highlight the key findings in the white cells.]
L1775: General Information
Goal of ER analysis	Characterize the relationships between Penpulimab serum exposures and key efficacy endpoints, including objective response rate (ORR) disease control rate (DCR), progression free survival (PFS), overall survival (OS), duration of response (DOR), and time to response (TTR)
Study Included	AK105-304
Endpoint	ORR, DCR, PFS, DOR TTR
No. of Patients (total, and with individual PK)	144 patients
Population Characteristics (Applicant – Table 38)	General	Age median (range): 51.0 yrs (31 ~75)
Weight median (range): 60.0 kg (32.0~101.6)
119 (82.6%) male
140 (97.2%) Asian
4 (2.8%) Caucasian
Pediatrics (if any)	Not applicable
Dose(s) Included	200mg Q3W
Exposure Metrics Explored (range)	Penpulimab exposure such as C_{avg,ss}, C_{min,ss}, were derived from PopPK analysis
Covariates Evaluated	The Kaplan-Meier survival curve, stratified by quartile of exposure, was applied to explore the correlation between PK exposure and efficacy endpoint (PFS) in the NPC patients. The effect of the following covariates on PFS was also explored: sex, age, weight, ECOG, C-reactive protein (CPR),
L1776: albmin (ALB), lactate dehydrogenase (LDH), Liver dysfunction groups (LDG), Kidney dysfunction groups (KDG), Anti-drug antibody (ADA), Neutralizing antibodies (NAB), Tumor stage (STAGE), Liver metastasis (LIVERMET).
Final Model Parameters	Summary	Acceptability
[FDA's comments]
Model Structure	
ORR and DCR
Univariate logistic regression model:
\log i t_i = \theta_1 + \theta_2(C_{avg,ss} - \text{Median}(C_{avg,ss}))P_i = \frac{e^{\text{Logit}_i}}{1 + e^{\text{Logit}_i}}
PFS, DOR and TTR
Kaplan-Meier Method: Plot survival curves for Progression-Free Survival (PFS), Duration of Response (DOR), and Time to Response (TTR) using the Kaplan-Meier method.
Yes.
Model Parameter Estimates	Applicant – Table 39	Yes.
Model Evaluation	
Associations were considered significant if the p-value was less than 0.05;
Applicant – Table 40
Applicant – Table 41
Yes.
Covariates and Clinical Relevance	
The exposure-efficacy response was evaluated by a univariate logistic regression model. ORR/DCR binary response was used as the dependent variable, and model-predicted Penpulimab exposure such as C_{min,ss}, C_{avg,ss} were used as independent variables to estimate the regression coefficients, confidence intervals, and significance levels.
The Kaplan-Meier survival curve, stratified by quartile of exposure, was applied to explore the correlation between PK exposure and PFS in the NPC patients. Exploratory analysis
Yes.
L1777: showed that covariates such as sex, age, weight, ECOG, CPR, ALB, LDH, LDG, KDG, ADA, NAB, STAGE, LIVERMET, didn not have significant effect on PFS.	
Simulation for Specific Population	Not applicable	
Visualization of E-R relationships	Applicant – Figure 12~Applicant – Figure 14
Applicant – Table 39	Yes.
Overall Clinical Relevance for ER	
ORR/DCR: In NPC patients, the logistic regression analysis suggested that there's a positive correlation between Penpulimab C_{avg,ss} and ORR (p < 0.05). Quartile analysis also indicated that ORR from Q1 was slightly lower than Q2-4. However, a boxplot of the ORR responder vs. non-responder showed that the PK exposure was comparable between the two groups.
The logistic regression analysis also showed a relative flat curve between Penpulimab C_{avg,ss} and DCR. Similar to ORR, the exposure between DCR responder vs. non-responder groups were comparable. Other exposure matrix, such as C_{max,ss} and C_{min,ss} showed the similar trends with ORR/DCR.
Since there's only one dose level in the E-E analysis, the relationships between Penpulimab and ORR/DCR may not be conclusive, and will be further evaluated when more data become available.
PFS: Kaplan-Meier survival curve, stratified by quartiles of exposures, was applied to explore the association between exposure and progression-free survival (PFS). The K-M curves showed that positive trend of median PFS increasing from 6.93 to 16.5 from Q1 to
Yes.
Positive E-R trend for ORR and PFS observed based on one dose level data should be interpreted with caution given the limited exposure range with only one dose level.
L1778: Q3 with median PFS for Q4 not being reached.
The C_{avg,ss} cutoff for Q1 was 36.4 \mug/mL. According to PopPK analysis, only 10% of population's exposure fell below 30.4 \mug/mL at 200 mg Q3W, indicating majority of the patients would achieve exposure above lowest quartile (Q2 –Q4), where median PFS are expected to be higher, compared to placebo. Similar trend was also observed in the K-M curve of C_{min,ss} and PFS.

L1810: ## 19.4.3.3. ER (Safety) Executive Summary
L1811: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1812: Across steady-state exposure over the dosing range from 1 to 10 mg/kg Q2W including 200 mg Q2W and 200 mg Q3W, the risk for Gr\ge3 TEAEs exhibits a flat trend, whereas the risk for immune related AEs (irAEs) exhibits a positive trend. The positive E-R trend does not appear clinically significant given the low incidences of interruptions (5.7%) and discontinuation (1.8%) due to irAEs.
L1813: ## 19.4.3.4. ER (Safety) Assessment Summary
L1814: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1815: The exposure-safety response was evaluated by a univariate logistic regression model. AE responses were used as the dependent variable, and model-predicted Penpulimab exposure such as Cmax,ss, Cavg,ss were used as independent variables to estimate the significance levels. In NPC population, Penpulimab (200 mg Q3W) in combination with chemotherapy, was safe and tolerable. E-R safety analysis indicated that no apparent correlation between exposure and incidence of TEAE/TRAЕ (Grade 3+) in the wide dose range (1 - 10 mg/kg Q2W) with either monotherapy or in combination therapy.
L1816: [Highlight the key findings in the white cells.]
L1817: General Information
Goal of ER analysis	Evaluate the Exposure-Safety (E-S) relationships of Penpulimab in monotherapy patients or all patients.
Study Included	Penpulimab monotherapy:
AK105-101, AK105-201, AK105-202, AK105-204
Penpulimab in combination with chemotherapy and others:
AK105-205, AK105-301, AK105-302, AK105-304
Population Included	Nasopharyngeal carcinoma, non-small cell lung cancer, solid tumors mainly including gastric cancer, ovarian cancer and esophageal cancer, hematologic malignancy R/R cHL.
Endpoint	Treatment-Emergent Adverse Event (TEAE):
Treatment-Related Adverse Event (TRAE):
CTCAE Grade 3 or Higher TEAE:
CTCAE Grade 3 or Higher TRAE:
Time to First Occurrence of TRAE:
Immune-Related Adverse Event (irAE):
Infusion-Related Reaction:
Drug-Related Serious Adverse Event (SAE):
L1818: TEAE of Special Interest: such as anaemia, WBC decrease, neutrophil count decrease, alanine aminotransferase increase, nausea, aspartate aminotransferase increase, platelet count decrease, hypothyroidism, vomiting, pyrexia, diarrhoea, hyponatraemia, rash, hypokalaemia, hypoesthesia, hypertriglyceridaemia, weight loss, cough, proteinuria, hyperuricaemia and fatigue.

No. of Patients (total, and with individual PK)	
A total of 771 patients received Penpulimab with monotherapy or combination therapy,
380 patients treated with Penpulimab monotherapy at doses of 1, 3, and 10 mg/kg Q2W or 200 mg Q2W.
391 patients were treated with Penpulimab 200 mg Q3W in combination with chemotherapy.

Covariates and Clinical Relevance	The exposure-safety response was evaluated by a univariate logistic regression model. Yes.
Simulation for Specific Population	Not applicable

Overall Clinical Relevance for ER	Monotherapy
A total of 380 patients received Penpulimab monotherapy at 1, 3, 10 mg/kg Q2W, or 200 mg Q2W. There's no significant dose or exposure-dependent increase in the incidence of TEAEs, TRAEs or CTCAE Grade3+ AEs, and drug-related SAEs. However, there's upper ward trend between PK exposure (C_{avg,ss}, C_{max,ss}) and irAE and the association with C_{avg,ss} being statistically significant.	Positive E-R trend observed for irAEs does not appear clinically significant given low incidence of interruption (5.7%) and discontinuation
L1820: Combination therapy
A total of 391 patients received Penpulimab 200 mg Q3W in combination with chemotherapy.
Similar to monotherapy, there was no significant increase in TEAE/IRAE (Grade 3+) with increasing of PK exposure, while association with irAE was found to be statistically significant.
For AEs with incidence rate of 10% or higher, white blood cell count decrease, hypothyroidism, rash and upper respiratory tract infection were associated with higher exposure, consistent with common adverse events observed in this population.
Overall, E-R safety analysis suggested a flat exposure-response relationships between Penpulimab exposure and most AEs in both monotherapy and combination therapy groups. However, higher exposure levels may be associated with a slightly increased risk of irAEs and some specific AEs.
(1.8%) due to irAEs.

L1854: ## **19.4.3.7. Overall Benefit-Risk Evaluation Based on E-R Analyses**
L1855: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1856: Penpulimab 200 mg Q3W demonstrated notable clinical benefits and tolerable safety profiles in NPC patients: the median PFS was 9.6 months (95% CI: 7.1, 12.5), compared to placebo of 7.0 months (95% CI: 6.9, 7.3). E-R analysis suggested a relative flat E-R (ORR/DCR) curve and no apparent difference in exposure between responders and non-responders. In NPC patients, Penpulimab (200 mg Q3W) in combination with chemotherapy was safe and tolerable. E-R safety analysis indicated that no apparent correlation between exposure and incidence of TEAE/TRAЕ (Grade 3+) in the wide dose range (1 - 10 mg/kg Q2W) with either monotherapy or in combination therapy.
L1857: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1858: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position. However, ER relationship for efficacy remains inconclusive due to the limited exposure range of penpulimab at one dose level.
L1859: ## 19.5. Additional Safety Analyses Conducted by FDA
L1860: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1861: FDA has no additional safety analyses to report.
L1862: ## 1. Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L1863: ## 1.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L1864: ## 1.1.1. Studies
L1865: ## 1.1.1.1. Study AK105-202
L1866: ## Trial Design
L1867: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1868: Study AK105-202 is a single-arm, open-label, multicenter phase II clinical study to evaluate the efficacy and safety of penpulimab in patients with metastatic nonkeratinizing NPC with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of therapy. Patients were excluded from the study based on history of autoimmune disease, medical conditions requiring systemic immunosuppression, prior organ or allogeneic stem cell transplantation, prior treatment with anti-PD-1, anti-PD-L1 or CTLA-4 antibodies, active central nervous system (CNS) metastases, or ECOG performance score &gt; 2.
L1869: Patients received penpulimab 200 mg intravenously every 2 weeks until disease progression as determined by the investigator, or unacceptable toxicity, or a maximum of 24 months, whichever occurred first. Administration of penpulimab was permitted beyond RECIST-defined disease progression if the patients were assessed as clinically beneficial and tolerated to the study drug by the investigator. Tumor measurements were performed every 8 weeks in first year and every 12 weeks thereafter, to determine response by RECIST v1.1 criteria. The primary objective of the study was to evaluate the objective response rate (ORR) assessed by blinded independent radiology review committee (IRRC) per RECIST v1.1. Secondary objectives included duration of response (DoR), progression-free survival (PFS) and overall survival (OS).
L1870: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1871: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the trial design for Study AK105-202. FDA clarifies that the study was conducted at multiple sites in China. Since Study AK105-202 is a single arm trial, FDA does not use any inferential procedures to evaluate the trial results. The efficacy decision is based on the lower limit of a 95% confidence interval of the response rate in the trial to exceed a clinically relevant response rate with adequate duration of response. Additionally, time-to-event endpoints such as PFS and OS are not interpretable in a single arm trial due to a lack of comparator and their corresponding analyses are considered as descriptive.
L1872: ## Study Endpoints
L1873: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1874: - Primary Efficacy Endpoint: ORR assessed by IRRC per RECIST v1.1 in the Full Analysis Set (FAS).
ORR is defined as the proportion of patients whose best overall response (BOR) is confirmed CR or PR. The confirmation of CR or PR by repeated imaging assessments is performed not less than 28 days (4 weeks) after the first observation of a CR or PR, and there is no evidence of PD between the follow-up of the first observed CR or PR and the follow-up for confirmation of CR or PR.
- Key Secondary Efficacy Endpoints: duration of response (DoR)
- Other Secondary Endpoints:Efficacy: ORR assessed by investigator, Disease control rate (DCR), time to response (TTR), PFS assessed by IRRC and investigator per RECIST v1.1 and OS
- Pharmacokinetic (PK) and immunogenicity assessment
- Safety: incidence and severity of adverse events, abnormal laboratory findings with clinical significance.
- Correlation analysis between the expression of EBV-DNA in blood and the anti-tumor activity of penpulimab.
L1875: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1876: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the study endpoints. As noted in the previous section, time-to-event endpoints such as PFS and OS are not interpretable in a single arm study, and their corresponding analyses are considered descriptive.
L1877: ## Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
L1878: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1879: The sample size calculation was based on the primary endpoint of ORR. A total of 110 patients were needed for an ORR of 15% to be excluded using the lower limit of a two-sided 95% CI. If an ORR of 26% is assumed for penpulimab, there is 82% chance of observing at least 25 responses (an ORR of 22.7%), with the lower limit of the 95% CI excluding a historical response rate of 15%. Considering an attrition rate of 15%, a total sample size of 130 was needed to ensure at least 110 evaluable patients. In this study, an interim analysis was originally planned after at least 110 patients were enrolled and completed at least 16 weeks of follow-up (an interval between the data cutoff date and the date of first dose \ge 16 weeks), and the results of the interim analysis was planned to be used for communicating with the regulatory agencies. There were no plans to stop the trial based on either of these analyses and no formal statistical tests were planned.
L1880: An interim analysis of efficacy was conducted after 110 patients treated with penpulimab had an opportunity to be followed for at least 8 months prior to data cutoff date (to allow at least 6
L1881: months follow-up past onset of response assuming the response occurs at the first follow-up scan).
L1882: The study was completed and the final analysis was conducted at 12 months after the last subject was first dosed were presented below.
L1883: ## Definition of Key Analysis Sets
L1884: ## - Enrollment Analysis Set [ENR]
L1885: The ENR includes all patients who have signed the ICF and been enrolled into the study.
L1886: ## - Primary Efficacy Population
L1887: The Primary Efficacy Population includes all patients in the ENR who have received at least one dose of penpulimab and meet the following inclusion criteria:
L1888: - Patients diagnosed with disease stage of IVb in accordance with Chinese Nasopharyngeal Carcinoma Staging 2017 (2008 Expert Consensus on Nasopharyngeal Cancer Staging Revision) at enrollment.
- Patients who had disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of therapy. Prior neoadjuvant chemotherapy, concurrent chemoradiotherapy, or adjuvant chemotherapy would be considered as the first line of therapy if recurrence/metastasis occurs within 6 months since the last dose of chemotherapy. Switching treatment regimen due to intolerability were not counted as additional lines of therapy.
- Patients with at least one measurable lesion at baseline per RECIST v1.1 as assessed by IRRC if efficacy summary based on IRRC assessment, or at least one measurable lesion at baseline per RECIST v1.1 as assessed by the investigator if efficacy summary based on investigator assessment.
- Patients who had an opportunity to be followed for at least 8 months prior to data cutoff date (to allow at least 6 months follow-up past onset of response assuming the response occurs at the first follow-up scan).
L1889: ## - Safety Analysis Set
L1890: The Safety Analysis Set includes all enrolled patients who have received at least one dose of penpulimab.
L1891: ## - Pharmacokinetics Set [PKS]
L1892: The PKS includes all enrolled patients in the safety analysis set who have at least one quantifiable concentration data at the scheduled time point and no major and critical protocol violations affecting the concentration data.
L1893: ## - Immunogenicity Set [IMMS]
L1894: The IMMS includes all enrolled patients for safety analysis who have received at least one immunogenicity assessment at baseline and post-baseline, respectively.

L1898: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1899: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the statistical analysis plan. In protocol version 3.0 dated July 6, 2020, the Applicant had stated that the primary analysis of ORR would be conducted once the last enrolled patient had completed at least 24 weeks of follow-up. The primary analysis was conducted based on a data cut-off date of February 3, 2021. An updated analysis of ORR was submitted on January 6, 2025, based on the data cut-off date of September 23, 2021, to provide additional follow-up from onset of response for patients who had a confirmed radiographic response.
L1900: Between March 27, 2019 and September 14, 2020, the study had enrolled 130 patients. FDA's efficacy analysis for Study AK105-202 was based on 125 patients who met the definition of the primary efficacy population, based on the data cut-off date of September 23, 2021. There were five enrolled patients who were not included in the primary efficacy analysis population. One of those patients had only one prior line of systemic therapy, and the other four patients did not have measurable disease at baseline per IRRC assessment.

L1948: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1949: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of demographic characteristics based on the 125 patients included in the primary efficacy population.
L1950: FDA notes that the male predominance of the study population is consistent with the U.S. patient population with NPC. Given that all patients in the efficacy population were of Chinese ethnicity, the efficacy population do not reflect the racial and ethnic demographics of the U.S. patient population with NPC. FDA was not able to examine the consistency of the treatment benefit by geographical location, race and ethnicity, or in a patient population that is more representative of the population of U.S. patients with NPC. A postmarketing commitment is being issued to obtain more data on penpulimab in patients with NPC in the U.S.
L1951: Demographic characteristics for the 128 patients in the investigator-assessed population were not independently verified by the FDA.

L1981: Subgroup analyses based on IRRC assessment showed that tumor response was observed across all subgroups (with exception for age &gt; 65 years [n=2]). While numerical differences in point estimates across some subgroups were noted, sample sizes were small and 95% CIs overlapped for most subgroup comparisons. In the subgroups, the patients with positive PD-L1 expression (defined as TPS\ge 50% using Shuwen SAB-028), baseline LDH &lt; ULN or EBV-DNA level in peripheral blood &lt; 500 IU/mL at baseline seems to have a higher response rate. It was also noticed that the ORRs in key subgroups were comparable, such as [REDACTED], prior line of therapy, patients with liver metastasis, disease status (primary or recurrent metastasis).
