L1987: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1988: Results based on an interim analysis of ORR demonstrate substantial evidence of effectiveness of penpulimab monotherapy in patients with metastatic nonkeratinizing NPC with disease progression on or after at least two prior lines of therapy, a serious condition where there is a
L1989: clear unmet medical need. The primary analysis showed promising ORR by IRRC in the Primary Efficacy Population. The confirmed ORR of 28.0% (95% CI: 20.3%, 36.7%) compares favorably against a historical overall response rate of &lt; 10% reported with chemotherapy agents.
L1990: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1991: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the primary efficacy analysis of the 125 patients included in the primary efficacy population for ORR per IRRC. Note that DCR is considered to be an exploratory endpoint and hence, the corresponding analysis was not verified by the FDA. There is a substantial numerical difference in ORR between the TPS \ge 50% (43%, (95% CI: 29, 59)) and the TPS &lt; 50% (20%, (95% CI: 11, 30)) subgroups. Given the exploratory nature of the subgroup analyses, the corresponding results should be interpreted with caution.
L1992: Additionally, in "Applicant – Figure 1 Forest Plot of Objective Response Rate (ORR) Based on RECIST 1.1 per IRRC by Subgroup Factors -- Primary Efficacy Population" the Applicant has mislabeled the subgroup analysis by prior lines of therapy, and instead had provided analysis by "Number of location of metastasis" twice. Among the 79 patients who received two prior lines of systemic therapy, ORR per IRRC was 30% (95% CI: 21, 42), whereas among the 46 patients who received at least three prior lines of therapy the ORR per IRRC was 24% (95% CI: 13, 39).
L1993: ## **Data Quality and Integrity**
L1994: ## **Data:**
L1995: **None.**
L1996: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1997: The submission contained all the required components of the electronic Common Technical Document (eCTD) and was of adequate quality to allow for review of the clinical trial data supporting the proposed indication.
L1998: The quality assurance procedures were implemented during the conduct of the study including but not limited to regular site monitoring, conducting investigator meetings and training sessions for clinical research associates, and site initiation visits to review the clinical protocol, CRF, and study procedures. Additionally, independent Quality Assurance audits were conducted for selected sites, and no concerns on data integrity were reported following site inspections by the independent Quality Assurance audits.
L1999: **The FDA's Assessment:** No major data quality or integrity issues were identified during the review of this application.
L2000: ## **Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints**
L2001: ## **Data:**
L2002: ## **DoR and TTR**
L2003: The median TTR in the primary efficacy population was 1.84 months (range: 1.6 to 7.4 months). As of the data cutoff date, the longest durable response was 24.9+ months (ongoing), 57.1% of responders remained ongoing, and the median DoR has not reached. The DoR \ge 6 months was 72.7% (24/33) at the data cutoff. The Kaplan-Meier estimate for the percent of patients with a 6-month duration of response is 88.0% (95% CI: 71.2%, 95.3%), and the estimate for the percent of patients with a 9-month duration of response is 69.8% (95% CI: 51.2%, 82.5%).
L2004: A summary of DoR and TTR in the primary efficacy population is presented below in Applicant – Table 7.

L2015: ## **PFS**
L2016: The median PFS based on IRRC assessment in the primary efficacy population was 3.65 months. The 6-month PFS rate was 41.3% (95% CI: 32.2%, 50.2%) and the 12-month PFS rate was 24.2% (95% CI: 16.7%, 32.6%).

L2025: ## Overall survival (OS)
L2026: The median OS in the safety analysis set was 22.74 months (95% CI: 17.1, NE). The 6-month OS rate was 80.6% (95% CI: 72.4%, 86.6%), and the 12-month OS rate was 66.4% (95% CI: 57.2%, 74.0%).

L2036: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2037: Results based on the final analysis of additional secondary efficacy variables including DoR, PFS and OS demonstrate substantial evidence of effectiveness of penpulimab monotherapy in patients with metastatic nonkeratinizing NPC with disease progression on or after at least two prior lines of therapy, a serious disease where there is a clear unmet medical need.
L2038: The responses were durable. After a median follow-up time of 22.1 months, median DoR has not yet been reached at the time of DCO, and 20 (57.1%) of 35 responders had an ongoing response. The OS results were encouraging in this heavily pretreated NPC population. The median OS was 22.74 months and the 12-month OS rate was 66.4% (95% CI: 57.2%, 74.0%). The percentage of DoR \ge 6 months, median OS, and the 12-month OS rate compare favorably against those seen with chemotherapy agents.
L2039: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2040: FDA's analysis of DOR assessed by IRRC is provided in the table below. The median DOR was not reached. The proportion of patients with observed DOR of at least 6 months was 83%. In addition to the landmark DOR rates provided by the Applicant, that were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier method, FDA calculated the landmark DOR rates based on observed response duration.

L2046: ## Assessment Aid Addendum for Study AK105-202 - BLA 761258
L2047: ## Penpulimab
L2048: Source: FDA analysis based on Applicant-provided analysis datasets adrs.xpt and adtte.xpt. DCO: September 23, 2021
L2049: Note that, in a single arm study such as AK105-202, time-to-event endpoints such as PFS and OS are considered descriptive only due to the absence of a comparator arm. Hence, FDA considers PFS and OS as descriptive endpoints for Study AK105-202. Additionally, the Applicant provided analysis of OS in “Applicant – Table 9” is based on 130 enrolled patients, which is not the efficacy population under consideration.
L2050: ## Dose/Dose Response
L2051: Data:
L2052: N/A.
L2053: The Applicant's Position:
L2054: All patients with NPC in the primary efficacy population received penpulimab 200 mg every two weeks. Therefore, dose response relationships for efficacy could not be evaluated. Univariate logistic regression analyses showed a flat relationship between penpulimab PK exposures and model-predicted ORR (CR/PR).
L2055: The FDA's Assessment:
L2056: The FDA agrees that a dose-response relationship is not evaluable given that all patients
L2057: received the Q2W dosing regimen of penpulimab.
L2058: ## **Durability of Response**
L2059: ## **Data and Applicant's Position:**
L2060: A summary of DoR in the primary efficacy population is presented earlier (see Applicant – Table 7). Given refractory population with metastatic NPC with disease progression on or after at least two prior lines of therapy, the responses were durable, which compare favorably with those seen with historical commonly used chemotherapy agents.
L2061: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2062: Please refer to the section titled “Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints” for FDA’s assessment of durability of response.
L2063: ## **Persistence of Effect**
L2064: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2065: The observed effect of ORR in the primary efficacy population is consistent across pre-specified subgroups as illustrated in Applicant – Table 7. Notably, patients with positive PD-L1 status exhibited substantially higher response rates compared to the overall average. Secondly, as highlighted in the previous section, the responses are durable, with more than half of responders (20 out of 35) maintaining an ongoing response at a median follow-up of 22.1 months.
L2066: In summary, patients experienced sustained clinical benefits with penpulimab monotherapy, demonstrating persistent efficacy across key measures.
L2067: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2068: The FDA agrees that there may potentially be persistence of effect based on the DOR data and that higher response rates was observed in the PDL1 \ge50% population. However, as above, the secondary endpoints of PFS and OS as this is a single arm study and these results are considered descriptive only.

L2133: ## 1. Executive Summary
L2134: ## 1.1. Product Introduction
L2135: Penpulimab is a human IgG1 monoclonal antibody targeting PD-1, thereby blocking the immunosuppressive interaction between PD-1 and PD-L1 and PD-L2. It is not approved for any indication in the U.S.
L2136: The recommended dosage regimen for the proposed indication is penpulimab 200 mg intravenously (IV) every two weeks until disease recurrence or unacceptable toxicity.
L2137: ## 1.2. Conclusions on the Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
L2138: Data from Study AK105-202, a single arm, open label study of penpulimab in patients with metastatic non-keratinizing NPC who had received prior therapy, conducted entirely in China, was provided as the primary evidence in support of the proposed indication. A total of 130 patients with metastatic non-keratinizing NPC, who had progressed on platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other line of therapy, were enrolled and received penpulimab 200 mg every 2 weeks (Q2W). The primary efficacy outcome measure was overall response rate (ORR) by Independent Radiologic Review Committee (IRRC) per RECIST v1.1. The key secondary endpoint was duration of response (DOR).
L2139: Amongst the 111 patients in the primary efficacy population, the ORR was 30% (95% CI 21, 39) (data cutoff date of February 3, 2021). The median DOR was not reached (range 3.8, 17.1+); a total of 39% of the 33 responding patients had a DOR \ge 9 months.
L2140: During FDA's review of this BLA, FDA toripalimab-tpzi, an anti-PD-1 antibody, in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine was granted traditional approval for the first-line treatment of adult patients with metastatic or recurrent, locally advanced NPC based on a randomized trial which demonstrated a 3.7-month improvement in progression-free survival (PFS) with the addition of toripalimab-tpzi to cisplatin and gemcitabine. This approval changed the standard of care for patients with metastatic or recurrent, locally advanced NPC with the inclusion of anti-PD-1-based therapy in the first-line treatment setting. At the time of this approval, toripalimab-tpzi was also approved as a single agent for patients with unresectable or metastatic NPC with disease progression on or after a platinum-containing chemotherapy based on ORR and DOR results from a single arm study of patients with metastatic NPC (ORR 21% (95% CI 15, 28); median DOR 14.9 months (95% CI 10.3, not estimable)).
L2141: Given the approvals of toripalimab-tpzi for patients with NPC, the efficacy of penpulimab, based on the results of Study AK105-202, does not demonstrate a clear advantage over currently available therapeutic options for patients with metastatic, non-keratinizing NPC.
L2142: According to the U.S. Code of Federal Regulations (CFR), Section 21 314.500, new drug products that have been studied for their safety and effectiveness in treating serious or life-threatening
L2143: illnesses and that provide meaningful therapeutic benefit to patients over existing treatments (e.g., ability to treat patients unresponsive to, or intolerant of, available therapy, or improved patient response over available therapy), may be considered for accelerated approval. This regulation is generally considered applicable to original biologic products in addition to new drug products.
L2144: In addition, following the pre-license inspection (PLI) of Akeso Biopharma, Co., Ltd., Guangdong, China (FEI 3017057933), deficiencies in CGMP operations were identified in penpulimab drug substance and drug product manufacturing facility listed in this application. FDA has reviewed the responses from the facility and concluded that not all deficiencies have been satisfactorily resolved.
L2145: Further, during the review of this application, the Office of Pharmaceutical Quality (OPQ) identified various deficiencies related to the cell bank system used to manufacture penpulimab, process control strategy, validation of analytical procedures and drug product filling operation. After reviewing the Applicant's responses, OPQ concluded that the data submitted in this application are not sufficient to support a conclusion that the manufacture of penpulimab-kcqx is well-controlled and will lead to a product that is pure and potent.
L2146: The FDA recommendation is Complete Response.
L2147: ## 1.3. Benefit-Risk Assessment (BRA)
L2148: ## Benefit-Risk Summary and Assessment
L2149: NPC is a rare, life-threatening disease with distinct geographical distribution and histologies. In 2018, approximately 129,000 new cases of NPC were diagnosed globally (0.7% of all cancers diagnosed in 2018) (Chen Lancet 2019). There are three pathological subtypes of NPC: keratinizing squamous cell carcinoma (type 1), non-keratinizing differentiated carcinoma (type 2), and non-keratinizing undifferentiated carcinoma (type 3). Types 2 and 3 are typically associated with Epstein Barr virus (EBV), and account for 97% of NPC cases diagnosed in China. In North America, Type 1 and Type 3 NPC account for 25% and 63% of cases respectively.
L2150: The prognosis for patients with recurrent, unresectable, or metastatic NPC is poor. On October 27, 2023, FDA approved toripalimab-tpzi with cisplatin and gemcitabine for the first-line treatment of adults with metastatic or recurrent, locally advanced NPC. In the randomized, double-blind JUPITER-O2 trial, toripalimab-tpzi with cisplatin and gemcitabine demonstrated a median progression-free survival (PFS) of 11.7 months and a median overall survival (OS) not yet reached. Patients who received placebo with cisplatin and gemcitabine had a median PFS of 8.0 months and median OS of 33.7 months (LOQTORZI [toripalimab-tpzi] USPI). On October 27, 2023, FDA also approved toripalimab-tpzi as a single agent for adult patients with recurrent unresectable or metastatic NPC with disease progression on or after a platinum-containing therapy based on results of POLARIS-O2, an open-label, multicohort trial, which demonstrated an overall response rate (ORR) as assessed by a Blinded Independent Review Committee (BIRC) of 21% and median duration of response (DOR) of 14.9 months.
L2151: Penpulimab is a monoclonal antibody targeting programmed death-1 (PD-1), an immune checkpoint inhibitor with an established pharmacological class of PD-1 blocking antibody. The proposed dosing regimen is 200 mg intravenously (IV) every two weeks (Q2W) until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity.
L2152: The primary efficacy data providing evidence in support of the proposed indication is derived from Study AK105-202, a single arm, multicenter, open label study conducted in China that enrolled 130 patients with metastatic NPC who had disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of therapy. All patients received penpulimab 200 mg IV Q2W until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity.
L2153: The primary efficacy population from Study AK105-202 is comprised of 111 patients with non-keratinizing undifferentiated NPC (64%); 32% of patients had non-keratinizing differentiated NPC, and 3.6% of patients had had non-keratinizing NPC of unknown differentiation. In the primary efficacy population, the ORR by IRRC was 30% (95% CI 21, 39) (data cut off February 3, 2021). Median duration of response (DOR) was not reached (range 3.8, 17.1+); Among the 33 patients with an objective response, 39% had a DOR \ge 9 months.
L2154: The evaluation of safety for penpulimab was primarily based upon safety data from 130 patients enrolled in Study AK105-202 supported by a pooled population of 372 patients with advanced solid tumors or classical Hodgkin lymphoma treated with penpulimab 200 mg Q2W. Among the 372 patients in the pooled safety population, the most common adverse reactions (\ge10%) were hepatitis (27%), hypothyroidism (24%), rash (20%), upper respiratory tract infection (18%), pyrexia (15%), fatigue (13%), musculoskeletal pain (11%), weight increased (11%), weight decreased (11%), colitis (11%), and cough (11%). Among the 130 patients with NPC treated with penpulimab in Study AK105-202, the most common adverse reactions (\ge10%) were hypothyroidism (32%), hepatitis (28%), musculoskeletal pain (22%), weight decreased (17%) pyrexia (15%), rash (12%), upper respiratory tract infection (12%), and cough (10%). Serious adverse reactions occurred in 19% of the patients in the primary safety population; the most common serious adverse reactions (\ge 1%) were pneumonia, death, hepatitis, respiratory failure, pneumonitis, and rash.
L2155: Adverse reactions leading to permanent discontinuation of penpulimab occurred in five patients (3.8%) in the primary safety population including one event (0.8%) each of pleural effusion, herpes zoster disseminated infection, spinal cord compression, hepatitis, and pemphigoid. A total of four patients (3.1%) in the primary safety population had a fatal adverse reaction; these included one event of respiratory failure and three events of death due to unknown cause.
L2156: Immune-related adverse events (irAEs) occurred in 22% of patients, including Grade 3 to 4 irAEs in 2% of patients in the primary safety population. The most common irAE (\ge 10%) was hypothyroidism (15%). There were no fatal irAEs observed in the pooled or primary safety population.
L2157: The overall safety profile of penpulimab was similar to that observed with other PD-1 blocking antibodies.
L2158: A major limitation of the data is that the primary trial supporting the marketing application was conducted exclusively in China. Therefore, the patient population is not reflective of the racial and ethnic diversity of U.S.
L2159: patients with NPC. Although a flexible regulatory approach may be warranted for treatment settings with high unmet need, penpulimab no longer addresses an unmet need for patients with NPC given the recent approvals of toripalimab-tpzi for patients with NPC. Based on the approvals of toripalimab-tpzi, the Study AK105-202 results do not demonstrate a clear advantage over currently available therapy. While the accelerated approval program allows for the demonstration of a meaningful advantage over currently available therapy outside of efficacy (e.g., through a more convenient route of administration or improved toxicity profile), the mechanisms of action, routes of administration, and toxicity profiles of penpulimab and toripalimab-tpzi are very similar and do not support an advantage for penpulimab over toripalimab-tpzi.
L2160: Additionally, the Office of Pharmaceutical Quality (OPQ), CDER, has completed an assessment of penpulimab-kcqx manufactured by Akeso Biopharma Co., Ltd. The data submitted in this application are not sufficient to support a conclusion that the manufacture of penpulimab-kcqx is well-controlled and will lead to a product that is pure and potent.
L2161: Given the efficacy results that do not demonstrate a clear advantage over currently available therapy and the substantial identified manufacturing deficiencies, FDA does not consider the current data supportive of a favorable risk-benefit assessment for penpulimab and recommends a Complete Response action.
L2162: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Analysis of Condition	Nasopharyngeal carcinoma (NPC) originates in the nasopharynx from the base of the skull to the palate.
There were 133,354 cases of NPC worldwide in 2020 with the majority of cases (>75%) in South and East Asia; 1.6% of cases occurred in Northern America. Approximately 80,000 people died from NPC in 2020.
The incidence of NPC is substantially higher in Southeast Asia relative to the U.S. A higher proportion of NPC patients in North America develop the keratinizing squamous subtype compared with China, where the vast majority of patients develop the non-keratinizing undifferentiated subtype. The prognosis of metastatic NPC remains poor.
L2163: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
NPC is rare in the United States, with <1 case per 100,000 people each year. Approximately 220 cases of NPC were diagnosed within the US in 2018 (Chen Lancet 2019).
The incidence of NPC in children and adolescents varies between different regions and races and accounts for approximately 2% of cases of NPC in the US. According to SEER registries, of 129 children and adolescents reported to have NPC from 1988-2006 in the US, the median age was 16 years (range, 7-19 years) (Sultan et al, 2010).
There are three major histologic subtypes of NPC based on WHO classification, with differences in subtype distribution between North America and China: Type 1 keratinizing squamous cell carcinoma (25% vs 2%), Type 2 non-keratinizing differentiated carcinoma (12 vs 3%), and Type 3 non-keratinizing undifferentiated carcinoma (63% vs 95%).
For metastatic NPC, the median overall survival is 21 to 29 months with platinum-based chemotherapy.

Current Treatment Options	Toripalimab-tpzi-tpzi in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine is approved for the first-line treatment of NPC based on a median PFS of 11.7 months versus 8.0 months for placebo with cisplatin and gemcitabine in the JUPITER-O2 trial.
Toripalimab-tpzi is also approved for NPC with disease progression on or after a platinum-containing chemotherapy based on an ORR of 21% and median DOR of 14.9 months in the POLARIS-O2 trial.
Prior to the approval of toripalimab-tpzi, in the metastatic setting, standard of care treatment for first line therapy was cisplatin plus
Metastatic NPC represents a life-threatening disease. For patients with metastatic or recurrent NPC, FDA recently approved toripalimab-tpzi in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine in the first line setting and toripalimab-tpzi as a single agent with disease progression on or after a platinum-containing therapy.
L2164: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
gemcitabine, with a reported median PFS of 7.0 months and median OS of 29.1 months.
Treatment of NPC in adolescents generally follows the same treatment paradigms used in adults.

Benefit	The primary trial supporting this BLA is Study AK105-202, a single-arm, single-cohort study conducted solely in China of patients with metastatic non-keratinizing NPC with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of therapy.
The ORR per IRRRC observed in the primary efficacy population (n=111) was 30% (95% CI: 21, 39). Median duration of response (DOR) was not reached (range 3.8, 17.1+); 39% of the 33 responding patients had a DOR \ge 9 months.
Penpulimab demonstrated a durable response in patients treated in Study AK105-202. However, given the recent FDA approvals of toripalimab-tpzi, these results do not demonstrate a meaningful advantage over available therapies. Therefore, the proposed indication for penpulimab does not address an unmet need for patients with NPC.
Additionally, a major limitation of the data is that the primary trial supporting this marketing application was conducted exclusively in China and all patients enrolled were Asian. Therefore, the patient population in the trial is not reflective of the racial and ethnic diversity of patients with NPC in the United States. Regulatory flexibility is not warranted as penpulimab does not address an unmet need.

Risk and Risk Management	The primary safety population for this marketing application included 130 patients enrolled in Study AK105-202 who were treated with penpulimab 200 mg every two weeks (Q2W).
The overall safety profile of penpulimab was similar to that observed with other PD-1 blocking antibodies.
L2165: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Supportive safety data was provided from a pooled population of 372 patients with advanced solid tumors or classical Hodgkin lymphoma who were treated with penpulimab 200 mg Q2W.
Serious adverse reactions occurred in 19% of patients in Study AK105-202; the most common serious adverse reactions (\ge 1\%) were pneumonia (3.8%), death (3.1%), hepatitis (2.3%), respiratory failure (1.5%), pneumonitis (1.5%), and rash (1.5%).
There were 4 (3.1%) fatal adverse reactions in the primary safety population, including one event of respiratory failure and three events of death.
Permanent discontinuation of penpulimab due to an adverse reaction occurred in 3.8% of patients in Study AK105-202. Adverse reactions resulting in permanent discontinuation of penpulimab were pleural effusion, herpes zoster disseminated infection, spinal cord compression, hepatitis, and pemphigoid (0.8% each).
Immune-related adverse events (irAEs) occurred in 22% of patients (Grade 3 to 4, 2.3%) in Study AK105-202 and 20% of patients (Grade 3 to 4, 0.8%) in the pooled safety population.
In Study AK105-202, the most common irAEs (\ge 10\%) was hypothyroidism (15%).
Safety issues identified as significant and serious during the BLA review were irAEs and IRRs.
Penpulimab appears to have an acceptable safety profile when assessed in the context of a life-threatening disease.
A major amendment was issued on June 14, 2022, to allow the FDA review team additional time to evaluate the data submitted by Akeso to address OPQ deficiencies related to microbial control. While these microbial control deficiencies were resolved during review cycle, OPQ has determined that the data submitted in this application related to product quality and overall control strategy are not sufficient to support a conclusion that the manufacture of penpulimab-kcqx is well-controlled and will lead to a product that is pure and potent.
L2166: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Infusion-related reactions (IRRs) occurred in 3.8% of patients in the pooled safety population. No IRRs were reported in the primary safety population.
During the review of this application, the Office of Pharmaceutical Quality (OPQ) identified validation deficiencies related to drug product sterility and endotoxin control, which may potentially impact product safety. Clinically, failure to demonstrate adequate sterility assurance and endotoxin control for penpulimab manufacturing could result in microbial and endotoxin contamination of the product.

L2171: Cross-Disciplinary Team Leader
L2172: ## 2. Therapeutic Context
L2173: ## 2.1. Analysis of Condition
L2174: Nasopharyngeal carcinoma (NPC) is a kind of malignancy arising in the top or lateral wall of nasopharyngeal cavity. Although NPC, comprised of keratinizing, non-keratinizing differentiated, and nonkeratinizing undifferentiated subtypes, is endemic in Southeast Asia, it is rare in the US population (1-2 cases per 100,000). The keratinizing squamous cell subtype is more common in the US (25% vs. 2% of NPC cases) and has a poorer prognosis, while the non-keratinizing undifferentiated subtype is more common in Southeast Asia (95% vs. 63% of NPC cases). Based on high-level evidence, radiotherapy or chemoradiotherapy has become the mainstay of treatment for patients with early or locally advanced nasopharyngeal carcinoma, and the 5-year survival rate is approximately 85%. However, tumor metastasis remains the leading cause of treatment failure in these patients. Moreover, approximately 15% of NPC patients develop distant metastases at initial diagnosis, and patients with recurrent/metastatic NPC have a very poor prognosis, with a median overall survival of approximately 20 months.
L2175: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2176: There were approximately 133,354 cases of NPC diagnosed worldwide in 2020 with the majority of cases (&gt;75%) in South and East Asia and 1.6% in Northern America, and approximately 80,000 people died from NPC in 2020 (Globocan, 2020). NPC is rare in the United States, with &lt;1 case per 100,000 people each year (Cancer.Net, 2022).
L2177: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position but notes that the Applicant provided little information specific to the demographics or disease characteristics for patients with NPC in the U.S. A review of data from the Surveillance, Epidemiology, and End Results (SEER) registry reported by Sun et al (2017) identified 3919 patients with histologically confirmed NPC between 2004 and 2013. Among the 3919 patients, median age was 54 years (range 44, 63) and 71% of patients were male. Information on race was included for 3672 patients with 49% White, 12% Black, and 39% Asian (including 18% Chinese ethnicity). Information on histology was included for 1087 patients; of these, 83% were non-keratinizing and 17% were keratinizing.
L2178: ## 2.2. Analysis of Current Treatment Options
L2179: ## The Applicant's Position
L2180: Platinum-containing chemotherapy is generally considered the standard of care for patients with recurrent/metastatic for all types of nasopharyngeal carcinoma. Currently, cisplatin in combination with continuous intravenous infusion of fluorouracil is the global standard
L2181: treatment used in patients with recurrent/metastatic nasopharyngeal carcinoma, and available Phase II studies have reported objective response rates between 54% to 78% and the median progression-free survival of 7 to 11 months. However, adverse reactions, common mucosal complications, and requirements for deep venous catheterization remain major limitations to the fluorouracil plus cisplatin regimen. A Phase III randomized controlled study demonstrated that gemcitabine plus cisplatin significantly improved the objective tumor response rate from 42% to 64% and the median progression-free survival from 5.6 months to 7.0 months compared to fluorouracil plus cisplatin.
L2182: For patients who have failed platinum-containing first-line chemotherapy, the subsequent second-line therapy depends on patient performance status, toxicity, and the time interval to relapse after a platinum-containing regimen. For patients who have failed platinum-containing first-line chemotherapy but have no disease progression for more than 1 year, the re-treatment of platinum-containing combination chemotherapy may be considered. For patients who have failed first-line platinum-containing chemotherapy and whose disease has relapsed within one year after the chemotherapy regimen, the second-line chemotherapy includes gemcitabine, capecitabine, or paclitaxel, monotherapy. However, after relapse on 2nd line treatment there is no recognized standard third-line systemic therapy. Increasing evidence suggests that further use of chemotherapy after second- or third-line chemotherapy may not prolong survival in most patients.
L2183: It has been reported that NPC is closely associated with EB virus infection, and there is high PD-L1 expression in the tumor, suggesting that PD-1 blockade may be an ideal treatment option for NPC.
L2184: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2185: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position regarding the standard of care first and second line therapies for advanced NPC at the time of submission of this BLA (July 2021). However, on October 27, 2023, FDA granted traditional approval to toripalimab-tpzi in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine for the first-line treatment of adults with metastatic or recurrent, locally advanced NPC based on the results of the JUPITER-O2 trial. The median PFS was 11.7 months for patients treated with toripalimab-tpzi with cisplatin and gemcitabine versus 8.0 months for patients treated with placebo with cisplatin and gemcitabine (PFS HR 0.52; 95% CI 0.36, 0.74; p=0.0003). A statistically significant OS improvement was also observed with median OS not reached (95% CI: 38.7 months, not estimable) for the toripalimab-tpzi-containing regimen and 33.7 months (95% CI: 27.0, 44.2) for the placebo-containing regimen (HR 0.63 [95% CI: 0.45, 0.89], p\text{-value}=0.0083). On October 27, 2023, FDA also granted traditional approval to toripalimab-tpzi as a single agent for the treatment of adults with recurrent unresectable or metastatic NPC with disease progression on or after a platinum-containing chemotherapy based on results of the POLARIS-O2 trial. In 172 patients, the ORR by BICR was 21% (95% CI: 15, 28) and median DOR was 14.9 months (95% CI: 10.3, not estimable).

The clinical development program for penpulimab is being conducted under Investigational New Drug (IND) 138576 with Division of Regulatory Operations -Oncologic Diseases for DO2, and the relevant regulatory history is included in Section 3.2.

L2226: ## 4. Nonclinical Pharmacology/Toxicology
L2227: ## 4.1. Executive Summary
L2228: Penpulimab is a human IgG1 monoclonal antibody that targets programmed cell death protein 1 (PD-1); its established pharmacologic class is PD-1 blocking antibody. PD-1 is expressed on activated T and B cells, natural killer cells, and myeloid lineage cells. Binding of PD-1 to its ligands, programmed death-ligand 1 (PD-L1) and PD-L2, inhibits T-cell activity, T-cell proliferation, and cytokine production. In the tumor microenvironment, PD-1 signaling contributes to the inhibition of T cell-mediated immune surveillance of tumors. Thus, blockade of PD-1 signaling in the tumor microenvironment is intended to enhance tumor immunosurveillance and anti-tumor immune responses.
L2229: In in vitro studies, penpulimab bound to human and cynomolgus monkey PD-1 but did not bind rodent PD-1, supporting the use of cynomolgus monkeys as the single pharmacologically relevant species for toxicity assessment. In cellular assays, penpulimab blocked the binding of human PD-1 to human PD-L1 and PD-L2 and also competitively bound to PD-1 expressed on the surface of human PD-1-transfected 293T cells. Additionally, penpulimab induced the secretion of interleukin-2 (IL-2) and interferon-gamma (IFN-\gamma) in a mixed culture of peripheral blood mononuclear cells (PBMCs) and dendritic cells (DCs) and in a mixed culture of PBMCs, DCs, and Raji Burkitt lymphoma cells. Penpulimab also enhanced IFN-\gamma secretion by cytomegalovirus (CMV)-responsive human PBMCs during the recall response to CMV antigen.
L2230: Given that penpulimab does not bind mouse PD-1, the Applicant investigated the in vivo anti-tumor activity of penpulimab in a human PD-1 knock-in genetically engineered mouse model (HuGEMM) implanted subcutaneously with MC38 mouse colon adenocarcinoma cells. Intraperitoneal administration of penpulimab at doses of 0.8 and 8 mg/kg twice a week for a total of 5 doses resulted in tumor growth inhibition (TGI) compared to the isotype control. The Applicant also evaluated the antitumor activity of penpulimab in immunodeficient mice implanted subcutaneously with Raji cells expressing PD-L1 or a Raji-PD-L1/PBMC mixture. Intravenous (IV) administration of penpulimab (10 mg/kg) on Days 7, 13, and 21 resulted in greater TGI compared to the isotype control.
L2231: The Applicant evaluated the safety of penpulimab in cynomolgus monkeys in GLP-compliant repeat-dose toxicology studies of up to 13 weeks duration. In the 6- and 13-week studies, monkeys received IV infusion of penpulimab at doses up to 60 mg/kg once every 2 weeks. There were no mortalities or penpulimab-related findings of toxicological significance. Target organs of toxicity in the 6-week study were the kidney (increased cortical tubular pigment or pigmented interstitial macrophages), spleen (increased cellularity), adrenal gland (cortical hypertrophy), parotid salivary gland (acinar cell atrophy), thymus (increased weight); and in the 13-week study was lymph nodes (erythrophagocytosis and increased cellularity). The incidence of anti-drug antibody formation was 63.33% in the 6-week study and 93.33% in the 13-week
L2232: study, which may explain the limited findings noted in the 13-week study. In clinical trials done to support the approval of penpulimab in the proposed patient population, toxicities associated with penpulimab were primarily immune-mediated adverse reactions that are predicted by its mechanism of action. At the recommended clinical dose of 200 mg, the human AUC0-336 was 26200 µg*h/mL.
L2233: Based on its mechanism of action and data from the literature, there is potential for enhanced immune responses to infection following treatment with penpulimab. *Mycobacterium tuberculosis* (Mtb)-infected PD-1 knockout mice exhibited decreased survival compared to wild-type mice, which correlated with increased bacterial proliferation and inflammatory responses in these animals. It is unclear whether decreased survival resulted from an inability to mount an appropriate antibacterial response or a failure to control the immune response leading to normal tissue destruction and organ failure (Lazar-Molnar, et al. 2010). Furthermore, in mouse models of lymphocytic choriomeningitis virus (LCMV) infection, the absence of PD-1 pathway signaling led to fatal CD8+ T cell-mediated killing of virally infected vascular endothelial cells, systemic vascular leakage, and ultimately cardiovascular collapse (Frebel et al. 2012; Mueller et al. 2010). Similarly, PD-L1 deficient mice died early after chronic systemic LCMV infection (Barber et al. 2006). In addition, blockade of PD-1 with an anti-PD-1 antibody in Mtb infection in monkeys can exacerbate the infection and lead to death and severe pulmonary stress in infected monkeys (Kauffman et al. 2021). A brief description of these data was included in Section 13.2 of the label to convey these potential risks to prescribers.
L2234: No reproductive or embryo-fetal developmental toxicology studies were conducted with penpulimab. Rather, the Applicant provided a literature-based assessment of the potential reproductive toxicity of penpulimab, consistent with the alternative approach described in the FDA Guidance for Industry Oncology Pharmaceuticals: Reproductive Toxicity Testing and Labeling Recommendations. Data from the literature demonstrate that the PD-1/PD-L1 pathway plays a critical role in maintaining maternal immunological tolerance to the fetus during pregnancy. Blockade of PD-1/PD-L1 signaling in murine models of pregnancy resulted in an increased incidence of fetal loss but no fetal malformations; however, late onset autoimmunity can occur in PD-1-deficient mice. Based on these findings, the potential risks of administering penpulimab during pregnancy include increased rates of abortion or stillbirth and an increased risk in offspring of developing immune-related disorders or altering the normal immune response. As a result, the pharmacology/toxicology team recommends a warning for embryo-fetal toxicity in the prescribing information. Based on a half-life of penpulimab of 23.9 days, females of reproductive potential are advised to use effective contraception during treatment with penpulimab and for 4 months after the last dose. The Applicant did not evaluate transfer of penpulimab to the fetus or milk, but IgG antibodies can be present in milk. Because of the potential adverse effects of penpulimab on a breastfeeding child, the review team included recommendations in the label not to breastfeed during treatment with penpulimab and for 4 months after the last dose. Neither genetic toxicology or carcinogenicity
L2235: studies were conducted or required to support the use of an antibody in the currently proposed indication.
L2236: There are no outstanding issues from a pharmacology/toxicology perspective that would prevent the approval of penpulimab for the treatment of adult [REDACTED] (b) (4) with metastatic non-keratinizing nasopharyngeal carcinoma with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of therapy.

L2240: ## 4.3. Pharmacology
L2241: ## 4.3.1. Primary Pharmacology
L2242: ## The FDA's Assessment (all data presented by FDA):
L2243: Primary pharmacology studies NCS105-0001 through NCS105-00010 were reviewed under the original IND by Dr. Wimolnut Manheng. All other pharmacology studies were reviewed under the current BLA by Dr. Kelie Reece.
L2244: ## In Vitro Studies
L2245: The Applicant determined the binding affinities of penpulimab for human (Study NCS105-0001), cynomolgus monkey (Study NCS105-0005), mouse (Study NCS105-0006), and rat PD-1 (Study NCS105-0007) using the enzyme-linked immunoassay (ELISA). Penpulimab (0.0014–1 µg/mL) bound to human PD-1-mFc fusion protein (human PD-1 extracellular domain fused to murine IgG Fc domain) and monkey PD-1-hFc fusion protein (monkey PD-1 extracellular domain fused to human IgG1 Fc domain) with EC50 values of 0.053 and 0.215 nM, respectively, but did not bind to mouse or rat PD-1, supporting the use of cynomolgus monkeys as a pharmacologically relevant species for toxicity assessment.
L2246: The Applicant determined the binding activity of penpulimab to PD-1 expressed on the surface of human PD-1-transfected 293T cells (293T-PD-1 cells) by fluorescence activated cell sorting (FACS) analysis (Study NCS105-0008). Penpulimab (0.1–20 nM) bound to human PD-1 in a dose-dependent manner, with an EC50 of 2.53 nM. As assessed by surface plasmon resonance, penpulimab (1.56–50 nM) exhibited high binding affinity for human PD-1, with a KD of 0.588 nM (Study NCS105-0002), which was similar to the KD of the anti-PD-1 comparator antibody nivolumab.
L2247: ## Penpulimab
L2248: The Applicant used competitive ELISA to evaluate the activity of penpulimab in competing with PD-L1 (Study NCS105-0003) and PD-L2 (Study NCS105-0004) for binding to PD-1. Penpulimab (0.002–3 µg/mL) blocked the binding of immobilized human PD-1-hFc to human PD-L1-mFc and human PD-L2-His fusion proteins in a dose-dependent manner, with EC_{50} values of 0.878 and 1.483 nM, respectively. The Applicant used FACS to evaluate the activity of penpulimab in competing with PD-L1 for binding to 293T-PD-1 cells (Study NCS105-0009). Penpulimab (0.07–150 nM) competitively bound to PD-1 on 293T-PD-1 cells in a dose-dependent manner, with an EC_{50} of 2.526 nM (Figure 1). Binding of penpulimab was comparable to the positive control, nivolumab, which competitively bound to PD-1 with an EC_{50} of 3.281 nM.

L2252: To evaluate the effect of penpulimab on the secretion of IFN-\gamma and IL-2 from lymphocytes, the Applicant conducted an allogeneic mixed lymph reaction (MLR) experiment (Study NCS105-00010). In this MLR, investigators incubated in vitro-differentiated DCs of one donor (stimulator) with allogeneic PBMCs of another donor (responder) at a ratio of 10:1, followed by the addition of penpulimab (3, 30, 300 nM). Nivolumab Akeso (synthesized by the Applicant) was used as a positive control. IFN-\gamma and IL-2 levels were measured by ELISA. Compared with the isotype and negative controls, penpulimab significantly enhanced the secretion of IFN-\gamma (Figure 2) and IL-2 (Figure 3) in the mixed culture of PBMCs and DCs in a dose-independent manner.

L2266: The Applicant conducted a cellular assay to evaluate the effect of penpulimab on IFN-\gamma secretion by CMV-responsive human PBMCs during the recall response to CMV antigen (Study NCS105-0024). PBMCs from two donors were prescreened for a CMV recall response using a serially diluted CMV peptide pool. As assessed by ELISA on Day 4, the CMV peptide pool effectively induced the secretion of IFN-\gamma in a dose-dependent manner, demonstrating that the PBMCs were previously infected by CMV and could re-respond to CMV antigen. The CMV-responsive PMBCs were stimulated with CMV antigen in the absence or presence of penpulimab (0.1–300 nM). After a 4-day incubation, the antigen-induced IFN-\gamma response was measured by ELISA. Compared with the isotype and negative controls, penpulimab significantly enhanced the secretion of IFN-\gamma in a dose-dependent manner by CMV-responsive human PBMCs during the recall response to CMV antigen, showing comparable activity with nivolumab and
L2267: pembrolizumab (Figure 6).

L2271: ## In Vivo Studies
L2272: Because penpulimab does not bind to mouse PD-1, male C57BL/6 mice were genetically engineered to express a chimeric human/mouse PD-1 gene; these mice have a functional human PD-1 with expression regulated by the mouse PD-1 promoter. The Applicant investigated the in vivo antitumor activity of penpulimab in this human PD-1 knock-in genetically engineered mouse model (HuGEMM) implanted subcutaneously with MC38 mouse colon adenocarcinoma cells (Study E3174-B1701). Nivolumab (manufactured by Bristol-Myers) was used as a comparator control. Intraperitoneal administration of 0.8 and 8 mg/kg penpulimab twice a week for a total of 5 doses resulted in 74.1% and 54.5% TGI, respectively, compared to the isotype control (Figure 7). Penpulimab exhibited similar in vivo antitumor activity as nivolumab. There were no mortalities, and body weights remained stable during treatment.
L2273: **Figure 7: Penpulimab Has Antitumor Activity in PD-1 HuGEMM MC38-Bearing Mice**
L2274: Source: Excerpted from the Applicant's submission: Study E3174-B1701.
L2275: Abbreviations: BIW, twice a week; HuGEMM, human genetically engineered mouse model.
L2276: The Applicant also evaluated the antitumor activity of penpulimab in female SCID/Beige mice implanted subcutaneously with Raji cells expressing PD-L1 (Raji-PD-L1 cells) or a Raji-PD-L1/PBMC mixture, in order to provide an immune component that is responsive to anti-PD-1 antibody and can generate an anti-tumor immune response (Study NCS105-0014). Intravenous administration of penpulimab (10 mg/kg) on Days 7, 13, and 21 resulted in greater TGI compared to the isotype control (Figure 8), without inducing significant toxicity based on body weight loss.

L2280: ## 4.3.2. Secondary Pharmacology
L2281: ## The Applicant's Position
L2282: In the Fc domain of penpulimab, point mutations were introduced to eliminate a series of Fc receptor mediated effects, including antibody-dependent cell-mediated cytotoxicity (ADCC), antibody dependent cellular phagocytosis (ADCP), complement dependent cytotoxicity (CDC) and Fc receptor mediated cytokine release. The affinity of penpulimab to Fc-receptor Fc\gammaR\text{III}\alpha, Fc\gammaR\text{I} and C1q were evaluated by Fortebio Octet molecular interaction assay and confirmed lack of binding. Cellular assays were further conducted to evaluate the ADCC, ADCP, CDC and
L2283: ## Penpulimab
L2284: antibody dependent cytokine release (ADCR) activity of penpulimab, and penpulimab shows no ADCC, ADCP, CDC activity or ADCR of IL-6 and IL-8 from human macrophages. In binding selectivity assay, penpulimab binds to human PD-1 but not to human CTLA-4, which is a homologous protein of PD-1. Penpulimab also does not bind to human lymphocyte-activation gene 3 (LAG3) or human T cell immunoglobulin and ITIM domain (TIGIT), which are co-inhibitory receptors like PD-1. The activity of penpulimab to elicit cytokine IL-1\beta, IL-6 and TNF-\alpha release from peripheral blood mononuclear cells (PBMCs) was also tested and penpulimab does not elicit cytokine release of these cytokines from PBMC, suggesting the low risk of triggering cytokine release syndrome.
L2285: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2286: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's conclusions.
L2287: ## 4.3.3. Safety Pharmacology
L2288: ## The Applicant's Position
L2289: Safety pharmacology studies were conducted during cynomolgus monkey toxicity studies, and effect of penpulimab on vital functions in cardiovascular system, central nervous system (CNS) and respiratory system were investigated. The evaluation of safety pharmacology endpoints including body temperature, respiratory rate, blood oxygen saturation, electrocardiograms, and blood pressure upon treatment with penpulimab was incorporated into the repeat-dose toxicology study in cynomolgus monkey conducted under GLP condition. There were no treatment-related effects on body temperature, respiratory rate, blood oxygen saturation, electrocardiograms, or blood pressure found in the repeat dose toxicology study. Thus, no specific concerns were observed on vital functions in cardiovascular system, CNS, and respiratory system.
L2290: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2291: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's conclusions. In vivo, cardiovascular parameters were assessed pre-treatment; 2 to 5 hours after dosing on Days 1, 15, 29, and 43; and 1 day prior to recovery necropsy (Day 84) in the 13-week repeat-dose monkey study. Findings were reviewed under the General Toxicology below (Section 5.5.1). There were no toxicologically significant changes observed at doses up to 60 mg/kg/day.
L2292: ## 4.4. ADME/PK
L2293: ## The Applicant's Position
L2294: Single dose pharmacokinetics (PK) and repeat-dose toxicokinetics (TK) evaluation of penpulimab were performed in cynomolgus monkey which is the species showing cross-
L2295: reactivity to penpulimab. In single dose PK study, systemic exposure of penpulimab (AUC and Cmax) was approximately dose-proportional in cynomolgus monkeys from 1 mg/kg to 10 mg/kg. No significant differences in systemic exposure were noted. A high incidence of anti-drug antibody (ADA) formation was observed, which was related to the accelerated elimination of penpulimab in ADA-positive animals at the end of the elimination phase. Repeat dose PK (TK) of penpulimab was investigated in the 6-week and 13-week toxicity study. Dose-dependent increase in systemic exposure of penpulimab (AUC and Cmax) was noted following repeat intravenous dosing. Decreases in systemic exposure levels were noted in a few animals (Day 29 or 71, respectively), which is due to the formation of ADAs. There was no significant systemic accumulation after repeat dosing of penpulimab once every two weeks for 6 weeks or 13 weeks.
L2296: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2297: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of the nonclinical ADME/PK data. Traditional distribution, metabolism, and excretion studies were not conducted with penpulimab because it is a monoclonal antibody.

L2299: ## 4.5. Toxicology
L2300: ## 4.5.1. General Toxicology
L2301: ## The Applicant's Position
L2302: In 6-week repeat-dose toxicity study, no apparent toxicity was observed in cynomolgus monkeys following intravenous infusion of penpulimab at 4, 15, or 60 mg/kg once every two weeks for 6 weeks. Treatment-related histopathological change of kidneys (increased cortical tubular pigment or pigmented interstitial macrophages) was noted in some animals but no impact on kidney functions. Pigment in the cortical tubular epithelium of the kidneys is not reported as a common background finding in cynomolgus monkeys. In this study, there were no apparent changes in clinical chemistry, urinalysis, or organ weights which would indicate an impact of treatment on kidney function. There were also no other histopathological changes associated with the pigment which were indicative of damage to the kidneys. These changes were therefore considered not to be adverse. The no observed adverse effect level (NOAEL) was considered to be 60 mg/kg.
L2303: In 13-week repeat-dose toxicity study, no apparent toxicity and histopathological change of kidney was observed in cynomolgus monkeys following intravenous infusion of penpulimab at 4, 15, or 60 mg/kg once every two weeks for 13 weeks, the NOAEL was considered to be 60 mg/kg.
L2304: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2305: In general, the FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of the toxicology data. In the 6-week repeat-dose toxicology study, clinical chemistry findings included a dose-dependent decrease in total bilirubin and increase in creatine kinase levels at all dose levels in males, which were present at the end of the recovery period. In addition to the kidney, as noted by the Applicant, other target organs of toxicity in the 6-week study were the spleen (increased cellularity of the red and white pulp), adrenal gland (cortical hypertrophy), parotid salivary gland (acinar cell atrophy), and thymus (increased weight). Changes in the adrenal gland, spleen, and thymus (females) were present at the end of the recovery period. Of the 30 monkey serum samples tested, 19 were ADA-positive with an incidence of 63.33%.
L2306: In the 13-week toxicity study, histological findings included erythrophagocytosis and increased cellularity in the lymph nodes (inguinal, mandibular, mesenteric), which were present at the end of the recovery period. Limited findings may be reflective of ADA formation. Of the 30 monkey serum samples tested, 28 were ADA-positive with an incidence of 93.33%.
L2307: ## **Study title/number: A 13-Week Intravenous Infusion Toxicity Study of AK105 Injection with a 6-Week Recovery Period in Cynomolgus Monkeys/P18-172-RD**
L2308: ## **Key Study Findings**
L2309: - There were no mortalities or penpulimab-related findings of toxicological significance.
- Histological findings included increased cellularity and erythrophagocytosis in the inguinal, mandibular, and mesenteric lymph nodes.
- The NOAEL was 60 mg/kg.
L2310: GLP Compliance: Yes

L2320: ## 4.5.2. Genetic Toxicology
L2321: ## The Applicant's Position
L2322: Genetic toxicology studies were not conducted with penpulimab. As described in ICH Guidance S6, the range and type of genotoxicity studies routinely conducted for pharmaceuticals are not applicable to biotechnology-derived pharmaceuticals.
L2323: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2324: The FDA agrees that genetic toxicology studies are not needed to support the use of penpulimab per ICH S6.
L2325: ## 4.5.3. Carcinogenicity
L2326: ## The Applicant's Position
L2327: Carcinogenicity studies were not conducted with penpulimab, which is consistent with its use in patients with advanced malignancies (see the ICH Guidance S9). If penpulimab is developed for use in other indications, an assessment of its carcinogenic potential may be warranted;
L2328: however rodent bioassays are inappropriate, as penpulimab does not appear to be active in rodents.
L2329: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2330: The FDA agrees that carcinogenicity studies are not needed to support the use of penpulimab in the currently proposed indication per ICH S9.
L2331: ## 4.5.4. Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology
L2332: ## The Applicant's Position
L2333: Fertility, embryonic fetal development, or pre- and postnatal developmental toxicity studies were not conducted with penpulimab. As described in ICH Guidance S9, fertility and early embryonic developmental toxicity study is not warranted for marketing of pharmaceuticals for the treatment of patients with advanced cancer. Information available from general toxicology studies on the pharmaceutical's effect on reproductive organs were used as the basis of the assessment of impairment of fertility. In the 6-week and 13-week repeat-dose toxicity studies, there were no notable effects in the male and female reproductive organs.
L2334: Embryo-fetal development toxicity studies were not conducted with penpulimab. Literature data shows that a central function of the PD-1/PD-L1 pathway is to preserve pregnancy by maintaining maternal immune tolerance to the fetus. Blockade of PD-L1 signaling has been shown in murine models of pregnancy to disrupt tolerance to the fetus and to increase fetal loss. There are potential reproductive risks when giving penpulimab during pregnancy, including increased rates of miscarriage or stillbirth. Penpulimab is an IgG1 antibody drug with high affinity to human FcRn, thus it is expected to cross the placenta during third-trimester pregnancy in human, though the exposure of penpulimab in fetus under treatment dose level is undetermined. Based on its mechanism of action, fetal exposure to penpulimab may increase the risk of developing immune-mediated disorders or altering the normal immune response and immune-mediated disorders have been reported in PD-1 knockout mice.
L2335: Pre- and postnatal toxicity studies were not conducted with penpulimab. As described in ICH Guidance S9, pre- and postnatal toxicity study is not warranted for marketing of pharmaceuticals for the treatment of patients with advanced cancer.
L2336: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2337: The FDA agrees that dedicated reproductive and developmental toxicity studies are not needed to support this BLA submission. To address the potential effects of penpulimab on embryo-fetal development, the Applicant submitted an assessment of the potential reproductive toxicity of penpulimab using a weight-of-evidence approach. The FDA agrees that this approach is reasonable.
L2338: ## Penpulimab
L2339: The PD-1/PD-L1 pathway plays a critical role in maintaining maternal immunological tolerance to the fetus during pregnancy (Guleria et al. 2005). PD-L1 is expressed in the human placenta throughout pregnancy and at the maternal-fetal interface in fetal villous syncytiotrophoblasts and extravillous cytotrophoblasts (Petroff et al. 2003). Blockade of PD-L1 signaling in murine models of pregnancy disrupts tolerance to the fetus, resulting in increased fetal loss (Guleria et al. 2005).
L2340: Penpulimab is a humanized IgG1 antibody, and human IgG is known to cross the placenta; thus, penpulimab may be transmitted from the mother to the fetus. Based on its mechanism of action and findings from murine models of pregnancy (Guleria et al. 2005), administration of penpulimab is likely to disrupt the maintenance of normal pregnancy. Potential risks associated with administration of penpulimab during pregnancy include increased rates of abortion and stillbirth. In addition, as PD-1 knockout mice develop late onset autoimmune phenotypes (Okazaki and Honjo 2007), fetal exposure to penpulimab may alter the normal immune response or increase the risk of developing immune-related disorders. As inhibition of the PD-1/PD-L1 pathway can lead to increased risk of immune-mediated rejection of the developing fetus, resulting in fetal death, a warning for embryofetal toxicity is recommended for penpulimab. Based on a half-life of approximately 24 days, females of reproductive potential are advised to use effective contraception during treatment with penpulimab and for 4 months after the last dose of penpulimab.
L2341: Fertility studies have not been conducted with penpulimab and are not needed to support a BLA for the proposed indication. In 6- and 13-week repeat-dose toxicity studies in monkeys, there were no notable effects on male and female reproductive organs up to the highest dose tested of 60 mg/kg (approximately \ge 4.7 times the human exposure based on AUC at the 200 mg clinical dose); however, most animals in these studies were not sexually mature.
L2342: ## 4.5.5. Other Toxicology Studies
L2343: ## The Applicant's Position
L2344: The investigation of tissue cross-reactivity of penpulimab with normal human and normal cynomolgus monkey frozen tissues revealed that penpulimab reacted with normal human spleen and lymph nodes and normal cynomolgus monkey spleen, mesenteric lymph node, thymus, jejunum, and colon. PD-1 is expressed on the surface of activated T cells, B cells, and macrophages, and as the results of penpulimab tissue cross-reactivity (TCR) studies showed no unexpected binding of penpulimab to human or cynomolgus monkey tissues was found. In hemolysis study, penpulimab at the concentration of 10 mg/mL had no hemolytic or aggregation effect on human red blood cells.
L2345: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2346: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's conclusions. Standard positive controls confirmed the sensitivity and validity of these GLP-compliant studies.
L2347: ## Literature-Based Assessment of Potential for Effects of Infection
L2348: Reviewed by Drs. Shawna Weis and Stephanie Augst.
L2349: While loss of PD-1 function enhances clearance of some tumors and viral infections, it increases susceptibility to certain other pathogens such as tuberculosis in some animal models.
L2350: PD-1-deficient mice (C57BL/6) infected with M. tuberculosis exhibited a decrease in survival, which correlated with uncontrolled bacterial proliferation and a larger inflammatory response in the lungs of PD-1-deficient mice compared with wild-type controls. Thus, PD-1 appears to be required to control infection and the inflammatory responses in the lungs of mice infected with M. tuberculosis (Lazar-Molnar, et al. 2010); however, the pathogenesis of this observation has not been clearly-defined. In particular, it is unclear whether the decreased survival reflects rampant bacterial growth resulting from an inability to mount appropriate antibacterial responses and/or whether it is a failure to downregulate the immune reaction that leads to massive tissue destruction and organ failure.
L2351: In a recent publication, Kauffman et al. (2021) evaluated the role of PD-1 during *Mycobacterium tuberculosis* (Mtb) infection of rhesus macaques. Monkeys (~2 years old; males) were infected with 30-50 colony forming units of fluorescently labeled Mtb into the right lower lung lobe. Tissue from Mtb infected monkeys was harvested 15-16 weeks post infection and assessed for PD-1 expression using flow cytometry. Additional Mtb infected monkeys were intravenously infused with 10 mg/kg of isotype control (DSPR4) or a primatized anti-PD-1 monoclonal antibody (n=6/group) at 2, 4, 6-, 8-, 10-, and 13-weeks post infection to determine the role of PD-1 during an Mtb infection. PET scans and 18FDG (18F-fluoro-2-deoxy-D-glucose) uptake, granuloma size and number, and colony forming units per granuloma were used to evaluate infection. Monkeys in both studies were necropsied at 15-16 weeks post infection. Treatment of Mtb infected monkeys with an anti-PD-1 antibody led to early euthanasia in one monkey due to severe pulmonary stress and another monkey developed cachexia and showed elevated 18FDG uptake in the lungs but survived until study termination. PD-1 expression in Mtb infected monkeys occurred more frequently on antigen specific CD4 and CD8 T cells in lung granulomas compared to other tissues. Mtb infected monkeys treated with an anti-PD-1 showed similar numbers of granulomas compared to controls, but these were characterized as larger granulomas, with increased 18FDG uptake and a higher bacterial load. The increased bacterial loads noted with PD-1 blockade occurred primarily within the site of infection (pulmonary lymph nodes and granulomas), suggesting it did not result in disseminated infection. The mechanism underlying the increased bacterial loads after PD-1 blockade in Mtb infection are not clear, but IFN\gamma and TNF have been previously implicated. Evaluation of cytokines in granulomas of isotype and anti-PD-1 treated monkeys indicated that IFN\gamma, TNF, and
L2352: IL-18 were the most dysregulated cytokines and positively correlated with bacterial loads after PD-1 blockade. Data from Kauffman et al. (2021) indicate that blockade of PD-1 with an anti-PD-1 antibody can exacerbate Mtb infection in monkeys consistent with previous findings noted in PD-1 knockout mice infected with Mtb (Lazar-Molnar et al. 2010).
L2353: X	X
L2354: Kelie M. Reece, PhD
Primary Reviewer
L2355: Claudia P. Miller, PhD
Acting Supervisor
L2356: ## 5. Clinical Pharmacology
L2357: ## 5.1. Executive Summary
L2358: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2359: Penpulimab is a programmed death receptor 1 (PD-1) blocking antibody and it is a humanized immunoglobulin G1 (IgG1) immunoglobulin. The Applicant seeks accelerated approval of penpulimab for the treatment of patients with metastatic non-keratinizing nasopharyngeal carcinoma (NPC) in patients with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of therapy. The proposed recommended dosage of penpulimab is 200 mg every 2 weeks (Q2W) in adult (b) (4)
L2360: The Clinical Pharmacology review team evaluated the immunogenicity, population pharmacokinetic (PPK), and exposure-response (E-R) data obtained from Study AK105-202. PPK analyses of the adult data suggest that there was no clinically significant effect of various covariates (e.g., age, sex, body weight, mild and moderate renal or hepatic impairment, and ECOG status) on penpulimab PK. The effect of race and ethnicity on penpulimab PK was not fully characterized, given that the patients in PPK dataset did not represent the overall racial and ethnic diversity of the US patient population. As part of the post-marketing requirement (PMR), the Applicant will collect sparse PK samples in the confirmatory clinical trial that includes sufficient representation of racial and ethnic diversity of the US patient population for supportive PPK analyses. The E-R analysis for efficacy was considered to be inconclusive, given that data were limited with one dose level. The E-R analyses for safety did not identify any apparent correlation between exposures and treatment emergent adverse events (any TEAEs or TEAEs \ge Grade 3).
L2361: As Study AK105-202 was conducted in China, FDA assessed whether the proposed penpulimab dosing regimen is appropriate for the US population. Simulation based on the PPK model showed similar exposure of penpulimab in virtual patients from the US as compared to that of patients from China with mean exposure (C_{max,ss}, C_{min,ss}, and AUC_{ss}) difference less than 20% between these two populations. Therefore, the 200 mg Q2W dosing regimen appears appropriate for US patients based this analysis; the confirmatory trial will provide additional data to evaluate and confirm the acceptability of the recommended dosage in the US patient population.
L2362: (b) (4)
L2363: (b) (4) Refer to Section 6.2.2 for further details on therapeutic individualization.
L2364: The incidences of treatment emergent anti-drug antibodies (ADAs) and neutralizing antibodies (NAbs) against penpulimab were 27% and 7%, respectively in Study AK105-202. There was no clear evidence of any clinically relevant impact of anti-penpulimab antibody development on penpulimab PK. There is insufficient information to characterize the effects of anti-penpulimab antibodies on the pharmacodynamics, safety, and efficacy of penpulimab.
L2365: ## 5.1.1. Recommendations
L2366: The Office of Clinical Pharmacology has reviewed the information and data submitted in BLA 761258. This BLA is approvable from a clinical pharmacology perspective with no major review issues as described in Table 3 below.
L2367: **Table 3 – FDA: Clinical Pharmacology Review Issues and Recommendations**
L2368: Review Issue	Recommendations and Comments
Pivotal and supportive evidence of effectiveness	The primary evidence of effectiveness is derived from 111 adult patients with metastatic nonkeratinizing NPC who were enrolled in Study AK105-202 and were treated with penpulimab 200 mg Q2W. The ORR (95% CI) was 30% (95% CI 21, 39); 73% of responders had a DoR greater than 6 months and 39% had a DoR greater than 9 months (median DoR not reached). Refer to the FDA's assessment in Section 8.1.2, for details.
General dosing instructions	The Applicant's proposed dosing regimen for penpulimab is 200 mg Q2W in adult (b) (4). This dosing regimen in adult patients is supported by the clinical benefit as demonstrated by the ORR and DoR with a manageable safety profile observed in Study AK105-202. (b) (4). Refer to Section 6.2.1 and 6.2.2, General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization for further details.
Dosing in patient subgroups (intrinsic and extrinsic factors)	No dosage adjustment is recommended for intrinsic and extrinsic patient factors (age, body weight, sex, serum albumin, baseline LDH, mild and moderate renal or hepatic impairment, tumor type, and ECOG status) according to the results of PPK analyses.
Immunogenicity	Of the 119 evaluable patients in Study AK105-202 with NPC who received penpulimab 200 mg Q2W, 27% of patients tested positive for treatment emergent ADAs and 7% of patients for treatment-emergent NAbs against penpulimab. Per the PPK analysis, treatment emergent ADA had no significant impact on penpulimab PK. There is insufficient information to characterize the effects of anti-penpulimab antibodies on the pharmacodynamics, safety, and efficacy of penpulimab.
Labeling	Review of the proposed labeling recommendations is pending due to complete response action.
