L2370: Abbreviations: ADA, anti-drug antibody; CI, confidence interval; DoR, duration of response; ECOG, Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group; LDH, lactic dehydrogenase; Nab, neutralizing antibodies NPC, nasopharyngeal carcinoma; ORR, overall response rate; PK, pharmacokinetic; PPK, population pharmacokinetic; Q2W, every 2 weeks.
L2371: ## 5.2. Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Assessment
L2372: ## Data
L2373: Penpulimab is a humanized immunoglobulin G1 (IgG1) monoclonal antibody targeting programmed cell death-1 (PD-1). Penpulimab binds specifically to human PD-1 with high affinity and competitively blocks the binding of PD-1 to its ligand programmed cell death-1 (PD-L1) and programmed cell death ligand-2 (PD-L2), thereby relieving immunosuppression of T lymphocytes, promoting the proliferation of lymphocytes and the secretion of related cytokines, and ultimately recovering cytotoxic activities of T cells to block tumor growth.
L2374: The typical antibody structure of penpulimab is composed of two heavy chains of IgG1 and two light chains of kappa subclass, which are covalently linked to each other by disulfide bonds. Penpulimab was engineered to eliminate Fc receptor mediated effector function, consequently, may prevent ADCC and ADCP mediated depletion of PD-1 expressing T lymphocytes. This may help preserve maximal anti-tumor activity of lymphocytes. The Fc mutation also causes reduced induction of proinflammatory cytokines like IL-6 and IL-8, which may help reduce immune related adverse events commonly seen for a typical PD-1 antibody.
L2375: Pharmacokinetic (PK) data of penpulimab were derived from 4 clinical studies. A total of 379 patients were included in the population PK analysis and 320 patients (including 31 patients who underwent intensive sampling) were included in the non-compartmental model-based PK analysis. The above patients received penpulimab at 1, 3 or 10 mg/kg or 200 mg Q2W. According to the non-compartmental analysis, the drug exposure, expressed as peak concentration (C_{max}) and area under the concentration-time curve from 0 h to day 14 postdose (AUC_{0-14d}) increased proportionally within the dose range of 1 mg/kg to 10 mg/kg. Following single-dose and multi-dose IV infusion of penpulimab (200 mg Q2W, n=11), the mean peak concentration at steady-state (C_{max,ss}) was 136 \mug/mL, the mean average concentration at steady-state (C_{avg,ss}) was 88.5 \mug/mL, the mean trough concentration at steady-state (C_{min,ss}) was 58.7 \mug/mL, the mean area under the concentration-time curve at dose interval (AUC_{(0-\tau)}) was 29900 h\cdot\mug/mL. The mean accumulation rate (\pmSD) calculated based on C_{max} and area under the concentration-time curve (AUC) on Cycle3 Day15 (the 6th dose) was 1.84 \pm 0.495 and 2.69 \pm 0.749, respectively, a likely tendency of drug accumulation following multi-dose administration of penpulimab.
L2376: Penpulimab is administered via IV infusion and the drug directly enters blood circulation. According to population PK simulation, after a single IV infusion of penpulimab at 200 mg, the volume of distribution (V_z) (\pmSD) was 7.18 \pm 2.52 L, the volume of distribution at steady-state (V_{ss}) (\pmSD) was 6.59 \pm 2.01 L, the clearance (CL) (\pmSD) was 0.223 \pm 0.0740 L/d, and the half-life (t_{1/2}) (\pmSD) was 23.9 \pm 9.98 days.
L2377: Receptor occupancy (RO) is an important measure of the in vivo biological activity of penpulimab and also an important parameter underlying the determination of the recommended dose in clinical studies. In the Phase Ia clinical study conducted in Australia
L2378: (AK105-101 study), patients with advanced solid tumors received penpulimab therapy (1, 3 and 10 mg/kg Q2W). PD-1 RO reached 80% to 100% starting from two days post-dose for all these cohorts. No apparent correlation between RO value and dose level was noted.
L2379: ## The Applicant's Position
L2380: Penpulimab exhibited linear PK in patients who received penpulimab by intravenous administration within the dose range of 1 mg/kg to 10 mg/kg. Population pharmacokinetic analysis indicated that distribution of penpulimab was limited to the central vascular compartment with an elimination half-life of 23.9 days and mild drug accumulation upon repeat dosing.
L2381: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2382: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that the exposure to penpulimab increased dose proportionally over the dose range of 1 mg/kg to 10 mg/kg after a single dose. It is unknown whether the clearance of penpulimab is time-dependent after multiple doses given the limited availability of PK data.
L2383: ## 5.2.1. General Dosing
L2384: ## Data
L2385: In the Phase Ia dose-escalation trial, no DLT occurred at the highest dose cohort of 10 mg/kg Q2W and no maximum tolerated dose (MTD) was explored, therefore the maximum administered dose (MAD) for this study was 10 mg/kg Q2W. Phase Ia PK data showed a linear relationship between exposure of penpulimab and dose over the dose range of 1 mg/kg Q2W to 10 mg/kg Q2W; receptor occupancy analysis showed 80% to 100% PD-1 receptor occupancy starting on day 2 after administration. There was no significant difference in receptor occupancy between 1, 3 and 10 mg/kg doses level. The efficacy results showed anti-tumor activities at doses ranging from 1 mg/kg to 10 mg/kg Q2W. Since higher drug concentrations can be obtained with the 3 mg/kg dosing regimen than with the 1 mg/kg dosing regimen, this may ensure to achieve more stable blood concentrations after multiple doses for better sustained receptor occupancy.
L2386: Results of study AK105-101 showed that, following administration of penpulimab 1 mg/kg, 3 mg/kg, and 10 mg/kg as a single IV infusion, drug exposure expressed as C_{max} and AUC_{(0-14d)} increased proportionally with dose from 1 mg/kg to 10 mg/kg; the 90% confidence interval (CI) of \beta values of AUC_{(0-14d)} and C_{max} was 0.676-1.15 and 0.844-1.13, respectively; namely, a linear PK profile was exhibited.
L2387: Based on the final PopPK model, serum concentrations were simulated for 379 patients after IV infusion of 200 mg penpulimab, and PK parameters were calculated by non-compartmental modeling. Simulated PK parameters following single-dose IV infusion of penpulimab 200 mg are
L2388: ## Penpulimab
L2389: as follows: the median T_{max} was 1.00 h, the mean C_{max} was 62.7 \pm 14.3 \mu g/mL, the mean AUC_{(0-inf)} was 23700 \pm 7560 \mu g \cdot h/mL, the mean CL was 0.223 \pm 0.0740 L/d, the mean V_z was 7.18 \pm 2.52 L, the mean V_{SS} was 6.59 \pm 2.01 L, the mean t_{1/2} was 23.9 \pm 9.98 d, and the mean residence time (MRT) extrapolated to infinity (MRT_{inf}) was 31.9 \pm 12.9 d.
L2390: Based on the final population PK model, serum penpulimab concentrations at each time point within 42 weeks following multi-dose (200 mg Q2W) IV infusion in 379 patients were simulated. Following multi-dose administration of penpulimab 200 mg Q2W, the steady state was reached at about Week 16 (after the 8th dose); the mean steady-state trough concentration (\pm SD) at Week 16 was 49.4 \pm 17.5 \mu g/mL.
L2391: Population PK simulation of steady-state drug concentrations following administration of penpulimab 200 mg Q2W in 379 patients are presented. *In vitro* studies indicated 0.2 \mu g/mL penpulimab was associated with a receptor occupancy close to 90%. Population PK analysis showed that, with a penpulimab dose of 200 mg Q2W, the steady-state trough concentration in more than 97.5% of patients was &gt;10 \mu g/mL, which is 100-fold higher than those at which 80% of the receptors (0.1 \mu g/mL) could be occupied or 20-fold higher than those at which 90%~100% of the receptors (0.5 \mu g/mL) could be occupied.
L2392: Simulated drug concentrations of 379 patients after IV infusion of penpulimab with fixed dose regimen (200 mg Q2W) and body weighted adjustment (3 mg/kg Q2W) using a population PK model showed that the trough concentration, peak concentration, and variation range were basically the same between these two regimen, thus supporting a fixed dose regimen of 200 mg Q2W.
L2393: A logistic regression analysis was performed to examine exposure-efficacy and exposure-safety relationship. Results of the univariable analyses evaluating relationships between efficacy (Complete response or partial response: CR/PR) and PK exposure (observed serum concentration at: Cycle 1 Day 1 end of infusion, Cycle 2 Day 1 before infusion, Cycle 5 Day 1 before infusion, or simulated steady state exposure) demonstrated a relatively flat exposure-response relationship without dose dependency.
L2394: The relationship between PK exposure (the same as above) and safety response (any TRAE or TRAE \ge 3) were analyzed by logistics regression. And the result showed no apparent correlation between safety and drug exposure.
L2395: ## The Applicant's Position
L2396: The recommended dosage of penpulimab was 200 mg Q2W administered with intravenous infusion (IV). Dose selection is based on the following considerations:
L2397: - In all patients treated with penpulimab therapy, penpulimab 200 mg Q2W was safe and tolerable, and there was no apparent correlation between the incidence of TRAEs and drug exposure.
L2398: ## Penpulimab
L2399: - Penpulimab 200 mg Q2W showed notable clinical benefits in patients with NPC and demonstrated a relatively flat exposure-response curve without apparent correlation between efficacy (CR/PR) and drug exposure.
- In vitro studies showed that 0.5 µg/mL of penpulimab resulted in 90-100% receptor occupancy. According to the population PK analysis of penpulimab, at the dose of 200 mg Q2W, more than 97.5 % of patients achieved steady-state trough concentrations (10 µg/mL), which is at or above 20 times the concentrations corresponding to 90-100% receptor occupancy.
- In all patients who received penpulimab 200 mg Q2W, treatment emergent ADA did not have a significant impact on PK exposure.
- Population PK modeling analysis showed that concentration ranges of penpulimab following administration at a fixed dose (200 mg Q2W) and at a body-weighted dose (3 mg/kg Q2W) were basically consistent, supporting the 200 mg Q2W fixed-dose regimen.
L2400: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2401: FDA generally agrees that the proposed recommended dosage of 200 mg Q2W is supported by the observed clinical safety, efficacy, and pharmacodynamic data. FDA disagrees that it can be concluded that the E-R analysis for efficacy is relatively flat; FDA concludes that the E-R analysis for efficacy is inconclusive given that the analysis only included one dose level. FDA also found no correlation between safety and penpulimab exposure given FDA did not identify any apparent correlation between the incidence of TEAEs and drug exposure (refer to Appendix 19.4 for details).
L2402: As Study AK105-202 was conducted in China, FDA assessed whether the proposed penpulimab dosing regimen is appropriate for the US population. Simulation based on PPK model showed similar exposure of penpulimab in US virtual patients as compared to that in Chinese patients with mean exposure (C_{max,ss}, C_{min,ss}, and AUC_{ss}) difference less than 20% between these two populations. Therefore, the 200 mg Q2W dosing regimen appears appropriate for US patients based on PK comparability with Chinese patients.
L2403: ## 5.2.2. Therapeutic Individualization
L2404: ## Data
L2405: Intrinsic factors that might have an impact on penpulimab PK were subjected to covariate analysis by the population PK approach to identify main contributors to the PK variability.
L2406: Covariate analysis was performed with respect to sex, age, race, weight, immunogenicity, hepatic function related factors [including total bilirubin (TBIL), aspartate aminotransferase (AST), alanine aminotransferase (ALT), alkaline phosphatase (ALP)], renal function related factors (creatinine clearance (CLCr) estimated by Cockcroft Gault formula), serum albumin
L2407: (ALB), lactic dehydrogenase (LDH), tumor type, and Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) score.
L2408: The results of the covariate analyses demonstrated that body weight and sex had statistically significant effects on CL and V_1, and ALB and ECOG had significant effects on CL. Initial analysis found a trend of AST effect on V_2, which may result from the influence of an extreme value; the final covariate model removed the effect of AST on V_2. To further investigate the impacts of covariates on PK of penpulimab, the relationship between the identified covariates and exposure in steady state was assessed. Impacts of covariates retained in the final model on the area under concentration-time curve within the dosing interval in steady state (AUC_{SS}), the steady-state maximum observed concentration (C_{max,SS}) and the steady-state minimum observed concentration (C_{trough,SS}) are described in Table 4 the table below. Based on these results, FDA concludes that the effects of these covariates were not clinically meaningful (defined as within 30% percentage of change).

L2414: ## Age
L2415: Among the 379 patients included into the population PK analysis set, the median age was 51 years (range: 18-91 years); patients &gt;65 years of age accounted for 20.1% (n=76). Age within this range did not have a statistically significant impact on population PK parameters of penpulimab.
L2416: ## Sex
L2417: Females accounted for 37.2% (n=141), and males accounted for 62.8% (n=238). The CL and apparent volume of distribution of central compartment V_1 of penpulimab were 15.9% and 12.8% lower in females than in males with the same weight and conditions, indicating sex had a statistically significant impact on CL and V_1. The female/male geometric mean ratio of exposure indicators AUC_{SS}, C_{max,SS} and C_{trough,SS} was 0.866, 1.002 and 0.794, respectively; namely, the
L2418: differences were all within 30%. Overall, the impact of sex on PK exposure was not clinically significant.
L2419: ## Race
L2420: There were 291 (76.8%) Asians, and 88 (23.2 %) non-Asian patients. Population PK covariate screening results showed that race did not have a statistically significant impact on population PK parameters of penpulimab.
L2421: ## Body Weight
L2422: The median weight was 62.0 kg (range: 35.2 - 113.0 kg). Population PK covariate screening results showed that penpulimab Volume of distribution of central compartment (V_1) increased with CL and body weight. In a patient with an extremely low weight (42 kg, 2.5% quantile) comparing to the patient with median weight (62 kg), V_1 and CL was 16.0% and 13.2% lower, respectively; In a patient with an extremely high weight (98.46 kg, 97.5% quantile) comparing to the patient with median weight (62 kg), V_1 and CL was 29.2% and 24.1% higher, respectively. Weight had a statistically significant impact on CL and V_1. The geometric mean ratios of PK parameters (AUC_{SS}, C_{max,SS}, C_{trough,SS}) in patients with weights at 10% and 90% quantile levels versus the typical patient (weight at the median level) were between 0.8 and 1.2; namely, the differences were less than 30%. Hence, it is considered that the impact of weight on AUC_{SS}, C_{max,SS} and C_{trough,SS} was not clinically significant.
L2423: ## Renal Impairment
L2424: In accordance with the Guidance on Pharmacokinetics in Patients with Impaired Renal Function released by FDA in 2020, renal impairment in the 379 patients included into the population PK analysis set was graded by creatinine clearance (CLCr). The grading criteria and included patients are summarized as follows. Normal renal function, CLCr \ge 90 mL/min (n=220); mild renal impairment: CLCr &lt; 90 mL/min and CLCr \ge 60 mL/min (n=113); moderate renal impairment: CLCr &lt; 60 mL/min and CLCr \ge 30 mL/min (n=45); 1 patient with severe renal impairment (CLCr &lt; 30 mL/min) has been included.
L2425: Through population PK covariate screening, it was found that the renal function related factor (CLCr) did not have a statistically significant impact on population PK parameters of penpulimab. The results showed that there was no significant difference in clearance between patients with mild or moderate renal impairment and patients with normal renal function.
L2426: Data of penpulimab in patients with severe renal impairment are not adequate.
L2427: ## Hepatic Impairment
L2428: Hepatic impairment of the 379 patients included into the population PK analysis set was graded by Total bilirubin (TBIL) and Aspartate Aminotransferase (AST). Grading criteria and included
L2429: ## Penpulimab
L2430: patients are summarized as follows. Normal hepatic function: TBIL&lt;upper limit of normal (ULN) and AST&lt;ULN(n=286); mild hepatic impairment: TBIL\leULN and AST&gt;ULN or TBIL=1.0 to 1.5\timesULN, regardless of AST (n=84); moderate hepatic impairment: TBIL=1.5 to 3.0\timesULN, regardless of AST(n=9); no patient with severe hepatic impairment (TBIL&gt;3.0\timesULN, regardless of AST) has been included.
L2431: Through population PK covariate screening, it was found that the hepatic function related factors (TBIL, AST, Alanine Aminotransferase [ALT] and Alkaline Phosphatase [ALP]) did not have a statistically significant impact on population PK parameters of penpulimab. The results showed that there was no significant difference in clearance between patients with mild or moderate hepatic impairment and patients with normal hepatic function. Data of penpulimab in patients with severe hepatic impairment are not adequate.
L2432: ## Tumor Type
L2433: Among the 379 patients included into the population PK analysis set, there were 130 (34.3%) patients with nasopharyngeal carcinoma and 249 (65.7%) with other tumor types. Population PK covariate screening results showed that tumor type did not have a statistically significant impact on population PK parameters of penpulimab.
L2434: ## Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) Score
L2435: Among the 379 patients included into the population PK analysis set, there were 120 (31.7%) patients with an Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) score of 0 and 259 (68.3%) patients with an ECOG score of 1. Population PK covariate screening results showed that ECOG score did not have a statistically significant impact on PK parameters of penpulimab.
L2436: ## Serum Albumin
L2437: Among the 379 patients included into the population PK analysis set, the median albumin (ALB) was 42.50 g/L (range: 22.00 - 54.50 g/L). Population PK covariate screening results showed that penpulimab CL decreased with increase of ALB; Clearance (CL) of the patient with ALB at the 2.5% quantile level was 25.6% higher than CL of the patient with ALB at the median level; CL of the patient with ALB at the 97.5% quantile was 12.30% lower than CL of the patient with ALB at the median level. Therefore, ALB had a statistically significant impact on CL. The geometric mean ratios of PK parameters (AUC_{SS}, C_{max,SS}, C_{trough,SS}) in patients with ALB at 10% or 90% quantile levels versus the typical patient (ALB at the median level) were between 0.8 and 1.1; namely, the differences were less than 30%. Hence, the impact of ALB on AUC_{SS}, C_{max,SS} and C_{trough,SS} was not clinically significant.
L2438: ## Impacts of Extrinsic Factors on PK
L2439: Impacts of extrinsic factors (e.g., food, alcohol, or smoking) on penpulimab PK have not been assessed in this study.
L2440: ## Impacts of Immunogenicity on PK
L2441: Impacts of post-baseline positive/negative ADA on PK parameters were analyzed by population PK analysis approach. Population PK covariate screening results showed that immunogenicity did not have a statistically significant impact on population PK parameters of penpulimab.
L2442: ## **The Applicant's Position**
L2443: The population PK profile of penpulimab could be described using a two-compartmental model with first order elimination. Covariate analysis showed that age, race, immunogenicity, hepatic function related factors (TBIL, AST, ALT, ALP), renal function (CLCr), tumor type and LDH score did not have an impact on PK parameters of penpulimab; the impacts of weight, ALB, ECOG and sex on CL and impacts of weight and sex on V_1 were statistically significant, but these factors were not clinically significant.
L2444: According to population PK analysis results, compared to patients with normal renal function, mild or moderate renal impairment did not have a significant impact on PK and does not necessitate dose modifications; compared to patients with normal hepatic function, mild or moderate hepatic impairment did not have a significant impact on PK and does not necessitate dose modifications; data in patients with severe hepatic or renal function are inadequate.
L2445: The recommended dosage of penpulimab was 200 mg Q2W administered intravenous infusion (IV). This fixed dose (200 mg Q2W) with consideration to the covariate estimated and analyzed by PopPk is appropriate.
L2446: No dose adjustment is required based on age, race, body weight or sex. No dose adjustment is required in patients with mild, moderate renal or hepatic impairment, data of penpulimab in patients with severe renal or hepatic impairment are not adequate.
L2447: Use of penpulimab in this age group is supported by evidence from adequate and well-controlled studies of penpulimab in adults with additional population pharmacokinetic data demonstrating that age and body weight had no clinically meaningful effect on the steady state exposure of penpulimab
L2448: (b) (4)
L2449: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2450: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that no dosage adjustment is required based on intrinsic factors including age (18-91 years), sex, body weight (35-113 kg), serum albumin (22-54.5 g/L), baseline LDH, ECOG score, tumor type, and mild or moderate renal and hepatic impairment according to the results of PPK analyses. However, as 77% of subjects in the population PK dataset were from a single region (China) and the non-Asian population in the population PK analysis does not represent the racial and ethnic diversity of the US population, the effect of race and ethnicity on the PK of penpulimab has not been fully characterized. As a post-marketing requirement, the Applicant will conduct a multiregional randomized clinical trial that includes sufficient representation of racial and ethnic subpopulations reflecting the US population of patients with non-keratinizing nasopharyngeal carcinoma to confirm the clinical benefit of penpulimab in the intended patient population. The Applicant will collect sparse PK samples in the trial for supportive PPK analyses of the effect of race and ethnicity on penpulimab PK.
L2451: (b) (4)
L2452: ## 5.2.3. Outstanding Issues
L2453: ## Data
L2454: Not Applicable.
L2455: ## The Applicant's Position
L2456: Not Applicable.
L2457: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2458: There are no outstanding issues.
L2459: ## 5.3. Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review
L2460: ## 5.3.1. General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics
L2461: ## Data
L2462: The clinical pharmacology of penpulimab has been studied in patients with malignant solid tumors such as NPC or non-solid tumors such as cHL who received penpulimab by intravenous infusion administration once every 2 weeks. Levels of serum penpulimab were measured using validated methods and the results were used to evaluate PK parameters.
L2463: PK data of penpulimab were derived from 4 clinical studies. A total of 379 patients were included into population PK analysis, and 320 patients (including 31 who underwent intensive sampling) into non-compartmental model-based PK analysis. The above patients received penpulimab at 1, 3 or 10 mg/kg or 200 mg Q2W. The PK profile is outlined as follows:
L2464: ## Absorption
L2465: Penpulimab is administered via IV infusion, and the drug directly enters blood circulation.
L2466: ## Distribution
L2467: As the sampling interval after the first dose was short (Day 1 to Day 15), the serum concentration of penpulimab had not reached the terminal part of the elimination phase, so V_z calculated in non-compartmental PK analysis was analyzed based on population PK simulated data.
L2468: According to population PK simulation, following administration of penpulimab 200 mg as a single IV infusion, the V_z (\pmSD) of penpulimab was 7.18 \pm 2.52 L, and the V_{ss} (\pmSD) was 6.59 \pm 2.01 L.
L2469: ## Elimination
L2470: As the sampling interval after the first dose was short (Day 1 to Day 15), the serum concentration of penpulimab had not reached the terminal part of the elimination phase, so CL and t_{1/2} calculated in non-compartmental PK analysis were analyzed based on population PK simulated data.
L2471: ## Penpulimab
L2472: According to population PK simulation, following administration of penpulimab 200 mg as a single IV infusion, the CL(\pmSD) of penpulimab was 0.223 \pm 0.0740 L/d, and the half-life(t_{1/2})(\pmSD) was 23.9 \pm 9.98 d.
L2473: ## Linearity/Non-Linearity
L2474: According to the non-compartmental analysis, the drug exposure, expressed as C_{max} and AUC_{(0-14d)}, increased proportionally with dose within the dose range of 1 mg/kg to 10 mg/kg following single-dose IV infusion of penpulimab. Following multi-dose IV infusion of penpulimab (200 mg Q2W, n=11), the mean accumulation rate (\pmSD) calculated based on C_{max} and AUC on Cycle3Day15 (the 6th dose) was 1.84 \pm 0.495 and 2.69 \pm 0.749, respectively.
L2475: ## Steady-State
L2476: According to population PK simulation, the penpulimab concentration was approximate to the steady state at Week 16 following IV infusion of penpulimab 200 mg Q2W. The mean steady-state trough concentration (\pmSD) at Week 16 was 49.4 \pm 17.5 \mug/mL.
L2477: ## Other Population PK Analysis Results
L2478: The population PK profile of penpulimab could be described using a two-compartmental model with first order elimination. Typical values of penpulimab PK parameters based on the final model were as follows: CL, 0.233 L/day; V_1, 3.40 L; distribution rate from central compartment to peripheral compartment (Q), 0.706 L/day; volume of distribution of peripheral compartment (V_2) 3.02 L. Covariate analysis showed that age, race, immunogenicity, hepatic function related factors (TBIL, AST, ALT, ALP), renal function (CLCr), tumor type and LDH score did not have an impact on PK parameters of penpulimab; the impacts of weight, ALB, ECOG and sex on CL and impacts of weight and sex on V_1 were statistically significant, but these factors were not clinically significant.
L2479: According to population PK analysis results, compared to patients with normal renal function, mild or moderate renal impairment did not have a significant impact on PK and does not necessitate dose modifications; compared to patients with normal hepatic function, mild or moderate hepatic impairment did not have a significant impact on PK and does not necessitate dose modifications; data in patients with severe hepatic or renal function are inadequate.
L2480: ## Immunogenicity Results
L2481: In clinical studies in patients treated with penpulimab at a dose of 1 mg/kg every 2 weeks, 3 mg/kg every 2 weeks, 200 mg every 2 weeks, or 10 mg/kg every 2 weeks, 43 (12.3%) of 349 evaluable patients tested positive for treatment-emergent anti-penpulimab antibodies of whom 14 (4.0%) patients had neutralizing antibodies against penpulimab. The treatment emergent ADA did not have a significant impact on the PK exposure or safety.
L2482: ## Penpulimab
L2483: For NPC patients receiving penpulimab 200 mg Q2W, the incidence of ADA positive after administration was 5.9% (7/119), with neutralizing antibodies detected in 3.4% (4/119) patients. Treatment emergent ADA did not have a significant impact on PK exposure, safety, or efficacy (CR/PR).
L2484: ## The Applicant's Position
L2485: The information from clinical studies contributing to the clinical pharmacology evaluation of penpulimab was included in the submitted reports (*Module 2.7.2 Summary of Clinical Pharmacology, Module 5.3.5.2 AK105-202 CSR*). Penpulimab exhibited linear PK (from dose 1 mg/kg to 10 mg/kg) with an estimated terminal half-life of 23.9 days and indicating a likely tendency of drug accumulation following multi-dose administration of penpulimab.
L2486: No dedicated clinical drug-drug interaction studies have been conducted in humans. Penpulimab is a humanized monoclonal antibody, PK interactions with other drugs have not been studied. As monoclonal antibodies are not metabolized by cytochrome P450 (CYP) or other drug metabolizing enzymes, inhibition, or induction of these enzymes by co-administered medicinal products is not anticipated to affect the pharmacokinetics of penpulimab.
L2487: Overall, penpulimab administered by intravenous infusion at fixed 200mg Q2W regimen, demonstrated a favorable benefit-risk profile for patients with metastatic NPC patients in patients with metastatic nonkeratinizing NPC with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of therapy.
L2488: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2489: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of the PK characteristics of penpulimab. Regarding the immunogenicity results, two different assays were used to characterize the ADAs and NAbs in the Phase 1 study conducted in Australia and the three Phase 2 studies conducted in China, respectively. (b) (4)
L2490: In an Information Request, the Applicant was asked to re-analyze the immunogenicity data by considering ADA positivity as the occurrence of treatment-emergent ADAs at one or more post-dose time points relative to the baseline for each individual patient. According to the updated analysis, of the 119 evaluable patients in Study AK105-202 with NPC who received penpulimab 200 mg Q2W, 27% of patients tested positive for treatment emergent ADA and 7% of patients for treatment-emergent NAb. Considering all clinical studies conducted in China using the same bioanalytical assay at the proposed recommended dosage of 200 mg Q2W, 34% of patients tested positive for treatment emergent ADA and 7% of patients for treatment emergent NAb. Per PPK analysis, treatment emergent ADA had no significant impact on penpulimab PK. There is insufficient information to characterize the effects of anti-penpulimab antibodies on the safety and efficacy of penpulimab.
L2491: ## 5.3.2. Clinical Pharmacology Questions
L2492: ## 5.3.2.1. *Does the Clinical Pharmacology Program Provide Supportive Evidence of Effectiveness?*
L2493: Yes, the clinical pharmacology program provides supportive evidence of penpulimab effectiveness in patients with metastatic nonkeratinizing NPC with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of therapy.
L2494: ## Data
L2495: For AK105-202 study, patients with stage IVb NPC who have failed at least 2 lines of prior therapy containing platinum-based chemotherapy were enrolled in the study. Among the 111 patients included in the primary efficacy population, 33 patients had a BOR of confirmed CR or PR based on IRRC assessment, with a confirmed ORR of 29.7% (95% CI: 21.4%, 39.1%).
L2496: To support the clinical efficacy observed with penpulimab dosed at fixed 200mg Q2W regimen, exposure-efficacy relationship was evaluated in NPC population (AK105-202 study), see [Module 2.7.2, Section 3.3.1]. Univariate logistic regression analysis was conducted to assess the relationship between penpulimab PK exposures and model-predicted ORR response (CR/PR). Results of the univariate analyses demonstrated a relatively flat exposure-response curve without apparent correlation between efficacy (CR/PR) and PK exposure (observed serum concentration: Cycle 1 Day 1 end of infusion, Cycle 2 Day 1 before infusion, Cycle 5 Day 1 before infusion, or simulated steady state exposure such as AUC_{t,ss}, C_{max,ss}, C_{trough,ss}).
L2497: ## The Applicant's Position
L2498: Penpulimab 200 mg Q2W showed notable clinical benefits in patients with metastatic NPC and demonstrated a relatively flat exposure-response curve as above without apparent correlation between efficacy (CR/PR) and drug exposure. Penpulimab therapy is recommended treatment until disease progression or intolerable toxicity to allow continued blockade of the PD-1 pathway. The safety profile of penpulimab 200 mg Q2W in 130 patients with NPC from AK105-202 is consistent with the well-characterized safety profile of penpulimab 200 mg/kg Q2W in 372 patients with various solid tumors or blood tumors and in line with the known safety profile of other mAbs with similar mechanism of action.
L2499: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2500: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position. However, FDA notes that the Applicant's E-R analysis for efficacy is inconclusive due to limited exposure range of penpulimab at one dose level.
L2501: ## **5.3.2.2. Is the Proposed Dosing Regimen Appropriate for the General Patient Population for Which the Indication Is Being Sought?**
L2502: Yes, penpulimab administered by intravenous infusion at 200mg Q2W regimen is appropriate for patients with metastatic nonkeratinizing NPC with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of therapy. The proposed dosing regimen is supported by the observed efficacy, safety, and PK data.
L2503: ## **Data**
L2504: In the Phase Ia dose-escalation trial, no DLT occurred at the highest dose cohort of 10 mg/kg Q2W and no MTD was explored, therefore the maximum administered dose(MAD) for this study was 10 mg/kg Q2W. Phase Ia PK data showed a linear relationship between exposure of penpulimab and dose over the dose range of 1 mg/kg Q2W to 10 mg/kg Q2W; receptor occupancy analysis showed 80% to 100% PD-1 receptor occupancy starting on day 2 after dosing. There was no significant difference in receptor occupancy between the 1, 3 and 10 mg/kg doses. According to the non-compartmental analysis, the drug exposure, expressed as Cmax and AUC(0-14d), increased proportionally with dose within the dose range of 1 mg/kg to 10 mg/kg following single-dose IV infusion of penpulimab.
L2505: In vitro studies showed that 0.5 µg/mL of penpulimab resulted in 90-100% receptor occupancy. According to the population PK analysis of penpulimab, at the dose of 200 mg Q2W, more than 97.5 % of patients achieved steady-state trough concentrations (10µg/ml), which is at or above 20 times the concentrations corresponding to 90-100% RO.
L2506: Univariate logistic regression analysis was conducted to assess the relationship between penpulimab PK exposures and model-predicted ORR response (CR/PR). Results of the univariate analyses demonstrated a relatively flat exposure-response curve without apparent correlation between efficacy (CR/PR) and PK exposure (observed serum concentration: Cycle 1 Day 1 end of infusion, Cycle 2 Day 1 before infusion, Cycle 5 Day 1 before infusion, or simulated steady state exposure such as AUCt,SS, Cmax,SS, Ctrough,SS).
L2507: Similarly, univariate logistic regression analyses were performed to evaluate the relationship between safety and PK exposure. The same reference points described above were used for PK exposure, while safety endpoints were any TRAE or TRAE ≥ grade 3 occurred in the integrated penpulimab 200 mg population from 4 clinical monotherapy studies. The analysis showed no apparent correlation between safety parameters of interest and PK exposure.
L2508: The population PK result showed that age, race, immunogenicity, hepatic function related factors (TBIL, AST, ALT, ALP), renal function (CLCr), tumor type and LDH score have no impact on PK parameters of penpulimab. While weight, ALB, ECOG and sex on CL and impacts of weight and sex on V1 were statistically significant, but these factors were not clinically significantly relevant. Renal function and hepatic function did not have a significant impact on PK and does not necessitate dose modifications; data in patients with severe hepatic or renal function are inadequate.
L2509: ## The Applicant's Position
L2510: The recommended dose of penpulimab is 200 mg Q2W via intravenous infusion (IV). The dose is selected based on efficacy and safety results, population PK modeling, simulation analyses, and ER analysis, we well as pharmacokinetic and pharmacodynamic analysis. These data and analysis support the selection of 200 mg Q2W as the dosing regimen for proposed indication.
L2511: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2512: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position. Refer to FDA's assessment in Section 6.2.1 on general dosing for more details.
L2513: ## 5.3.2.3. *Is an Alternative Dosing Regimen or Management Strategy Required for Subpopulations Based on Intrinsic Patient Factors?*
L2514: No, penpulimab administered by intravenous infusion at 200mg Q2W regimen is appropriate for patients with metastatic nonkeratinizing NPC with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of therapy.
L2515: ## Data
L2516: The population PK profile of penpulimab could be described using a two-compartmental model with first order elimination. Covariate analysis showed that age, race, hepatic function related factors (TBIL, AST, ALT, ALP), renal function (CLCr), tumor type and LDH score did not have an impact on PK parameters of penpulimab; the impacts of weight, ALB, ECOG and sex on CL and impacts of weight and sex on V_1 were statistically significant, but these factors were not clinically significantly relevant. There was no significant difference in clearance between patients with different grades of renal function or hepatic function.
L2517: In summary, mild or moderate renal impairment did not have a significant impact on penpulimab PK as compared with patients with normal renal function. Data of penpulimab in patients with severe renal impairment are not adequate. Mild or moderate hepatic impairment did not have a significant impact on penpulimab PK as compared with patients with normal hepatic function. Data of penpulimab in patients with severe hepatic impairment are not adequate.
L2518: ## The Applicant's Position
L2519: No dose adjustment is required based on age, race, body weight or sex. No dose adjustment is required in patients with mild, moderate, or severe renal impairment, data of penpulimab in patients with severe renal impairment are not adequate. No dose adjustment is required in patients with mild or moderate hepatic impairment. Data of penpulimab in patients with severe hepatic impairment are not adequate.
L2520: Use of penpulimab in this age group is supported by evidence from adequate and well-controlled studies of penpulimab in adults with additional population pharmacokinetic data demonstrating that age and body weight had no clinically meaningful effect on the steady state exposure of penpulimab, (b) (4)
L2521: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2522: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position. Refer to FDA's assessment in Section 6.2.2. on therapeutic individualization for more details.
L2523: ## 5.3.2.4. *Are There Clinically Relevant Food-Drug or Drug-Drug Interactions, and What Is the Appropriate Management Strategy?*
L2524: No, penpulimab is intended for intravenous infusion administration; therefore, food-drug interactions are not expected.
L2525: ## The Applicant's Position
L2526: Penpulimab is intended for intravenous infusion administration; therefore, food-drug interactions are not expected.
L2527: Penpulimab is a humanized monoclonal antibody, and PK interactions with other drugs have not been studied. As monoclonal antibodies are not metabolized by cytochrome P450 (CYP) or other drug metabolizing enzymes, inhibition, or induction of these enzymes by co-administered medicinal products is not anticipated to affect the pharmacokinetics of penpulimab.
L2528: Penpulimab belongs to a class of drugs called immune checkpoint inhibitors. Because the mechanism of action of this class of drugs is to block inhibitory signal that prevents activation of T cells, there is a potential that these drugs may cause immune-mediated adverse drug reactions. Withhold or permanently discontinue penpulimab depending on severity. In general, if penpulimab requires interruption or discontinuation, administer systemic corticosteroid therapy (1 mg to 2 mg/kg/day prednisone or equivalent) until improvement to Grade 1 or less. Upon improvement to Grade 1 or less, initiate corticosteroid taper and continue to taper over at least 1 month. Consider administration of other systemic immunosuppressants in patients whose immune-mediated adverse reactions are not controlled by corticosteroid therapy.
L2529: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2530: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that no food-drug or drug-drug interactions for penpulimab are anticipated.

L2548: ## 7. Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L2549: ## 7.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L2550: ## 7.1.1. Study AK105-202
L2551: ## 7.1.1.1. *Trial Design*
L2552: ## The Applicant's Description
L2553: Study AK105-202 is a single-arm, open-label, multicenter phase II clinical study to evaluate the efficacy and safety of penpulimab in patients with metastatic nonkeratinizing NPC with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of therapy. Patients were excluded from the study based on history of autoimmune disease, medical conditions requiring systemic immunosuppression, prior organ or allogeneic stem cell transplantation, prior treatment with anti-PD-1, anti-PD-L1 or CTLA-4 antibodies, active central nervous system (CNS) metastases, or ECOG performance score &gt; 2.
L2554: Patients received penpulimab 200 mg intravenously every 2 weeks until disease progression as determined by the investigator, or unacceptable toxicity, or a maximum of 24 months, whichever occurred first. Administration of penpulimab was permitted beyond RECIST-defined disease progression if the patients were assessed as clinically beneficial and tolerated to the study drug by the investigator. Tumor measurements were performed every 8 weeks in first year and every 12 weeks thereafter, to determine response by RECIST v1.1 criteria.
L2555: The primary objective of the study was to evaluate the objective response rate (ORR) assessed by blinded independent radiology review committee (IRRC) per RECIST v1.1. Secondary objectives included duration of response (DoR), progression-free survival (PFS) and overall survival (OS).
L2556: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2557: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the trial design for AK105-202. FDA notes that the study was conducted at 26 sites in China.
L2558: ## 7.1.1.2. *Study Endpoints*
L2559: ## The Applicant's Description
L2560: - Primary Efficacy Endpoint: ORR assessed by IRRRC per RECIST v1.1.ORR is defined as the proportion of patients whose best overall response (BOR) is confirmed CR or PR. The confirmation of CR or PR by repeated imaging assessments is performed not less than 28 days (4 weeks) after the first observation of a CR or PR, and there is no evidence of PD between the follow-up of the first observed CR or PR and the follow-up for confirmation of CR or PR. BOR is defined as the best response recorded from the start of treatment until progressive disease or the last evaluable tumor assessment without prior to initiation of subsequent anti-cancer therapy.
- Key Secondary Efficacy Endpoints: duration of response (DoR)
- Other Secondary Endpoints:ORR assessed by investigator, Disease control rate (DCR), time to response (TTR), PFS assessed by IRRRC and investigator per RECIST v1.1 and OS.
- Pharmacokinetic (PK) and immunogenicity assessment
- Safety: incidence and severity of adverse events, abnormal laboratory findings with clinical significance.
- Correlation analysis between the expression of EBV-DNA in blood and the anti-tumor activity of penpulimab.
L2561: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2562: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the study endpoints. The final efficacy analysis is based upon a data cutoff of February 3, 2021. In a single arm trial such as Study AK105-202, FDA considers time-to-event endpoints such as PFS and OS as not interpretable and descriptive only. The efficacy evaluation of this study was based on ORR and DOR.
L2563: ## 7.1.1.3. *Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments*
L2564: ## The Applicant's Description
L2565: The sample size calculation was based on the primary endpoint of ORR. A total of 110 patients were needed for an ORR of 15% to be excluded using the lower limit of a two-sided 95% CI. If an ORR of 26% is assumed for penpulimab, there is 82% chance of observing at least 25 responses (an ORR of 22.7%), with the lower limit of the 95% CI excluding a historical response rate of 15%. In this study, an interim analysis was originally planned after at least 110 patients were enrolled and completed at least 16 weeks of follow-up (an interval between the data cutoff date and the date of first dose \ge 16 weeks), and the results of the interim analysis was planned to be used for communicating with the regulatory agencies. There were no plans to
L2566: stop the trial based on either of these analyses and no formal statistical adjustments were planned.
L2567: Per communication with the FDA with respect to marketing application, in order to provide an adequate assessment of durability of response in a marketing application, all responders would have follow-up of at least 6 months past onset of response. Given the refractory population with metastatic NPC, approximately 50% of responders having follow up of at least 6 months past onset of response is acceptable to support filing of the market application. An interim analysis of efficacy was conducted after approximately 110 patients treated with penpulimab had an opportunity to be followed for at least 8 months prior to data cutoff date (to allow at least 6 months follow-up past onset of response assuming the response occurs at the first follow-up scan). The efficacy results from this interim analysis included in the initial submission for marketing application were presented below.
L2568: ## Definition of Key Analysis Sets
L2569: ## *Enrollment Analysis Set*
L2570: The Enrollment Analysis Set (ENR) includes all patients who have signed the ICF and been enrolled into the study.
L2571: ## *Primary Efficacy Population*
L2572: The Primary Efficacy Population includes all patients in the ENR who have received at least one dose of penpulimab and meet the following inclusion criteria:
L2573: - Patients diagnosed with disease stage of IVb in accordance with Chinese Nasopharyngeal Carcinoma Staging 2017 (2008 Expert Consensus on Nasopharyngeal Cancer Staging Revision) at enrollment.
- Patients who had disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of therapy. Prior neoadjuvant chemotherapy, concurrent chemoradiotherapy, or adjuvant chemotherapy would be considered as the first line of therapy if recurrence/metastasis occurs within 6 months since the last dose of chemotherapy. Switching treatment regimen due to intolerability were not counted as additional lines of therapy.
- Patients with at least one measurable lesion at baseline per RECIST v1.1 as assessed by IRRC if efficacy summary based on IRRC assessment, or at least one measurable lesion at baseline per RECIST v1.1 as assessed by the investigator if efficacy summary based on investigator assessment.
- Patients who had an opportunity to be followed for at least 8 months prior to data cutoff date (to allow at least 6 months follow-up past onset of response assuming the response occurs at the first follow-up scan).
L2574: ## *Safety Analysis Set*
L2575: The Safety Analysis Set includes all enrolled patients who have received at least one dose of penpulimab.
L2576: ## *Pharmacokinetics Set*
L2577: The pharmacokinetics set (PKS) includes all enrolled patients in the safety analysis set who have at least one quantifiable concentration data at the scheduled time point and no major and critical protocol violations affecting the concentration data.
L2578: ## *Immunogenicity Set*
L2579: The immunogenicity set (IMMS) includes all enrolled patients for safety analysis who have received at least one immunogenicity assessment at baseline and postbaseline, respectively.
L2580: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2581: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the statistical analysis plan.

L2606: ## 7.1.2.3. *Patient Disposition*
L2607: ## Data
L2608: A total of 171 patients were screened and 130 patients were enrolled. The reason for all screen failure was not meeting eligibility criteria. The first patient was enrolled on March 27, 2019, and the last patient was enrolled on September 14, 2020.
L2609: As of the data cutoff, 114 patients with metastatic NPC had an opportunity to be followed for at least 8 months (i.e., initiated penpulimab therapy at least 8 months prior to the data cut-off date). Of those 114 patients, 1 patient had only one line of prior therapy, 1 patient did not have measurable lesion at baseline as assessed by both the investigator and IRRC, and 1 patient did not have measurable lesion at baseline as assessed by IRRC.
L2610: At the data cutoff for the interim analysis, 111 patients comprised primary efficacy population that was used for efficacy data based on IRRC assessment, and 112 patients comprised primary efficacy population that was used for efficacy data based on investigator assessment. The median duration of follow-up was 14.7 months (range: 0.9 to 22.3) for the primary efficacy population. In the primary efficacy population, 28 patients (25.2%) were continuing on penpulimab, and 83 patients (74.8%) had patients discontinued penpulimab. Table 12 summarizes the reasons for discontinuation. The most common reason for discontinuation was radiographic disease progression (53.2%).

L2616: ## The Applicant's Position
L2617: As of data cutoff date, the median duration of follow-up was 14.7 months (range: 0.9 to 22.3) for the primary efficacy population. The data can be used to assess durability of response based on percentage of patients with DoR for at least 6 months, but the data was not mature for assessing the durability of response based on percentage of patients with DoR for at least 12 months and 12-months PFS rate.
L2618: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2619: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. At least 6 months of post-response follow up is generally considered adequate to evaluate the durability of responses.

L2668: ## 7.1.2.7. *Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use*
L2669: ## Data
L2670: Treatment compliance was not analyzed because penpulimab was administered as an intravenous infusion at the study center. No patient was discontinued from study treatment due to protocol non-compliance. The most common concomitant medications were anti-inflammatory and anti-rheumatic drugs, followed by analgesics.
L2671: ## The Applicant's Position
L2672: Noncompliance, concomitant, and rescue medication use are variables unlikely to affect the study results or the overall assessment of efficacy of penpulimab for patients with metastatic NPC.
L2673: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2674: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L2675: ## 7.1.2.8. *Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses)*
L2676: ## Data
L2677: Among the 111 patients included in the primary efficacy population, 33 patients had a BOR of CR or PR based on IRRC assessment, with a confirmed ORR of **29.7%** (95% CI: **21.4%, 39.1%**).
L2678: Table 12 provides further details of the responses for the primary efficacy population.

L2683: Response rates were compared across several subgroups of interest to examine consistency of effect. These subgroups were defined by age, [REDACTED] ECOG performance status score, PD-L1 expression, tumor stage at study entry, liver metastasis, number of metastatic sites, prior line of therapy, prior radiotherapy, prior radiotherapy to primary lesions, EBV DNA level in peripheral blood at baseline, and LDH at baseline. The comparison of response rates across these subgroups are presented in Figure 9.
L2684: Subgroup analyses based on IRRC assessment showed that tumor response was observed across all subgroups (with exception for age &gt; 65 years [n=2]). While numerical differences in point estimates across some subgroups were noted, sample sizes were small and 95% CIs overlapped for most subgroup comparisons. In the subgroups, the patients with positive PD-L1 expression (defined as TPS\ge 50% using Shuwen SAB-028), baseline LDH &lt; ULN or EBV-DNA level in peripheral blood &lt; 500 IU/mL at baseline seems to have a higher response rate. It was also noticed that the ORRs in key subgroups were comparable, such as [REDACTED] prior line of therapy, patients with liver metastasis, disease status (primary or recurrent metastasis).

L2688: ## The Applicant's Position
L2689: Results based on an interim analysis of ORR demonstrate substantial evidence of effectiveness of penpulimab monotherapy in patients with metastatic nonkeratinizing NPC with disease progression on or after at least two prior lines of therapy, a serious disease where there is a
L2690: clear unmet medical need. The primary analysis showed promising ORR by IRRC in the Primary Efficacy Population. The confirmed ORR of 29.7% (95% CI: 21.4%, 39.1%) compares favorably with the overall response rate of &lt; 10% of historical commonly used chemotherapy agents.
L2691: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2692: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's summary and position of the efficacy results of the primary endpoint of ORR as assessed by IRRC. Note that FDA considers DCR to be an exploratory endpoint only. The subgroup results of ORR by PD-L1 expression (TPS &lt;50 vs. TPS ≥50% by the Shuwen SAB-028 assay) were summarized in Figure 9. Table 13 provides the results of an exploratory subgroup analysis of ORR by PD-L1 TPS &lt;1%, TPS 1-49%, and TPS ≥50%. The response rates across PD-L1 expression levels are somewhat discrepant, with a lower ORR observed for patients with PD-L1 &lt; 50%, although the 95% confidence intervals overlap with those observed at patients with PD-L1 TPS ≥ 50%. Given that this post-hoc analysis is exploratory in nature and that the impact of PD-L1 status and cutoff on NPC outcomes is not well defined, the significance of these results is unknown and should be interpreted with caution. In addition, the ORR of 21% (95% CI: 12, 33) for patients with TPS &lt;50% likely represents an improvement over available therapies for this refractory population.

L2708: ## 7.1.2.10. *Efficacy Results – Secondary and Other Relevant Endpoints*
L2709: ## Data
L2710: ## DoR and TTR
L2711: The median TTR in the primary efficacy population was 1.84 months (range: 1.64 to 7.39 months). As of the data cutoff date, the longest durable response was 17.05+ months (ongoing), 75.8% of responders remained ongoing, and the median DoR has not reached. The DoR \ge 6 months was 72.7% (24/33) at the data cutoff. The Kaplan-Meier estimate for the percent of patients with a 6-month duration of response is 93.4% (95% CI: 75.9%, 98.3%), and the estimate for the percent of patients with a 9-month duration of response is 77.6% (95% CI: 52.6%, 90.4%).

L2740: ## **The Applicant's Position**
L2741: Results based on an interim analysis of additional secondary efficacy variables including DoR, PFS and OS demonstrate substantial evidence of effectiveness of penpulimab monotherapy in patients with metastatic nonkeratinizing NPC with disease progression on or after at least two prior lines of therapy, a serious disease where there is a clear unmet medical need.
L2742: The responses were durable. After a median follow-up time of 14.7 months, median DoR has not yet been reached at the time of DCO, the DoR \ge 6 months was 72.7% (24/33), and 25 (75.8%) of 33 responders had an ongoing response. The OS results were encouraging in this heavily pretreated NPC population. The median OS was 18.63 months, and the 9-month OS rate was 73.6% (95% CI: 64.1%, 80.9%). The percentage of DoR \ge 6 months, median OS, and the 9-month OS rate compare favorably with those seen with historical commonly used chemotherapy agents.
L2743: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2744: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's summary of the efficacy results of DoR. Per FDA's request, the Applicant also submitted the updated DoR data with a DCO of May 12, 2021, for the 33 patients who had a response as assessed by IRRC. The data was reviewed and included in the product labeling. At the updated DCO, the range of the DoR was (3.8+, 22.3+) and the median DoR was still not reached; the percentage of responding patients with DoR \ge 6 months was 85% and the percentage of responding patients with DoR \ge 9 months was 73%. The updated DoR data showed a longer durability of response compared to the data from the DCO of February 3, 2021.

L2765: ## 7.1.2.11. *Dose/Dose Response*
L2766: ## Data
L2767: Not Applicable.
L2768: ## The Applicant's Position
L2769: All patients with NPC in the primary efficacy population received penpulimab 200 mg every two weeks. Therefore, dose response relationships for efficacy could not be evaluated. Univariate logistic regression analyses showed a flat relationship between penpulimab PK exposures and model-predicted ORR (CR/PR).
L2770: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2771: The FDA agrees that a dose response relationship is not evaluable given the Q2W dosing regimen of penpulimab.
L2772: ## 7.1.2.12. *Durability of Response*
L2773: ## Data
L2774: A summary of DoR and TTR in the primary efficacy population is presented in Table 14 provides a graphic representation of time to response and duration of response of the confirmed responders in the primary efficacy population.
L2775: ## The Applicant's Position
L2776: As of the data cutoff date, after a median follow-up time of 14.7 months, 75.8% of responders remained ongoing, the median DoR has not reached, and the DoR \ge 6 months was 72.7%. The Kaplan-Meier estimate for patients with a 6-month duration of response is 93.4% (95% CI: 75.9%, 98.3%), and the estimate for patients with a 9-month duration of response is 77.6% (95% CI: 52.6%, 90.4%).
L2777: Given refractory population with metastatic NPC with disease progression on or after at least two prior lines of therapy, the responses were durable, which compare favorably with those seen with historical commonly used chemotherapy agents.
L2778: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2779: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position regarding the durability of response.
L2780: ## 7.1.2.13. *Persistence of Effect*
L2781: ## Data
L2782: Not Applicable.
L2783: ## The Applicant's Position
L2784: As of data cut off, the median duration of follow-up was 14.7 months. The data can be used to assess durability of response based on percentage of patients with DoR for at least 6 months, but the data was not mature enough for assessing the durability of response for a longer period. Additional follow-up past onset of response was warranted to better estimate the DoR and assess persistence effect of penpulimab monotherapy in patients with metastatic NPC.
L2785: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2786: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.

L2806: ## 7.1.3. Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
L2807: This section is not applicable because the application is supported by one pivotal trial (AK105-202).
L2808: ## 8. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L2809: ## Data
L2810: The key efficacy results for the primary efficacy population were summarized below:
L2811: - ORR: Confirmed ORR assessed by IRRC was 29.7% (95% CI: 21.4%, 39.1%).
- DCR: The DCR assessed by IRRC was 49.5% (95% CI: 39.9%, 59.2%).
- DoR: At of the data cutoff, 75.8% of responders had ongoing response, the median DoR has not reached, and the DoR \ge 6 months was 72.7%. The Kaplan-Meier estimate for patients with a 6-month DoR is 93.4% (95% CI: 75.9%, 98.3%), and the estimate for patients with a 9-month DoR is 77.6% (95% CI: 52.6%, 90.4%).
- PFS: Median PFS assessed by IRRC was 3.65 months (95% CI: 1.87, 6.60) and the 9-month PFS rate assessed by IRRC was 37.1% (95% CI: 27.8%, 46.4%).
- OS: The median OS was 18.63 months (95% CI: 13.77, not estimable), and the 9-month OS rate was 73.6% (95% CI: 64.1%, 80.9%).
L2812: ## The Applicant's Position
L2813: Results based on an interim analysis of ORR and DoR demonstrate substantial evidence of effectiveness of penpulimab monotherapy in patients with metastatic nonkeratinizing NPC with disease progression on or after at least two prior lines of therapy, a serious disease where there is a clear unmet medical need. The primary analysis showed promising ORR and DoR by IRRC in the Primary Efficacy Population. The confirmed ORR of 29.7% (95% CI: 21.4%, 39.1%) compares favorably with the overall response rate of &lt; 10% of historical commonly used chemotherapy agents. The responses were durable. After a median follow-up time of 14.7 months, median DoR has not yet been reached at the time of DCO, the DoR \ge 6 months was 72.7% (24/33), and 25 (75.8%) of 33 responders had an ongoing response.
L2814: The OS results were encouraging in this heavily pretreated NPC population. The median OS was 18.63 months (95% CI: 13.77, not estimable), and the 9-month OS rate was 73.6% (95% CI: 64.1%, 80.9%).
L2815: Collectively, these data provide compelling evidence of the clinically meaningful benefit of penpulimab 200mg Q2W in patients with metastatic nonkeratinizing NPC with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of therapy.
L2816: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2817: The assessment of efficacy of penpulimab in patients with metastatic NPC with disease progression on platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other therapy was based on results from the primary efficacy population enrolled on Study AK105-202, a single arm trial conducted entirely in China. The ORR observed in the primary efficacy population was 30% (95% CI 21, 39). The median DOR was not reached (range 3.8, 17.1+); 39% of responders had a DOR \ge 9 months.
L2818: The overall response rate and duration of response do not provide a meaningful advantage over available therapy given the recent approvals of toripalimab-tpxi for the treatment of patients with metastatic or recurrent, locally advanced NPC, with overlapping 95% confidence intervals around the ORR observed in a similar population of patients who received toripalimab-tpxi.
L2819: Another major limitation of the data is that the primary trial supporting this marketing application was conducted exclusively in China and all patients enrolled were Asian. For discussion of this issue, see Section 1 and Section 9.2 of this review. Regulatory flexibility is not warranted in this instance as penpulimab does not address an unmet need.
L2820: ## 8.1. Review of Safety
L2821: ## Data
L2822: The integrated safety analysis of penpulimab include 372 patient's data from the following four studies:
L2823: - AK105-101 (phase Ib)
- AK105-201
- AK105-202
- AK105-204
L2824: The primary evidence for the safety of penpulimab in patients with metastatic NPC comes from the pivotal study AK105-202.
L2825: The study AK105-101 phase Ib, AK105-201, AK105-202, and AK105-204, which comprise all patients treated with 200 mg Q2W penpulimab monotherapy for various tumor types, will provide safety data in support of the penpulimab safety profile.
L2826: ## The Applicant's Position
L2827: Data from the pivotal study AK105-202 allowed for a comprehensive and adequate assessment of the overall benefit-risk in adult patients with metastatic NPC at a dose of 200 mg Q2W. Together with other penpulimab monotherapy studies, the 372 patients included in the safety
L2828: ## Penpulimab
L2829: population are considered adequate for the detection of common AE signals and characterization of the safety profile of penpulimab.
L2830: The following 2 safety populations were included in the overall safety evaluation, and the comparisons between those two safety populations were discussed in this document.
L2831: ## Integrated Penpulimab 200 mg Safety Population
L2832: This population included all the patients who received at least 1 dose of penpulimab 200 mg Q2W in Study AK105-101 (phase Ib), AK105-201, AK105-202, and AK105-204.
L2833: ## NPC Safety Population (Targeted Population)
L2834: This population included all the patients with NPC in Study AK 105-202.
L2835: ## **The FDA's Assessment**
L2836: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the safety populations included in this application. FDA's review of the safety profile of penpulimab 200 mg Q2W was based on data from 130 patients with NPC who received at least one dose of penpulimab on Study AK105-202 supported by pooled data from multiple trials of penpulimab 200 mg Q2W as a single agent for patients with advanced solid tumors and classic Hodgkin's lymphoma (cHL).
L2837: ## **8.1.1. Safety Review Approach**
L2838: ## **Data**
L2839: Not Applicable.
L2840: ## **The Applicant's Position**
L2841: The safety evaluation performed as part of the penpulimab clinical development program was consistent with ICH guidelines.
L2842: The safety analyses supporting this application were comprehensive and included all SAEs, all AEs, especially Grade \ge 3 all-causality and treatment-emergent AEs, AEs leading to discontinuations, AEs leading to interruption, laboratory abnormalities.
L2843: A consistent approach for the categorization of an AE as immune-related AE (irAE) was also applied following a prespecified case definition consisting of both programmatic checks and medical review (see Module 2.7.4 Appendix 9.1, irAE charter)
L2844: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2845: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that the safety data permit adequate assessment of penpulimab's safety profile. The FDA utilized the safety datasets generated for Study AK105-202 and pooled safety population to review the safety profile of penpulimab. Throughout the review of safety data, the FDA sent multiple information requests (IRs) requesting additional clinical information on various data elements, including medical causes of death when the patient narratives were unclear or vague.
L2846: ## 8.1.2. Review of the Safety Database
L2847: ## 8.1.2.1. *Overall Exposure*
L2848: ## Data
L2849: The summary of clinical safety includes safety data from 372 patients, including 130 patients with NPC (target population) and 242 patients with various other solid tumors or classic Hodgkin's lymphoma (cHL) (Study AK105-101 phase Ib, AK105-201, and AK105-204).
L2850: ## NPC Safety Population (Targeted Population)
L2851: In the NPC safety population, 130 patients were treated with penpulimab at a dose of 200 mg Q2W. The median total duration of exposure was 139.5 days (range: 14-644 days), median number of penpulimab doses was 9.5 (range: 1-43), median relative dose intensity was 97.67% (range: 50.0% - 103.7%). Approximately 68.5% of patients were treated for more than 3 months, 43.1% for more than 6 months, 18.5% of patients received treatment for at least 12 months.
L2852: ## Integrated Penpulimab 200 mg Safety Population
L2853: A total of 372 patients have been treated with penpulimab at a dose of 200 mg Q2W. The median duration of exposure was 168 days (range, 13 to 778 days). The median number of doses received was 11.5 (range: 1-54), median relative dose intensity was 98.3% (range: 50.0% - 103.7%). Approximately 66.1% of patients were treated for more than 3 months, 48.7% for more than 6 months, 28.0% of patients received treatment for at least 12 months.
L2854: ## The Applicant's Position
L2855: The eligibility criteria in the pivotal study were clinically relevant for the target population who would receive penpulimab following regulatory approval in the proposed indication.
L2856: The exposure to study drug was considered adequate to support characterization of the safety profile of penpulimab and allow for a comprehensive assessment of safety in all patients treated with penpulimab.
L2857: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2858: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position regarding the overall exposure of penpulimab being adequate to support characterization of its safety profile. Patients in the primary safety population had a median total duration of exposure of 4.6 months (range 0.5 to 21.2 months) whereas patients in the pooled safety population had a median duration of exposure of 5.5 months (range 0.4 to 25.6 months).
L2859: ## 8.1.2.2. *Relevant Characteristics of the Safety Population*
L2860: ## Data
L2861: ## NPC Safety Population (Targeted Population)
L2862: The median age was 49.5 years (range: 20 to 66 years), 76.2% of whom were male, 23.8% were female. All patients had an ECOG score of 0 (30.8%) or 1 (69.2%). The median BMI was 21.48 kg/m2 (range:13.2-21.4).
L2863: ## Integrated Penpulimab 200 mg Safety Population
L2864: The median age was 51 years (range: 18 to 91 years), 64.0% of whom were male, 36.0% were female. All patients had an ECOG score of 0 (41.9%) or 1 (58.1%). The median BMI was 22.81 kg/m2 (range:13.2-42.7).
L2865: ## The Applicant's Position
L2866: The demographic characteristics were generally comparable across NPC safety population and all other studies where penpulimab was used as a monotherapy.
L2867: ## The FDA's Assessment

L2874: The FDA agrees that the male predominance of the safety population is consistent with the U.S. patient population with NPC (Cancer.Net, 2022). However, the median age of patients in AK105-202 (49 years) is slightly younger than the median age for US patients with NPC (53-54 years). Furthermore, given that all patients in the primary safety population were of Chinese ethnicity, the safety populations do not reflect the racial and ethnic diversity of the US patient population with NPC.
L2875: Despite the differences noted above, the FDA agrees that the safety data from AK105-202 and the pooled population provide sufficient data for evaluations of the safety profile of penpulimab in adults with metastatic non-keratinizing nasopharyngeal carcinoma (NPC) with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of therapy.
L2876: ## 8.1.2.3. *Adequacy of the Safety Database*
L2877: ## Data
L2878: Integrated penpulimab 200 mg safety population included 372 patients who received at least 1 dose of penpulimab 200 mg Q2W from four clinical studies. Of those patients, 130 patients in proposed patient population were included for overall safety evaluation.
L2879: ## The Applicant's Position
L2880: The safety database was considered adequate to support the characterization of the safety profile of penpulimab in the proposed patient population.
L2881: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2882: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. The data submitted was organized and adequate to perform a complete review of the safety of penpulimab. FDA issued several information requests during the review cycle to obtain clarification and additional information regarding safety data included in the NDA and all requests were addressed appropriately.
L2883: ## 8.1.3. *Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments*
L2884: ## 8.1.3.1. *Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality*
L2885: ## Data
L2886: All safety evaluations conducted during NPC safety population (Study AK105-202), including monitoring for AEs, reporting SAEs, physical examinations, vital sign measurements, clinical laboratory tests, and ECGs are considered standard (i.e., widely used and generally recognized as reliable, accurate, and relevant).
L2887: Study AK105-202 was conducted according to procedures that incorporate the general principles of GCP.
L2888: ## The Applicant's Position
L2889: No issues were identified in the quality and integrity of the submitted datasets and individual case narratives.
L2890: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2891: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. However, throughout the review of safety data, FDA sent multiple IRs requesting additional clinical information about medical causes of death
L2892: when the patient narratives were unclear or did not provide sufficient information. The FDA also sent an IR requesting submission of a revised dataset of laboratory results.

L2898: ## The Applicant's Position
L2899: The safety of penpulimab was evaluated on the basis of the:
L2900: - Frequency, type, severity, and causal relationship of AEs to study treatment.
- Frequency of deaths, SAEs, and other clinically significant AEs (including AEs leading to discontinuation and AEs requiring dose interruption).
- Frequency and type of AEs in key demographic subgroups.
- Changes in laboratory variables.
L2901: The approach to the analysis of AEs was appropriate in evaluating the safety data.
L2902: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2903: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the categorization of adverse events (AEs) for Studies AK105-101, AK105-201, AK105-202, AK105-204. MedDRA v 22.0 was used for mapping of AEs and CTCAE v 4.03 was used for grading of AEs.
L2904: ## 8.1.3.3. *Routine Clinical Tests*
L2905: ## Data
L2906: Clinical laboratory data were collected routinely throughout the study. Clinical laboratory values were graded according to NCI CTCAE version 4.03, when applicable. Results outside predefined normal ranges and NCI CTCAE grade were flagged in the data listings for further investigations.
L2907: Clinical laboratory tests items included hematology, blood chemistry, coagulation function tests, thyroid function tests, urinalysis, virological tests, and pregnancy tests. The schedule of main laboratory tests for each study is presented in Module 2.7.4, Table 4.
L2908: ## The Applicant's Position
L2909: The laboratory evaluation (laboratory tests and timepoints) as described in the protocols was reasonable and adequate to monitor the safety of the penpulimab.
L2910: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2911: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.

L2914: ## Data
L2915: ## NPC Safety Population (Targeted Population)
L2916: A total of 47 (36.2%) deaths occurred in NPC safety population. A total of 2 (1.5%) deaths occurred within 30 days after the last dose.
L2917: Eighteen (13.8%) patients had AEs that resulted in death, regardless of causality. Excluding disease progression, AEs with an outcome of death were reported in 7 (5.4%) patients:
L2918: unexplained death in 3 patients (SID [REDACTED] (b) (6)), respiratory failure in 2 patients, septic shock in 1 patient, and sudden death (SID [REDACTED] (b) (6)) in 1 patient. Drug-related AE leading to death occurred in 2 patients (SID [REDACTED] (b) (6) "death" as PT, causality unable to be assessed; SID [REDACTED] (b) (6) "death" as PT, causality unable to be assessed).
L2919: SID [REDACTED] (b) (6): On Day 3 after the 4 doses, the family found the patient fell in the bathroom at home without vital signs. The investigator judged the death was highly likely caused by trauma. SID [REDACTED] (b) (6): The patient received the two doses after enrollment in the study and died 34 days after the last dose. No autopsy was performed, and the family members were unable to provide medical records and subsequently refused to answer the telephone, so a clear cause of death could not be obtained. SID [REDACTED] (b) (6): On [REDACTED] (b) (6), the patient slipped on the floor at home and did not hurt the head. At about 3:00 pm on [REDACTED] (b) (6), the patient was accompanied by his family member for urinate at home, and the patient was unable to stand up and showed no response to voice. The family moved the patient to bed and did not send him to the hospital. SID [REDACTED] (b) (6): On [REDACTED] (b) (6), the patient experienced a car accident, and radiography showed multiple fractures of ribs and ruptured spleen. L2920: the patient was discharged from the hospital. On [REDACTED](b) (6), the patient was unconscious, and sent to the hospital by ambulance. The patient died from failure to rescue on the same day and was judged as sudden death by the local hospital.

L2941: ## The Applicant's Position
L2942: Most death cases in the clinical studies were related to disease progression. This is consistent with patients' underlying medical conditions, and possibly concomitant medications.
L2943: ## The FDA's Assessment
L2944: The FDA reviewed all fatal adverse events that occurred in patients from the primary safety population in Study AK105-202. A total of 5% of patients (n=7) had an event of death due to AEs that were not clearly related to disease progression. Of the 7 patients with a fatal AE, FDA only considers four events (3.1%) to be adverse reactions, including one event of respiratory failure (Patient (b) (6)) and three events of death (Patients (b) (6)) to be fatal adverse reactions. Patient (b) (6) (sudden death) died after a car accident. Patient (b) (6) (respiratory failure) likely died of disease progression. Patient (b) (6) (septic shock) died of fungal pneumonia. Table 25 below describes the fatal AEs and FDA's determination of death causality in greater detail.
L2945: **Table 25 – FDA: Assessment of Causality for Fatal Adverse Events Not Clearly Attributable to Disease Progression in Primary Safety Population**
L2946: Patient ID	Brief Narrative	FDA's Assessment of Causality	(b) (4)
(b) (6)	65-year-old female was hospitalized for Grade 3 bullous herpes simplex (pemphigoid), penpulimab was interrupted for 8-weeks during which the patient fella at home without injuring her head. A few days later she was unable to stand and died at home. No autopsy was performed.	Death
• Given the history of Grade 3 bullous herpes simplex (pemphigoid), a CT scan performed a few weeks prior to death showed stable disease, and symptoms of being unable to stand developing several days after the fall, penpulimab's contribution to the patient's death cannot be ruled out.	Yes
(b) (6)	61-year-old male who was found by family fallena in the bathroom with no vital signs three days after the patient's last dose of penpulimab.	Sudden death
• Given the lack of information describing the circumstances of this patient's death, penpulimab's contribution to the event cannot be ruled out.	Yes
(b) (6)	63-year-old male was hospitalized for Grade 2 pneumonia.a Chest X-ray showed pulmonary inflammation, treated with antibiotics and antipyretics. The patient was subsequently lost to follow up with unknown outcome of pneumonia. Patient died 34 days after the last dose of penpulimab; family was unreachable.	Death
• Given the lack of information describing the circumstances of this patient's death, penpulimab's contribution to the event cannot be ruled out.	Yes
(b) (6)	32-year-old male who received a total of 3 doses of study drug; CT scans post 3rd dose showed disease progression with new hydrothorax in thoracic cavity and bilateral lower lungs, multiple new bone metastases. Patient was taken off the study and died of respiratory failurea 2 weeks later.	Disease progression
• Given the patient's known widespread metastatic disease, FDA determined that this patient's death was most likely due to disease progression.	No
