L2995: ## 8.1.4.3. *Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Effects*
L2996: ## Data
L2997: ## NPC Safety Population (Targeted Population)
L2998: Disease progression was the primary cause of treatment discontinuation (66/130, 50.8%). AE leading to discontinuation was reported in 5 (3.8%), the AEs were transaminase abnormal, spinal cord compression, disseminated herpes zoster, pemphigoid, and pleural effusion (1 patient each).
L2999: ## Integrated Penpulimab 200 mg Safety Population
L3000: Disease progression was the primary cause of treatment discontinuation (176/372, 47.3%). AE leading to discontinuation was reported in 22 (5.9%) patients. AEs resulting in permanent discontinuation of penpulimab reported in &gt; 1 patient each were immune-mediated pneumonitis (in 3 patients), pleural effusion (in 2 patients), and gastrointestinal hemorrhage (in 2 patients).

L3010: ## The Applicant's Position
L3011: The incidence of AEs resulting in permanent discontinuation of penpulimab was low and similar between AK105-202 and studies on dosing regimen of penpulimab 200 mg Q2W. There were no appreciable differences between the two populations for any of the SOCs or PTs. AEs resulting in discontinuation of penpulimab were distributed across a broad range of PTs, with no specific SOC or PT accounting for most of the events (occurred in ≤1% of patients).
L3012: ## **The FDA's Assessment**
L3013: In Study AK105-202, permanent discontinuation of penpulimab due to an adverse event occurred in 5 (3.8%) patients. These were pleural effusion, herpes zoster disseminated infection, pemphigoid, spinal cord compression, and hepatitis (0.8% each).
L3014: In the pooled safety population, permanent discontinuation of penpulimab due to an adverse event occurred in 22 (6%) patients. These were immune mediated pneumonitis (0.8%), pleural effusion (0.5%), gastrointestinal hemorrhage (0.5), hepatitis (0.3%), pemphigoid (0.3%), herpes zoster disseminated infection (0.3%), ascites (0.3%), cerebral infarction (0.3%), cholangitis (0.3%), colitis (0.3%), epilepsy (0.3%), hemophagocytic lymphohistiocytosis (0.3%), hypersensitivity (0.3%), nephritis (0.3%), pelvic infection (0.3%) septic shock (0.3%) and spinal cord compression (0.3%).

L3022: ## **8.1.4.4. Dose Interruption/Reduction Due to Adverse Effects**
L3023: ## **Data**
L3024: ## **NPC Safety Population (Targeted Population)**
L3025: Thirty-three (25.4%) patients experienced at least one AE leading to drug interruption. AEs resulting in drug interruption in &gt; 1% of patients were alanine aminotransferase increased, pneumonia, hypothyroidism, hepatic function abnormal, anemia, and rash.
L3026: ## **Integrated Penpulimab 200 mg Safety Population**
L3027: Eighty-eight (23.7%) patients experienced at least one AE leading to drug interruption. AEs resulting in drug interruption in &gt; 1% of patients were alanine aminotransferase increased, aspartate aminotransferase increased, pneumonia, diarrhea, hypothyroidism, hepatic function abnormal, anemia, musculoskeletal pain, and rash.

L3057: ## 8.1.4.6. *Treatment Emergent Adverse Events and Adverse Reactions*
L3058: ## Data
L3059: ## Study AK105-202
L3060: ## *NPC Safety Population (Targeted Population)*
L3061: One hundred and twenty-two out of 130 (93.8%) patients had at least one AE. Most patients had Grade 1 or 2 events; 41(31.5%) patients had at least one \ge Grade 3 AEs. The incidence of drug-related AEs was 69.2% (90/130), and the incidence of \ge Grade 3 drug-related AEs was 8.5% (11/130).
L3062: The most commonly (the incidence of PT \ge 20%) reported AEs, included: anemia, hypothyroidism, and musculoskeletal pain. Common Grade 3-4 AEs (with an incidence of \ge 2%) were rash, hyponatremia, and anemia.
L3063: ## *Integrated Penpulimab 200 mg Safety Population*
L3064: Three hundred and fifty-seven out of 372 patients had at least one AE. Most patients had Grade 1 or 2 events; 136 (36.6%) patients had at least one \ge Grade 3 AEs was. The incidence of TRAEs was 70.4% (262/372), and the incidence of \ge Grade 3 TRAEs was 9.1% (34/372)
L3065: The most commonly (the incidence of PT \ge 20%) reported AEs, included: anemia, hypothyroidism, rash, and musculoskeletal pain. Common Grade 3-4 AEs (with an incidence of \ge 2%) were rash.
L3066: The summary of all AEs is presented in Table 34. AEs reported by patients are summarized by SOC and PT in Table 35. The PT terms represent a group of relevant medical events were grouped together as one grouped term for the presentation.
L3067: Adverse drug reactions (ADRs) with penpulimab have been determined include the known risks associated with the anti-PD-1/PD -L1 drug class and most frequently AEs (\ge10% of patients).
L3068: The ADR for proposed patient population presented in the prescribing label excluded lab abnormality AE. The lab abnormality by the CTCAE grade derived based on actual value was presented under a separate table included in the prescribing label.

L3103: Adverse event types were generally similar between the primary safety population (NPC) and the pooled safety population (all tumor types), with the exception of a higher incidence of hypothyroidism in the NPC group compared to the all-tumor group (32% vs. 28%), which may be due to tumor location and history of radiation in the NPC patient population. There were higher incidences of rash and colitis in the all-tumor group compared to the NPC group (17% vs. 9% and 11% vs. 3.1%), although the significance of this finding is not clear.

L3108: ## Integrated Penpulimab 200 mg Safety Population
L3109: Most patients had Grade 1 or 2 laboratory abnormalities, common Grade 3-4 laboratory abnormalities (with an incidence of \ge 5\%) included gamma glutamyl transferase increased, phosphate decreased, sodium decreased, and lymphocytes decreased.

L3138: ## The Applicant's Position
L3139: Electrocardiogram (ECG) assessments are part of routine safety monitoring. There was no evidence of clinically meaningful cardiac effects associated with penpulimab exposure.

L3142: ## 8.1.4.10. *QT Interval*
L3143: ## Data
L3144: ## NPC Safety Population (Targeted Population)
L3145: No patients had a QTcF value &gt; 500 msec or an increase from baseline &gt; 90 msec. No patients had a QTcF value &gt; 480 msec, 3 patients (2.3%) had an increase from baseline &gt; 60 msec.
L3146: ## Integrated Penpulimab 200 mg Safety Population
L3147: Two patients (0.5%) had a QTcF value &gt; 500 msec and 4 patients (1.1%) had an increase from baseline &gt; 90 msec. Seven patients had a QTcF value &gt; 480 msec and 14 patients (3.8%) had an increase from baseline &gt; 60 msec.
L3148: ## **The Applicant's Position**
L3149: Individuals with clinically meaningful QTcF values and changes from baseline were found to have had either cardiac medical history or were receiving potentially cardioactive concomitant medications at the time of the abnormality or had single occurrences above the defined threshold.
L3150: ## **The FDA's Assessment**
L3151: The FDA agrees with Applicant's position.
L3152: ## **8.1.4.11. Immunogenicity**
L3153: ## **Data**
L3154: ## NPC Safety Population (Targeted Population)
L3155: The incidence of ADA positive after administration was 5.9% (7/119), with neutralizing antibodies detected in 4.2% (5/119). Three patients with ADA positive had at least one AE, no patient occurred drug related AE. Most frequently AEs for patients with ADA positive (\ge 1% of patients) included weight decreased (2.3%), pyrexia (1.5%), and anemia (1.5%).
L3156: ## Integrated Penpulimab 200 mg Safety Population
L3157: The incidence of ADA positive after administration was 12.5% (42/335), with neutralizing antibodies detected in 4.2% (14/335). Twenty-four patients had at least one AE, three patients occurred drug related AE. Most frequently AEs for patients with ADA positive (\ge 1% of patients) included alanine aminotransferase increased (2.2%), upper respiratory tract infection (2.4%), pyrexia (2.4%), and anemia (2.2%).
L3158: ## **The Applicant's Position**
L3159: In ADA-positive patients, the incidence and occurrence type of AE were generally similar to those of all patients, with no specific type of AE and no increased incidence of infusion-related reactions/hypersensitivities. Treatment emergent ADA had no significant impact on safety.
L3160: ## The FDA's Assessment
L3161: The FDA agrees with Applicant's position.

L3171: ## Immune-Mediated Pneumonitis
L3172: Immune-mediated pneumonitis occurred in 1.1% (4/372) of patients receiving penpulimab, including two Grade 3 (0.5%), and two Grade 2 (0.5%) adverse reactions. Pneumonitis led to permanent discontinuation of penpulimab in three of those four patients. High-dose of systemic corticosteroids were administered to all four patients with pneumonitis. Pneumonitis resolved with sequelae in 1 patient.
L3173: ## Penpulimab
L3174: ## Immune-Mediated Colitis
L3175: Immune-mediated colitis occurred in 0.8% (3/372) of patients receiving penpulimab. All were Grade 2 (0.8%) adverse reactions.
L3176: Systemic corticosteroids were administered to all 3 patients with colitis. Colitis led to withholding of penpulimab in all 3 patients. Of those 3 patients from whom penpulimab was withheld for colitis, 2 patients reinitiated penpulimab after symptom improvement; 1 of those 2 patients experienced recurrence of colitis. Colitis resolved in 2 of those 3 patients.
L3177: ## Immune-Mediated Hepatitis
L3178: Immune-mediated hepatitis occurred in 0.5% (2/372) of patients receiving penpulimab. Both were Grade 3 (0.5%) adverse reactions. Hepatitis led to withholding of penpulimab in both patients.
L3179: Systemic corticosteroids were administered to those 2 patients with hepatitis. Hepatitis resolved in one of those 2 patients. Of the 2 patients from whom penpulimab was withheld for immune-mediated hepatitis, one reinitiated penpulimab after symptom improvement and this patient experienced recurrence of hepatitis.
L3180: ## Immune-Mediated Endocrinopathies
L3181: ## *Hypothyroidism*
L3182: Immune-mediated hypothyroidism occurred in 9% (35/372) of patients receiving penpulimab, including twenty-two Grade 2 (6%) and thirteen Grade 1 (3.5%) adverse reactions. No patient permanently discontinued penpulimab due to hypothyroidism. Hypothyroidism led to withholding of penpulimab in 1.9% (7/372) of patients.
L3183: All patients (35/35) received endocrine therapy. 1 patient received additional immunosuppressant therapy. Of the 7 patients from whom penpulimab was withheld for hypothyroidism, all reinitiated penpulimab after symptom improvement: 5 experienced recurrences of hypothyroidism. Hypothyroidism resolved in 37% (13/35) of patients.
L3184: ## *Hyperthyroidism*
L3185: Immune-mediated hyperthyroidism occurred in 6% (23/372) of patients receiving penpulimab, including four Grade 2 (1.1%) and nineteen Grade 1 (5.1%) adverse reactions. No patient permanently discontinued of penpulimab due to hyperthyroidism. Hyperthyroidism led to withholding of penpulimab in 0.5% (2/372) of patients. Events resolved in 87% (20) of the 23 patients. Endocrine therapy was administered in 13% (3/23) of patients.
L3186: ## Penpulimab
L3187: ## *Thyroiditis*
L3188: Thyroiditis occurred in 0.5% (2/372) of patients receiving penpulimab. Thyroiditis led to withholding of penpulimab in 0.3% (1/372) of patients. One of them (1/2) received endocrine therapy. Events resolved in these two patients.
L3189: ## Immune-Mediated Nephritis With Renal Dysfunction
L3190: Immune-mediated nephritis occurred in 0.5% (2/372) of patients receiving penpulimab, including one Grade 3 (0.3%) and one Grade 2 (0.3%) adverse reactions. All patients were permanently discontinued penpulimab due to nephritis. Systemic corticosteroids were administered to those 2 patients. Events resolved in one of these 2 patients.
L3191: ## Immune-Mediated Dermatologic Adverse Reactions
L3192: Immune-mediated rash or dermatitis occurred in 7.5% (28/372) of patients receiving penpulimab, including three Grade 3 (0.8%), eight Grade 2 (2.2%) and seventeen Grade 1 (4.6%) adverse reactions. Systemic corticosteroids were administered in 17.9% (5/28) patients with immune-mediated rash or dermatitis. Immune-mediated dermatologic adverse reactions led to permanent discontinuation of penpulimab in 0.3% (1/372) of patients and withholding of penpulimab in 1.1% (4/372) of patients. Of the 4 patients from whom penpulimab were withheld for dermatologic adverse reactions, all reinitiated penpulimab after symptom improvement; three experienced recurrences of hypothyroidism. Events resolved in 64.3% (18/28) of patients.
L3193: ## Other Immune-Mediated Adverse Reactions
L3194: The following clinically significant, immune-mediated adverse reactions occurred at an incidence of &lt;1% in patients who received penpulimab 200 mg Q2W: nerve injury, and arthralgia.
L3195: ## Infusion-Related Reactions
L3196: The infusion-related reactions occurred in 15 patients (4%) following dosing regimen of penpulimab 200 mg Q2W, including Grade 1 in 3 patients, Grade 2 in 12 patients. No patients occurred Grade 3-4 infusion-related reactions. One patient (0.8%) experienced Grade 1 hypersensitivity in proposed patient population (Study AK105-202).
L3197: ## **The Applicant's Position**
L3198: The common irAEs (\ge 5%) were hyperthyroidism, hypothyroidism, and rash. The majority of irAEs in studies on dosing regimen of penpulimab 200 mg Q2W were Grade 1 or 2 in severity. \ge
L3199: Grade 3 irAEs generally occurred at a low rate (&lt; 1%) and were spread among the different categories.
L3200: ## The FDA's Assessment
L3201: The Applicant defined an immune related AE as a suspected event which based on medical review is consistent with an immune mediated mechanism of action, and there were no alternative etiologies. When applicable, serologic, immunologic, and histologic (biopsy) data were used to support the characterization of an irAE. Potential irAEs, included but were limited to immune-mediated colitis, dermatitis, pneumonia, hepatitis, encephalitis, nephritis, hypothyroidism, and hyperthyroidism.
L3202: In the primary safety population (N=130), the most common irAEs (\ge 0.5\%) were hypothyroidism, hyperthyroidism, dermatitis, and pneumonitis. In the pooled safety population (N=372), the most common irAEs (\ge 0.5\%) were hypothyroidism, hyperthyroidism, dermatitis, hepatitis, pneumonitis, colitis, and nephritis. There were no fatal irAEs observed in the pooled safety population or the primary safety population. No IRRs were reported in the primary safety population, and infusion related reactions occurred in 4% of patients in the pooled safety population.

L3217: Additional results of FDA analyses and review used to inform Section 5 (Warnings and Precautions) of the proposed labeling are presented below.
L3218: ## Pneumonitis
L3219: Primary safety population (N=130): Immune-mediated pneumonitis occurred in 0.8% (1/130) of patients receiving penpulimab (Grade 2), which required corticosteroids. There were no Grade 3 or 4 adverse events.
L3220: Pooled safety population (N=372): Immune-mediated pneumonitis occurred in 1.1% (4/372) of patients, including two Grade 2 (0.5%) and two Grade 3 (0.5%) adverse reactions. Systemic corticosteroids were required in all four patients. Penpulimab was permanently discontinued due to pneumonitis in three patients and was interrupted and reintroduced following resolution in one patient.
L3221: ## Colitis
L3222: Primary safety population (N=130): No patients developed immune-mediated colitis.
L3223: Pooled safety population (N=372): Immune-mediated colitis occurred in 0.8%, (3/372) of patients; all were Grade 2 in severity and required systemic corticosteroids. Penpulimab was interrupted and reintroduced following resolution in two patients and permanently discontinued in one patient.
L3224: ## Hepatotoxicity and Immune-Mediated Hepatitis
L3225: Primary safety population (N=130): No patients developed immune-mediated hepatitis or hepatotoxicity.
L3226: Pooled safety population (N=372): Immune-mediated hepatitis occurred in 0.5% (2/372), both of which were Grade 3. Systemic corticosteroids were required in both patients. Penpulimab was interrupted and reintroduced following resolution in one patient and permanently discontinued in one patient.
L3227: ## Endocrinopathies
L3228: ## *Hypothyroidism*
L3229: Primary safety population (N=130): Immune-mediated hypothyroidism occurred in 14% (19/130) of patients, including six Grade 2 (6%) and thirteen Grade 1 (8%) adverse reaction. Penpulimab was withheld in 3.1% of patients and not permanently discontinued in any of the patients due to hypothyroidism.
L3230: Pooled safety population (N=372): Immune-mediated hypothyroidism occurred in 9% (35/372) of patients, including 22 Grade 2 (6%) and thirteen Grade 1 (3.5%) adverse reactions. Penpulimab was withheld in 1.9% (7/372) of patients, all of whom reinitiated penpulimab after symptom improvement. All patients required endocrine therapy.
L3231: ## *Hyperthyroidism*
L3232: Primary safety population (N=130): Immune-mediated hyperthyroidism occurred in 6% (8/130) of patients, including two Grade 2 (1.5%) and six Grade 1 (4.6%) irAEs. No patient permanently discontinued of penpulimab due to hyperthyroidism. Hyperthyroidism led to withholding of penpulimab in 0.8% (1/130) of patients. Antithyroid drug therapy was administered in 13% (1/8) of patients.
L3233: Pooled safety population (N=372): Immune-mediated hyperthyroidism occurred in 6% (23/372) of patients, including four Grade 2 (1.1%) and 19 Grade 1 (5%) adverse reaction. Penpulimab was withheld in one patient.
L3234: ## *Thyroiditis*
L3235: Primary safety population (N=130): Thyroid disease occurred in 1.5% (2/130) of patients, including one Grade 1 event of thyroiditis, one Grade 2 event of thyroid disorder. Penpulimab was withheld in one patient and not permanently discontinued in any of the patients due to immune mediated thyroiditis.
L3236: ## Penpulimab
L3237: Pooled safety population (N=372): Thyroiditis occurred in 0.5% (2/372) of patients, including one Grade 2 and one Grade 1 adverse reaction. Penpulimab was withheld in one patient and not permanently discontinued in any patient due to thyroiditis.
L3238: ## Immune-Mediated Nephritis With Renal Dysfunction
L3239: Primary safety population (N=130): No patients developed immune-mediated nephritis.
L3240: Pooled safety population (N=372): Immune-mediated nephritis occurred in 0.5% (2/372) of patients, including one Grade 2 and one Grade 3 adverse reaction. Systemic corticosteroids were required and penpulimab was permanently discontinued due to nephritis in both patients. Nephritis resolved in one patient.
L3241: ## Immune-Mediated Rash or Dermatitis
L3242: Primary safety population (N=130): Immune-mediated rash or dermatitis occurred in 9% (11 patients), including two Grade 3 rash, one Grade 3 pemphigoid, five Grade 1 /2 rash, one Grade 1 eczema, one Grade 2 allergic dermatitis, one Grade 2 dermatitis acneiform. Systemic corticosteroids were required in 18% (2/11) of the patients with immune-mediated rash or dermatitis. Penpulimab was withheld in 1.5% (2/130) and permanently discontinued in 0.8% (1/130) of patients.
L3243: Pooled safety population (N=372): Immune-mediated rash or dermatitis occurred in 8% (28/372) of patients, including three Grade 3 (0.8%). Systemic corticosteroids were required in 18% (5/28) of patients with immune-mediated rash or dermatitis. Penpulimab was withheld in 1.1% (4/372) of patients and permanently discontinued in 0.3% (1/372) of patients. Dermatologic adverse reactions resolved in 64% (18/28) of patients.
L3244: ## **8.1.5.2. *Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability***
L3245: ## **Data**
L3246: Not Applicable.
L3247: ## **The Applicant's Position**
L3248: None of the studies supporting this application collected clinical outcome assessment (COA) data.
L3249: ## **The FDA's Assessment**
L3250: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L3251: ## 8.1.6. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
L3252: ## Data
L3253: This section summarizes the relationship between various intrinsic factors and the safety profile of penpulimab. These data are relevant to patient management based on demographic or other 'intrinsic' factors. Only notable differences (\ge 10-percentage points) are discussed.
L3254: The safety profile of penpulimab was assessed in relation to the following intrinsic factors:
L3255: - Sex (male, female)
- Age (&gt; 65, \le 65 years)
- ECOG performance status (0, 1)
L3256: ## Sex
L3257: In proposed patient population, the AE profile of penpulimab was generally similar in both females and males. AEs leading to drug discontinuation occurred at a higher rate in females than males (6.5% vs 3.0%, respectively), this observation should be interpreted in the context of the small sample size of the female subgroup.
L3258: For the integrated penpulimab 200 mg population, there was no appreciable difference between females and males for any of the AE categories.
L3259: ## Age
L3260: For the integrated penpulimab 200 mg population, there was no appreciable difference between age subgroups for any of the AE categories. The differences between age subgroups in the proposed patient population cannot be assessed due to only 2 patients with age &gt; 65 years enrolled.
L3261: ## ECOG Performance Status
L3262: In proposed patient population, the incidence of events was similar in patients with a baseline ECOG performance status of 0 and 1 for most AE categories. However, notable differences were observed for Grade 3 or higher AEs (17.5% vs 37.8%) and SAEs (12.5% vs 26.7%), all of which occurred at a higher rate in patients with an ECOG performance status of 1 compared with ECOG performance status of 0.
L3263: For the integrated penpulimab 200 mg population, the AE profile of penpulimab was generally similar in patients with a baseline ECOG performance status of 0 and 1, except for Grade 3 or higher AEs (31.4% vs 40.3%) and SAEs (20.5% vs 29.6%), both of which occurred at a lower rate in patients with an ECOG performance status of 0.
L3264: ## The Applicant's Position
L3265: The AE profile for integrated penpulimab 200 mg population was generally similar across subgroups based on age and sex.
L3266: The incidence of AEs of any grade was generally similar between ECOG subgroup, and lower incidence rate of grade 3 or higher AEs and SAEs in patients with ECOG performance status of 0 were observed.
L3267: ## The FDA's Assessment
L3268: FDA reviewed the incidence of AEs by age and sex in the primary and pooled safety populations. A review of safety by race and ethnicity was not possible for the primary safety population given that all patients enrolled in Study AK105-202 were Asian.
L3269: In Study AK105-202, 128 (98%) and 2 (1.5%) patients treated with penpulimab were &lt; 65 and ≥ 65 years, respectively. Given that only 2 patients in the primary safety population were older than 65 years of age, no conclusions can be drawn about the differences in AEs between older and younger patients. In the pooled safety population, 303 (81%) and 69 (19%) of patients were age &lt;65 and ≥65 years, respectively. In Study AK105-202, 32 (25%) patients &lt; 65 years had at least one Grade 3-4 AE while 2 (100%) of patients ≥ 65 years of age had a Grade 3-4 AE. In the pooled safety population, Grade 3-4 AEs occurred in 90 (30%) and 28 (41%) patients age &lt;65 and ≥65 years, respectively.
L3270: Among the 130 patients who received penpulimab in Study AK105-202, 99 (76%) were male and 31 (24%) were female. In the pooled safety population, 238 (64%) patients were male and 134 (36%) were female. In Study AK105-202, the incidence of Grade 3-4 AEs in male patients vs female patients were 27 (28%) vs 4 (13%), respectively. In the pooled safety population, the incidence of Grade 3 and 4 AEs among male patients vs female patients were 85 (38%) and 32 (24%), respectively.
L3271: In the primary safety population, 90 (69%) patients had an ECOG performance status (PS) of 1 and 40 (31%) patients had an ECOG PS of 0. The incidence of all grade AEs was similar among ECOG PS of 1 or 0; however, Grade 3 or higher AEs occurred in 38% of patients with ECOG PS 1 compared to 18% of patients with ECOG PS of 0. In the pooled safety population, 216 (58%) patients had an ECOG PS of 1 and 156 (42%) patients had an ECOG PS of 0. The incidence of Grade 3 or higher AE occurred in 150 (40%) patients with ECOG PS of 1 and 117 (31%) with an ECOG PS 0. The higher incidence of Grade 3 or higher AEs in patients with ECOG PS of 1 compared to those with an ECOG PS of 0 is likely due to the relatively poorer baseline condition of these patients.
L3272: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that age and sex did not appear to have a major impact on the safety profile of penpulimab.

L3292: ## 8.1.8.2. *Human Reproduction and Pregnancy*
L3293: ## Data
L3294: Not Applicable.
L3295: ## The Applicant's Position
L3296: Penpulimab has not been studied in pregnant or breastfeeding women. As of the DCO dates for studies on dosing regimen of penpulimab 200 mg Q2W, there were no reports of pregnancies or pregnancy-related events in either study. Based on its mechanism of action, penpulimab has the potential to impact maintenance of pregnancy and may cause fetal harm when administered to a pregnant woman. Human IgG1 is known to cross the placental barrier; therefore, penpulimab has the potential to be transmitted from the mother to the developing fetus. Penpulimab is not recommended during pregnancy and in women of childbearing potential not using effective contraception during treatment and for at least 3 months after the last dose.
L3297: There is no information regarding the presence of penpulimab in human milk, the absorption and effects on the breastfed infant, or the effects on milk production. Human IgG is excreted in human milk. Because of the potential for adverse reactions in breastfed infants from penpulimab, advise a lactating woman not to breastfeed during treatment and for at least 5 months after the last dose.
L3298: ## The FDA's Assessment
L3299: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.

L3307: ## 8.1.8.4. *Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound*
L3308: ## Data
L3309: There are no available safety data for penpulimab overdose.
L3310: ## The Applicant's Position
L3311: There is no specific treatment in the event of penpulimab overdose and possible symptoms have not been established.
L3312: As a result of its use, mode of action, physiological and pharmacological activity, and lack of stimulant properties, penpulimab monotherapy is unlikely to be abused. Because penpulimab has no known potential for dependence and is not continuously dosed, withdrawal or rebound events are not relevant to penpulimab dosing.
L3313: ## The FDA's Assessment
L3314: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.

L3330: ## 8.1.10. Integrated Assessment of Safety
L3331: ## Data
L3332: Not Applicable.
L3333: ## The Applicant's Position
L3334: The safety profile of proposed patient population is consistent with the well-characterized safety profile of the integrated penpulimab 200 mg population. The overall safety profile of penpulimab was tolerable, manageable, and consistent with other marketed PD-1 inhibitors.
L3335: The demographic and disease characteristics of patients in the NPC safety analysis set were representative of the intended population to be treated in clinical practice.
L3336: In proposed patient population, the most commonly (the incidence of PT \ge 20%) reported AEs, included: anemia, hypothyroidism, and musculoskeletal pain. Common Grade 3-4 AEs (with an incidence of \ge 2%) were rash, hyponatremia, and anemia.
L3337: The incidence of high-grade AEs and AEs resulting in discontinuation of penpulimab was low and consistent between the two populations. In proposed patient population:
L3338: - Common Grade 3-4 AEs (with an incidence of \ge 2% of patients) included hyponatremia, anemia, and rash. These were generally manageable and reversible.
- Excluding disease progression, 18 patients (13.8%) experienced at least one SAE. The most commonly (the incidence of PT \ge 1%) reported SAEs (excluded disease progression) included, pneumonia, respiratory failure,.
- 5 (3.8%) AEs led to treatment discontinuation, included transaminase abnormal, spinal cord compression, disseminated herpes zoster, pemphigoid, and pleural effusion (1 patient each).
L3339: ## Penpulimab
L3340: Overall, the incidence of irAE were similar across proposed patient population and integrated penpulimab 200 mg safety population. In proposed patient population:
L3341: - Twenty-nine (22.3%) patients experienced at least one irAE; a majority of the confirmed irAEs were Grade 1 or 2 severity. The most common irAEs (\ge 1\% of patients) including hypothyroidism (17, 13.1%), rash (7, 5.4%), hyperthyroidism (6, 4.6%), blood thyroid stimulating hormone decreased (3, 2.3%), and thyroxine free increased (2, 1.5%).
- 3 patients (2.3%) experienced Grade 3 irAEs, including rash (Grade 3) in 2 patients (1.5%) and pemphigoid (Grade 3) in 1 patient (0.8%). There were no CTCAE Grade 4 or 5 irAEs. One patient (0.8%) experienced hypersensitivity and no infusion-related reactions occurred.
L3342: Changes in laboratory parameters were predominantly Grade 1 or 2 in severity, and the incidence of any grade worsening to Grade 3 or 4 was generally low. Laboratory abnormalities reported as AEs infrequently resulted in discontinuation of penpulimab, suggesting a tolerable safety profile.
L3343: The safety profile was generally not affected by age, [REDACTED], and other factor (e.g., ECOG performance status).
L3344: ## The FDA's Assessment
L3345: FDA conducted the safety review of penpulimab in 130 patients with metastatic NPC who had disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other prior line of therapy who received penpulimab 200 mg Q3W on Study AK105-202 (primary safety population) and in 372 patients with other solid tumors who received penpulimab 200 mg Q3W across the development program (pooled safety population).
L3346: FDA's review of deaths due to AEs, SAEs, irAEs, or AEs leading to treatment discontinuation in patients enrolled in Study AK105-202 and in patients in the pooled safety population did not raise concern for an unexpected safety signal relative to other agents in this class.
L3347: Serious adverse events occurred in 19% of patients in the primary safety population and in 23% of patients in the pooled safety population. Among patients in the primary safety population, the most common SAEs (\ge 2\%) were pneumonia, death, and hepatitis.
L3348: Discontinuation of penpulimab due to an adverse event occurred in a total of 6% of patients in the pooled safety population and in 3.8% of patients in the primary safety population. In the primary safety population, the most common events leading to treatment discontinuation were pleural effusion, herpes zoster disseminated, spinal cord compression, hepatitis, and pemphigoid, occurring in 1 patient (0.8%) each.
L3349: Adverse events leading to drug interruptions occurred in 25% of patients in the primary safety population and in 24% of patients in the pooled safety population. Among patients in the primary safety population, the most common events (\ge 1\%) leading to drug interruption were hepatitis, pneumonia, hypothyroidism, anemia, pneumonitis, and rash.
L3350: Immune-related adverse events (irAEs) occurred in 22% of patients in the primary safety population (Grade 3-4, 2.3%) and in 20% of patients in the pooled safety population (Grade 3-4, 2.2%). In the primary safety population, the most common irAEs (\ge 10\%) was hypothyroidism. There were no fatal irAEs observed in the primary safety population and the rates of irAEs overall and Grade 3-4 irAEs are similar to that reported for other agents in this class, with the exception of hypothyroidism. The relatively higher incidence of hypothyroidism observed in the primary safety population (15%) is likely related to the high proportion of patients who received prior radiation as treatment for NPC.
L3351: No infusion-related reactions (IRRs) were reported in the primary safety population; 4% of patients in the pooled safety population had an IRR. All events were Grade 1-2 in severity.
L3352: In Study AK105-202, the most frequent AEs (\ge 10\%) were hypothyroidism, hepatitis, weight decreased, pyrexia, musculoskeletal pain, upper respiratory tract infection, and cough. The most frequent laboratory abnormalities (\ge 20\%) worsened from baseline were decreased phosphate, decreased sodium, decreased lymphocytes, increased triglycerides, increased gamma-glutamyl transferase, and decreased hemoglobin.
L3353: The safety profile of penpulimab is similar to other PD-(L)1 blocking antibodies when administered as single agents. The most significant adverse reactions include immune-related adverse events. Penpulimab appears to have an acceptable safety profile when assessed in the context of a life-threatening disease.
L3354: ## 9. Summary and Conclusions
L3355: ## 9.1. Statistical Issues
L3356: ## The FDA's Assessment
L3357: There were no major statistical issues identified during review of this application. In the primary efficacy population (n=111) enrolled in Study AK105-202, the confirmed ORR per IRC assessment was 30% (95% CI: 21%, 39%) at the DCO of February 3, 2021, and the tumor responses appeared durable with a median follow-up time of 11.9 months at the DCO of May 12, 2021. The treatment benefit appeared to be similar across all relevant subgroups, with the exception of patients with PD-L1 TPS &lt; 50%, in which the point estimate of the ORR was 21%.
L3358: Follow-up was not sufficient to estimate the median duration of response and the upper limit of its 95% confidence interval. In addition, there are limitations to interpretation of certain study results from a non-randomized study; the analyses of time-to-event endpoints such as PFS and OS are not interpretable in the absence of a comparator arm.
L3359: Study AK105-202 was conducted in a single country resulting in homogeneity of patient race and region. FDA analyses of marketing application data generally include subgroup analyses by
L3360: key demographic characteristics such as age, sex, race, and region, to assess the consistency of findings across these populations and whether the study results are applicable to the U.S. population. Given the population of Asian patients enrolled on this trial, FDA was not able to conduct subgroup analyses by race or region, resulting in the inability to examine the consistency of the trial results by these factors.
L3361: ## 9.2. Conclusions and Recommendations
L3362: ## The FDA's Assessment
L3363: Study AK105-202 is a single-arm, open-label study conducted solely in China that enrolled 130 patients with metastatic NPC with disease progression on platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other line of therapy and received penpulimab 200 mg Q2W. The primary outcome measure was ORR assessed by independent radiology review committee (IRRC) using Response Evaluation Criteria in Solid Tumors (RECIST) v1.1. The key secondary outcome measure was DOR. Among the 111 patients with measurable disease at baseline who had the opportunity for at least 8 months of follow-up who comprised the primary efficacy population, the ORR was 30% (95% CI: 21, 39) (data cut off February 3, 2021). The median DOR was not reached (range 3.8, 17.1+); 39% of the 33 responding patients had a DOR \ge 9 months. Given the approvals of toripalimab-tpzi during the review period for this application, the magnitude of effect of penpulimab on the ORR and DOR does not demonstrate a meaningful therapeutic benefit over currently available therapies for the treatment of patients with metastatic non-keratinizing NPC.
L3364: The safety profile of penpulimab is similar to that observed with other PD-1 blocking antibodies. Safety issues identified as significant and serious during the BLA review were immune-related adverse events and infusion-related reactions.
L3365: Study AK105-202 was conducted exclusively in China. Section 21 CFR 314.106(b) outlines three requirements to establish the acceptability of foreign data as the sole basis for marketing approval:
L3366: - The foreign data are applicable to the U.S. population and U.S. medical practice.
- The studies have been performed by clinical investigators of recognized competence.
- FDA is able to validate the data through an onsite inspection or other appropriate means.
L3367: FDA will apply this policy in a flexible manner according to the nature of the drug and data being evaluated.
L3368: At the time of submission of this BLA, a flexible regulatory approach was warranted given that there were no FDA-approved treatment options for the proposed population. The incidence of NPC is much higher in China relative to the United States, where NPC is a relatively rare disease, making enrollment to clinical trials more feasible in China. However, during the review of this BLA for penpulimab, FDA granted traditional approval to toripalimab-tpzi, another anti-PD-1 antibody, in combination with cisplatin and gemcitabine for the first-line treatment of adult
L3369: ## Penpulimab
L3370: patients with metastatic or recurrent, locally advanced NPC. This approval was based on a randomized (1:1) trial in 289 patients which demonstrated a 3.7-month improvement in progression-free survival (PFS) with the addition of toripalimab-tpzi to cisplatin and gemcitabine. This approval changed the standard of care for patients with metastatic or recurrent, locally advanced NPC, with the inclusion of anti-PD-1-based therapy in the first-line treatment setting. Toripalimab-tpzi also received traditional approval as a single agent for patients with unresectable or metastatic NPC with disease progression on or after a platinum-containing chemotherapy.
L3371: The Office of Pharmaceutical Quality (OPQ) also determined that the data submitted are not sufficient to support a conclusion that the manufacture of penpulimab-kcqx is well-controlled and will lead to a product that is pure and potent. See Section 3.2 for additional details.
L3372: The results from Study AK105-202 do not demonstrate a meaningful advantage over available therapy, given the recent approvals of toripalimab-tpzi for patients with NPC and therefore do not meet the requirements for accelerated approval as described in 21 CFR 314 subpart H.
L3373: Therefore, the FDA recommendation is Complete Response.

L3396: ## 13. Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategies (REMS)
L3397: ## The Applicant's Position
L3398: The risks of penpulimab could be managed by routine pharmacovigilance activities and labeling. REMS is not necessary to ensure that the benefits outweigh the risk for penpulimab for the following reasons:
L3399: - The benefit of penpulimab in the treatment of NPC was demonstrated in the pivotal study of AK105-202.
- The risks observed with penpulimab will be communicated through labeling. The risks associated with penpulimab are in part due to its inhibitory effects of PD-1. If approved, penpulimab will belong to a class with many other drugs (nivolumab, pembrolizumab, atezolizumab, durvalumab and avelumab, etc.) in which irAEs are labeled in Warnings and Precautions. The prescribers are likely aware of the risks of irAEs and the management with other drugs in this class.
- Sponsor has robust pharmacovigilance system, which includes data intake, case management, regulatory reporting, literature monitoring, signal management and aggregate reporting.
- In the postmarket setting, Akeso will conduct routine surveillance of the safety profile of penpulimab including all reports of adverse drug experiences and product compliant reports, and will revise RMP, RSI, and USPI if an evaluation of the safety data yields significant new information.
L3400: ## The FDA's Assessment
L3401: A risk evaluation and mitigation strategy (REMS) is not required given the Complete Response action.

Uncertainty and Variability (RSE, IIV, Shrinkage, Bootstrap)	All parameters were estimated with good precision (|\%RSE| < 30\%) except additive residual error (\%RSE of 59.7%). The magnitude of the IIV was moderate for CL (28 %CV) and V1 (19.6 %CV), but high for V2 (52.8%). IIV for Q was fixed to 0% due to large shrinkage. Residual variability was small (3.18 SD for additive error, 17.2% CV for proportional error). Eta and epsilon shrinkages were reasonable (<30\%) for diagnostics and EBE estimation of most parameters except V2 (52.8%).	Yes. Though V2 shrinkage is too high to accurately predict trough concentrations, an impact on the E-R analysis was not anticipated given the Applicant used observed concentrations as exposure metrics in these analyses.
BLQ for Parameter Accuracy	BLQ excluded in PPK analysis.
Figure 11 & Figure 12	Yes. Post infusion BLQ is <1\%.
Goodness of Fit (GOF) and Visual Predicted Check (VPC)	GOF plots showed good agreement between observed and model-predicted concentrations, though time-dependent effect was observed. VPC showed no apparent bias in the overall model fit till 140 day (10 doses). From 140 days, observations were above or close to the upper bound of the corresponding percentile of the predicted concentrations, suggesting an underprediction.	The PPK model underpredicted concentrations around steady state. Thus, FDA conducted the E-R analysis only using exposures prior to steady-state.
The PPK model can be used to compare PK between Chinese and US patients.

L3493: ## 19.4.2.3. *Review Issues and Reviewer's Independent Analysis*
L3494: (b) (4)
L3495: ## 19.4.2.3.2. Adequacy of ADA Evaluation
L3496: Two ADA assays were used to identify ADA positivity and titer. These data were not discriminated in PPK analysis. Pre-existing ADA was identified in &gt;5% of subjects in Study AK105-202 with titers of &gt;800 in some patients. Since ADA records were imputed as the prior ADA observation, most of the ADA records till 2nd infusion were the same as the baseline except for the pre-2nd dose record. Due to the false positivity ADA rate and the high titer of 800 (compared to the lowest titer of 50), these ADA records may impact PPK assessment. In addition, titer may have incremental impact on PK. As described in the decision tree (Figure 21), FDA conducted sensitivity analysis to evaluate the impact of ADA with assays of adequate sensitivity and specificity, evaluate treatment emergent ADA (treatment-induced and treatment-boosted), and titer levels in PPK analysis. Overall, ADA was not found to produce a statistically significant effect on PK.

L3526: ## 19.4.3.3. *Review Issues and Reviewer's Independent Analysis*
L3527: ## 19.4.3.3.1. Steady-State Exposures Are Not Applicable in the Applicant's ER Analysis
L3528: Two errors were identified in the Applicant's analysis: 1) The Applicant applied 200 mg Q2W when simulating steady-state concentrations for all subjects, including those who did not receive this dosage; 2) The Applicant's PPK model is not adequate to describe steady-state PK due to underprediction. Therefore, FDA only verified the E-R analysis with C1D1 EOI, C2D1 and C5D1 pre-dose concentrations as the exposure metrics.
L3529: ## 19.4.3.3.2. Discrepancies in the Subject Number (C2D1, C5D1) and p-value in ER for Safety Assessment Identified
L3530: The Applicant identified a positive relationship between C5D1 pre-dose concentration and 2 safety events of hematology (decreased neutrophil count and decreased WBC), and 1 safety event of clinical biochemistry (increased cholesterol). These TEAEs led to no dosage interruption or discontinuation.
L3531: FDA verified the E-R analysis and found discrepancies in the subject number and p-value from the Applicant's analysis. The discrepancies are due to exclusion of C2D1 pre-dose records and C10D1 (9 doses) of Study AK105-204; however, sample size reported in the Applicant's analysis enumerated exposure records including NAs. Of note, the cycle number of Study AK105-204 is different in that each dose is counted as a cycle compared to 2 doses counted as a cycle in other studies. The numbers of observed C2D1 pre-dose and C5D1 (and C10D1 of Study AK105-204 for similar number of doses) pre-dose concentrations were 330 and 187, respectively reported in FDA's analysis compared to 283 and 176, respectively used in the Applicant's analysis.
L3532: FDA was only able to confirm the positive relationship (p&lt;0.05) for decreased WBC (Figure 18). For this particular AE, multivariate logistic regression was performed which included age, sex, race, weight, albumin, baseline ECOG, LDH, ALP, AST as covariates. Stepwise covariate selection using AIC criteria reduced the model to C5D1 pre-dose as the only covariate. With inclusion of the imbalanced baseline covariates across quartiles of C5D1 pre-dose concentrations, namely age, sex, weight, and baseline ECOG, C5D1 pre-dose concentration was still identified as a significant covariate. Overall, given no dosage interruption or discontinuation, the positive relationship between C5D1 pre-dose and decreased WBC is not considered clinically meaningful.

L3617: Figure 1: A dose-response curve showing Median Fluorescence Intensity (MFI) versus Antibody Concentration (nM) for Penpulimab competing with PD-L1 for binding to Cell Surface PD-1. The Y-axis (MFI) ranges from 0 to 1000. The X-axis (Antibody Conc. (nM)) ranges from -2 to 3. Four curves are plotted: human IgG4 (diamond), Human IgG1DM (inverted triangle), Nivolumab (BMS) (circle), and AK105 (triangle). All curves show a dose-dependent decrease in MFI, indicating competition. Nivolumab (BMS) and AK105 show the steepest dose-response curves, reaching low MFI values around 1 nM.

L3628: Visual Predictive Check (VPC) plot for Penpulimab PPK Model in Log Scale. The X-axis is the Independent variable (time in hours, ranging from 0 to 25000). The Y-axis is the Dependent variable (penpulimab concentrations in µg/mL, ranging from 0 to 500). The plot shows individual observed concentrations (solid dots) and the predicted median (solid red line), along with the 5th and 95th percentiles (solid blue lines) and the 90% prediction interval (shaded area). The observed data generally follows the predicted median, indicating a good fit of the model.

L3638: Decision Tree for Evaluating ADA Impact:
Start: Same ADA assay?If Yes: ADA record is time-dependent and includes titer?If Yes: Does pre-existing ADA exceed 5%?If Yes: Generate treatment-induced and boosted ADA#.
If No: Applicable for evaluation with LOCF*&.

If No: Request information.

If No: Assays comparable in sensitivity & specificity?If Yes: ADA record is time-dependent and includes titer? (Loop back).
If No: Conduct sensitivity analysis to evaluate the ADA impact with study(ies) tested by ADA assay of adequate sensitivity & specificity.
